# Known Issues & Failed Approaches
- **Fixed 2026-09-28: SSH addition was hidden and required relay details.** Add disappeared while a dense inline form was open; inputs lacked visible borders, machine IDs were manual, and saving required private VPN addresses. Replace with a prominent Add action, styled short dialog, generated IDs, and independent SSH-only profiles. Loopback relay regression needs elevated execution in this sandbox; all nine backend cases pass there. The browser recheck initially retained a phone viewport where the desktop Settings sidebar is absent; restore desktop width before testing that entry point.

- **Unverified private PDF font corruption (2026-09-24):** screenshot shows incorrect glyphs in DAN while the external viewer is readable. The user declined file access, so the exact cause is unknown. Repair text bypasses browser font rendering through PDF.js glyph outlines; generated embedded-font fixture passes, but do not claim the private file is repaired.
- **Font-mode validation notes (2026-09-24):** browser file copies intentionally have new identities on each import; test persistence using a project-file path. Wait for smooth navigation/position saving and the new canvas after reload before capturing screenshots or closing. Reportlab was unavailable; generated the fixture with installed PyMuPDF instead.

- **Fixed 2026-09-24: right-side tools restarted on tab switches.** Conditional sibling panels remounted drafts/logs and replayed arrival animations; Files and Preview also used different frames. A shared frame with lazy-on-first-visit `PersistentPanel` bodies now hides inactive tools and preserves state. Project/PDF/thread keys still reset state at context boundaries. The regression note fixture initially missed its required rotation field; corrected before the production build.
- **Fixed 2026-09-24: local update option missing in installed Electron.** Node filesystem tests passed, but Electron treated `app.asar` as a virtual directory; archive stream reads failed and were silently treated as absent builds. Use `original-fs` for physical archive stats/hashes and test real Electron/ASAR behavior. Initial retry found app process identities had changed and correctly refused to guess; a later clean launch passed health/PDF checks.
- **Fixed in source 2026-09-24: Chinese PDF failed before rendering.** Raw Unicode `Content-Disposition` caused a Latin-1 encoding error/HTTP 500. FileResponse now encodes the filename. A second bug resolved relative decoder URLs from the worker directory, missing JBIG2 assets; absolute app-based URLs render scanned pages. Verified the actual 411-page book. Filename route matching still rejects embedded newlines (separate pre-existing edge case).
- **Fixed 2026-09-24: update selection and error dumps.** GitHub latest-release 404 exposed headers/stacks in Settings and obscured the prepared local build. Discover builds automatically and show concise release/access status. Automatic approval review blocked the initial installed-app replacement pending explicit authorization and flagged a `ps comm` path check; use full process arguments before a retry.
- **Fixed 2026-09-24: document drops and drafts.** Global file drops previously lacked a document-opening path. New text editors initially lost drafts when Notes unmounted Work; workspace-owned draft snapshots now retain them. Browser PDF identities cannot resolve in server OCR cache, so blob-backed PDFs skip those requests.
- **Document validation environment (2026-09-24):** sandbox denied loopback binding in the existing relay test; the reviewed run passed all eight document/remote tests. Anaconda lacked reportlab for the fixture; the installed pdf-lib generated the test PDF instead.
- **Installed-app browser acceptance interruption (2026-09-23):** backend PID 16429 disappeared after Codex extracted the fixture citation; the app/backend reappeared as PIDs 21018/21027. The polling runner recorded connection refusal. Do not count the partial run as success or infer a browser failure; rerun on the healthy instance.
- **Browser app acceptance (2026-09-23):** the installed app’s default native Claude account returned “Not logged in · Please run /login” before any browser action. This is an account setup blocker, not a browser-controller failure; do not claim native Claude live acceptance until sign-in succeeds. The closed-window fixture initially closed only one of two tabs; corrected it to close all tabs, then verified reopen behavior.
- **Remote access acceptance (2026-09-23):** ny's active UFW blocks VPN peers on new relay port 8765, although ny-local authenticated forwarding works. Its adam account has no passwordless sudo and root SSH is unavailable; the exact private-only rule is in [6-1](plans/6-1-private-relay.md). Initial browser tests used a temporary SSH SOCKS path. Resolved: user applied the private-only rule, and direct VPN API/browser checks passed; temporary proxy removed. Physical-phone/cellular acceptance remains.
- **Remote live-model probe (2026-09-23):** `backend=codex` selects the older CLI adapter, not Workbench's independent source/profile adapter. Corrected live probes use `native_codex`; both native Astra and OpenRouter DeepSeek then returned the expected reply. Claude uses `backend=claude`.
- **Fixed in source 2026-09-23: browser chat integration.** Default GUI capability discovery, a native-lead browser bridge, run isolation/cleanup, desktop-aware visibility, and transient search interaction now work. Live Codex → Google Scholar → exact BibTeX passed. The earlier audit below records the pre-fix behavior. Existing backends must restart; native Claude/Antigravity live acceptance was not run.
- **Browser acceptance environment (2026-09-23):** sandbox loopback binding failed with EPERM before the browser test began. The reviewed unsandboxed acceptance runner passed fixture search/citation/download and live Google Scholar export. This was an execution-environment restriction, not a DAN failure.
- **Remote service validation (2026-09-23):** systemd rejects shell-style quotes around `WorkingDirectory=` and `EnvironmentFile=` as nonabsolute paths. Keep those directives unquoted (escape specifiers), while quoting `Environment` and `ExecStart` arguments. Retrying the same uploaded release reuses its files/venv. Mini already had a listener on 8765; DAN now uses target port 18765, with relay port 8765. Initial installs preflight address/port availability, and rollback preserves the original failure instead of masking it with an older invalid-unit error.
- **Mini dependency installation (2026-09-23):** PyPI package downloads were too slow despite working SSH/index access. Tsinghua mirror completed all 47 dependencies; the profile now records package source. An optional wheelhouse bundle supports networks without reliable package downloads.

- **Browser chat integration audit (2026-09-23):** normal GUI requests do not supply browser capability packs; Super DAN dispatch selects no `browser_*` tools without an explicit pack (all 11 appear with `browser_control`). Shell automation remains possible. Native leads receive team/process bridges, not a DAN browser bridge. Browser sessions default to headless; inspect/transient policy exposes navigation but reserves clicks/fill/download for external-write scope. Scholar-to-BibTeX acceptance remains unverified. Repository `.venv` lacks Playwright; `/opt/anaconda3/bin/python` has it, so dependency readiness must be checked per execution environment.
- **Remote bootstrap (2026-09-23):** a shared SSH ControlMaster socket stalled new mini sessions/uploads; `ControlPath=none` avoids inheriting it. Direct Mac→mini VPN uploads also proved slow; SSH bootstrap through the profile's relay is now configurable/default. Existing alias/VPN services were left unchanged.
- **Remote validation (2026-09-23):** Starlette TestClient WebSocket URLs default to testserver despite a custom HTTP base URL; specify the WS authority in origin tests. `/usr/local/bin/python3` lacks pytest; use the installed Anaconda `pytest` executable.
- **Fixed 2026-09-22: headless server catalog missed `.env` credentials.** `python -m dan.server`/direct app startup did not load `.env`, so the model catalog reported OpenRouter unavailable even with `DAN_LLM_API_KEY` and an OpenRouter `DAN_LLM_BASE_URL` present. Load environment before server configuration and app creation; regression verifies configured status without returning the key.
- **Model-selection validation notes 2026-09-22:** the steering fixture inherited an unwritable account-home workspace; set its surface workspace to `tmp_path`. The old UI test matched “Model source” when looking for “Model”; use the exact label. An unused legacy model setter failed typecheck and was removed. An initially guessed backend-test filename did not exist; the actual server suite passes. npx package resolution stalled and racing browser opens invalidated a session; use the cached Playwright CLI with a unique session.
- **Packaging workaround 2026-09-21:** electron-builder's runtime download failed because sandbox DNS could not reach GitHub. Packaging succeeded with `-c.electronDist=node_modules/electron/dist -c.npmRebuild=false`, using the installed Electron runtime; signed bundle and launched backend were verified.
- **Icon export workaround 2026-09-21:** `iconutil -c icns` rejected the `sips`-generated standard iconset as invalid. Packaged the resized PNG representations directly into ICNS chunks with Python's standard library; `iconutil -c iconset` successfully decoded the resulting asset. ICO likewise uses PNG entries.
- **Failed approach 2026-09-21: finding/calling Jev like a chat model.** `typesafe/jev-*` is not listed by OpenRouter `/api/v1/models` (even authenticated), and `/chat/completions` rejects it (“is a decisions model”). Use `POST https://openrouter.ai/api/alpha/decisions` with model `jev-latest`; `typesafe/jev-latest` is rejected there, `~typesafe/jev-latest` and `typesafe/jev-1.13` work. See [4-3](plans/4-3-token-usage-analyzer.md).
- **Known limit 2026-09-21: token analyzer estimates.** Tool-result and prompt tokens are chars/4; attachments and injected reminders are not attributed (shown as “not attributed”, 8–21% on real sessions). Model-call totals are exact.
- **Fixed in source 2026-09-20: native/legacy sessions retained placeholder titles.** The earlier API-only naming fix missed direct store writes and existing records. Shared-store naming covers append/snapshot writes and read-only legacy recovery.
- **Fixed in source 2026-09-20: project creation/switching made unwanted blank chats.** Removed implicit session creation and cross-project last-thread fallback for empty projects.
- **Fixed in source 2026-09-20: older checkpoint queue receipts escaped transcript filtering.** Recognize “Queued for checkpoint append” in the legacy pair matcher; Up next starts expanded. Installed clients still require deployment.
- **Browser validation limitation (2026-09-20):** preview server startup on 127.0.0.1:4188 failed with sandbox EPERM; approval escalation unavailable in this turn. Unit tests and production build passed; no live browser acceptance claimed.
- **Fixed in source 2026-09-20: native Steer was permanently disabled.** Codex exec had no input channel and the composer forcibly chose continuation. Runtime-backed Codex leads now use app-server steering with a capability gate; rejected inputs remain queued. Live authenticated installed-app acceptance remains pending.
- **Steering validation notes (2026-09-20):** initial custom queue event failed the event enum; use existing status_reported. Initial test queued before startup and was admitted at the initial checkpoint; enqueue after live readiness to test mid-turn delivery. Extracting the queue keeps the shell within its bundle budget. A mistyped test filename caused a no-tests run; the corrected suite passes.
- **Fixed 2026-09-20: follow-up promotion was not always FIFO.** Queue position is derived from current waiting count per lane, so ranks are reused. Sorting those ranks at terminal promotion let a newer rank-2 message pass an older rank-3 message. Use append-only persisted list order, matching the displayed follow-up order; regression covers partial drains and reload.
- **Fixed 2026-09-20: session titles followed the latest request.** Default named sessions never received a durable first-request title, while cards substituted the latest task text on every refresh. Persist initial names and display saved titles. An old test intentionally expected the former substitution and was updated to assert stable names with changing progress.
- **Activity summary validation (2026-09-20):** initial event fixtures omitted required `event_type`; corrected before successful production build. Browser raw-event inspection must expand both the outer Work details disclosure and its nested event.
- **Queue transcript validation (2026-09-20):** TypeScript rejected an underspecified queue fixture after runtime tests passed. Added the complete task/queue fields; build and budgets then passed.
- **Attachment validation (2026-09-20):** run npm checks from `editor/` and use paths relative to that directory; the initial root invocation and doubled `editor/` test path failed before tests ran. Corrected invocations pass.
- **Stop/resend browser validation (2026-09-20):** initial browser check could not connect because the prior dev server had exited. Restarted the isolated server and reran successfully.
- **Fixed in source 2026-09-20: Stop hid subsequent continuation work.** Promoting a queued follow-up retained the previous run's stop flags on the task; terminal-event matching also accepted the old run by shared task ID. Clear interruption flags when assigning a new run and prefer run identity for event matching. The reported two later transcript placeholders have no run IDs: the client previously treated any admission without a run as queued. It now shows the admission question/status/reason and clears pending state. Installed-app acceptance remains pending; live records were not changed.
- **Latest-request edit validation (2026-09-20):** initial eager implementation exceeded the workspace shell budget by about 0.2 kB; lazy-loading settings and request actions resolves it. A timer-based mocked stream completion was unreliable during resend setup; the browser fixture now explicitly delivers completion after admission. The answer also appears in activity detail, so assertions target the first visible answer match.
- **Palette validation notes (2026-09-20):** settings persistence tests need a browser environment because Zustand storage is disabled without `window`; moved the existing settings-store test to happy-dom. Browser checks must wait for the settings dialog and locate the select by its accessible combobox name; immediate option counts and exact label-text lookup were unreliable with nested option text.

- **Desktop update release boundary (2026-09-20):** this Mac has no Developer ID signing identity ; the private GitHub release channel is now configured but no releases exist. Local verified bundles can be installed with the new handoff; online delivery requires a configured signed release channel. The Python backend remains separately installed. Initial eager import of update settings exceeded the shell budget; lazy loading resolved it.
- **Fixed 2026-09-20: saved queues remained stranded after restart.** Recovery stopped orphaned runs and requeued injected messages but launched no consumer. Server lifespan now resumes those queues automatically. Account/model overrides previously lived only in memory; persist execution configuration and recover older native profiles from saved lead records. Startup tests use isolated stores; the repository venv has no pytest, so run the installed system pytest.
- **Fixed 2026-09-19: injected messages stayed in the composer queue.** Follow-up rows marked injected had `active: false`; filtering only on active retained them. Composer now filters waiting statuses before applying any history display limit, preserving delivered records in history. The first regression fixture omitted required queue `task_id`/`position` fields; TypeScript caught this and the fixture was corrected.

- **Validation notes (2026-09-19):** the first backend test invocation inherited the active app’s `DAN_GRAPHS_DIR`; import-time server recovery attempted a write and the sandbox denied it. Rerun all server tests with an isolated temporary graphs directory. Initial native-child replay missed shared session storage because it compared a resolved rollout path against an unresolved symlink root; resolve both roots before enforcing containment.

- **Team display integration gap (2026-09-19), fixed in source:** two real built-in Codex review subagents ran, but the current DAN run returned `workers: []`. The panel polls NativeTeam records only; built-in collaboration is not mapped into those records. No `.dan-team-*` bridge existed for this turn. The new observer reads typed parent-linked Codex rollout events independently of enabled Team delegates. Deployment/live installed-app acceptance remains pending.
- **Cursor fallback exclusions (2026-09-19):** workspace `allComposers` fallback can reintroduce chats excluded by global `composerHeaders` flags; a reviewer reproduced a globally marked subagent being listed. The fallback only checks its local archived flag. Split transcripts also silently stop at 20,000 bubble headers. Fixes pending.

- **Fixed 2026-09-19: Work displayed "Codex · CLI default running" with the app account selected.** The status string used the legacy model selection's fixed CLI-default label, while execution independently sends the actual account/model in `lead_profile`. Removed routine running/queued text and the empty status row; loading/errors and terminal failure feedback remain.

- **Native worker limitations (2026-09-17):** local Claude 2.1.66 lacks documented headless fast support; agy 1.2.5 is now installed but requires sign-in. Antigravity documents interactive /fork, not a verified headless fork endpoint, so import is disabled rather than continuing the source. Live authenticated worker/fork acceptance remains pending.
- **Fixed 2026-09-17: isolated HOME hid codexx accounts.** Resolve its config from the OS user's home and expand configured ~/ paths against that home; keep each subprocess's CODEX_HOME/CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR separate.
- **Validation notes (2026-09-17):** the model-menu assertion initially expected only Default/Kimi; updated it for the requested OpenRouter choices and added payload routing coverage. Initial NativeWorkerActivity import was unused until the actual conversation render branch was wired; corrected before build verification.

- **Fixed 2026-09-17: Create project remained disabled after folder selection.** The name field was independently required; it now defaults to the folder basename until manually edited.

- **Fixed 2026-09-17: unassigned legacy chats appeared as a fake Scratch project and every new chat used its bucket.** New chats use the project ID; old unassigned chats render as plain Other chats and retain their original storage paths. Browser/desktop registry synchronization remains a separate open persistence gap.

- **Fixed 2026-09-17: custom Work header lacked a native drag region.** Electron uses hiddenInset, so custom header content must opt into app-region:drag for conventional dragging/double-click zoom. Buttons/inputs/dialogs opt out to preserve interactions.

- **Folder-drop platform limit (2026-09-17):** browser File/directory entries do not expose absolute local paths; never fill the field with an invented path based on entry.name/fullPath. Electron resolves the dropped File through webUtils and verifies the path is a directory. Browser failure preserves the existing path.
- **Keyboard implementation note (2026-09-17):** React KeyboardEvent exposes IME state on nativeEvent.isComposing, not directly; corrected the type-check failure during wheel chord implementation.

- **Fixed 2026-09-17: project management opened both legacy settings and Files, while the shelf duplicated empty messages.** Work now uses a focused project dialog and conventional sidebar with one search-empty state. When restricting the old header to Notes, TypeScript narrowed legacy Work comparisons to unreachable branches; the render gate uses a boolean condition while the Notes toolbar remains intact.

- **P2 validation environment: existing HTML sanitizer tests fail under the installed happy-dom/DOMPurify combination (2026-09-16).** The full editor suite reports two failures in unchanged `sanitizeHtml.test.ts` (event-handler stripping and safe data-attribute retention). Workbench/workspace tests pass, and equivalent sanitizer assertions pass in Chromium. Investigate the DOM emulation/dependency mismatch; do not weaken the sanitizer assertions to obtain a green suite.
- **Fixed 2026-09-16: switcher Tab focus could leave the native dialog at the last control.** Native modal behavior alone was insufficient for the intended in-app keyboard loop; explicit forward/backward wrapping and focus restoration now pass browser checks. Also give session swipe rows an opaque background so the underlying Archive action does not bleed through at rest.
- **Workbench validation notes (2026-09-16):** run npm scripts from `editor/`; the repository root has no package.json. Restricted loopback bind required approved execution. The Playwright CLI sandbox does not expose the Node `URL` global in route callbacks; use the request URL string or browser evaluation. Captures must wait for the 200ms entrance animation to settle.

- ~~**P1: The old test allowlist hid legacy files from the pre-archive Git snapshot.**~~ **FIXED 2026-08-02.** The repository ignored `/tests/*` and selectively re-included named tests, leaving 357 obsolete Python tests and nine snapshot/scenario assets physically present but absent from commit `7972238a`. The cutover force-added and pushed those files in `8594143c` and `0d630dca` before removing them, then replaced the allowlist with ordinary cache/result ignores. A plain repository-level `pytest` now collects only the retained suite and passes.
- **Failed approach recorded:** `git status` without ignored files is not sufficient proof that a destructive archive snapshot is complete. Before deletion, audit ignored source-like files and test assets, back up any missed material, and avoid source allowlists in `.gitignore`.

- ~~**P1: The first archive boundary removed runtime modules still used lazily by active Super DAN tools.**~~ **FIXED 2026-08-02.** The retained `shell_command` imports the subprocess guardrail only when sandboxed execution begins, and browser/desktop/notification tools imported small policy/config helpers only when invoked. The cutover restored the shell guardrail as an active dependency, moved browser/app allowlist helpers into `dan.tools.control_policy`, and made webhook notification configuration local to the tool.
- **Failed approach recorded:** import-time smoke tests and capability discovery do not prove lazily invoked tools are dependency-complete. Archive reviews must inspect dynamic imports and exercise representative tool calls before declaring a support module obsolete.

- **P3 environment note: frontend test filters are relative to `editor/`, validation script names come from `editor/package.json`, and sandboxed process/listen diagnostics may be unavailable.** The first focused collection-page test command used the repo-prefixed `editor/src/...` path while already running from `editor/`, so Vitest correctly found no test files; the corrected `src/...` filter passed. A diagnostic `ps` probe and the first restricted Vite loopback bind were denied by the command sandbox. The approved local preview then started on an available port, and the real in-app-browser collection smoke passed without console errors. The guessed `check:workspace-bundle` script also did not exist; the documented `bundle:check` command passed.
- **Failed approach recorded:** do not prepend `editor/` to Vitest filters when the command working directory is already `editor/`, do not guess validation script names when `package.json` is available, and do not treat sandbox-denied process enumeration or loopback binding as an application failure; prefer bounded health/readiness probes and use the disclosed local-preview authority for browser acceptance.

- ~~**P2: Notes rendered Hugo `paperPDF` shortcodes as inline code.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-28.** The generic Hugo-preview fallback intentionally converted unknown shortcodes into safe code text, but `paperPDF` had no dedicated renderer even though the PDF existed in `static/papers` and Hugo rendered it correctly. Notes now parses only the canonical filename and height arguments, resolves the file through the root-contained workspace preview endpoint, and renders a bounded PDF iframe with an explicit open control.
- **Failed approach recorded:** allowing arbitrary iframe HTML through the shared Markdown sanitizer would broaden the trust boundary for every rendered message. Rich Hugo features should be promoted one narrow shortcode contract at a time and resolved through existing contained file routes.

- ~~**P1: Shell rollback and Git guards had command-shape bypasses.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-28.** A command containing any `mv` token previously exempted every missing protected path from catastrophic-loss rollback, even when the move concerned a different file, and `git restore FILE` / `git checkout REV -- FILE` were not covered by the Git preflight. Move exemptions are now resolved to the direct command's actual source paths, while checkout/restore/reset/clean variants fail before execution. Adversarial tests cover an unrelated move plus scripted deletion, a valid protected-file move, Git variants, and harmless prose containing Git words.
- **Failed approach recorded:** command-level mutation labels are too broad for rollback exemptions. Recovery decisions must bind the allowed operation to exact resource paths, and destructive version-control families must be classified by parsed command position rather than one narrow spelling.

- ~~**P1: Human-assist benchmark acceptance could pass placeholder market code and unrelated artifacts.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-28.** The market gate previously accepted identity functions and `assert True` tests because it inspected names/keywords without executing the tests; the capability score counted any changed paths without matching required outputs or invoking the domain gate. Market tests now require backtest imports plus substantive leakage/cost/walk-forward calls and assertions, execute under bounded pytest, and feed the domain verdict into capability scoring. Required outputs are matched by declared path. Academic ledgers also require manuscript claim links and resolvable supported source refs. The flagship rubric now resolves cited screenshots under the rubric directory and rejects missing, empty, invalid-signature, unsupported, or path-escaping evidence refs instead of trusting viewport-shaped filenames.
- **Failed approach recorded:** filenames, function names, keywords, and artifact counts are useful preflight evidence but cannot stand in for behavioral execution, required-path identity, or provenance linkage.

- ~~**P2: Atomic new-file creation changed normal workspace permissions to `0600`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-28.** The first atomic helper used `mkstemp`, whose private temporary mode survived publication for new targets. Sibling temporaries now use exclusive random names with creation mode `0666`, letting the process umask produce the same effective permission as ordinary `open(..., "w")`; existing targets still preserve their mode bits.
- **Failed approach recorded:** atomicity does not excuse metadata regressions. Temporary-file creation policy must distinguish private scratch data from a temporary inode intended to become a normal workspace file.

- ~~**P1: Write-capable live workers could destroy inspected files through direct shell mutation.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-27.** During the premium-site repair, a builder validated the correct 193-line document, then ran BSD-incompatible `sed -i '' '0,/<\\/html>/!d' index.html` and truncated it to zero bytes. Super DAN now rejects direct in-place `sed`/`perl`, `truncate`, shell deletion, destructive Git cleanup/restoration, and redirection-over-existing-file mutations; structured `file_write`/`file_edit` replacements are fully written and synced to a sibling temporary file before atomic replacement; and the local runtime snapshots inspected or explicitly named files up to 16 MiB within a 128 MiB envelope, rolling back failed commands or successful commands that delete/empty/catastrophically shrink protected content. Rollbacks emit a first-class `tool.rolled_back` event and do not count restored paths as material workspace changes.
- **Failed approach recorded:** prompt-level preference for `file_edit` is not a safety boundary. Mutation safety must be enforced below the model through atomic structured tools, shell preflight, exact-byte recovery state, and an observable rollback result. Arbitrary successful project scripts can still make semantically wrong non-catastrophic changes, so tests/review remain required; the runtime does not claim filesystem isolation for every possible subprocess side effect.

- **P3 environment note: loopback browser acceptance is blocked by the default restricted command sandbox.** The first 2026-07-27 opt-in Chromium run failed before browser launch because `ThreadingHTTPServer` could not bind `127.0.0.1`. Re-running the exact suite with approved loopback/browser authority passed all 18 tests in 15.48 seconds and regenerated both flagship reports. No application defect was implicated.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a real-browser test that serves local artifacts is not a pure filesystem test. When loopback bind is denied, report the environment boundary and rerun with the disclosed local-browser authority instead of treating the permission error as an artifact failure.

- **P2: The final RTS visual-repair run did not settle within its 20-minute outer limit.** The provider timed out repeatedly across builder, repair, and validator calls. A material engine improvement was persisted and independently tested, but the internal second validator never produced a trustworthy terminal pass; the run was stopped after 342,852 tokens and judged from its last materialized artifact.
- **Failed approach recorded:** repeatedly retrying a broad visual brief inside the same growing context is expensive and does not guarantee convergence. Bound visual repair by one focused mutation pass plus external screenshot review, compact or reset the repair context, and let the independent gate reject the artifact without further retries.

- ~~**P2: A scoped “read-only checks” instruction could forbid the edits it was meant to validate.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-25.** The operator asked for explicit HTML/CSS repairs and then said to use read-only checks after the edits. The intent parser matched bare `read-only` anywhere in the objective and downgraded the entire run to `workspace_read`. Policy resolution now recognizes after/following/once-scoped read-only checks, validation, or verification when the same objective explicitly requests mutation; genuine no-edit/read-only requests remain protected.
- **Failed approach recorded:** permission classifiers cannot treat every occurrence of `read-only` as document-wide scope. Temporal and stage qualifiers matter, especially in edit-then-validate workflows.

- ~~**P1: Binary screenshots could be replayed as candidate source and corrupt terminal persistence.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-25.** A premium-site repair generated PNG screenshots inside the workspace. Timeout recovery decoded those binary files with replacement characters and embedded them in the final candidate, preventing the Chat V2 terminal event from persisting cleanly. Materialized-source snapshots now accept only valid UTF-8 text files up to 256,000 bytes and skip binary, non-UTF-8, and oversized artifacts.
- **Failed approach recorded:** “generated in the workspace” does not imply “safe provider source context.” Candidate recovery needs text/size gates before reading artifacts back into model or event payloads.

- ~~**P1: A narrow `file_edit` could append an entire document below the requested range.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-25.** A premium-site repair aimed a whole HTML document at lines 1–5, duplicating the page below the first valid `</html>` while its own weak validator still passed. The edit guard now rejects near-whole-file replacement content aimed at a narrow range when it begins with the original prefix; a focused regression reproduces the exact failure shape.
- **Failed approach recorded:** range width alone does not reveal a malformed replacement. Compare the proposed content with the existing document prefix and reject a whole-document payload before writing.

- ~~**P2: Flagship acceptance could trust weak static markers and post-restart screenshots.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-25.** Early premium checks treated SVG namespace text and `-apple-system` as remote-brand evidence, strict Playwright locators crashed on duplicate CTA/state nodes, scroll-reveal sections photographed blank before they entered the viewport, and the RTS screenshot was captured only after restart. The harness now parses actual remote runtime refs, ignores the system-font token, checks duplicate selectors explicitly, scrolls every story section before judging paint, ignores recoverable worker failures when locating terminal settlement, and stages representative live RTS play after the restart check for screenshot evidence.
- **Failed approach recorded:** keyword presence, self-reported validation, and a single convenient screenshot state are not sufficient artifact evidence. Tests must exercise the user-visible state transitions that the rubric is meant to judge.

- ~~**P2: The capability scorer could not faithfully score the runner's exported Agent trace.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-24.** The historical scorer expected raw `.dan-super` rows with `event`, top-level timestamps, raw `usage`, and mutation fields. The flagship runner intentionally exports the durable Chat V2 event stream, which uses `type`, `artifact_refs`, `token_usage_delta`, and nested raw payload timing/validation. The scorer now understands both shapes, and the flagship CLI includes its result in the same hard pass/fail report as browser, rubric, and steering evidence.
- **Failed approach recorded:** two individually valid reports are not an authoritative acceptance decision if they consume different trace dialects and neither gates the other. Normalize the durable evidence once and require every score in one final verdict.

- ~~**P1: Flagship steering could trigger on an imprecise artifact event or a terminal poll.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-24.** Raw Super DAN `tool.completed` rows for `file_write`, `file_edit`, and shell commands with workspace diffs were normalized as generic `tool_used`, while broad worker/hook event families could appear as `artifact_changed` without a concrete path. The flagship poller could also return immediately on an artifact row before noticing a later terminal row in the same snapshot. Mutation-tool normalization now emits deduplicated concrete artifact refs, the trigger requires those refs, and any terminal event in the observed snapshot suppresses injection.
- **Failed approach recorded:** event labels alone are not a durable proof of material progress, and event order inside a completed polling snapshot does not make an intervention live. Steering gates need resource-level evidence plus a non-terminal observation boundary.

- ~~**P3: The first flagship browser-acceptance references emitted a missing-favicon console error.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-24.** Both otherwise-valid reference artifacts loaded and passed their interactions, but Chrome requested an undeclared favicon and logged a 404, so the clean-console gate correctly rejected them. The frozen prompts, static contract, and reference fixtures now require a local or data-URL favicon; both desktop/phone Chromium cases pass on rerun.
- **Failed approach recorded:** do not whitelist a generic browser resource 404 merely because it is probably a favicon. A polished artifact should declare the asset and keep the same zero-console-error standard used for functional resources.

- ~~**P1: Workspace-read-only filtering removed explicitly authorized transient browser navigation.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-24.** The Super TUI correctly admitted `browser_open` for a structured `browser_control + transient_execute` probe, but the shared tool loop later inferred workspace-read-only from the prompt and removed every action-like tool, so the provider's first browser-open call was rejected while later extraction still ran. The runtime now restores only the UI tools granted by structured surface policy: transient scope permits `browser_open`, external-write scope is still required for click/fill/type/select/download and desktop input, and no prompt text grants authority.
- **Failed approach recorded:** one undifferentiated read-only tool list conflates workspace mutation with transient UI control. Apply authority per resource/action domain and restore capabilities only from typed policy, never from prose.

- ~~**P2: Final frontend/browser revalidation was blocked on local dependency restoration.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-24.** The data volume reached 100% and rejected zero-byte writes while the final post-composer Vitest run was creating transform files; 400 tests passed before 17 suites stopped on `ENOSPC`, with no assertion failure observed. Regenerable task artifacts were removed, including `mobile/build`, Vite/Playwright caches, the frontend `dist`, and finally ignored `editor/node_modules`, restoring normal writes. The exact lockfile tree was restored with install scripts disabled; the full suite then passed 439/439, production bundle verification passed, and real Chromium confirmed the repaired 390x844 composer plus 1440x900 desktop, Work/Notes switching, legacy-route normalization, exact page widths, and a clean console. The preserved `mobile/dist/dan-phone.apk` was not removed.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a nominal 100 MiB shown by APFS was still below the filesystem's effective writable reserve, and removing a stale 18 MiB headless Playwright profile did not restore writes. Treat an actual write probe—not `df` alone—as the capacity gate, and keep multiple GiB free before browser installs or full frontend runs.

- ~~**P1: Checkpoint steering could be lost after queue claim.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-24.** The store previously changed an append item to `injected` before the next provider request consumed it, the backend drained that item into process memory, restart recovery did not requeue the lease, and `model.responded` could claim a steer after the final useful model round. Queue items now have an observable queued/injected/completed lifecycle, keep their run lease until a successful model-response acknowledgement, requeue interrupted leases, stay visible while in flight, exclude terminal response rows from admission, and promote terminal-race appends to a continuation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** queue claim is not delivery. Durable steering needs a persisted acknowledgement boundary tied to the successful model call, plus explicit recovery for every unacknowledged lease.

- ~~**P2: The phone-width Work composer clipped its model/action controls.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-24.** DOM-level page overflow stayed at 390 px, but the first 390x844 screenshot showed the inner composer control row clipping its trailing model control. The redundant one-option Agent dropdown is now removed, the row can wrap, and the model control carries the fixed `DAN` identity. The final real-browser screenshot shows New, Next, Auto, DAN · Default, and Send fully visible; document/body width remains exactly 390 px with no console warnings or errors.
- **Failed approach recorded:** document-level `scrollWidth` is not sufficient visual evidence for nested overflow-hidden controls. Responsive acceptance must include screenshot inspection after metrics.

- ~~**P2: Authenticated live MVP acceptance was pending explicit outbound-provider approval.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-07-25.** The operator explicitly approved sending both frozen prompts, relevant repository context, tool outputs, and generated artifact context to `https://api.moonshot.ai/v1` with the configured DAN credential. Both live cases were executed and independently scored; the premium site passed, while the playable RTS was rejected by the visual rubric.
- **Failed approach recorded:** do not route around a denied authenticated server launch through a different CLI or indirect subprocess. Live-provider evaluation must use the same disclosed authority boundary.

- **P3: Installing the newest Playwright Chromium exceeded available disk space.** The pinned 178.4 MiB archive exhausted a volume with roughly 217 MiB free near 90% download. The failed install left a 96 MiB temporary archive, which was removed immediately, and the existing in-app Chromium path completed the same production browser checks without another installation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** check free disk and existing browser-control surfaces before downloading another full browser. Do not retry the newest Playwright browser install while the data volume has less than a safe installation margin.

- ~~**P3: Workspace-only selector narrowing briefly failed TypeScript parsing.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-24.** The first selector edit closed `DEFAULT_MODEL_SELECTION_BY_AGENT` with `];` instead of `};`; `npm run build` caught the syntax error before runtime, and the corrected build and full selector regression now pass.
- **Failed approach recorded:** even small option-catalog removals need an immediate TypeScript build, because a focused behavioral test cannot run through a parse error.

- **P3: The current development environment does not expose a `ruff` executable.** A targeted lint invocation failed with `command not found`; Python compilation plus the complete modified worker test file and focused control-plane tests pass. Install or expose the repository's preferred Python linter before treating lint as an available local gate.
- **Failed approach recorded:** check the configured toolchain before naming a lint command as validation evidence; a missing executable is not a code-quality result.

- ~~**P3: Imported math manuals depended on pseudo-math and renderer inference.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-21.** The five copied math manuals mixed inline-code notation, plain ASCII operators, ad hoc matrix literals, formula-shaped text fences, and three calculus practice packs accidentally wrapped as code. Their knowledge-base copies now use explicit `$...$` and `$$...$$` LaTeX that passes strict KaTeX and live Notes rendering checks, while all learner-facing section and assessment markers remain preserved.
- **Failed approach recorded:** renderer heuristics are useful for legacy notes but are not a stable source format for maintained course manuals. Canonical math content should carry explicit delimiters and LaTeX commands in Markdown.

- ~~**P3: Notes KaTeX `aligned` blocks could render `amp;` beside equals signs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-09.** Some Notes preview paths could hand KaTeX HTML-escaped math such as `X&amp;=XXX`; TeX then treated the leading `&` as the alignment marker and rendered the remaining `amp;` text before `=`, producing visibly broken aligned equations. The shared Markdown renderer now decodes common HTML entities inside math input before KaTeX rendering, with a regression covering escaped `aligned` ampersands.
- **Failed approach recorded:** do not fix this by asking note authors to avoid `&` in `aligned`. The preview boundary must preserve TeX semantics even when earlier Hugo/HTML normalization escaped the source.

- ~~**P3: Hugo callout shortcodes rendered as literal inline-code text or generic quote blocks in Notes.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-06.** The desktop Notes formatter and backend preview helper treated every unknown Hugo shortcode as an inline-code placeholder, so `{{< callout warning "Critical Caveat" >}}...{{< /callout >}}` became visible `callout warning "Critical Caveat"` and `/callout` tokens around the prose. The first fix mapped callouts onto generic blockquotes, which still did not read as callouts. Known callout shortcodes now normalize to first-class `aside` callout cards with the kind/title label, marker, and body preserved.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a generic shortcode-to-code fallback is useful for unknown shortcode visibility, but content-bearing shortcodes need explicit normalization before the fallback runs.

- ~~**P3: Notes currency ranges could be captured as inline math and break bold/table prose.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-06.** The shared Markdown renderer pre-rendered any single-dollar span as KaTeX before Marked parsed bold text or table cells. Escaped business currency such as `\$2M–\$4M` and adjacent unescaped amounts such as `$2M–$4M vs. ~$300K` could therefore split the bold span or table figure and leak markers/backslashes in Notes preview. The inline math pass now skips escaped dollars, rejects currency-collision spans, leaves SaaS as prose instead of auto-code, and has direct table regressions for market-sizing dollar figures.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a plain regex over `$...$` is too broad for mixed prose notes. Inline math detection needs delimiter awareness and currency guards before Markdown structure is parsed.

- ~~**P3: Imported course manuals mixed knowledge with generation/runtime scaffolding.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-05.** The copied Learning Assistant manuals still contained utility material such as source refs, knowledge-point inventories, recursive expansion ledgers, teacher editions, learner derivative headings, session mappings, runtime loop ids, verifier metadata, audit-source notes, and benchmark blueprint machinery. The knowledge-base copies now keep the learner-facing manual content only: course spines, chapter maps, detailed knowledge, examples, practice, solutions, and final-readiness checks.
- **Failed approach recorded:** preserving every copied artifact inside the readable manual makes the note feel like a build log rather than a knowledge pool. Runtime/session/source/audit material should live in sidecars or audit files, while the manual body stays clean study content.

- ~~**P3: Imported math manuals rendered algebra notation as code chips.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-05.** The Learning Assistant manuals used inline backticks for textbook notation such as `ab in P`, `IJ subset P`, `I cap J`, and `R/P`. The shared Markdown renderer only sent `$...$` delimiters to KaTeX, so Notes preview showed these expressions as monospace code instead of math. Math-enabled Notes pages now render math-like code spans through KaTeX, while source ids and file paths remain code.
- **Failed approach recorded:** converting every explicit code span globally would break technical notes and Work answers. The math-code reinterpretation must be gated by note frontmatter (`math: true`) and conservative notation detection.

- ~~**P2: Ambiguous website-learning prompts were write-capable.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-05.** The wording "please learn this website, then let me know what to improve" did not explicitly authorize mutation, but Chat V2 recomputed the backend run as `workspace_change` with optional mutation permission. Workspace requests now default to `workspace_read` with forbidden mutation and no shell permission unless the operator explicitly asks for mutation or artifact-producing action.
- **Failed approach recorded:** expanding the assessment classifier with phrase-specific review/recommendation regexes is brittle. Permission should fail closed by default; deterministic text guards should enforce explicit no-edit constraints and explicit mutation commands, not try to enumerate every read-only wording.

- **P3: Adjacent learning assistant source folder is absent in this checkout.** The requested `../learning-assitant` integration source was not present next to `/Users/lizhi/Downloads/local_projects/deep-agent-network`; the common corrected spelling `../learning-assistant` was also absent. **Workaround 2026-07-05:** implemented the DAN-side Learn panel and durable `.dan-learn` course sidecar API directly in the Notes workspace, leaving the nonexistent copy path unmodified.
- **Failed approach recorded:** do not assume a sibling learning-assistant repo exists just because the UI request names one. Check the actual parent directory first, then keep the integration boundary stable so copied source can be slotted in later if the folder appears.

- ~~**P3: Optional final-response suggestions could look like unresolved attention.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-05.** Work Preview treated `next_steps`/optional continuation wording and model-authored `Remaining Attention` headings like blockers, so successful runs could look as if they still required operator action even when the text was only "if you want..." follow-up guidance. Optional continuation text now renders as Optional next steps, while hard blocker/error words still keep Needs Attention.
- **Failed approach recorded:** one generic "remaining attention" bucket is too broad. Follow-up suggestions, optional polish ideas, blockers, and required approvals need separate UI semantics, otherwise successful work looks suspicious.

- ~~**P2: Hard prompt compaction could corrupt pasted screenshot attachments.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-04.** Request-understanding runs with screenshots could fail before the model answered because the replay prompt compactor recursively truncated the `image_url.url` data URL and inserted a text marker inside base64. Providers rejected the mutated payload as invalid base64, and the Work Plan then surfaced the downstream `request understanding stage did not return model-authored acceptance criteria` blocker. Provider prompt compaction now preserves image blocks, uses compact placeholders only for budget counting and debug logs, and keeps debug prompt events from storing raw base64.
- **Failed approach recorded:** canonicalizing incoming clipboard data URLs is necessary but not sufficient. Later prompt-replay compaction must also understand multimodal blocks; generic recursive string truncation is unsafe for provider-owned payload fields.

- **P3: Static website artifacts cannot be reverted through git history.** The root `.gitignore` ignores `/website/`, so `git log -- website/...` returns no recoverable history for the local static site even when files exist on disk. **Workaround 2026-07-04:** recovered the biology/network landing page from local `.dan-super` trace snapshots and restored `website/index.html`, `website/styles.css`, and `website/apps/index.html` manually.
- **Failed approach recorded:** trying to use git history for ignored website artifacts cannot work unless the files are first tracked or a separate snapshot/export process exists.

- ~~**P3: Final response could show done while execution was blocked by a parser-limit failure.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-04.** The final-answer recovery path could accept an answer-shaped operational promise such as "I'll trace the website history..." as a usable final response even while the same run had an execution attention task from the Codex output-size parser limit. Final response status now treats attention/blocker state as higher priority than answer chunks, and first-person operational progress promises are rejected as terminal answers.
- **Failed approach recorded:** answer-shaped Agent chunks are not always final answers. A progress promise should stay live/status evidence, while the final card should only turn done when there is no active blocker and the text is a real answer or completion summary.

- ~~**P3: Blueprint planning card could be done while child rows still said in progress.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-04.** A final response could render as done from terminal answer evidence while the selected task snapshot still briefly reported `running`. The broad Blueprint planning card was marked done, but its checklist still derived child rows from stale `active_task_ids` / `state="active"` graph context. Final-answer evidence now settles stale active graph/checklist rows whenever there is no explicit attention task, even before task persistence catches up.
- **Failed approach recorded:** requiring the active task snapshot to clear before settling graph rows creates a visible terminal-state race. The UI should not show child planning work as `in progress` under a done Final response; terminal answer evidence is enough to settle stale graph state unless blocker/attention evidence is present.

- ~~**P3: Message Queue rows echoed rough operator requests verbatim.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-04.** The Message Queue card used the raw task or queue item text as its visible detail, so conversational and typo-prone requests such as "Oh I think you're right. But can you..." appeared directly under `Active run`. Active/queued task and follow-up rows now show a compact cleaned intent summary while retaining `rawDetail` for chat-chunk matching and selected Work Plan request context; stale/blocked rows keep diagnostic detail literal.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using one `detail` field for both user-facing queue copy and raw request identity forces either ugly UI copy or broken source matching. Queue rows need separate display and raw text channels.

- ~~**P3: Work composer draft text followed the user across session cards.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-04.** The Work composer kept one shared `input` state while session selection swapped messages, tasks, and events. Typing a draft in one session, then clicking another session card, left the same draft visible under the new session instead of restoring the target session's own text or an empty composer. Composer text drafts are now keyed by workflow/thread id, restored on session open/auto-restore/new-session selection, and cleared only for the owning session when submitted or converted into a Notes tmp draft.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a single workspace-level composer text state is too broad for a session-card UI. The visible composer must be treated as session-owned state, while attachments can remain transient unless they get their own explicit per-session persistence.

- ~~**P3: Validation-stage tool progress could also appear on the completed Execute card.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-04.** The broad Execute card used the run's latest working chunk for its body and did not treat validation start as a phase handoff. During validation, model/tool activity such as `Thinking with kimi-k2.6` or `Using git log` could therefore appear on both Execute and Validate. Validation start/completion now settles Execute as done, and workspace-change Execute cards use a static validation-handoff summary instead of the latest live run chunk.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a run-wide latest-progress fallback is too broad for phase cards. Broad Run Step cards need phase ownership: live model/tool progress belongs to the currently active phase, while completed phases should keep stable summaries.

- ~~**P2: Request-understanding preflight could block after successful context reads because the model did not emit acceptance criteria.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-04.** A Super DAN run could read the relevant workspace files, then fail before execution with `request understanding stage did not return model-authored acceptance criteria` because the final model response did not include `aspect_reviews`, `confidence_scoped_acceptance`, and `stop_rule`. The preflight now has a JSON-schema-backed output contract, worker-core structured-output repair, stricter partial-output rejection, and one no-tool self-heal attempt before reporting a real blocker.
- **Failed approach recorded:** an unbounded `while True` retry would make the live lane hard to audit and could burn model budget indefinitely. Request-understanding recovery should be bounded, no-mutation, and explicitly logged as a self-heal attempt.

- ~~**P2: Browser-pasted screenshots could reach providers with invalid folded base64.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** When the desktop bridge was unavailable, the Work composer sent clipboard screenshots as inline `data_url` attachments. The UI preview and evidence card looked correct, but the provider adapter forwarded the raw data URL; OpenAI-compatible multimodal endpoints could then reject whitespace/folded base64 with `invalid base64 data`. The multimodal helper now strips base64 whitespace, validates the decoded bytes, canonicalizes the outgoing data URL, and also normalizes folded short-form OpenAI image aliases.
- **Failed approach recorded:** passing raw browser data URLs through the attachment surface is too trusting. Inline image adapters need a canonicalization boundary before provider dispatch, because provider error messages point at base64 bytes after the UI has already accepted the attachment.

- ~~**P3: Preview Card leaked workspace-wide previewable files across sessions.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** The Work Preview Card combined true run artifact refs with a broad scan of every previewable file in the active workspace file tree. A blank/new session in the same workspace could therefore show unrelated `index.html` files from older work or sibling app folders. The card now scopes entries to the selected session: emitted artifacts, files reported changed by current run events, and files explicitly mentioned in the submitted surface context or visible session text.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a workspace-wide preview shelf is too broad for session work. Preview discovery needs a session allow-list first; the file tree can provide metadata for allowed files, but it should not decide what belongs in the session's output card.

- ~~**P2: Selected session could show a completed Final response while the rail showed the same session running.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** The Sessions rail used the recent background task index and correctly saw a newer active follow-up, while the center Work pane used only the selected thread task fetch. If that fetch lagged, users saw the previous completed Final response even though the session card showed the live Activity icon. The Work pane now merges background tasks for the selected thread before computing active task, queue, blueprint, elapsed counter, and preview state.
- **Failed approach recorded:** keeping background task snapshots out of the visible selected-session blueprint avoids cross-thread leakage, but it also hides a newer selected-thread active run when the selected task fetch lags. The boundary should filter background tasks by selected thread, not ignore them entirely.

- ~~**P2: Queued Steer/Next messages could scramble Work Panel cards during an active run.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** Optimistic user messages created while a run was active had no future run id yet, while their durable queue item still used the current run as a container. The card builder could therefore treat the latest queued message as the active run's Operator request, and in multi-run histories could also create a separate synthetic pending-run group for the same queued text. Queue rows now attach back to their source optimistic user chunk, active-run request selection ignores queue-backed chunks, and pending-run grouping skips queue-backed user turns until the backend promotes a true next run.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using the queue item's current `runId` as if it were the queued message's eventual run identity is too blunt. Before promotion, Steer/Next queue items need a UI-local source-message identity so chat cards pair with queue cards without rewriting the active run.

- ~~**P2: Source-structure recovery could stay write-only after a guarded edit rejection.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** After the write-stage final read was consumed, a CSS/source-shape guard rejection emitted the right recovery instruction but looked for `file_read` in the already narrowed write-only tool set and left the stale `write_stage_final_read_consumed` flag active. The next round therefore received `file_edit`/`file_write` only plus the older "already used the final targeted file_read" message, so it could not perform the focused re-read the guard requested. Source-structure recovery now rebuilds tools from the original allowed catalog, clears the stale consumed flag when reopening the recovery read, and emits the recovery-enabled tool list.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a recovery nudge is not enough if the controller state still says the read budget is exhausted. Safety-guard recovery has to update the tool catalog and budget flags together, because a rejected edit did not mutate disk.

- ~~**P3: Preview pane Open button did not open the visible preview.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** The Preview header button only knew how to open prompt-log files or raw file paths, while Preview Card HTML/PDF/image/text artifacts were displayed through `/api/workspace-files/preview/...`. The button now opens the currently visible preview file through that same app-served URL, and disables itself when the visible preview is a Work card/chunk rather than a file.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a universal `Open` button cannot rely on stale file selection state. It must target the currently rendered Preview branch, otherwise it can open nothing or open a different file than the one on screen.

- ~~**P2: Work session auto-restore could start duplicate history loads and look like constant refreshing.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** The auto-restore effect did not mark the target thread as in-flight until after fetching every historical Agent run record and event list. While that fetch fan-out was pending, ordinary thread/workspace refresh effects could enter the same auto-restore path again, producing repeated `/api/v2/agent-runs/...` and `/events` bursts even though the backend was not restarting. Auto-restore now uses a hidden restoring-thread guard and commits the restored board only after history is ready, so restore does not visibly clear/reload the Work panel.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating every visible repaint as a backend reload misses frontend rehydration loops. History loading is valuable for old sessions, but long rehydration work needs a local in-progress guard before async fetches start.

- ~~**P2: `workspace_check syntax=auto` could hard-block on unsupported frontend files.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** A run checking `website/styles.css` used `workspace_check(check="syntax", syntax="auto")`, but the tool only auto-detected Python, JSON, HTML, and registered profiles. The resulting `ValueError` was treated as a deterministic validation blocker even though the CSS file itself was not proven invalid. CSS is now a registered lightweight source-shape profile, and unsupported `syntax=auto` file types return structured informational results instead of exceptions.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on thrown exceptions for unsupported syntax profiles confuses "tool cannot check this file type" with "artifact failed validation." Validation tools should distinguish unsupported check coverage from real invalid artifacts.

- ~~**P3: Blocked Work cards could say no error reason even when Preview showed the blocker.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** Task status/progress could be generic (`Super DAN needs attention`) while the concrete failure reason lived only in promoted worker evidence, for example `workspace_check failed: ValueError: workspace_check syntax=auto only supports .py, .json, .html, .htm...`. The blocked Execution and Final response cards now consult related shared evidence capsules before falling back to the generic no-reason message.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating task `latest_progress` / `blocker` as the only source of blocked-card detail loses evidence that was promoted for Preview. Blocked cards need a bounded fallback to the same shared blocker evidence, not a separate generic explanation.

- ~~**P3: Work Run Steps future cards appeared during understanding but disappeared during execution.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-03.** The early active-run skeleton rendered future execution/validation/final cards while DAN was still understanding the request, but once execution evidence arrived those broad future cards were hidden unless graph/validation/final evidence existed. The main Run Steps timeline now filters to done, active, or blocked cards only; future/planned nodes remain available for the Live Task Graph and preview context.
- **Failed approach recorded:** showing placeholder future phase cards as primary Run Steps makes the UI feel inconsistent when execution starts. Future state belongs in graph/evidence surfaces, while the card stack should reflect work that is done, active, or needs attention.

- ~~**P2: Live workspace board could route this project to the stale `/Volumes` checkout.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-02.** Saved GUI workspace/session metadata could recover `/Volumes/data/Dropbox/Projects/deep-agent-network` from old task history and then use that root for file browsing and new Agent run payloads even though the backend process and editable checkout were under `/Users/lizhi/Downloads/local_projects/deep-agent-network`. Shared runtime path normalization, Chat V2 run admission, workspace file/log APIs, and the workspace GUI now canonicalize that stale project root to the local checkout; the GUI also self-heals pinned workspace paths.
- **Failed approach recorded:** debugging policy from the wrong physical workspace hides the actual control-plane state. Root identity must be normalized before session grouping and Agent run submission.

- ~~**P2: Flattened GUI context could turn write follow-ups read-only.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-02.** Continuation objectives can arrive as one long line containing `Original operator request`, `Current follow-up`, generated context, and internal instructions such as "do not quote context labels" or "do not treat older chat context as extra tasks." The policy parser previously required newline boundaries, so it could swallow generated context into the operator request and treat those `do not` phrases as a read-only/no-shell instruction. Policy resolution now stops at the next GUI label even when flattened and classifies only the operator request plus current follow-up.
- **Failed approach recorded:** string-scanning the whole normalized packet is too broad for permissions. Policy gates must parse the operator-labeled fields first, then ignore generated context labels and UI handling instructions.

- ~~**P3: Full Preview could show copied selected-card/file context dumps.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-02.** When a worker echoed Context Composer material, the Preview pane could render `Selected plan/card context`, `selected_chunk`, `active_note`, or active-file JSON as if it were answer prose, and automatic maybe-code highlighting made the dump look even noisier. Full Preview now hides these internal context echoes, disables automatic maybe-code highlighting for selected Work chunks/cards, and backend objectives tell workers not to quote selected-card or active-file JSON unless the operator asks for diagnostics.
- **Failed approach recorded:** putting machine context directly beside answer prose invites the model and renderer to treat it as user-facing content. Context packets should ground the worker and appear as curated Evidence when useful, not leak as raw JSON in the reading pane.

- ~~**P3: Inline Markdown highlights used one visual treatment for unrelated things.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-02.** File paths, links, short config keys, ordinary code identifiers, and long inline snippets all used the same raised inline-code/badge treatment, especially in Factory Worn light mode. This made dense evidence previews look like a wall of identical labels. The shared Markdown renderer now classifies inline code spans and auto-highlights as file/url/snippet/code roles, and the surface theme gives each role distinct visual weight.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a single generic inline-code pill is too strong for mixed evidence prose. Highlighting should be reserved for navigational or scan-critical fragments: file paths, links, explicit status/tool tokens, short config identifiers, and true code snippets. Long snippets need code formatting, not badge emphasis.

- ~~**P3: Work Preview evidence showed raw Markdown and noisy highlight chips.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-02.** Shared evidence and Live Status rows used a short-line `StatusLine` tokenizer even when the content was a full Markdown answer or flattened Markdown summary. That leaked raw `###`, `**bold**`, and fences into evidence cards, while every backticked file/code fragment became a separate chip. The Work Preview now detects long Markdown-shaped status/evidence text, normalizes flattened headings/fences, and renders it through the shared Markdown renderer with automatic code-like prose highlighting disabled; short operational status rows still use compact file/tool/state tokens.
- **Failed approach recorded:** applying the status-token highlighter to every preview line is too blunt. Status chips work for short operational updates, but full evidence/answer summaries need Markdown layout first and only restrained explicit code styling.

- ~~**P2: Queued follow-ups could inherit stale read-only mutation policy from the previous run.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-02.** `_start_payload_from_queue_item` copied `mutation_policy` from the prior run payload when the follow-up queue item did not provide one. A read-only inspection/review run could therefore stamp `permission=forbidden` onto the next admitted follow-up even when the new text said "please implement these." Queued human follow-ups now carry previous profile/tool/approval context but leave mutation policy unset so the backend reclassifies it from original request plus current follow-up.
- **Failed approach recorded:** carrying every previous payload field forward treats policy decisions as reusable context. Follow-ups should preserve evidence and routing context, but permission must be recomputed unless the new queue item explicitly supplies a policy override.

- ~~**P2: Backend run requests could trust stale payload mutation policy.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-02.** Even after queue payload reset, `build_agent_backend_request` could merge an old or caller-supplied `mutation_policy` into the backend request without rechecking the current objective packet. Backend run requests now recompute the effective mutation policy from operator intent immediately before adapter execution, allowing stale forbidden permissions to upgrade on explicit implement/apply follow-ups and stale write permissions to downgrade when hard no-edit constraints remain.
- **Failed approach recorded:** policy should not be recomputed mid-run after tools are already narrowed, but it must be recomputed at every new run admission boundary. Treat the backend request build as the authoritative policy gate for the new run.

- ~~**P2: "Please implement these" follow-ups stayed read-only after assessment runs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-02.** Continuation policy resolution collapsed every follow-up back to the original operator request except saved-artifact cues, so a read-only inspection/review turn followed by "ok sounds good, please implement these?" still inherited `mutation_policy=forbidden`. Super DAN now rechecks the current follow-up during each continuation policy pass: explicit implement/apply/patch/save cues can upgrade an assessment-style original to workspace-change, while original hard no-edit/read-only wording remains binding.
- **Failed approach recorded:** resolving all continuation policy from the original request is too sticky for real operator decisions made in later rounds. Resolving all policy from the newest follow-up would be too loose and could erase earlier safety constraints. The correct boundary is original request plus current explicit write cue, with hard no-edit constraints preserved.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel could show Planning future while Understanding and Execution were active.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-02.** File/tool activity could activate `Execute workspace change` while the skeleton still inserted a grey `Blueprint planning` card because no semantic task graph had been emitted. The Work Panel now treats tool work as a handoff out of understanding and only renders Blueprint planning when a real planning event, plan file, or task graph exists.
- **Failed approach recorded:** showing every broad phase for every run makes small one-off tasks look like they skipped or misordered a required stage. Planning should be visible when there is planning evidence, not as obligatory chrome between understanding and execution.

- ~~**P3: Scheduler soft-budget ledger selection could ignore persisted leases.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-01.** The new scheduler lease helper originally accepted the first `scheduler_budget_extensions` list it found. A queue payload could carry an older empty list while run metadata held the real lease ledger, making later decisions misread lease history. The helper now chooses the longest valid ledger across payload and metadata sources before deciding.
- **Failed approach recorded:** first-source-wins metadata merging is brittle for carried continuation payloads. Lease/audit ledgers should prefer the most complete persisted history, while still copying bounded records forward to queued continuations.

- ~~**P2: New follow-up cards could be hidden or sequenced below older Work timeline anchors.**~~ **FIXED 2026-07-01.** A follow-up such as `try again` could reach the backend and persist in the thread, but the Work panel still scrolled to a ready/done fallback blueprint card when no task was genuinely active. If the new user turn did not pair cleanly with a backend request node, it could also be absent from the visible Run Steps list until a later refresh/run-link. Older unsynced user turns with no run id could be sorted after task-backed active runs because their temporary run slots had no timestamp. The timeline now keeps unmatched user turns as selectable `You` cards, orders visible runs by chat-message sequence when that evidence exists, and only status=`active` nodes can become the scroll/current anchor.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating ready or done cards as a fallback active anchor makes completed/stopped mixed-backend sessions feel like they are still working on an older request. The UI should preserve visible user input first, then attach it to backend run metadata when that evidence arrives.

- ~~**P2: Work sends could queue behind an invisible stopped Codex continuation.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-30.** Codex's bounded backend auto-continuation could spawn a synthetic follow-up run with text like `The previous Codex run ended...`; if the operator pressed Stop, the run received `stop_requested` metadata but still had persisted status `running`. The frontend correctly hid it from active controls, while the backend async board still counted it as an active blocker, so later Sends looked queued behind nothing. The async board now treats stop-requested runs as completed for admission capacity, and the Work Codex execute payload opts out of synthetic backend auto-continuation by default.
- **Failed approach recorded:** showing less UI around the queued card does not fix the lane ownership mismatch. The backend board and the Work active-run selector must agree on whether a stopped/requested-stop run can hold the active lane; synthetic continuations should be opt-in or backend-policy driven, not the default for ordinary Work sends.

- ~~**P2: Follow-up sends could look swallowed after a run had stopped.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-30.** Once terminal evidence cleared the active run, the composer could still visually say `Steer`, while multi-run Work timelines ignored a just-typed user message until the backend created and linked a new run id. The send path also persisted from a state-update return value instead of the live message ref, making the typed message vulnerable to save/refresh races. The Work composer now shows `Send`/`New` when there is no active run, appends/persists from `messagesRef.current`, and creates a temporary local timeline slot for unsynced user messages in multi-run sessions.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on backend-assigned run ids before showing a user message makes the UI feel like it swallowed input. User turns should render optimistically first, then attach to a real run when the backend response arrives.

- ~~**P2: Final response could coexist with stale active queue/session controls.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-30.** The Work Panel could render a completed Final response from Agent events while the selected task snapshot still had `status="running"`, leaving the Message Queue, session card, composer placeholder, and Stop button in active-run mode. The frontend now derives a settled task view from matching terminal run events before computing active task, queue rows, elapsed counters, session status, and Stop controls.
- **Failed approach recorded:** waiting only for a refreshed task snapshot leaves a visible race at the terminal boundary. The UI should reconcile terminal event evidence locally, while avoiding over-correction from stage-level `*.completed` events that do not close the run.

- ~~**P2: Work/Preview live status showed raw Codex shell invocations.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-30.** Codex item summaries previously rendered command payloads as `Codex ran \`/bin/zsh ...\``, and the Work Panel treated those strings as human progress. Preview/browser checks could therefore fill active cards and the selected Preview with huge highlighted command chips instead of the result the operator asked to see. The Codex backend now summarizes command activity semantically, and the frontend sanitizes already-persisted raw command summaries before live-status/card rendering.
- **Failed approach recorded:** truncating or wrapping the command is still the wrong layer because it keeps implementation machinery in the user's path. Preserve raw command payloads in logs/debug metadata; render only short operator-facing activity labels in Work and Preview.

- ~~**P2: Follow-up context was prompt-only instead of GUI-visible evidence.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-30.** The normalized request compiler carried historical context into backend prompts, but the GUI did not have a compact shared evidence layer that matched what the backend saw. Follow-ups could therefore be better grounded internally while the Work Preview still showed only live status or raw worker activity. Chat V2 now persists a bounded `context_composer` / `shared_evidence_context` packet to run/task metadata, and the Work Preview renders that evidence separately from live status.
- **Failed approach recorded:** dumping raw terminal/tool/task traces into Work cards or the Live Task Graph makes the UI noisy without making context auditable. Keep worker evidence local by default, then promote only answer-, blocker-, validation-, selected-artifact-, or continuation-relevant evidence into a shared GUI/session ledger.

- ~~**P2: Stop requests could leave the Work composer looking blocked.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Stop-requested Agent task snapshots were projected through the same Message Queue path as active/queued work, so pressing Stop could leave a visible `Stop requested` queue item and synthetic queue card even though Stop is a control action.
