The Work UI now filters stop-control task states out of queue rows while keeping the stopped run's attention/final-response state available on the run itself.
- **Failed approach recorded:** interrupts should not be modeled as user-message queue items. Queue surfaces are for future operator work; stop/pause/cancel states belong to run status and attention details.

- ~~**P3: Notes tmp drafts leaked into the visible content catalog.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Tmp-first Notes creation originally used a `tmp/drafts/...` relative path that could show up as a `tmp` folder, recent modified page, pageID suggestion, graph input, or taxonomy count. The GUI now treats temporary drafts as internal working state: visible only in Working Now/editor chrome as an uncategorized draft until explicit save materializes them into a real Hugo bundle.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using a real-looking temporary relative path is convenient for persistence, but any catalog/tree/recent/graph code that consumes `relativePath` must filter temporary drafts before rendering user-facing content structure.

- ~~**P2: Follow-up runs lost selected Work card and active file context.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** The `#workspace` frontend sent `selected_blueprint_node`, `selected_chunk`, and `active_file` inside `surface_context`, but the Chat V2 surface-context allow-list dropped those keys before Agent runs were persisted. Follow-ups such as "try again" or "continue from this card" could therefore keep chat history while losing the exact selected plan/card/file state the operator was looking at. Chat V2 now preserves those keys and the backend compiles them into `normalized_request` before Super DAN or Codex starts.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on visible GUI state alone is not enough once the request crosses the backend boundary. Selected UI context must be part of the durable Agent-run payload and the normalized request compiler packet.

- ~~**P2: Agent cap exits could end the visible goal instead of continuing responsibly.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Native/Super DAN hard round/tool caps previously had only local soft-budget nudges; once the hard cap was crossed, useful in-progress work could be forced into a partial answer. Codex-backed runs were harder because `codex exec` owns its own loop, so a limit-shaped or incomplete CLI exit could stop the session without carrying the original goal into a continuation. Super DAN tool loops now run a no-tool budget-auditor pass at hard cap boundaries and can grant one small recorded lease by default, while Codex failed/incomplete/limit-shaped exits queue a bounded continuation run with the original request, workspace/history/surface context, backend policy, and previous failure summary.
- **Failed approach recorded:** simply raising global tool caps or letting the same worker self-approve more calls would hide runaway behavior. Limit recovery needs a separate no-tool auditor or a new wrapper run with explicit previous-failure context and a bounded continuation count.

- ~~**P2: Work Panel Preview promoted internal graph telemetry and parser errors as live status.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** `live.task_graph.updated` rows from Codex observable traces could render as `Task graph updated to codex.r...`, the same graph revision text could be persisted as task progress, and a low-level output chunk parser failure could become the selected step's `Now` text or a green Final response body. Live-status and final-answer formatting now suppress observable graph-update telemetry across event/task-progress paths and maps the known chunk parser error to a concise technical note without treating it as an answer.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating every event summary or task `latest_progress` string as user-facing progress makes the Preview noisy and misleading. Live Status needs a display policy that separates semantic progress from technical evidence.

- ~~**P2: Live Task Graph rendered Codex/tool execution traces as task graph nodes.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Observable Codex events such as `codex-item-*`, terminal commands, and tool/file-read steps are runtime activity, not semantic plan structure. Rendering them as graph nodes made the Live Task Graph unreadable and confused execution evidence with plans/subplans/items. The Work Panel now filters clear observable execution traces out of semantic plan contexts, while preserving them as live status/evidence.
- **Failed approach recorded:** making every observable backend item into a graph node gives the UI plenty of data but no user meaning. The graph surface should be reserved for semantic work decomposition; runtime traces belong under card status, Preview evidence, or logs.

- ~~**P2: Live Task Graph could keep showing active tasks after the Final response was done.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Some runs emit a graph snapshot with `active_task_ids` or task `state="active"`, then finish with a valid final response without emitting a closing `task_graph_state`. The Work Panel's final card correctly showed done, but the Live Task Graph kept rendering the last snapshot as running. The GUI now settles the latest graph context after a usable final response by clearing stale active ids and marking those frontier tasks done.
- **Failed approach recorded:** waiting for every backend to emit a perfect terminal graph snapshot leaves historical sessions and partial emitters visually stale. The UI still preserves older revision history, but the current/latest graph must reconcile terminal final-answer evidence.

- ~~**P2: Work Panel Preview could show incomplete raw `{"answer": ...` JSON.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Some persisted `model.responded` events carry only the first 400 characters of the provider response text. When that text began with structured answer JSON, the final-answer detector accepted the malformed fragment as plain prose, so Preview showed a raw/incomplete `"answer": ...` line instead of the complete answer saved on the task progress. The detector now rejects incomplete JSON-looking payloads and falls through to complete task progress or another valid final-answer source.
- **Failed approach recorded:** if `JSON.parse` fails, do not automatically treat the string as human prose. Payloads that start like JSON, fenced JSON, or answer-field key/value data are transport fragments until they parse cleanly.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN answered but the Work Panel final card showed blocked.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Super DAN could return a valid in-session answer inside the `model.responded` token-usage event payload as JSON `{"answer": ...}`, then emit a generic `run.log.completed` event whose summary was only `completed`. The Work Panel only looked at completed events, terminal progress, and agent-message deltas for final-answer content, so it displayed `The run finished, but DAN did not return the in-session answer this request asked for.` even though the task `latest_progress` contained the answer. The Work Panel now recovers structured answer payloads before the generic completed receipt, and new backend completion rows include `summary` and `final_text`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating token-usage events as pure telemetry is too narrow for Super DAN because the provider response text can currently ride on the same event. The durable boundary is to emit `final_text` on completion, while the UI still has to recover persisted runs that predate that backend shape.

- ~~**P2: Grey Work Panel future steps disappeared after request understanding.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** The first active-run frame could show grey validation/final-response placeholders, then a `live.request_understanding.briefed` row with `type="completed"` was mistaken for terminal evidence and collapsed the future workflow tail back to only Understand request. The Work Panel now treats request-understanding completion as a stage event, not an answer/terminal run event, and keeps the early ghost sequence stable until a real graph, execution stage, validation stage, repair, or terminal answer replaces it.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating all `type="completed"` Agent events as terminal evidence is too coarse. Stage-specific sources such as `live.request_understanding.briefed` can complete without ending the run.

- ~~**P2: Plan-level Super DAN DAGs were flattened into task cards.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** The Work Panel treated every `task_graph_state.tasks` row as the same level, so a plan graph such as `Plan 1 -> Plan 3 -> Plan 6` could not show plan cards, plan-to-plan arrows, or the child task tree inside a selected plan. The GUI now recognizes explicit `node_type: plan` rows, renders those as top-level Work cards with ghost blueprint states and dependency arrows, and moves child tasks into selected-plan checklists with active/done/ready status.
- **Failed approach recorded:** making new graph metadata mandatory breaks older fallback tasks and request-derived nodes. Plan hierarchy fields such as `plan_id`, `node_type`, and generation dependency metadata must stay optional and backward-compatible; the renderer should detect explicit plan nodes instead of assuming every graph has them.

- ~~**P3: Markdown auto-highlighting marked prose abbreviations such as `e.g.` as code.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** The shared renderer's dotted-identifier rule treated any letter-dot-letter token as code-like, so ordinary abbreviations such as `e.g.` could receive the same inline-code chip style as real fields or objects. The renderer now skips common dotted prose abbreviations while keeping higher-confidence code/file tokens highlighted.
- **Failed approach recorded:** broad dotted-token highlighting is too noisy for prose. Preserve useful scan tokens, but explicitly protect common writing abbreviations.

- ~~**P2: A Work session could drift into the currently visible file root.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** A follow-up in a `_scratch` session always sent the current GUI file root in the Agent-run payload, and session grouping trusted local thread bindings before task evidence. A ra-neo session could therefore appear under `deep-agent-network` after one accidental follow-up, even though most of its task history and answer content belonged to ra-neo. The Work surface now resolves a dominant session workspace/root from task history, lets task evidence override conflicting local bindings, and sends follow-ups to that resolved session root.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using the newest task as the grouping source makes one bad follow-up move the whole session. A Work session needs a stable home workspace; recency can update live state, but it should not override dominant historical workspace evidence unless the user explicitly starts or moves a different session.

- ~~**P2: Restart could show old Chat V2 sessions as still running and under Project: Scratch.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Chat V2 task/run JSON persisted `running` after the backend process died, and unbound `_scratch` threads could only group by their internal storage workflow when workspace bindings were missing. Startup now recovers interrupted running records to `stopped` with a process-restart reason, the Sessions rail ignores stale saved running records for live controls, and grouping falls back to saved `workspace_root` labels.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on users to click a session or refresh only hides the problem locally. Durable state must be normalized at startup, while the rail still needs root-based grouping when a workspace slot id is gone.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel Preview could repeat the same live progress under Now, Latest Update, and Recent Updates.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Codex and DAN can emit the same natural-language progress through both active task progress and latest event summaries. The Preview card then showed the identical sentence in multiple sections, making the labels feel redundant. Live-status rendering now fingerprints normalized lines, shows the current action in `Now`, hides `Latest Update` when it is the same, and filters repeated lines from `Recent Updates`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** removing one of the labels globally would lose useful structure for cases where they differ, such as current task state plus a distinct file/tool result. The right boundary is de-duplication at render time.

- ~~**P2: Codex-backed Work Panel runs never filled the Live Task Graph.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** The graph panel correctly waited for real `task_graph_state` snapshots, but the Codex backend only forwarded raw Codex item/status events and never emitted `live.task_graph.updated`. Native Super DAN runs could show model-authored graph versions, while Codex sessions stayed on the waiting card even as work progressed. The Codex adapter now projects observable CLI events into run-local graph revisions for the request, workspace activity, answer, and final completion branches.
- **Failed approach recorded:** do not copy the fixed Run Steps scaffold into the graph area to make it look populated. Codex cannot expose hidden planning thoughts, so the honest fallback is an observable event graph, not a pretend model-authored plan.

- ~~**P3: Operational progress text could be recovered as an operator request.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** Task progress fallback strings such as `DAN is queued` are useful for live progress cards, but they are not user intent. Request recovery now keeps progress available for card content while filtering operational agent-status phrases out of the operator-request selection path.
- **Failed approach recorded:** removing progress fallback everywhere fixes fake requests but breaks live progress cards. The right boundary is request extraction, not progress rendering.

- ~~**P3: Opening a session card could leave Files showing the previous workspace while history loaded.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** The session-card click path only switched the active workspace after the thread and Super DAN history finished loading, so the Files rail could temporarily show the old workspace root even though the selected session lived under another workspace. Session-card opening now switches the active workspace immediately before loading history, and the desktop Work pane order puts Sessions first, then Files, matching that dependency.
- **Failed approach recorded:** Files should not behave like an independent project picker when the user is navigating sessions. Session/workspace selection is the source of truth, and the file rail should follow it optimistically.

- ~~**P3: Session cards could keep showing a completed run as running until clicked/refreshed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** The Sessions rail merged the selected-session task cache after the background task index, so an older selected `running` snapshot for the same task could override a fresher background `completed` snapshot. Terminal Agent events now immediately mark matching local snapshots terminal, and task merging prefers fresher/terminal snapshots instead of blindly trusting list order.
- **Failed approach recorded:** manual refresh or opening a session should not be the way stale run state settles. Session status needs event-time reconciliation plus freshness-aware merging across selected and background task sources.

- ~~**P3: Work header root path stayed too wide on narrow desktop windows.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-29.** The saved root-picker width was also used as the top-bar root field width, so a long workspace path could keep a fixed desktop-header footprint and crowd the pane titles/status controls when the browser became narrow. The header now lets the left group flex into the remaining space, keeps the right control cluster at natural size, and clamps the root field to its parent.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a user-resizable picker width should not be treated as a fixed chrome width. Header context fields need to truncate within the available layout space, while overlays can still use their saved preferred width when there is room.

- ~~**P3: Markdown auto-highlighting could turn dense code fragments into noisy chip walls.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The shared renderer treated arbitrary function calls with arguments and ordinary snake_case words as code-like prose, so unbackticked implementation snippets such as signatures, loops, and indexed expressions received many inline-code chips in one paragraph. Auto-highlighting now stays to stronger signals such as filenames, class/object names, dotted references, leading-underscore callbacks, and constants, and skips argument-heavy calls, indexed expressions, and ordinary snake_case unless explicitly backticked.
- **Failed approach recorded:** broad regex highlighting is too blunt for mixed prose/code answers. The renderer should recover only high-confidence scan tokens automatically and leave dense code-shaped fragments to explicit Markdown code spans or fences.

- ~~**P1: `#workspace` could render blank after the composer placeholder patch.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The active-run composer placeholder fix referenced `selectedBlueprintNode` before the component initialized that value, causing a React render crash and leaving the web page blank even though the backend and Vite server were healthy. The placeholder calculation now runs after blueprint nodes and the selected node are derived.
- **Failed approach recorded:** even small derived UI strings must respect React component initialization order. If a derived value depends on blueprint selection, compute it after the blueprint selection state has been built.

- ~~**P3: Active-run Work composer looked filled by the selected task title.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The composer input itself was empty, but while a run was active the placeholder could be built from the selected Work node title. Long active task titles then appeared inside the chat box like a real draft, while the empty-input Stop action reinforced the impression that the user could not type a steer/follow-up message. Active runs now use short action placeholders for Steer and Next, and selected-context placeholders are reserved for idle sessions.
- **Failed approach recorded:** reusing selected-card titles as composer placeholder text is helpful only when the session is idle. During active work, the composer must communicate the available action, not mirror process-card content.

- ~~**P2: Live Task Graph could stay empty because only optional planning emitted graph snapshots.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The Work Panel correctly refused to invent graph nodes, but many answer/direct/narrow runs skip the optional planner, so no `live.task_graph.updated` event ever arrived and the graph area stayed in its waiting state. The request-understanding preflight now asks the model for a compact initial task graph, emits the first `task_graph_state` immediately, and reuses that graph as run context when the heavier planner is skipped.
- **Failed approach recorded:** the UI should not synthesize fake graph nodes from fixed workflow phases. The durable fix is to emit a real model-authored graph from the earliest stage that understands the request, then let later stages revise it.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel showed ghost future phases without an actual task graph.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The fallback projection drew Planning, Validation, Final response, and sometimes execution cards as future placeholders simply because a run existed. Those broad cards now render only when the phase has real evidence, while future/deferred cards remain visible when they are actual planned task-graph nodes.
- **Failed approach recorded:** stable broad phases should not pretend to be a live plan. If DAN has not emitted a task graph or phase event, the Work Panel should wait instead of showing ghost steps.

- ~~**P3: Markdown bold markers stayed visible inside Work Panel lists.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The shared Markdown renderer's custom text renderer ignored nested inline tokens inside Marked list-item text tokens, so `**Browser prototype**` and `**M1.5**` stayed literal while the auto-code highlighter still decorated code-like words. The text renderer now parses nested inline tokens first, preserving bold and explicit code spans before auto-highlighting plain prose.
- **Failed approach recorded:** auto-highlighting cannot treat every text token as already-flat prose. Marked list items can wrap inline Markdown in child tokens, and those children must be parsed before custom prose decoration runs.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN validation did not explain branch repair versus graph closure.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** Validation could report a single generic pass/fail or "needs attention" result even when the real workflow was branch-wise execution, branch validation, branch-local repair, then graph-level closure. Validators now return explicit validation scope, branch/task ids, branch results, deterministic checks, LLM semantic checks, and graph-level validation status, and the Work Panel Preview renders those buckets separately.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a final score or completed status is not enough validation evidence. Users need to see which branch/frontier was checked, what deterministic checks actually ran or were inspected, what LLM semantic judgment was made, and whether the overall task graph is ready to close or needs targeted repair.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN could still rely on the deterministic request-understanding brief.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The deterministic packet was correctly shaped as a meta-rules brief, but planner/builder stages could still proceed before a model-authored `request_understanding` was available. Generic live runs now execute a dedicated no-mutation request-understanding preflight first, require tailored aspect reviews, confidence-scoped acceptance criteria, and a stop rule, and fail clearly when the model does not return them.
- **Failed approach recorded:** asking later stages to optionally include request understanding is not strong enough. Understanding must be an explicit gate before planning or execution can treat the run as ready.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel needs-attention cards could hide the actual problem.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** When a run was blocked but only emitted generic status text, the UI fell back to the original user request as the attention detail and could also treat "Super DAN needs attention" as a completed final response. Attention cards now only use explicit blocker/error/reason/progress evidence, otherwise they state that DAN did not emit a specific reason; generic attention text is not accepted as a final answer.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a red card label is not an explanation. If the backend does not provide a blocker reason, the UI should say that plainly rather than substituting the operator's request or marking the run complete.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel execution could become active before request understanding had handed off.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The broad phase fallback treated any active run as enough to mark Planning and Execute/Prepare active, so the board could show multiple current phases while DAN was still in request understanding. Planning now needs planning evidence, execution now needs build/tool/execution evidence, and the early active state belongs to Understand Request.
- **Failed approach recorded:** active run is not the same thing as active phase. Broad phase cards need stage-specific evidence or a clear handoff from later events.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel duration was ambiguous and too repeated.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The Sessions rail could show a worked/working duration, but in sessions with multiple runs that number was an aggregate; the first Work Panel pass then repeated duration chips on each run card. The UI now keeps one compact total label in the Sessions rail and one aggregate live clock in the Work Panel Run Steps header.
- **Failed approach recorded:** putting timing only in the session card is too easy to miss, but repeating it on every run card adds noise. The clearer model is one total counter in the working surface, ticking while work is active.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel could show execution done while earlier phases stayed active.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** Understanding and planning cards used broad active-run fallback rules, so missing `request_understanding.updated` or `planning.completed` events could leave them active even after execution had started or completed. The projection now treats later execution/validation/final evidence as a handoff/completion signal for earlier broad phases, without pretending a Live Task Graph exists when no `task_graph_state` was emitted.
- **Failed approach recorded:** each phase cannot rely only on its own ideal event. The UI must reconcile the whole run timeline: later work supersedes earlier broad phases, while detailed graph views still require real graph payloads.

- ~~**P3: Markdown prose missed code-shaped highlight targets unless the model used backticks.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** Work Panel answers could leave terms such as `GameLoop.gd`, `AgentNeighborSystem`, `_ready()`, `_ecs.positions_x`, and `query_2d` as plain prose when the model omitted Markdown code spans. The shared Markdown renderer now auto-highlights file names, CamelCase objects, snake_case fields, dotted members, and function calls from text tokens while preserving explicit code spans, fenced code, links, and KaTeX math placeholders.
- **Failed approach recorded:** asking the model to add more backticks is not reliable enough for scan-heavy UI. The renderer should recover obvious code-shaped prose locally without making ordinary words look technical.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel cards could stay active after a run finished.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** The selected Work Panel task list refreshed when Agent events arrived, but a terminal event could be processed before the final task snapshot write was visible to the selected-session fetch. The stream then closed with no second selected-task refresh, leaving main Work cards stale even though the sidebar/background task index could catch up later. Terminal events now trigger immediate and delayed selected-session/background refreshes, and task snapshots include created/updated timestamps for reliable freshness checks.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on a single event-time task fetch is too brittle at the terminal boundary. The UI should reconcile once from the event, then again after persistence settles.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN prompt logs exposed too much raw brief metadata to the model.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** After prompt logging made provider calls inspectable, Super DAN live prompts showed long `Role`, policy, payload, and runtime sections before the actual user request. The user intent was present, but buried, and useful context hooks were mixed with developer-facing fields. Super DAN live briefs now opt into a compact renderer with user request, work contract, current step, allowed tools, motivated context hooks, known context, constraints, stage guidance, evidence, and return format.
- **Failed approach recorded:** making every structured field visible to the model is not the same as giving useful context. Keep internal metadata available for logs/control-plane code, but render a short prompt hierarchy that motivates when to use history, files, links, web search, and validation evidence.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel Preview could show Markdown tables as plain pipe text.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-28.** Final answers sometimes arrived with a heading, table header cells, separator row, and body rows flattened into one paragraph. The existing heading recovery could split the title but left the table separator and rows glued together, so the Markdown renderer never saw a valid table block. The Work Panel now detects flattened pipe-table separators, restores row breaks, and keeps the heading separate before preview rendering.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding more table CSS would not help because the renderer never received a table. Table recovery belongs in the final-answer Markdown normalization step before sanitized Markdown rendering.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN live prompts described the worker instead of the work.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-27.** Some provider-facing prompts still opened with abstract language such as "universal execution cell" or broad "Super DAN plan" framing. That made the model optimize for completing an internal lane instead of first grounding the run in the user's request, project rules, important files, current graph, satisfaction gap, and expected human response. The invariant worker system prompt now describes a bounded worker for the current run, and Super DAN live stage prompts describe concrete context-gathering, graph-versioning, frontier execution, validation, repair, and final-delivery duties.
- **Failed approach recorded:** naming the agent architecture is not a useful instruction to the model. The prompt should supply the concrete request packet, source context, work contract, task graph/version state, and output quality bar; architecture names belong in metadata and logs.

- ~~**P2: Follow-up/retry runs could chase internal tickets instead of the original user request.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-27.** After a run completed poorly, follow-up text such as "yes please proceed" or retry commands could become the new backend objective while the original request only survived in chat history. Super DAN then classified work mode from the continuation phrase or execution lane, so answer-style project questions could drift back into build/edit behavior. Chat V2 now stores the initial `original_request`, carries follow-ups as `satisfaction_gap`, includes previous-run summary context, and wraps continuation objectives as original request plus current gap. Super DAN's policy resolver extracts the original request before deciding `chat_answer`, `workspace_read`, or `workspace_change`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** retry should not mean "complete the prior internal run lane again." It must mean "continue toward the same user-visible goal, using the latest dissatisfaction as the missing piece," and mutation should be allowed only when that original goal or an explicit new follow-up requirement calls for it.

- ~~**P3: Live Task Graph duplicated the fixed Run Steps scaffold.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-27.** The graph overview was built from the same blueprint node list as the request/understand/execute/validation/final cards, so when real graph nodes were missing it looked like a second copy of the workflow, and when graph nodes existed they were visually mixed under generic phase nodes. The Work Panel now collects actual structured task-graph snapshots, shows revision chips and branch-grouped task nodes, and leaves the fixed scaffold in Run Steps only.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a stable phase scaffold is useful for the Work Panel, but it should not be presented as a task graph. The graph surface must prefer backend-emitted graph nodes and version state, with an empty/waiting state when the graph has not arrived.

- ~~**P3: Work Panel could show one-line fenced JSON as the final answer.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-27.** Some Super DAN task progress arrived as compact Markdown fence text such as ```json { "answer": "...", ... } ``` instead of a full multiline code fence. The frontend JSON parser only unwrapped multiline fences, so the Work Panel rendered the whole payload as raw code in the Final response card and Preview even though an `answer` field was present. The shared parser now accepts both multiline and one-line JSON fences before routing through the structured human-readable formatter.
- **Failed approach recorded:** fixing only the Markdown presentation would still leave users reading the transport envelope. Structured response parsing must happen before Markdown rendering, including malformed-but-recoverable fence shapes.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN mutation allowance was doing two jobs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-27.** The live path previously used a single "workspace mutation allowed" idea both to decide whether tools could write and to decide whether a file edit was required for success. That made answer-style turns drift into artifacts, while later contract text could accidentally trip the local runtime's legacy no-write scanner and remove write tools from real build tasks. Super DAN now resolves `work_mode`, `mutation_policy`, and `evidence_policy` separately, treats optional mutation as permission rather than an obligation, and makes the local runtime trust structured policy fields before scanning prompt text.
- **Failed approach recorded:** broad prompt text such as "actually mutate files" or glossary lines saying what "forbidden" means should not be the control plane. Intent must become structured policy first; prompts and UI can explain that policy but should not be the thing that enforces it.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN Work Plan task DAG was static after planning.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** The planner could emit ready/deferred task ids, but the event stream did not expose a first-class evolving graph object. Execution and validation could therefore change the true frontier while the Work Plan still looked like the original plan. Super DAN now emits git-like `task_graph_state` snapshots with shared roots, branch-local refs, parent version ids, inferred owned-path completion, active/done/ready/deferred ids, and parallel groups; the Work Plan renders the latest graph version.
- **Failed approach recorded:** replacing or hardcoding fallback plan rules in the UI would make the board feel deterministic but stale. The durable boundary is a model-authored graph plus small meta rules for how later stages report scoped graph updates.

- ~~**P2: Concrete request-understanding fallback criteria could overrule the operator's answer intent.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** The initial Super DAN request-understanding packet carried fixed acceptance criteria before the model generated request-specific ones. For casual explanation prompts such as "whats this game", that could make the Work Plan show artifact-shaped criteria like creating a file and verifying the write, even though the operator wanted an in-session answer. The backend now emits a `rule_generation_brief` with meta-rules only, including chat-first handling and artifact-only-when-explicit handling, and the Work Plan shows it as `generating rules` until model-authored criteria arrive.
- **Failed approach recorded:** deterministic request-understanding text should tell the model how to generate criteria, not provide concrete criteria itself. Concrete fallback bullets can become accidental policy and make the UI trust the wrong completion target.

- ~~**P3: Notes and Work file rows used a different card skin than Sessions.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** The Pages rail, Notes tree rows, and Work file/folder rows could read as separate UI systems from the Sessions cards, especially under Factory Worn where file rows had their own darker override. The rail rows now use one shared compact card tone for active, hover, and drop-target states, and the old Factory Worn-only file-row override was removed.
- **Failed approach recorded:** theme-specific row skins drift away from the rest of the rail. Keep theme texture around a shared card treatment instead of making Files or Notes maintain a separate visual language.

- ~~**P2: Work Plan could show `Run finished` as the final human response.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** Completed runs with no answer could create an `agent-terminal` chunk whose only body was `Run finished.`. The final-response selector treated that as usable text because the generic completion filter only blocked phrases such as `completed` and `Super DAN completed`. Answer-style requests such as "Help me understand what this project is about" could therefore show a green done Final response without the requested summary. The formatter now treats `finished`, `Run finished`, and related completion receipts as machine telemetry, so the final card stays `Final answer missing` unless a real in-session answer or structured human summary exists.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding a conversation box or final-response card does not guarantee the answer exists. The output-to-display layer must reject status receipts before selecting final-answer content.

- ~~**P2: Missing final answers were detected too late to recover.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-27.** After the UI correctly rejected `Run finished` and similar receipts, answer-style Super DAN runs could still end as blocked because the backend had already marked the no-mutation path valid before checking whether a real answer was present. The generic live lane now validates answer-only outputs before final completion, rejects operational receipts and too-thin text, and runs a bounded read-only `live.answer_recovery` pass that must either produce the requested in-session answer or a clearer blocker.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a red missing-delivery card is useful honesty, but it is not recovery. The quality gate must sit before terminal completion, and each recovery attempt must be bounded, read-only for answer intent, and monotonic toward either a substantive answer or an explicit blocker.

- ~~**P2: Fixed Super DAN stage prompts could over-shape broad answer requests into artifact work.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** The shared live stage snippets carried fixed per-stage question bundles, while the optional run-local planner still treated `review` / `analyze` plus broad project terms as planning-worthy mutation work. Even with answer-only intent policy elsewhere, this made broad requests vulnerable to drifting from "answer me here" into plan/artifact/change behavior. Stage snippets now ask the model to generate request-specific checks from the operator request, intent policy, tools, and evidence instead of receiving a canned checklist; deterministic gates remain explicit, answer-only policy blocks planner entry, and plain review/analyze wording no longer triggers planning by itself.
- **Failed approach recorded:** improving the visible Work Plan response is not enough if the backend prompt path still turns answer requests into saved artifacts. The durable boundary is policy-first: determine whether mutation is allowed, then ask the model to author request-specific checks under that boundary.

- ~~**P3: Work Plan could hide the chat trail behind the process graph.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** After the middle pane became blueprint-first, the user's actual chat turns were still present in data but not visible as conversation cards. The board could feel like a developer process view even when the user expected a chat. Recent user turns now render as selectable message boxes above Run Steps and open in the preview panel when selected.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a single Operator request node is not a substitute for a conversation history. The process graph should sit beside recent user intent, not replace it.

- ~~**P3: Work Plan final response could show fenced JSON code blocks.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** Some completed task progress arrived as a Markdown `json` code fence containing fields such as `answer`, `candidate_id`, `change_summary`, and file lists. The structured formatter only parsed bare JSON objects, so the final-response canvas card handed the fenced payload to Markdown rendering and exposed a developer-shaped code block. The JSON parser now unwraps full Markdown JSON fences before structured display formatting, so the card and selected preview render readable answer/summary sections and still drop internal ids.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on Markdown rendering to make structured output "look nicer" still leaves the user reading a machine payload. The boundary must classify fenced JSON as data first, then render user-facing prose and sections.

- ~~**P3: Work Plan live status could show a bare `denied` for blocked tool calls.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** Super DAN emits `tool.policy_denied` with a concrete reason such as `operator_intent_blocks_file_read:<path>` before the lower-level `tool.denied` row, but the selected-step preview treated the later raw summary as the visible update. Users saw "Using file read" followed by only "denied" with no path, policy, or next implication. The Work Plan now treats policy-denied tool rows as first-class status updates, skips empty raw denied rows, explains the denied tool/path/reason, and highlights tools, files, links, and blocked states as scan tokens.
- **Failed approach recorded:** raw event summaries are not good status copy. Tool lifecycle rows need interpretation, especially when the denial reason lives in a neighboring policy event rather than in the final `tool.denied` row.

- ~~**P2: Project-summary requests could be delivered as files instead of readable session answers.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** Phrasings such as "what is this project about?", "help me summary this project", or "write me a project summary" could fall through to mutation-style work because generic mutation cues like `write` or `create` outweighed the user's answer intent. Super DAN then created `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` / `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md` and the Work Plan accepted the changed-file receipt as the final response, leaving the session without the actual summary the user asked to read. Project/about/repo summary asks now default to in-session answer work unless the operator explicitly names a saved file/artifact or edit target; the final-response guard rejects file/change-only receipts for answer-style runs while keeping them as workspace evidence.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a file artifact can be useful support, but it is not a substitute for the requested session answer. Intent policy must separate "answer me about this project" from "save this answer into a file", and the UI must validate the final response against that intent rather than accepting any completed-run payload.

- ~~**P2: Exact "what is this project" could bypass answer-only project policy.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-27.** The project-answer detector covered "what is this project about?" but missed the shorter exact form "what is this project". That let Super DAN keep mutation-capable tools in a turn that should have been a readable in-session answer, so the local runtime could nudge the model toward file writes after enough inspection. The detector now covers the exact form and contractions such as "what's this repo", and the local runtime applies operator-intent tool narrowing before provider calls plus suppresses write-stage first-write nudges when mutation is forbidden.
- **Failed approach recorded:** prompt wording alone is not a durable no-write boundary. Answer-only intent must be reflected in the actual tool schema set and runtime nudges, or later execution stages can still steer the model toward artifacts.

- ~~**P3: Archived `#workspace` sessions could still appear in the active Work Plan.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-26.** The Sessions rail correctly filtered archived threads into the `Archived` group, but the selected Work Plan state, workspace `activeThreadId`, and `LAST_THREAD_STORAGE_KEY` could still point at the archived thread. That made an old completed plan remain visible even while the active workspace group said `No sessions`. Startup restore now ignores archived targets, refreshed archived summaries clear the selected Work Plan, archive actions remove matching workspace/open-thread and last-session pointers, and archived cards require Restore before opening.
- **Failed approach recorded:** filtering archived cards out of the normal rail is not enough. Archive is a selection boundary as well as a grouping flag; restore, open, and persisted-last-session paths must all enforce it.

- ~~**P3: Queued follow-up chats appeared as one loose queue/status row instead of planned Work Plan steps.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-25.** Follow-up messages sent while a Super DAN run was active were durable Agent-run queue items, but the canvas appended them as a single `queue` node or active-run status row after the main phases. Users could see that something was queued but not the expected ghost/solid request, planning, work, and response slots; active runs without emitted task graphs could also leave `Blueprint planning` as a misleading future/ghost card. Queue-backed steer/next messages now carry their queue kind/lane and expand into follow-up request, plan, work, and response nodes; active no-DAG runs keep planning solid/active; "don't edit anything" wording also maps to direct-response work so follow-up summaries do not appear as workspace mutation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a queue/status row is not enough for the Work Plan. The queue panel can remain operational, but the canvas needs a user-facing projection of what will happen next, derived from queue state rather than from fixed prompt phrases.

- ~~**P2: Broad project-review requests could trigger unsolicited workspace edits and hide the answer behind file-change evidence.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-25.** A request like "help me review this project" was treated as generic workspace deliverable work unless it explicitly forbade edits, so Super DAN could create review documents or edit README files even though the user only asked for an assessment. The Work Plan then emphasized changed files and could omit a clear Summary. Super DAN now classifies assessment/audit/inspection/check/explain requests as answer-only by default, removes mutation tools unless explicit edit/artifact language is present, lets validation complete without changed files, labels the Work Plan lane as a review response, and renders final details with Summary first.
- **Failed approach recorded:** defaulting every broad request to mutation makes "review" behave like "write a review artifact." The durable rule is intent-based: answer from inspection by default, and require explicit saved-artifact or edit wording before mutating files.

- ~~**P3: Work Plan final response could be replaced by workspace outcome evidence.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-25.** Agent-run compaction can emit both an answer chunk and a later outcome chunk for the same completed run. The blueprint builder treated `answer|outcome|terminal` as one final-response family and picked the newest matching chunk, so a useful final summary could be replaced by a `Final Outcome` node that only listed changed files. The builder now classifies final-answer chunks separately from outcome chunks, keeps changed-file evidence in the final preview's Workspace Changes section, and recovers useful structured terminal summaries from event payloads when the visible event summary is only generic completion text.
- **Failed approach recorded:** terminal display rows are not interchangeable. Changed-file evidence proves that something happened, but it is not the answer to the operator; the final node must prefer user-facing answer/summary data and show outcome evidence only as support.

- ~~**P3: Selected Work Plan previews were static detail panels instead of live status views.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-25.** Selecting a step could show request understanding or validation detail but not the current run progress, latest update, or new results as Agent events arrived. Selected-step previews now include a live status card with status, now, latest update, recent updates, and results so far from task snapshots and Agent-run events.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a preview that only explains the selected step is not enough during an active run. It must also surface the freshest progress and results from the stream, otherwise users cannot tell what is happening now.

- ~~**P3: Work-plan pane chrome used developer-facing blueprint language and repeated selected step titles.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-25.** The middle pane title read `Blueprint View`, the inner rail read `Live Blueprint`, and the header subtitle could repeat an internal node title such as `Blueprint planning`. The visible UI now uses `Work Plan`, `Run Steps`, `Step`, and a status subtitle mapper such as `Planning next steps`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** exposing internal node titles as pane subtitles makes the surface feel like a developer event viewer. Header copy should describe the user's current state, not the storage/event name of the selected node.

- ~~**P3: Operator request blueprint card duplicated the request as subtitle and body.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-25.** The request node used the exact operator text for both `detail` and `body`, and the preview also started with the same text before derived expectations. Request nodes now keep the exact text as internal source data, render a non-duplicative canvas summary/body, and show the request in its own selected-node preview card.
- **Failed approach recorded:** the request node should not solve exact-text visibility by repeating the same sentence in multiple visible fields or by labeling implementation terms. Preserve exact input as source data, then render it once in a dedicated request panel.

- ~~**P3: Blueprint final response could render raw structured completion JSON.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-25.** Completed task progress could contain structured fields such as `candidate_id`, `change_summary`, `validation_summary`, and execution scoring; when terminal events were thin, the final blueprint node used that payload as display text and showed a raw JSON blob in the canvas/preview. The blueprint now formats structured progress into concise user-facing changes, files, checks, and attention sections while ignoring internal ids and scoring fields.
- **Failed approach recorded:** clipping card height is not enough if the content is still machine payload. The formatter boundary must convert structured run data into user-facing display text before the blueprint node is built.

- ~~**P3: Request-derived blueprint fallback could hardcode feature-specific nodes from prompt wording.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-25.** The frontend fallback previously matched particular product-feature words and expanded them into specific canvas nodes before the backend emitted a real plan. The canvas now stays phase/state-oriented, selected-node previews render structured details, and target-derived fallback nodes are generic target-scope/current-slice/coverage steps based on explicit targets only.
- **Failed approach recorded:** the frontend should not act like a second planner by mapping natural-language feature words into fixed task templates. Specific work items should come from backend plan/task metadata or remain preview-level request details until there is evidence.

- ~~**P3: Clicking `#workspace` Sessions `+` could briefly reopen the previous live blueprint.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** The New Session path clears the visible thread while the backend creates the new blank session. During that short gap, the startup/restore effect could see "no active thread" and restore the workspace's previous active thread, making the old blocked/live blueprint reappear in the supposedly blank session. Restore now checks the in-flight New Session guard before choosing a fallback thread.
- **Failed approach recorded:** separating selected-session tasks from background task status is necessary but not sufficient. The selection restore path must also know when the operator intentionally requested a new blank session, otherwise it can undo the user's click before the create-thread response returns.

- ~~**P3: Notes Tags/Categories clicks jumped back to Pages instead of showing matching articles.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** `selectNoteFacet(...)` hard-coded `setNoteRailView("pages")`, so choosing a tag such as `#italy` or a Categories rail item moved the user back into the folder hierarchy. The Notes rail now resolves the selected facet back to Tags or Categories, keeps that rail active, and renders a focused article list for the selected taxonomy facet.
- **Failed approach recorded:** facet filtering alone is not enough when the visible rail changes to a folder tree. Taxonomy navigation needs its own drill-down list so users can inspect matching articles without losing the tag/category context.

- ~~**P3: Repeated `#workspace` New Session actions could create a stack of irrelevant blank Super DAN sessions.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** Live GUI testing left seven unarchived `_scratch` threads titled `New Super DAN Session`, each with zero messages and zero Agent task rows, which cluttered the `Project: Scratch` group. Those exact blank threads were archived, and the GUI now guards the New Session path with an in-flight lock plus an already-active-blank check that focuses the composer instead of creating another empty thread.
- **Failed approach recorded:** blank-session creation should not be used as a harmless smoke-test side effect. Session-create smoke tests must clean up or rely on an idempotent blank-session guard.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN generic live runs could stop at artifact-shape completion for broad requests.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** Broad build/report requests could look complete after creating the named artifact even when the run had not explicitly checked audience, deliverable scope, target location, method constraints, hidden quality criteria, and proof. Generic live execution now creates a deterministic `request_understanding` packet, logs it, carries stage-specific aspect review guidance into planner/build/validation/retry/repair prompts and input payloads, and asks validators to report aspect coverage before accepting the run.
- **Failed approach recorded:** artifact existence is not a quality gate. Completion needs an aspect-by-aspect request review plus evidence for the confidently doable requirements, with blockers or remaining work stated instead of silently omitted.
- **Failed approach recorded:** write-capable prompts must avoid incidental `read-only` / `read only` wording. During this fix, phrases like "read-only inspection" and "Re-read only the focused range" triggered the local runtime's no-mutation detector and disabled file-write tools; write stages now use neutral wording such as separate inspection or refresh-at-most.

- ~~**P2: New `#workspace` sessions could appear under `Project: Scratch` even when started from `ra-neo`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** GUI sessions are stored in the backend `_scratch` workflow, while the sidebar groups them by a frontend workspace binding. If a new/implicit session missed that binding or only had Super DAN task metadata, the fallback grouping used `workflow_id=_scratch`, so the card landed under `Project: Scratch` despite the active workspace/files being `ra-neo`. New session and implicit send paths now bind to the explicit active workspace id, update `openThreadIds`, include `workspace_id` in the Super DAN surface context, and let the sidebar recover the active thread's group from task workspace metadata. Opening or startup-restoring an already-misplaced thread now also inspects its saved task metadata and self-binds it back into the matching workspace group.
- **Failed approach recorded:** changing only the backend workflow id would conflate storage namespace with UI workspace identity. The durable fix is to keep `_scratch` as the storage workflow while making workspace binding explicit and recoverable from task/run metadata.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` header root dropdown could overlap Sessions and Blueprint on narrow windows, then feel trapped once compacted.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** The header root picker used a hard-coded 560px absolute menu from the root field, so a screenshot-sized window could show the menu spilling across the Sessions rail and Blueprint pane. The first compact pass fixed the width but left no visible parent-folder, browse-down, or close affordance; the second pass added those controls but still left the size fixed. The header now owns a higher stacking layer, the dropdown is bounded and resizable, the chosen width/list height persist, and the picker header/rows expose use, parent, browse-inside, and close controls.
- **Failed approach recorded:** local Playwright visual smoke could not launch system Chrome from this sandbox because Chrome tried to access its Crashpad settings under Application Support and aborted with a permission error. The frontend build and whitespace checks passed; rerun a visual browser smoke once browser control is available again.

- ~~**P2: `#workspace` session cards could focus but not open on mouse click.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** The session-card wrapper installed swipe-to-archive pointer capture on ordinary mouse `pointerdown`, so Playwright and human mouse clicks could focus the card without firing the button's session-open path. Keyboard Enter still opened the session, which made the bug look like stale data rather than a click-path problem. The swipe handler now skips mouse pointers so the card button receives normal clicks; browser smoke confirmed `Project Review Request` and the recovered `Phone app smoke test admission verification` card both open via mouse and issue `/api/chats/_scratch/...` plus task/run requests.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating this as only an empty-history or stale-task problem missed the missing network request. Session-card click regressions need request-level evidence, and swipe gestures should not capture the whole desktop mouse card.

- ~~**P2: Old Super DAN sessions could look live or empty instead of explaining stale task state.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** Historical cards such as `Phone smoke` and `Project Review Request` had sparse chat projections but durable task/run metadata. The GUI could lose the original operator text hidden in `last_surface_turn`/run payloads and could treat a 33-day-old saved `running` task as active. The history loader now carries run timestamps and command text into task snapshots; blueprint projection recovers operator text, marks stop-requested rows as blocked, and labels stale running rows with their last update age.
- **Failed approach recorded:** `message_count` and raw task status alone are not enough for session reopen UX. Reopened Agent sessions need the task/run ledger, run timestamps, and original surface turn text before deciding whether a task is active, stale, stopped, or empty.

- ~~**P2: `#workspace` blueprint hid dissected request steps until a backend DAG arrived.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** The seminar-planner live smoke could show only a broad execution/final lane while the model was still waiting or after timeout because no `plan_context.task_graph` had been emitted. The frontend now derives a conservative request blueprint from the operator text, including target setup, deliverable files, feature slices, README/open-file instructions, and smoke-check placeholders; emitted backend DAGs still take precedence when available.
- **Failed approach recorded:** waiting for model-authored planning makes the blueprint least useful during long model waits and failures. The UI needs a request-derived placeholder plan first, then a richer backend DAG later.

- **P2: Full seminar trip planner live smoke is not yet end-to-end proven.** Observed 2026-06-24 in `#workspace` using the prompt to build `/private/tmp/dan-blueprint-smoke/trip-planner`. The blueprint surface reached the correct active/future shape, but the live Super DAN run timed out before writing files, a backend reload interrupted a follow-up run, and then the in-app browser denied further control of `http://127.0.0.1:5174`. No planner files were produced under `/private/tmp/dan-blueprint-smoke/trip-planner`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating a clean blueprint state as proof of generated artifacts is too weak. The remaining smoke must verify actual files plus the opened generated app once provider execution completes and browser control for localhost is available again.

- ~~**P3: New Session can stick in `Creating session` while another Agent run is stopping or reloading.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** Observed during the seminar-planner GUI smoke: the backend created a blank thread, but the frontend could let late old-thread task refreshes or Agent-run stream events repopulate the visible blueprint/status after the user pressed `+`. The GUI now keeps selected-session tasks separate from a background task index, uses the background index only for session-card running dots/stop controls/grouping, and gates task refreshes plus stream events by the currently selected thread before updating the blueprint.
- **Failed approach recorded:** repeatedly clicking New Session while an old run is stopping adds state churn without proving the selection model. The durable fix is a selected-thread guard plus a separate background task index, not another retry of the create-thread call.

- ~~**P2: `#workspace` blueprint could leak an old completed answer into a newer active run.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** The seminar-planner smoke showed an older `blueprint smoke test ok` final answer rendered inside a new active trip-planner run because blueprint construction read all thread chunks/events/tasks instead of the current `activeRunId` slice. The blueprint builder now scopes answer chunks, Agent events, task snapshots, terminal progress, validation, repair, and final nodes to the active run when run ids are available.
- **Failed approach recorded:** thread-level history is not enough for live blueprint projection. A thread can contain multiple completed/active runs, so current-state nodes must be keyed by run id before falling back to whole-thread history.

- ~~**P2: `model.timeout` could render as a solid final answer while retry work was active.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** During the same smoke, a timeout event became a done `Final response` node even though Super DAN had entered retry/repair. `answerEventFromAgentEvents` now accepts only terminal `completed` events as final-answer evidence; timeout/failure rows remain progress or blocker evidence.
- **Failed approach recorded:** any terminal-looking event summary is not a user-facing final answer. The final node should only solidify on completion evidence or successful terminal task progress.

- ~~**P3: Chat V2 operator context missed absolute target folders and kept punctuation.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** The seminar-planner prompt named `/private/tmp/dan-blueprint-smoke/trip-planner?`, but `operator_context.target_paths` only captured extension-bearing filenames, so Super DAN did not receive the requested folder as a structured target. Chat V2 now extracts absolute folders, strips trailing punctuation such as `?`, and avoids treating URL paths as local target paths.
- **Failed approach recorded:** target inference based only on filenames with extensions misses common human requests that name a destination folder rather than a file.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN live smoke can fail provider-side on malformed tool schema.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** Observed 2026-06-24 during a browser-submitted `#workspace` smoke prompt asking for a no-edit exact reply. The Agent task failed with `Invalid request: tools.function.parameters.type is required and must be "object"` and trace refs under `.dan-super/runs/turn-25/events.jsonl`. `OpenAIProvider` now applies a Moonshot/Kimi compatibility profile that recursively normalizes tool schemas and flattens root `anyOf`/`oneOf` parameter unions into an object schema when that endpoint requires it.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a GUI smoke can prove surface-state honesty even when the backend run fails, but it cannot prove Super DAN execution health. The provider/tool-schema path needs its own backend regression before treating browser prompt execution as fully healthy.

- ~~**P1: Super DAN no-edit/no-command requests could still expose mutation paths.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** A read-only exact-reply smoke explicitly said not to edit files or run external commands, but the generic execution lane still treated the turn like workspace mutation work, allowing write/shell tools and validation retry pressure for missing changed files. Super DAN now derives an operator intent policy for no-mutation/no-shell requests, removes file write/edit and shell tools from both Super DAN and the local tool-loop projection, and lets no-mutation runs complete successfully with zero changed files.
- **Failed approach recorded:** prompt text alone is not a sufficient guardrail. No-edit/no-command constraints must become structured execution policy, tool filtering, validation expectations, and completion logic.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` blueprint could lose the selected New Session or hide a completed direct answer after reload.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** Live browser testing found a stale async restore could overwrite a freshly created blank session, reopening an older stopped/completed thread. A second live check showed completed read-only runs rendered as done but with only `Super DAN completed.` in the final node body, because terminal task progress was not used when the answer event stream was thin. Session selection now uses a sequence guard, and the blueprint final node falls back to completed task progress for human final answers while keeping failed/blocked/stopped runs blocked.
- **Failed approach recorded:** persisted thread messages and event streams are not always enough to rebuild a completed GUI state. The blueprint should treat the task snapshot's completed progress as an authoritative final-answer fallback, but only for successful completed tasks.

- ~~**P2: `#workspace` blueprint could overstate current work for queued, waiting, or failed tasks.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** Follow-up review found the GUI selected the first non-terminal task as active even though the task API is newest-first, so a newer queued/waiting/paused task could outrank a real running task. The queue panel also labeled any non-queued non-terminal task as `Active run`, and a live browser smoke showed a failed backend task continuing to display a ready execution node after reload. The GUI now selects only `status=running` as active, explicitly labels queued/waiting/input-needed/paused states, ignores backend untitled placeholder titles, and renders terminal failed/blocked/stopped tasks as blocked execution/final nodes.
- **Failed approach recorded:** "non-terminal" is not equivalent to "active." UI anchors and steer controls must distinguish running, queued, waiting, input-needed, paused, and terminal-attention states instead of collapsing them into active/queued.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` blueprint view could show fake work for a blank new session and miss early planning DAG detail.**~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24.** Screenshot review showed a new blank "New Super DAN Session" rendered as a completed Operator request plus queued Final response before any prompt was sent. Separately, Super DAN's planner had a `task_graph`, but the live planning/validation events only exposed ids/counts, so the GUI could not draw detailed future task placeholders until later payloads. The frontend now requires real user/task/run evidence before adding request/final blueprint nodes, and `live.planning.completed` / `live.plan_validation.completed` carry `task_graph`, ready/deferred ids, first-build slice, and dependency revision data.
- **Failed approach recorded:** deriving blueprint nodes from thread title alone is too eager, and emitting only ready/deferred ids forces the GUI to invent or flatten future work. The planner event payload itself should be the source of truth for projected task nodes.

- **P1: Super DAN existing-workspace polish can block itself from reading the files it must modify.** Observed 2026-05-26 in the disposable `medium-existing-site-polish` live eval. The objective explicitly said to improve an existing static site, but the operator-intent policy set `allow_existing_artifact_reuse=false` with constraint `Do not reuse existing workspace artifacts as source material`, causing repeated `file_read` denials for `index.html`, `styles.css`, `app.js`, and `validate_site.py`. The run was stopped incomplete after 227 events, 258.09s, and 112,530 tokens; no site files changed and `python validate_site.py` still fails with `expected a grid-based feature layout`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** retrying `file_read` or writing a plan around denied reads just burns live tokens. The intent classifier/policy projector must distinguish "create without reusing existing artifacts" from "modify/polish/repair this existing workspace", where reading target files and validators is required.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN capability scorer treated expected pre-fix test failures as final validation failures.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-26.** The `medium-source-repair` live eval correctly fixed `stats_tools.py` and ended with pytest passing, but the scorer initially kept the pre-fix failing pytest command in `tests_failed` and marked the run as missing fresh validation. The scorer now records `latest_test_exit_code` from shell validation commands and uses that latest command for validation scoring while preserving historical pass/fail counts for diagnostics.
- **Failed approach recorded:** total failure counts alone are insufficient for repair tasks, where an initial failing test is often the ground truth. Score the final validation state separately from historical diagnostic failures.

- ~~**P1: Super DAN live shell commands can hang past the requested validation budget.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-26.** The disposable `short-validation-truth` live eval supplied `working_directory: "."`; the runtime resolved that against the parent process cwd instead of the active workspace, starting repo-level `python -m pytest -q` instead of the tiny fixture. The sandbox timeout then killed only the shell wrapper, leaving the child pytest process alive and the tool call open. Relative shell working directories now resolve against the workspace root, and raw/sandboxed shell timeouts kill the subprocess session.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on `--validation-timeout`, `--max-tool-rounds`, or local command speed is not sufficient if a shell wrapper can leave a child process alive. Timeout cleanup must kill the process tree, and workspace-relative cwd handling must happen before invoking shell tools.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN capability scorer misread live event logs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-26.** The first scorer pass missed artifacts that live traces store in `tool.completed.result.path`, `changed_required_files`, and implementation capsules; it also over/under-counted tokens by mixing cumulative `usage_totals` with per-call usage and treated generic `tool.completed` rows as run completion. The scorer now parses live mutation fields, sums per-call model usage, recognizes pytest shell evidence, and requires a terminal `run.log.completed`/`run.log.failed` event.
- **Failed approach recorded:** event-log scoring cannot assume one flat synthetic schema. Live traces include staged workers, reused model-call ids, cumulative usage totals, capsules, and incomplete heartbeat-only tails; the evaluator must treat those fields explicitly.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` Notes graph pages rendered like ordinary Markdown instead of the knowledge-base graph.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-26.** The Notes preview now detects Hugo `layout: graph`, `graph.md`, and `/graph/` pages and renders an interactive pageID citation graph built from backend `layout` and `citations` metadata.
- **Failed approach recorded:** graph pages should not be approximated with raw Markdown or a separate app mode. The Notes preview can stay inside the same workspace surface and render the graph from Hugo/pageID metadata.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` development-root switching was too hidden in the Files rail.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-26.** The Work header now exposes a compact typed root-path field directly under the workspace selector, shows recent/current/nearby suggestions, and labels workspaces by the selected root folder name.
- **Failed approach recorded:** making users open the Files rail just to change root makes workspace switching feel like file browsing. Workspace identity belongs in the header; the file rail can still show the selected tree.

- ~~**P2: Flutter phone Work/Notes lagged behind the latest workspace GUI.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The phone app could view notes after backend connection, but there was no discoverable workspace-root setup, Work Chat did not submit through the GUI Agent-run contract, Files did not provide a phone-suitable root/file preview flow, and Notes Read lacked the cached live Hugo preview behavior. Flutter now has workspace-root suggestions in Settings/Files, API-backed file browsing/preview, Super DAN Agent-run send/steer, cached `/api/workspace-notes/preview`, Edit/Read autosave plus scroll restoration, and `@pageID` suggestions after one typed character.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a phone shell that only mirrors navigation labels is not enough. Phone pages need the same backend contracts as the GUI, with controls adapted to narrow screens instead of hiding workspace/session/root state in a crowded header.

- ~~**P2: Telegram Agent turns could serialize through one chat/thread lane.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Chat V2 built `surface_topic_key` from Telegram chat/thread identity even when the adapter supplied a narrower `conversation.lane_key`, so separate Telegram sessions could still see each other during admission. Chat V2 now scopes admission by `conversation.lane_key`; Telegram `/new` and parallel private-DM turns fork per-message lanes, and `/reset` / `/clear` force-clear the selected Telegram run state.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a reset command alone only treats the symptom. Telegram needs GUI-like lane/session keys to keep independent incoming messages from congesting one active task board.

- **P2: DAN phone relay peer registration is sudo-gated on `ny`.** The DAN phone Android peer config now exists separately from Codexx and is baked into `mobile/dist/dan-phone.apk`, but the NY relay still must add the phone public key as `10.77.77.6/32` on `wgny` before the phone can handshake. `ssh ny 'sudo -n ...'` reports `sudo: a password is required`, so the helper at `~/register-dan-phone-peer.sh` / `ops/wireguard/register-dan-phone-peer.sh` must be run with an interactive sudo password on the relay.
- **Failed approach recorded:** baking a phone config into the APK is not sufficient if the relay does not know that peer. Do not reuse Codexx's `10.77.77.5/32` phone config to bypass this; register the DAN phone peer separately.

- ~~**P2: Async chat-board admission mixed tasks from different surface-topic lanes.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** `build_task_board_snapshot` and foreground admission refresh paths filtered only by `thread_id`, so sessions from different surfaces/lanes sharing a thread could classify against each other. Async admission now threads `surface_topic_key` into board snapshots so each lane only sees its own active/queued work.
- **Failed approach recorded:** thread-only filtering is insufficient where GUI/Telegram/TUI use shared thread ids. Lanes need a lane-scoped key (`surface_topic_key`) for independent admission decisions.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` Work sessions had no visible stop or archive lifecycle controls.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Running sessions now expose a square stop request in the session rail and a Stop button in the active conversation header, both backed by the V2 Agent-run `stop` command. Session cards also expose soft archive/restore actions plus swipe archive/restore gestures, and archived threads move into an `Archived` group while remaining in server history.
- **Failed approach recorded:** hard-deleting sessions or relying on hidden command text would be the wrong lifecycle model. The GUI needs discoverable controls, stop must use the existing safe-boundary Agent command, and archive should be reversible metadata.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` made workspace switching look like session management.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The only visible controls were the Work/Notes switcher and the Sessions rail, so changing development workspaces was not discoverable when the file rail or rail footer was hidden. The header now has a direct workspace selector plus compact add/open/close controls, while the Sessions rail remains focused on sessions inside workspaces.
- **Failed approach recorded:** horizontal workspace tabs grew noisy and the session rail alone was too indirect. Workspace identity belongs in the header; session lists belong in the left rail.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` Notes autocomplete and taxonomy navigation were too broad.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** A blank `@` could match the whole pageID index, and frontmatter categories duplicated the Hugo root-folder organization. The mention menu now opens only after at least one character after `@`, while the Notes rail uses Tags plus first-level content Folders and preview chips navigate into those filters.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using frontmatter categories as a primary rail is redundant for this knowledge base, where root folders such as `blogs` and `notes` are the meaningful content-level buckets.

- **P2: DAN phone APK cannot be fully live-handshake tested without a phone peer config and attached Android device.** The APK now contains an isolated Android `VpnService` WireGuard bridge (`dan/wireguard`, tunnel `dan-phone`) and the host backend is live at `10.77.77.2:8000`, but no DAN phone WireGuard peer config exists in the repo and `adb` / `flutter devices` found no Android device or emulator. Structural APK/build/API tests pass; real VPN permission, tunnel start, and phone-to-host HTTP must be tested after connecting a phone/emulator and providing a DAN-specific peer config.
- **Failed approach recorded:** do not copy codexx WireGuard config or reuse codexx channel/tunnel settings to make the DAN APK appear connected. That would risk cross-app/service conflict; DAN needs its own peer config and app-scoped bridge.

- ~~**P2: `dan-up` phone auto-binding could break the desktop Vite proxy.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The server process could bind only to the phone/WireGuard address while the browser frontend still proxied `/api` to `127.0.0.1:8000`, producing `ECONNREFUSED` in Vite. `dan-up` auto mode now binds `0.0.0.0` when the phone WireGuard host exists so loopback and phone access both work, while local chat/health still use the loopback connect URL and `--host phone` remains the explicit phone-only path.
- **Failed approach recorded:** switching auto mode from loopback to the phone host fixes the APK but breaks the desktop frontend. The default needs one server reachable through both addresses; phone-only binding should be opt-in.

- ~~**P2: Telegram could get stuck when two DAN processes polled the same bot token.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The server log showed Telegram `getUpdates` conflict errors, which happen when a backend-owned adapter and a fleet/other bot process poll the same token. `dan-up` backend-adapter mode now disables Telegram adapter autostart while the server boots, stops a stale fleet before starting the backend adapter, and refuses to start a second backend poller if the fleet is still alive.
- **Failed approach recorded:** letting server adapter autostart and launcher-managed Telegram startup race is unsafe. Telegram polling ownership must be exclusive: default single adapter or explicit fleet, never both for the same token.

- ~~**P2: Super TUI browser-control probes could answer from workspace files instead of browser tools.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The router could correctly select `browser_control` with `permission_scope=transient_execute`, but the read-only model loop still exposed workspace tools and the fallback source preview could produce unrelated local Markdown/code as the answer. External-only browser/desktop probe policies now project UI tools only, preserve browser/desktop tool evidence for answer synthesis, and report UI-tool failure without substituting workspace source previews.
