- **Failed approach recorded:** treating every read-only/probe answer as eligible for bounded workspace inspection is too broad. The fallback must respect the structured evidence policy; when the policy excludes workspace, workspace previews are not a valid substitute.

- ~~**P3: Telegram `/sessions` exposed raw task ids as the primary session labels.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Telegram does not have the GUI's persistent card rail, so showing `task-...` values made session switching hard to reason about. Session menus now use the original task text/objective as the primary label, group recent available sessions by workspace, and move raw task ids into the per-session status detail.
- **Failed approach recorded:** task ids are stable machine keys, not user navigation labels. Remote session menus need workspace grouping plus human objective text first, with ids retained only for diagnostics.

- ~~**P2: Playwright browser automation could trigger a macOS Chrome keychain prompt.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Persistent browser sessions could surface the native "cannot find keychain for Chrome" dialog in unattended runs, which blocks browser navigation and causes unrelated page-wait failures. Chromium now launches with non-interactive keychain/password-store flags by default while keeping `DAN_BROWSER_DISABLE_KEYCHAIN=0` and `DAN_BROWSER_ARGS` as explicit operator overrides.
- **Failed approach recorded:** asking the operator to click through the native keychain dialog is not acceptable for browser automation. Browser-control launch defaults must be non-interactive, and desktop-control tools should only be used for intentional GUI tasks, not to clear avoidable auth/keychain prompts.

- ~~**P2: Browser-control live smoke failed when Playwright's managed Chromium binary was missing.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The Python Playwright package was installed, but the expected `chromium_headless_shell` executable was absent from the local cache, so real browser manipulation failed before DOM actions could run. Browser control now prefers the managed binary when present, falls back to an installed system Chrome/Chromium/Edge/Brave, and exposes `DAN_BROWSER_EXECUTABLE` for explicit override or disable.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating `import playwright` as sufficient readiness is too weak. Browser readiness also depends on an executable browser and, on macOS, the launch may require permission to start a GUI application even for headless smoke tests.

- ~~**P2: `dan-up` could start chat/server without an immediately usable Telegram Super surface.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The rich Telegram `/workspace` and `/session` behavior initially lived mainly in the standalone fleet path, so the normal `dan-up` experience could leave the user in chat while Telegram still required separate manual adapter/fleet startup. `dan-up` now starts the backend-owned single Telegram adapter by default, keeps `--telegram fleet` explicit for multi-bot setups, and the server-managed adapter owns the same inline workspace/session menus and selected-session command routing.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating Telegram Super UX as fleet-only makes the default single-bot phone surface feel disconnected from DAN Super. The default launcher should start one backend-owned Telegram surface, while fleet mode stays an opt-in advanced topology.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` refresh could lose the operator's current place.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The route already persisted panel visibility and the last global thread, but refresh still reset Work/Notes pane selection, selected chunk/file, filters, expanded file/note folders, and collapsed session groups. The workspace shell now persists those UI-state fields and restores the active workspace's saved session before falling back to the global last session.
- **Failed approach recorded:** persisting only visible panels is too shallow for a workspace GUI. Session/page/file/chunk selection and rail expansion are navigation state, not cosmetic state.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` Notes mounted only `content/notes` and hid the rest of the Hugo knowledge base.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The notes bootstrap mirrored Content mode too narrowly, so pages under `blogs`, root content files, and other Hugo sections were invisible and tags/categories could not function as content-level navigation. The backend now prefers the full Hugo `content` tree, returns section/tags/categories/pageID/date metadata for Markdown pages, and the frontend filters by section/category/tag with previous/parent/next page navigation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating the knowledge base as a notes-only directory loses the Hugo information architecture. The GUI Notes workspace should mount the content root and let facets handle narrowing.

- ~~**P2: Telegram selected-session follow-ups could accidentally fork or block active DAN Super work.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Telegram cannot switch sessions like the GUI rail, so a selected session now persists per thread/lane, ordinary follow-up messages append to the selected active run through `/api/v2/agent-runs/{run_id}/commands`, `/continue` queues after the current run, and `/new` bypasses the selected session to create separate background work.
- **Failed approach recorded:** reusing only GUI session switching semantics or creating a fresh Agent run for every Telegram follow-up loses the active background task. Telegram needs explicit inline menus plus a persisted selected-session binding that maps chat messages onto durable Agent-run commands.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN live could not directly use the existing computer-use/browser automation substrate.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Browser automation existed in `dan.tools` and server-side computer capabilities, while desktop control existed as a controller, but Super DAN's generic live tool policy exposed only workspace/shell/git/web-search tools. The generic Super DAN policy now keeps that base set by default and expands browser tabs/inspect/html/element metadata/screenshot/click/fill/type/select/download plus gated desktop observe/focus/click/type/hotkey wrappers through structured `capability_packs`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding task-specific Godot/browser commands or duplicating clipboard as a desktop tool would make the tool surface larger without adding capability. The durable shape is a general observe/metadata/screenshot/mouse/keyboard/element-action basket, with desktop mutation gated by `DAN_COMPUTER_CONTROL=1`.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` notes could stay blank after a failed or persisted loading state.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Notes restored from localStorage could keep `status=loading`, and the load effect skipped them forever; read failures also lacked a catch path, leaving the editor/preview blank. The note loader now retries stuck loading notes and writes explicit error content/status on failure.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating `loading` as a durable note state is wrong. Loading is a transient request state and must not suppress a future read attempt after refresh or session restore.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` Notes treated Hugo pages as flat raw Markdown.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Hugo bundle files such as `content/notes/train-manager/index.md` appeared as flat note rows, duplicated titles in the editor header, and rendered YAML frontmatter as document body. Notes now builds a bundle-aware tree, keeps raw frontmatter editable, renders only the page body in preview, and shows Hugo metadata/tags/categories/pageID as page chrome.
- **Failed approach recorded:** stripping frontmatter from the editor would hide the source of truth. The editor should remain raw Hugo Markdown while the preview is Hugo-aware.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` note switching could be slow or reload local edits unnecessarily.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Small Dropbox-backed notes could still approach the frontend request timeout, and list refreshes risked invalidating already-loaded local edits. Note reads now use mtime/size-aware backend and browser caches, while dirty/saving notes remain authoritative until autosave completes.
- **Failed approach recorded:** caching by path alone is unsafe because notes can be edited by the GUI, Hugo tools, another editor, or Dropbox. Cache hits must be tied to file mtime/size, and local dirty state must win over list refresh metadata.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` queue panel could show completed tasks and raw Agent events.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The bottom queue area mixed task snapshots with recent Agent event rows, so completed runs and structured terminal payloads could appear beside the composer. The panel now reads only active tasks plus explicit queued steer/next message records.
- **Failed approach recorded:** event streams are useful for conversation chunks and diagnostics, but the queue panel should model operator intent, not backend internals.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` conversation chunks could hide the rest of a rendered answer.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Chunk-card bodies used a fixed preview height and overflow clipping, so long Super DAN answers still required the sidecar or looked cut off. The main conversation column now renders full chunk bodies and wraps paragraphs, list items, and inline code.
- **Failed approach recorded:** preview-style truncation is wrong for the primary conversation column. If large-history performance becomes an issue, solve it with list virtualization instead of clipping individual answers.

- ~~**P3: `#workspace` chunk cards could still flatten formatted Super DAN summaries into one paragraph.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** The first raw-log fix converted terminal JSON into Markdown-like text, but the middle chunk card rendered that body as plain text, so `**Files:** - ...` and `**Risks:** - ...` still appeared inline. Chunk cards now use the shared Markdown renderer, structured summaries emit real Markdown sections, older inline projections are normalized at display time, and Answer/Outcome cards use muted semantic accents.
- **Failed approach recorded:** fixing only the right preview pane is insufficient because the middle chunk list is the primary conversation surface.

- ~~**P2: `#workspace` could show Super DAN terminal JSON as the assistant answer.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Completed Super DAN runs can emit structured candidate/result JSON with fields such as `change_summary`, `files_created`, and `risks`. The workspace frontend now formats those summaries into readable Answer/Files/Risks/Checks text and groups raw provider/tool event rows into compact working/outcome chunks.
- **Failed approach recorded:** hiding all event cards is too blunt because users still need progress and changed-file visibility. The primary conversation should be compact and human, while detailed raw event names remain secondary diagnostics.

- ~~**P2: Super TUI could understand a fresh compile-check request but still route it as read-only inspection.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** Transcript review showed a turn whose rationale said "fresh validation truth" while the legacy lane was `simple read-only`, so the TUI returned a workspace directory listing instead of running the reusable validation probe. Canonical `surface_policy` now outranks legacy `lane`, no-execution routes with action/probe-shaped policy get a model consistency review, and validation-gate dispatch runs before the generic read-only fallback.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding compile/build keywords or project-specific Godot logic would make routing brittle. The durable fix is policy consistency: the model-authored orthogonal policy must own controller/permission/evidence/phase, and deterministic code should only enforce that structured policy.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI `Answer` panels could lose their bottom border under the next chatbox.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** Rich panels were rendered directly through prompt-toolkit's stdout proxy, and replacement line counts were estimated from wrapped input lines rather than the exact rendered panel. The TUI now renders each framed panel to a complete string first, writes it atomically, and counts that same rendered text for later clears.
- **Failed approach recorded:** estimating panel height as `2 + wrapped_lines` is fragile once Rich tables, ornaments, terminal width, and prompt-toolkit redraws are involved. The renderer should count the actual text it emits.

- ~~**P3: Terminal failed runs could still sound like follow-up repair work was queued.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** Internal `super.hook.packet_enqueued` events set `queue_status` during a run, but the status survived `run.log.completed` / `run.log.failed`, so later narrator answers described old immune/validation packets as if they were future work. Terminal run events now clear that internal queue from the narrator snapshot.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating every internal hook packet as durable future work confuses current state. Internal same-run packets are progress signals while the run is active; after terminal status, only durable task-board queued rows should be presented as queued work.

- ~~**P2: `#workspace` sessions could look empty even when Super DAN had run history.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** Some Agent sessions had an empty `/api/chats` message projection while their durable Super DAN task/run/event records existed, so clicking the session showed "no saved messages." The workspace loader now reads the thread task list, run command payload, and run event log, reconstructs visible conversation chunks, and saves that projection back to the thread.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating `message_count=0` as authoritative loses Agent-run history. For the workspace GUI, the durable Super DAN task/run ledger is the source of truth when the chat-thread projection is incomplete.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI chatbox timers could jump between routing and execution clocks.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** Background turns set `_tui_turn_started_at` when the user submits the message, but the live progress renderer could initialize `started_at_monotonic` later from `run.log.started`. The renderer and other TUI state paths now use a shared submitted-turn timestamp so `Routing` and `Working` elapsed time count from the same message start.
- **Failed approach recorded:** fixing only scheduler heartbeats leaves renderer/tool/stream progress on a separate clock. The timer source must be owned by the turn and inherited by every status producer.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN live workers could rediscover immediately previous TUI findings instead of acting on them.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21 minimally.** Surface conversation history still flows into live briefs, and now the latest assistant answer is also exposed as `surface_already_known` with an instruction to treat clear prior findings as starting facts unless file fingerprints or validation contradict them.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying only on raw transcript history makes prior findings soft background prose. The minimal fix is a named prompt packet, not task-specific extraction or regex routing.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI long routing waits could tempt canned progress prose.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** The chatbox scheduler now keeps routing heartbeats mechanical unless a real renderer/narrator event supplies detail. Long route waits can update elapsed time in the existing `Routing` box, but they do not inject fixed fallback sentences or fake `Narrator:` text.
- **Failed approach recorded:** replacing fake narrator text with a fixed scheduler sentence is still a canned response. During routing, the surface should show only mechanical wait state until model-authored routing or real execution progress exists.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI direct validation could hijack proposal/diagnosis turns after a compile check.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** Transcript review from `ra-neo/.dan-super/tui/transcript.jsonl` showed the router rationale correctly understood a lag/performance proposal request, but the canonical policy still returned `phase_shape=validation_gate`; the TUI then ran the cached compile script and answered "fresh validation passed" instead of proposing next steps. Validation-gate routes now get a model repair pass for pure-validation ambiguity, and the foreground fast path only runs when the execution policy is a pure no-repair probe.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding lag/compile/proposal keyword exceptions would make this task-specific. The durable boundary is structural: model-authored phase shape must be rechecked by meaning, and deterministic execution may only enforce a pure `validation_gate` without repair-loop authority.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI successful direct validation printed a redundant `Outcome` block above the next chatbox.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** The fast validation path marked `state.validation=passed` and reused the generic terminal renderer, which produced `DAN · Outcome: - Checks: passed` after the answer. Successful direct validation now renders just the answer plus elapsed footer; failed/blocked validation still shows outcome details.
- **Failed approach recorded:** globally hiding all validation outcomes would remove useful failure context. The fix is scoped to successful direct validation, where `Checks: passed` duplicates the answer and harms chatbox continuity.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI fresh validation/status questions could take the full executor path or answer from stale narrator state.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** The model router now explicitly treats current build/test/check truth as fresh validation evidence, and the TUI enforces `execute + transient_execute + validation_gate` with a foreground project-local validation command instead of starting broad workspace discovery or replaying stale snapshots.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding build/compile keyword shortcuts would recreate brittle deterministic routing. The generic boundary is the model-authored `surface_policy`; deterministic code only enforces the returned validation-gate policy and caches reusable workspace validation script discovery by fingerprint.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI intent routing conflated lane, latency, answer length, progress detail, and loop shape.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** The router now asks for a canonical orthogonal `surface_policy` with separate controller, permission, evidence, phase, autonomy, latency, response, progress, and admission axes. Legacy lane/communication/execution policies are preserved as compatibility projections instead of being the only policy shape.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding more lane names or treating `latency_preference` as a complete fast-path guarantee makes routing brittle. Lane should choose the controller owner; evidence freshness, phase shape, permissions, latency, response size, progress visibility, and admission relation need separate policy axes.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI progress could mechanically echo the user's objective and final answers could look clipped by policy.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** Routine progress text such as shell-command updates no longer embeds the objective text, renderer notes sanitize objective echoes before printing, and final-answer display preserves the full stored answer rather than mechanically truncating to `answer_budget`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using the communication policy as both a model-output budget and a terminal display cap makes correct answers look missing. The budget should guide the model prompt/tokens; the renderer should show the answer it actually received.

- ~~**P3: Repeated workspace reads could reuse or repeat context without proving the file was unchanged.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** Local organism tool loops and server capability loops now gate `file_read` cache hits on a fresh file fingerprint, and local cache hits skip ordinary read start/completion events so the TUI stops showing repeated "examining workspace" work when the same file/range is unchanged.
- **Failed approach recorded:** caching by path/range alone is not safe because the user, a shell command, or another worker may mutate the file between reads. File-read reuse must be invalidation-aware, and absent fingerprint evidence should fall back to a real read.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI long answers could appear incomplete in framed output or transcript replay.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-21.** Screenshot review showed a narrator answer whose final words were present in `.dan-super/tui/transcript.jsonl` but not reliably visible in the framed terminal output. Stream text now pre-wraps with extra margin for panel border/padding/ornament overhead, reducing second-stage Rich wrapping under the live composer, and transcript previews explicitly mark clipped long answer lines.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on Rich to perform a second wrap after the TUI has counted rows makes live prompt redraws fragile. The TUI should pre-wrap to the actual interior width of its framed layout and disclose replay truncation.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI chatbox status refresh could flash blank or erase queued-message boxes.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-20.** Screenshot review showed active `DAN · Chat -> Working:` refreshes occasionally disappearing and then returning, and queued messages being overwritten by later active work ticks. The clear/redraw helper now defers the erase flush until the replacement panel is printed, and queue notices live as prefix blocks inside the same replaceable status group as the active ticker.
- **Failed approach recorded:** printing a durable queue panel below a separately replaceable active status panel breaks the replace invariant: the active panel is no longer the last terminal block. Queue notices and active status need to be redrawn as one group while queued work is waiting.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI later chatbox progress could still stack `Chat -> Thinking` boxes and keep saying "Still routing".**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Screenshot review showed scheduler heartbeats, renderer notes, and narrator/progress updates printing separate thinking boxes at 11s, 16s, 26s, and later, while the latest ticker no longer behaved like one live status row. Chatbox thinking output now uses one shared replaceable lane across the scheduler and renderer paths; a later follow-up split that lane into `Routing` vs `Working` and removed scheduler-authored heartbeat prose entirely.
- **Failed approach recorded:** fixing only the scheduler redraw path leaves other progress producers free to append new boxes. The chat-thinking lane needs one shared terminal-state owner, not per-producer line counts.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI scheduler heartbeats could still mask true progress with fake narrator wording and a fixed `Thinking` label.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Follow-up review showed that `Narrator: Still working/routing...` was not a real narrator event and could overwrite more useful renderer/tool progress. The shared chatbox lane now distinguishes `Routing` from `Working`; scheduler ticks update only elapsed time and preserve the last real progress detail.
- **Failed approach recorded:** attributing scheduler-owned heartbeats to `Narrator:` makes progress text look semantically meaningful when it is only a timer. Elapsed-time refresh and model-authored/renderer-authored progress detail need separate authority.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI could print prompt-toolkit CPR warnings into the chatbox transcript in terminals without cursor-position replies.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Live pseudo-TTY review printed `WARNING: your terminal doesn't support cursor position requests (CPR).` between chatbox redraws. Prompt-toolkit chatbox sessions now construct an output object with CPR probing disabled, so unsupported-terminal capability warnings do not enter the user-visible transcript.
- **Failed approach recorded:** leaving prompt-toolkit defaults unchanged assumes every TTY path can answer CPR probes. The Super TUI chatbox should prefer stable framed input and clean transcript output over terminal capability probing.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI boxed thinking refreshes could stack repeated boxes and lose narrator attribution.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Live screenshot review showed each elapsed heartbeat printing a new `DAN · Chat -> Thinking:` box, and the follow-up question noted that narrator detail was missing. ANSI-capable terminals now clear and redraw the previous bounded thinking block before printing the next elapsed update, while the detail line stays explicitly prefixed as `Narrator:`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** updating elapsed time by emitting another complete progress panel is visually correct only in captured logs, not in an interactive chatbox. Long-running prompt progress needs a single replaceable lane when control sequences are available, with plain/captured output still allowed to remain line-oriented.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI chatbox thinking acknowledgement could stay at `Thinking 0s...` for long waits.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Live TTY review showed the boxed acknowledgement returning the composer correctly, but no later elapsed update appeared until the answer. `TuiChatboxTurnScheduler` now prints bounded refresh blocks in the same `DAN · Chat -> Thinking:` lane, starting after one second and backing off, while keeping the old loose prompt-adjacent clock disabled. Provider/clarification blockers now also report elapsed time from the original submitted turn.
- **Failed approach recorded:** removing the loose clock solved prompt collision but made long waits look frozen. The refresh belongs in the same boxed chat-thinking lane, not in a second progress surface.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI read-only follow-ups could still preview a report instead of reviewing it.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Screenshot review showed "I mean the open report" resolving the file but returning the beginning of the markdown report as the answer, while the source-inspection notice printed as a loose `DAN · Source inspected` row outside the chat-thinking lane. Read-only fallback now carries recent visible user turns into source resolution/answer-shape decisions, recognizes review/takeaway-style context across follow-ups, synthesizes markdown/report metadata plus prose takeaways, and routes background source-inspection progress through boxed `DAN · Chat -> Thinking:` output.
- **Failed approach recorded:** resolving the referenced file is not enough for a good read-only answer. The read-only fallback must preserve the requested answer shape from visible context and should not use raw previews as the default response to review/summarization requests.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI chatbox progress mixed boxed and loose thinking rows.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Screenshot review showed a boxed `DAN · Chat -> Thinking:` acknowledgement while later updates appeared as separate loose `Thinking Ns` / `DAN · ...` lines above the live composer. Background chatbox turns now suppress the scheduler's prompt-adjacent loose clock, and renderer/narrator progress for those turns is routed through boxed `DAN · Chat -> Thinking:` blocks with elapsed text.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a separate live prompt clock competes with the chatbox frame and makes the transcript look like two progress systems. Background chatbox progress should stay in one presentation lane; explicit board/detail views remain `/tasks`, `/status`, and `/inside`.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI "chatbox" still looked like a plain prompt under a help panel, could stretch over empty reserved rows, and had a colliding bottom menu.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Screenshot review showed the input as `super-tui>` below a large `DAN · Message` instruction panel even though background dispatch was chatbox-like, then live review showed the bordered prompt stretching because prompt-toolkit reserved idle completion-menu rows inside the box, and a later screenshot showed the permanent bottom control strip colliding with the composer. Prompt-toolkit sessions now render a rounded framed `DAN · Chat` input prompt with the title in the top border, a real bottom edge, and small inner margin; idle completion-menu reserved rows are zero; the permanent bottom menu is removed while keeping keybindings/completions; accepted/queued background turns print compact elapsed `Chat -> Thinking` / `Chat -> Queue` acknowledgements without objective echo; and the large `Message` panel remains only for readline/non-TTY fallback.
- **Failed approach recorded:** making the dispatcher asynchronous is not enough for perceived chatbox UX, and a prompt-toolkit `bottom_toolbar` is not a reliable way to visually close the composer. The input affordance needs to use the terminal prompt frame itself, while startup guidance should not visually compete with it in capable terminals.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI still auto-rendered board/session panels and repeated adjacent narrator lines.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Screenshot review showed ordinary turns printing `DAN · Board` automatically, a plain `DAN · Session` objective/mode preamble, and repeated narrator/progress statements while the fixed composer sat below the scrollback. Default live rendering now treats board rows as command-only source data, suppresses the plain session preamble, removes board snapshots from default recent/plain views, and applies generic semantic de-duplication before printing narrator/model-sidecar progress.
- **Failed approach recorded:** making the board more compact still leaves the ordinary chat surface feeling like a dashboard. The durable board should stay available through `/tasks`, `/status`, `/inside`, and debug/raw views, while the passive stream shows only answer/progress text that changed materially.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN live could exceed provider context limits after simple follow-up file operations.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** A cleanup turn over recent artifacts triggered a builder retry with a multi-megabyte protected prompt packet, bypassed normal old-tool replay compaction, and failed with a provider context-length error. Super DAN live now uses a 120k/180k prompt replay envelope and hard-compacts oversized protected user/system/structured prompt packets when replay still exceeds the active budget.
- **Failed approach recorded:** only compacting old tool messages is insufficient when the huge content is in protected prompt/evidence packets. Provider-facing replay needs a final hard budget pass that preserves head/tail context and points back to tools/logs for exact omitted details.

- ~~**P2: Shell-based file changes were not counted as material workspace mutations.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** A terminal-native cleanup command could remove/move files successfully but then trigger retry because completion logic only trusted structured `file_write`/`file_edit` paths. The local runtime now snapshots the workspace before/after `shell_command`, attaches `workspace_changes`, and feeds those changed paths into Super DAN mutation accounting.
- **Failed approach recorded:** parsing shell command text would be brittle and task-specific. The generic signal is filesystem state before/after the command, excluding runtime/cache/history directories.

- ~~**P3: Short Super TUI follow-ups could lose visible conversation context and over-search runtime stores.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-19.** Follow-up requests such as "combine them" or "remove the other reports" could be interpreted without the preceding visible TUI conversation, while read-only discovery surfaced `.dan-*`, `.agent-subsessions`, and memory-store artifacts as if they were user deliverables. Recent visible transcript turns now flow into async/live/backend request context, and read-only discovery skips DAN runtime/history stores unless explicitly named.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding report/OPEC-specific filters or canned follow-up handling would only hide one case. The surface should carry bounded conversation context and use general workspace-scope guidance for indirect artifact references.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI background thinking/final-answer clocks could collide with the live prompt.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** When an interactive turn was dispatched in a background thread, the route/model clock could render `Thinking 1s` or `Preparing answer 4s` on the same terminal line as `super-tui>`. Background-dispatched router, executor, and narrator-answer clocks now force line-safe output even when stdout supports ANSI control sequences, leaving prompt-toolkit to redraw the prompt cleanly.
- **Failed approach recorded:** in-place clock rendering is fine for blocking foreground waits, but unsafe while the prompt is already active. Background terminal progress must be line-oriented.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI overused plan mode for broad review requests.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** Requests such as "help me review this project" can now route as read-only workspace review instead of opening planning questions by default. The router prompt narrows `plan_mode` to explicit planning/refinement before execution, and any model-selected `plan_mode` route gets one stricter model recheck when it might really be review/audit/inspection.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating breadth or missing review scope as a reason for plan mode makes the TUI feel like it is refusing to work. Broad project review should inspect the current workspace and report findings unless the user explicitly asks to plan/refine first.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI advertised duplicate command names and one-shot steering commands could fall through to model routing.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** Help, autocomplete, composer hints, README docs, and board controls now use the canonical command surface (`/progress`, `/inside`, `/stop`) while compatibility aliases (`/last`, `/trace`, `/cancel`, `/continue`, `/queues`) remain accepted but hidden. One-shot `/new`, `/append`, `/stop`, `/reset`, and `/exit` now resolve locally instead of being sent to the model router.
- **Failed approach recorded:** listing every alias as a top-level command makes the TUI look larger and less predictable, and routing unsupported one-shot slash commands as free text creates provider failures for simple local controls. The visible registry should be minimal, with legacy aliases handled below the presentation layer.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI `/status` and `/tasks` could show a fake active admission row for a status-only request.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** Status-only `chat_or_status` async admissions are no longer bucketed as active executor work; the board now reports no active executor work while preserving real recent runs.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating every foreground admission decision as work-in-flight makes status commands look untrustworthy. Presentation code should distinguish status/reporting rows from executor rows.

- ~~**P3: Captured Super TUI working clocks could concatenate progress ticks.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** Non-TTY clock updates now render as separate plain lines instead of `Thinking 0sThinking 1s...`; TTY output still uses in-place control-sequence refresh.
- **Failed approach recorded:** writing no-newline clock text is only valid when the terminal supports line clearing. Captured logs need line-oriented progress.

- ~~**P3: Super TUI response shape was fixed after lane routing, so small/status turns and deep review/repair turns shared the same answer/progress budget.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** The shared model router now emits a normalized communication policy with answer budget, latency preference, progress detail, and interaction style; Super TUI enforces that policy in final-answer line/token caps, read-only loop limits, narrator sidecar verbosity, async admission metadata, and transcript metadata.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding deterministic keyword/regex rules for words such as "brief", "detailed", "fast", or "progress" would recreate the old route-drift problem. Those words remain free-text semantics for the model router; deterministic code only normalizes/enforces the structured policy the router returns.

- ~~**P2: Ranged `file_read` results could be misread as whole-file size/context.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** **Observed during the `ra-neo` terrain-command pass:** Super TUI narrator/model state repeatedly claimed `GameLoop.gd` had shrunk because a targeted read returned 155 lines from an 854-line file. `file_read` now reports `returned_line_count`, `total_line_count`, selected line bounds, and prompt replay line numbers preserve the original source offsets.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a single `line_count` field is ambiguous once tools support line windows. Tool summaries and context capsules must distinguish returned evidence from total artifact size.

- ~~**P2: Source-file repair objectives with non-Python/web extensions could enter run-local planning.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** **Observed during `ra-neo` terrain repair:** a bounded `.gd` source-file repair wrote a temporary run-local plan and validated that plan instead of editing the named source file. The planner gate now recognizes a broader source-extension set plus explicit file/edit/implementation wording as direct interactive source implementation work.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on only a few extensions (`.py`, `.js`, `.html`, etc.) preserves planning detours for other real source files. Planner skip logic should key off named source files generically, not product/domain-specific commands.

- ~~**P2: GDScript source-shape guard missed duplicate top-level declarations and indentation corruption.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** **Observed during the `ra-neo` terrain-command loop:** Godot rejected duplicate `class_name`, duplicate member variables, missing key-branch indentation, and orphan over-indented flash lines that the lightweight profile initially missed. The registered profile now rejects duplicate `class_name`, duplicate member variables, and unexpected indentation in addition to malformed function headers, duplicate functions, empty blocks, duplicate locals, and unreachable statements.
- **Failed approach recorded:** passing a lightweight syntax/profile check is not enough if it only models function-local corruption. Source profiles need cheap top-level declaration and indentation sanity checks for common parse failures.

- ~~**P2: Source-structure edit failures could loop without a targeted reread.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** **Observed during `ra-neo` repair:** after duplicate-function, empty-control-block, and unexpected-indentation rejections, Super TUI kept repeating stale blockers or trying broad overwrites. The local runtime now emits a source-structure recovery nudge that treats the file as unchanged, forbids retrying the same patch, allows one focused `file_read`, and tells the worker to leave already-satisfied files alone.
- **Failed approach recorded:** generic malformed-tool-argument nudges do not cover semantic source guards. Structural mutation failures need a separate recovery path that preserves the same edit tool instead of disabling it or escalating to whole-file rewrites.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN live validation could wait on model validation before cheap operator shell gates.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** **Observed during the `ra-neo` terrain-command pass:** the run spent minutes in model-side validation while the custom source guard could have failed immediately on missing terrain/drag features and a bad `range_sq` repair. Explicit `--validation-command` checks now run before the model validator; failures skip model validation and go straight into repair/final validation state.
- **Failed approach recorded:** placing deterministic operator checks after model validation makes fail-fast ordering ineffective when the model validator is slow. Cheap structural/runtime gates should execute before model judgment when the operator supplies them.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN source tools could insert same-block statements after `return`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** **Observed during the `ra-neo` terrain-command pass:** a repair inserted `range_sq[slot] = 144.0` after `_pack_id(...)` had already returned, leaving the intended shot-range change unreachable and masking the real initialization site. The registered source-shape profile guard now rejects non-comment code immediately after a same-block `return`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** duplicate-function, empty-block, malformed-header, duplicate-local, and shrink checks still miss bad localized edits that preserve syntax but land in unreachable source. Source-shape guards need a small set of control-flow sanity checks, not only parser-like shape checks.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN could corrupt function signatures without triggering the source-shape guard.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** **Observed during the `ra-neo` tactical pass:** Super TUI turned valid signatures such as `_find_child_by_class(node: Node, class_name_str: String) -> Node` and `_update_selection_panel() -> void` into bare `func _find_child_by_class` / `func _update_selection_panel` lines. External Godot validation would catch this, but the pre-write guard did not. The registered source-shape profile guard now flags malformed function headers before `file_edit`/`file_write` can mutate disk.
- **Failed approach recorded:** checking only duplicate functions, empty blocks, duplicate locals, and file shrinkage is not enough. Common syntax-loss patterns need their own cheap line-shape checks before the model can write them.

- ~~**P3: `workspace_check(check="syntax")` ignored registered source-shape profiles.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18.** **Observed during `ra-neo` turn 19:** Super TUI correctly repaired the source and external guards/probes passed, but an internal `workspace_check syntax=auto` call failed because only Python/JSON/HTML were supported. `workspace_check` now consults the registered source-shape profile registry.
- **Failed approach recorded:** telling workers to use structured checks is counterproductive if common project source files are unsupported and surface as false tool blockers. Structured checks should degrade to language-appropriate lightweight validation where full parsing is unavailable.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN source tools could allow line-count-preserving source corruption.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18 for the observed generic patterns.** **Observed 2026-05-18 in the `ra-neo` Super TUI loop:** a focused `GameLoop.gd` repair preserved roughly the same file size but introduced duplicate `_start_round` blocks, empty control-flow structure, and repeated `var offset` declarations inside `_spawn_team`, which then caused parse/runtime failures and long validation timeouts. `file_write`/`file_edit` now share registered source-shape guards for duplicate function definitions, empty control blocks, and duplicate local variable declarations, in addition to the existing source-shrink guards.
- **Failed approach recorded:** line-count or character-ratio checks alone only catch stub/truncation damage. Mutation tools need cheap language-shape guards for obvious parser failures before any model-authored source edit reaches disk.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN validation kept running expensive later checks after a mandatory gate failed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-18 by default fail-fast.** **Observed 2026-05-18 during `ra-neo` validation:** after `python3 /private/tmp/ra_neo_source_guard.py` failed immediately, Super TUI still launched headless Godot gameplay/playability probes that timed out for minutes against a malformed source tree. Shell validation now stops at the first failed command by default and logs a `live.validation.shell_check.short_circuited` event; `--validation-continue-on-failure` preserves the old full-sweep behavior when desired.
- **Failed approach recorded:** collecting every possible validation failure is worse UX when checks are ordered from cheap structural gates to expensive runtime probes. For operator-provided validation lists, command order should be treated as a dependency chain unless the operator explicitly asks for a full sweep.

- **P3: Super DAN TUI can still answer a concrete continuation repair as a no-op.** **Observed 2026-05-18:** the prompt "Continue the generic Super TUI repair..." with a precise one-line `GameLoop.gd` edit returned `DAN · Answer: Done` immediately, created no new run, and left the source guard failing. Rephrasing the same request as "Edit the workspace now..." plus `--no-async-agent` admitted a live run and made the edit.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on route-model intent around the word "continue" is unsafe in one-shot non-interactive TUI usage. A workspace-changing prompt with an explicit path and replacement should not be allowed to finalize without either a live admission/tool mutation or a clear blocker.

- **P3: Super DAN TUI narrator summaries can preserve stale blockers after the file state changes.** **Observed 2026-05-18 during `ra-neo` turns 16-17:** narrator/final summaries continued to mention missing `agent_mesh_instance.multimesh = mm`, duplicate `offset`, and unrelated validator-scene parse errors after independent source inspection showed only the typed spawn assignment remained. Final successful outcomes now hide resolved historical blockers, but failed-turn summaries still need stronger current-state reconciliation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** appending validation failures to a bounded blocker list without invalidating them after repair makes the terminal surface look less reliable than the underlying file state. For failed turns, current blockers should be rederived from the latest validation event or an explicit final source check, not from the whole run history.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN TUI interactive source implementation requests can be over-routed into run-local planning or read loops before source edits.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-17 for strong interactive source-implementation signals.** **Observed 2026-05-17 on `ra-neo/.dan-super/runs/turn-02` and `turn-03`:** a request to make a tactical demo playable wrote/validated `.dan-super/runs/.../plans/*.md` or spent several minutes on context and immune/validation queue churn before material source edits. A negative phrase such as "do not stop after planning notes" also tripped the old bare `plan` substring detector. Super DAN now recognizes interactive source-implementation objectives, skips the optional planner unless the operator explicitly asks for planning, and passes metadata that lowers the first-write read threshold and removes the extra final-read allowance after the write-stage nudge.
- **Failed approach recorded:** prompt-only instructions such as "do not stop after planning notes" and generic "source edits first" wording were too weak. The planner gate and local runtime write-stage policy both needed task-shape-aware but non-project-specific signals.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN TUI live validation can pass a run that still fails an operator-requested runtime check.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-17 for explicit validation commands; ordering corrected 2026-05-18.** **Observed 2026-05-17 on `ra-neo/.dan-super/runs/turn-03/events.jsonl`:** the TUI run reported validation passed after repairing `GameLoop.gd`, but an independent `HOME=/private/tmp/godot-home /Applications/Godot.app/Contents/MacOS/Godot --headless --path godot --quit` review produced a large MultiMesh runtime-error flood (`set_instance_color` while colors were disabled). Subsequent Super TUI turns repaired it, but the first pass should not have been marked clean. `--validation-command` now runs workspace shell checks before model validation, logs command output summaries, and fails on nonzero exits or runtime/compiler error lines even when the process exits 0.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on the model validator to infer success from code shape and exit code is too weak for workloads with noisy stderr and tools that exit 0 despite script/runtime errors. Requested validation commands need deterministic stderr/error-pattern gates and surfaced command-output summaries before the model can declare pass.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN TUI targeted bugfix turns can still spend too long on repeated context reads before material edits.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-22.** **Observed 2026-05-17 during `ra-neo` turns 4 and 5:** narrow fixes for `GameLoop.gd` and `InstanceValidator.gd` first wrote `.dan-super/runs/.../plans/*.md`, retried wrong paths such as `godot/GameLoop.gd`, and required several minutes before the actual source edits landed. Super DAN live now recognizes generic targeted source/test/compiler/runtime repair prompts, skips the run-local planner for them, and carries `targeted_source_repair` into the builder brief so the worker must make a material source/test edit after focused inspection or report a precise blocker before validation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a plan-file-only write is not progress for a narrow compiler/runtime repair. The generic live lane should escalate to source-edit pressure faster when the objective names a concrete file, line, and validation command.

- ~~**P2: Team-leader `team-status --full` can crash on project status rendering.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-17.** **Observed 2026-05-17 during `ra-neo-steam-demo`:** `python3 .../team_leader.py team-status --project ra-neo-steam-demo --full --exit-when-settled` raised `NameError: name 'path_checkpoints' is not defined`, forcing monitoring back to `status`, `show`, and raw logs. The installed team-leader skill now loads path checkpoints/warnings before rendering the full status summary.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on the full compact project view during a live managed run is unsafe until this renderer path is covered; the fallback status commands worked, but the main high-level view should fail closed to a partial summary instead of crashing.

- **P2: Super DAN TUI live provider failures still need a failed-admission trace.** **PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-05-17.** **Observed 2026-05-17 while trying to develop `/Volumes/data/Dropbox/Projects/ra-neo` only through `dan super-tui`:** multiple non-interactive live attempts returned only `I could not route that request safely: Connection error.` before creating a new `.dan-super/runs/.../events.jsonl`, so there was no run trace to inspect and no clear missing-provider/config hint. Super TUI now reports the route model, provider/config nature of the failure, visible key/base-url sources, and local slash-command fallbacks. Explicit `--live` provider/runtime failures now fall forward into the normal live runner, replay the latest event log in the TUI failure summary, include trace path, and return a nonzero unified `dan` exit code; remaining work is the route-only case before any live run can be admitted.
- **Failed approach recorded:** retrying with a specific model (`kimi-k2.6`) and toggling the local/installed TUI surface did not unblock the run. The product needs a provider/config preflight that reports which config source is in use, whether a route model is available, and where the failure trace lives even when admission fails.

- ~~**P3: Team-leader can keep a child displayed as running after the provider process is no longer cancellable.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-17.** **Observed 2026-05-17 on run `20260517-202227-super-tui-worker`:** the child wrote a final blocked report to `last_message.md`, but project status still showed `running` with stale heartbeat; `team_leader.py cancel ...` returned `error: run is not currently alive`, and `reconcile` did not mark it terminal. The installed team-leader skill now reconciles terminal provider stdout events such as `turn.completed` into `completed` state and clears stale running metadata.
- **Failed approach recorded:** manual `cancel` plus `reconcile` was not enough to align the registry with process reality. Stale heartbeat handling should promote a no-longer-alive child with a final message into a terminal state, or expose a safe registry repair command.

- **P3: Super DAN TUI can promote tiny/read-only display requests into live planner/build runs.** **Observed 2026-05-12 from `io-similarity/.dan-super/runs/turn-09/events.jsonl`:** interactive `super-tui> show me the todo list` ran `live.planning`, wrote `.dan-super/runs/turn-09/plans/1-todo-consolidation.md`, validated that plan, and then entered `live.generic_build` with write tools instead of simply showing `ToDo.md`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a todo-specific local fast path was too narrow and was reverted the same day. The durable fix should be a generic intent gate for tiny/read-only display/status/introspection turns before `force_live=not plan_only`, not a growing list of task-specific exceptions.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN treated terminal-native copy/import work like ordinary text file editing.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-11.** **Observed from an `io-similarity` Super DAN TUI run:** the generic builder had `shell_command` available in early rounds but chose `list_directory` / `file_read` / `file_write`, then the shared runtime narrowed later rounds to direct file tools so terminal-native copy operations were no longer available.
- **Failed approach recorded:** only adding a transfer-specific detector would hard-code one command family and repeat the same routing brittleness. The generic fix is to improve root platform/tool context, describe `shell_command` as the local command-line toolbox for terminal-native work, keep install behavior cautious, and preserve already-enabled `shell_command` through narrowed write-capable stages.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN TUI dropdown suggestions fell back to readline immediately after the `/` / `$` trigger change.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-11.** **Observed from the TUI shell:** after the boxed composer rendered, the prompt printed `Dropdown suggestions unavailable; falling back to Tab completion.` before `super-tui>`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** unit-testing completion candidates and key bindings was not enough because the real `PromptSession` setup still imported `CompleteStyle` from an incompatible module path. The fix imports `CompleteStyle` from `prompt_toolkit.shortcuts`, extracts session construction into a tested helper, and keeps readline Tab completion only as the genuine fallback.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN broad-plan repair could overreact by treating deferred roadmap work as immediate validation failure.**~~ **FIXED 2026-05-11.** **Observed from `website/.dan-super/runs/turn-11/events.jsonl`:** the planner created a coherent foundation/app-showcase plan, but the first builder only made a trivial edit and falsely ticked a plan task; the validator then converted all missing downstream app work into one generic repair brief, which pushed the repair worker into broad HTML/JS surgery and left broken `index.html` / `app.js`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** choosing one hard first slice helps prevent scope leakage, but it is too rigid for Super DAN's intended parallelism. Current behavior records a predicted dependency-frontier DAG with ready/deferred tasks and owned paths; validators repair only current-frontier blockers, deferred downstream gaps stay queued as future DAG work, and `--worktree-parallelism` can run extra non-conflicting ready tasks through admission-gated isolated worktrees.

- ~~**P2: Telegram V2 over-routed ordinary chat into durable Agent/Super DAN runs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-30.** **Observed from live Telegram screenshot:** `hi again` consumed a full Agent run with ~219k tokens, then a short OPEC follow-up failed as an Agent task instead of continuing as chat.
- **Failed approach recorded:** forcing all ordinary Telegram V2 text into `/api/v2/agent-runs` avoided one legacy-stream fallback symptom but broke the product contract: normal phone chat became expensive long-running work. Current routing keeps plain text on V2 Chat, and sends only explicit `/agent` / `/run` / `/build` / `agent:` turns or high-confidence workspace/task text to durable Agent runs.

- ~~**P2: Telegram pure-V2 could still spam legacy "response stream ended" fallback messages for ordinary text.**~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-04-30.** **Observed from live Telegram screenshot:** a normal Telegram question without `/agent` went through the V2 chat bridge, which delegated to the legacy chat stream and produced repeated progress/fallback messages instead of one coherent reply.
- **Failed approach recorded:** setting Telegram's control-plane selector to V2 is not sufficient if the V2 Chat backend still emits legacy fallback failures. The fix is to harden the V2 Chat backend/progress stream, not to route every ordinary chat turn through Super DAN.

- ~~**P2: Telegram V2 chat could fail immediately with `An error occurred: value` when DAN-v2 runtime initialized.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-30.** **Observed from `/Users/lizhi/.dan/logs/server.log`:** `DANV2Runtime` construction initialized `IncidentCommanderController`, whose worker metadata tried `[state.value for state in IncidentTerminalState]`; `IncidentTerminalState` is a `typing.Literal`, so iterating it yielded typing aliases rather than enum members and raised `AttributeError("value")`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating Literal type aliases like runtime enums is brittle and hides the real error behind the chat router's friendly wrapper. Incident Commander now derives terminal-state metadata from `typing.get_args(IncidentTerminalState)`, and both the worker construction and DAN-v2 runtime construction paths have focused regressions.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN honored "do not read other files" but still burned the first model response on a read-only target check.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-29.** **Observed from `animation/.dan-super/runs/turn-01/events.jsonl`:** with `arena.html do not read other files`, the builder correctly avoided directory/git/source reads, but its only successful tool call was `workspace_check(path="arena.html")`; the follow-up builder call and both first-write recovery attempts then timed out before any `file_write`, so validation failed with no mutations.
- **Failed approach recorded:** policy enforcement alone is not enough if the allowed tool basket still invites read-only checking under a prompt-only creation constraint. When an operator forbids other workspace inputs and names a missing target, Super DAN now treats that as a generic constrained creation condition: the first worker gets a slim prompt, no unusable board/context evidence, and a mutation-only initial tool basket for the explicit target.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN dropped hard operator source constraints below the prompt layer.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-29.** **Observed from `animation/.dan-super/runs/turn-01/events.jsonl` after `/reset`:** the operator explicitly said "do not read other files in this folder", but validation still listed the workspace and used git status, while first-write recovery materialized `arena.html` by copying same-stem `arena-kimi-k26.html` after model timeouts.
- **Failed approach recorded:** passing only a target filename such as `arena.html` is target routing, not operator intent. Super DAN now extracts an operator-intent policy packet for hard workspace-access constraints, passes it through builder/recovery/validator/repair prompts and metadata, filters tool baskets/workspace snapshots, gates local tool calls, and disables same-stem artifact materialization when existing-artifact reuse violates the policy.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN had no in-shell way to reset stale context or queue state.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-29.** **Observed during interactive debugging:** operators had to exit the shell and manually move `.dan-super` or `.dan-super/state` aside to clear prior context before retrying a workspace.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on manual `mv` instructions is too brittle for an interactive CLI that already owns the workspace context directory. The shell now supports `/reset` for archival full-context reset and `/reset state` for queue/state-only reset, both implemented as timestamped renames rather than deletion.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN first-write recovery could still fail after target inference when the provider timed out before returning any write tool call.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-29.** **Observed from `animation/.dan-super/runs/turn-07/events.jsonl`:** the builder found the missing `arena.html` target via `workspace_check`, recovery inferred `recommended_write_paths=["arena.html", ...]`, but Kimi timed out twice inside first-write recovery without returning `file_write` or `file_edit`; final validation failed at `0.00` with no workspace mutations.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding a recommended target to the prompt is not enough when the provider spends the whole request budget thinking before tool emission. Super DAN now disables provider thinking for first-write recovery, forces `file_write` when the owned target is missing, avoids treating missing explicitly named targets as additive existing-artifact work, and can materialize an explicitly requested filename from a same-stem existing text artifact after recovery timeout only when the operator-intent policy allows existing artifact reuse.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN no-objective non-interactive invocations could start the default objective instead of waiting for operator input.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-29.** **Observed from interactive/operator use:** entering a workspace or invoking `dan super-organism` without a target could fall through to `_run_super_turn(...)` when stdin was not a TTY, allowing the default universal-agent objective to run without the operator typing an objective.
- **Failed approach recorded:** the original showcase default was useful for demos, but it is unsafe as the implicit behavior of a workspace-mutating command. The CLI now opens the prompt only for true TTY no-objective calls, keeps `/status` and explicit report modes available, and errors before live/default execution for non-interactive no-objective calls.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN generic first-write recovery could still read-loop and time out after the no-mutation retry fired.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-29.** **Observed from `animation/.dan-super/runs/turn-06/events.jsonl`:** the main builder and both generic first-write recovery attempts only called `list_directory` / `file_read`, then Kimi timed out in the narrowed write stage; no successful `file_write` / `file_edit` call was logged, so validation failed at `0.00` with no workspace mutations.
- **Failed approach recorded:** detecting no-mutation and retrying is not enough if recovery remains a broad discovery loop. Generic first-write recovery now infers concrete target paths, logs them, restricts recovery to file read/write/edit tools, carries the primary target as direct-write context, and includes those targets in timeout/nudge prompts so recovery must either write a concrete artifact or fail as a true write-commit failure.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN generic first-write recovery could destroy an existing detailed report after a no-mutation builder pass.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-28.** **Observed from `research/.dan-super/runs/turn-14/events.jsonl`:** the initial generic builder made no mutations, first-write recovery then overwrote `semiconductor_supply_chain_equity_research.md` with a 2,726-byte generic report, shrinking the original 8,441-byte artifact by 68% and deleting section 3/4 tables.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating no-mutation recovery as "make any write" is too blunt for additive document work. Generic first-write recovery now detects additive/enrichment objectives before validation feedback exists, passes pre-run file-state metadata, and applies the shrink-preservation policy so a shorter whole-file overwrite is blocked before mutation.

- ~~**P3: `file_edit` could interpret a replacement-shaped call as deletion when the model combined `mode="delete"` with `new_string` / replacement content.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-28.** **Observed from `research/.dan-super/runs/turn-12/events.jsonl`:** the generic builder attempted to expand section 2 with an edit carrying `mode="delete"` and a large `new_string`, so the tool deleted the target range instead of applying the replacement; the repair worker later made the artifact worse by replacing the title with `# placeholder`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on prompt wording such as "prefer file_edit" is too weak when the tool contract accepts ambiguous shapes. `file_edit` now treats delete as delete-only, rejects exact-text compatibility replacements with non-replace modes, the local runtime mirrors those checks before approval, and tool-use nudges tell workers to map edit intent to replace/insert/delete/exact-text replacement before mutating files.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN generic live stopped after a no-mutation timeout instead of trying a bounded first-write recovery.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-28.** **Observed from `research/.dan-super/runs/turn-09/events.jsonl`:** the generic builder inspected the semiconductor report, timed out twice, returned through the provider-timeout fallback with zero `file_write` / `file_edit` mutations, final validation failed at `0.00`, and the hook projection misleadingly enqueued `repair_requested` even though there was no artifact to repair.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating all failed validation rows as repairable is too coarse, and one-pass repair after mutation does not help when no first write landed. Generic no-mutation live runs now get two bounded first-write recovery attempts, final validation records `first_write_recovery_attempted`, and hooks classify generic no-workspace-mutation failures as first-write recovery instead of ordinary repair.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN generic live still carried small-context output and tool-replay budgets after routing broad reports through the generic lane.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-28.** **Observed during the semiconductor equity-report tuning pass:** even after removing word/line caps and raising tool-call budgets, builder/repair output caps of `2.4k-2.8k` tokens plus `2k` tool-result replay kept long-document work biased toward small outline edits.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating context-window size and completion budget as identical is wrong, but keeping small 2024-era caps is also wrong for Kimi/frontier large-context runs. Super DAN now uses high bounded defaults (`64k` worker/repair output, `12k` validator output, larger tool-replay/read windows, `4 MB` full-file reads) while still avoiding an unbounded `160k` default that could exceed provider request limits when prompts are already large.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN report repair could stop after a marginal first edit because post-write soft budget was already spent by discovery.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-28.** **Observed from `dan super-organism --workspace ./research` with `can you review existing report, and make sure they are good enough as an equity report`:** the repair worker spent calls reading/searching, made one tiny `file_edit` to lines 1-7, grew the report only from 2025 to 2250 bytes, then `coding_postwrite` soft budget forced finalization and validation rejected the artifact as still superficial.
- **Failed approach recorded:** raising only the hard tool-call cap does not solve this failure because the run used 19 calls and failed on a phase-budget nudge, not the hard cap. Super DAN now defaults to `128` tool calls and gives generic validators up to `24`, but the key fix is phase-local soft-budget accounting so post-write budgets start when the first mutation lands.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN generic report repair could destroy a useful enriched artifact after a bogus git-baseline validation failure.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-28.** **Observed from `dan super-organism --workspace ./research` with `please enrich the equity research report`:** the generic builder appended a larger semiconductor report, the validator rejected it because `research/` was untracked and had no commit/diff baseline, then repair overwrote the richer candidate with a tiny baseline scaffold that failed final validation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** git history is not a valid before/after source for untracked operator workspaces, and a repair pass must not create a fake baseline or shrink an additive document artifact. Generic validation now receives pre-run/post-run file-state metadata for mutated paths, repair feedback sanitizes git-baseline complaints into content-level repair guidance, and the shared tool loop blocks additive repair `file_write` overwrites that would shrink an existing repair target.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN still selected specialized live lanes after accepting generic workspace deliverables.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-28.** **Observed during the architecture cleanup after the semiconductor report failure:** report objectives could use the generic lane, but website prompts and existing website workspaces still selected `super-dan-live-website`, and the CLI still branched into `_run_live_website_build`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** keeping website as a privileged Super DAN lane preserves the same taxonomy problem under an artifact name. Super DAN live now routes every mutable super-organism objective through `super-dan-live-general`; artifact hints such as website may remain evidence, but execution uses the generic `role_brief` workspace-deliverable worker/validator.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN live rejected broad report/research objectives with a website/coding allowlist.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-28.** **Observed from `dan super-organism --workspace ./research` with an equity-research semiconductor supply-chain prompt:** the interactive shell set live mode, the deterministic report family became `research`, and the CLI stopped before model/tool execution with `--live currently supports website-like and general coding/build objectives`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** hardcoding objective families, adding a dedicated Super DAN research prompt branch, or keeping website as a special Super DAN live lane repeats the same architectural mistake. Super DAN live now treats explicit/interactive live context as permission to execute a generic workspace deliverable. The generic lane is `super-dan-live-general`, uses a general `role_brief` deliverable contract, and exposes `web_search`, `workspace_check`, read, write, shell, and git tools so report/markdown objectives can materialize files without becoming a separate research mode.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN misclassified single-file HTML artifacts as full websites and then routed generic failed validations to first-write recovery.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-27.** **Observed from `dan super-organism --workspace ./animation`:** after a successful single-file `index.html` animation build, a follow-up asking to make smoke more vivid and "only edit index.html" was routed to the website lane because the objective mentioned an HTML file, then failed on missing `styles.css`, `app.js`, and `README.md`. Later generic smoke-quality validation failures also enqueued `first_write_recovery` even though `index.html` had changed.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using the website lane for every HTML mention conflates artifact shape with product profile, treating an empty `changed_required_files` field as no-write ignores generic live runs that do not have required website files, stopping after the first generic validator rejection wastes useful feedback, and a generic "live execution failed validation" hides that feedback. This was first fixed by recognizing single-file HTML workspaces structurally; it is now superseded by the 2026-04-28 generic-lane cleanup, where Super DAN live no longer selects a website lane at all.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN live could leave OpenAI/httpx async client cleanup to run after the event loop closed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-27.** **Observed during a live validation run:** Python printed `Task exception was never retrieved` from `httpx.AsyncClient.aclose()` with `RuntimeError('Event loop is closed')` while Super DAN continued running.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on SDK finalizers or closing providers after `asyncio.run(...)` is too late because the transport belongs to the loop used by the model call. Super DAN now wraps live execution and provider cleanup in one coroutine, `OpenAIProvider.close()` awaits the underlying client close, and the gateway-backed adapter closes its resolved providers.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN live rejected artifact-creation requests that did not use the old build/website vocabulary.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-27.** **Observed from `dan super-organism --workspace ./animation` with `help me generate an animation, playable...`:** the interactive live turn failed before model/tool execution because `generate` / `animation` did not match the deterministic mutation or website cue lists.
- **Failed approach recorded:** expanding the cue list with more English creation words repeats the same brittle pattern. Super DAN now tries the normal selector first, but if an explicit/interactive live turn would otherwise be rejected and the objective is not an explicit read-only/document-reader turn, it injects an explicit `IntentSignal(operation="mutate", artifact_target="workspace")` from the live context itself.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN static anti-template failures were too vague for the repair worker and hook state could imply a second repair after the bounded repair was exhausted.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-27.** **Observed from `can you keep patching this website for me`:** final validation failed with "generic Super DAN contract/demo template" even though the concrete blocker was exact page copy such as "execution contract", "objective contract", and "native execution lane"; the terminal hook projection could also enqueue or display repair intent after the one real repair pass had already run.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a generic "looks like a template" failure is not enough evidence for an autonomous repair pass. Static gates now report exact phrase hits, the repair brief says to remove or rename those exact hits until fewer than two remain, and final validation events carry `repair_attempted` / `repair_exhausted` so hook routing suppresses phantom repair packets.

- **P3: Super DAN still has deterministic semantic fallbacks and legacy website helper code after the `IntentSignal` boundary.** **Observed 2026-04-28 during the generic-lane cleanup:** Super DAN live no longer selects the website lane, but `dispatch.py` still has private English cue lists for intent signals, `worker/organisms/super_organism.py` still classifies showcase execution family through cue scans, and `cli/super_organism.py` still contains legacy website helper functions that should be deleted or moved out once no compatibility tests need them.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating the new generic live lane as the full cleanup would hide remaining brittleness. The next durable step is a replaceable intent-signal producer and deletion/extraction of unused website helper code, not adding more central task-family branches.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN still needs an explicit intent-resolution contract.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-27.** **Observed during routing review:** expanding deterministic cue lists for multilingual objectives would just move the brittleness around and make central orchestration task/language-specific.
- **Failed approach recorded:** public cue-list expansion is the wrong abstraction. The dispatcher now resolves a typed `IntentSignal` (`operation`, `artifact_target`, `mutation_permission`, confidence, rationale, evidence) before lane selection. The resolver is still deterministic, but it is now an explicit replaceable boundary rather than hidden routing branches.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN rejected existing-website layout repair prompts before the live builder could run.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-27.** **Observed from interactive `can you think harder, the layout now is completely messy`:** the prompt was entered inside `dan super-organism --workspace ./website`, but the live support gate rejected it with `--live currently supports website-like and general coding/build objectives` because the text did not contain explicit `website`/`html`/`css` cues and the deterministic report family looked like a general operator request.
- **Failed approach recorded:** objective text alone is not enough for an interactive workspace shell, and workspace context must not force every non-build ask into a website patch. Super DAN routing now passes existing artifact context into `select_orchestrator(...)` as evidence, but the 2026-04-28 cleanup keeps execution on the generic workspace lane rather than selecting a website patch lane.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN labeled no-edit website failures as repair work.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-27.** **Observed from interactive `can you make sure the website would look properly...` run:** the builder read the required files, hit `finish_reason=length`, produced no `file_write`/`file_edit`, then final validation failed with no changed files while the hook projection still enqueued a confusing `repair_requested` packet.
- **Failed approach recorded:** "validation failed" is too coarse as a hook predicate. No first write is a builder/retry problem, not a repair problem. Super DAN now runs one bounded first-write recovery pass for website no-op builds, logs it as `[retry]`, revalidates if it lands a patch, and suppresses repair hooks when no-write recovery is already exhausted.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN treated a fixed two-file website change count as a terminal validation rule.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-27.** **Observed from interactive `patch the website ?` runs:** a small valid patch could be rejected because only one required file changed, even when the model validator judged the result materially acceptable.
- **Failed approach recorded:** file-count coverage is a weak proxy for product quality. Current behavior keeps the deterministic no-op/template/prompt-echo gates, but lets the validator decide whether a focused single-file patch is sufficient or broader coordination is required.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN stopped after validator feedback instead of using the feedback for a bounded repair.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-26.** **Observed from `patch the website ?`:** the build worker updated only `README.md`, the validator correctly failed the run for changing only one required file, and the CLI stopped instead of immediately asking the website worker to repair the coordinated-file gap.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating validator feedback as final output is too passive for an interactive build surface. Super DAN now runs one bounded website repair pass after a concrete validation rejection, then revalidates and records `[repair]` events in the same `.dan-super` trace.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN live runs looked silent until the final summary even though `.dan-super` events were being written.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-26.** **Observed from interactive use:** after entering an objective, the CLI appeared to hang until the model/tool/validator loop finished, forcing operators to open the JSONL trace to know whether work was moving.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying only on the durable event log is too opaque for an interactive build surface. Super DAN now attaches a compact terminal renderer to the same event stream, emits sparse idle heartbeats during quiet model/tool waits, keeps `--json` clean, and provides `--quiet-progress` for scripts.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN asked operators to over-specify vague existing-website continuation turns.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-26.** **Observed from the interactive command `can you keep patching this website`:** the worker changed only one required file and the validator failed with a repair brief asking for maintainability/component/changelog guidance even though that was the obvious default product behavior for a patch-continuation request.
- **Failed approach recorded:** replying with "be more specific" pushes product policy onto the operator. Super DAN now expands vague continuation language against an existing website into a default maintainability patch brief, carries previous `.dan-super` validation feedback into the next turn, and logs the normalization event for auditability.
