- ~~**P3: Super DAN's CLI UX required too much special knowledge compared with `dan code`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-26.** **Observed from operator use:** `dan super-organism --workspace ./website` produced only the default execution contract because no objective was supplied and `--live` was required for real execution; when live website output was requested with that workspace shape, the default artifact layout could imply `./website/website`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating Super DAN as a showcase-first command made sense for the first demo but is too indirect for daily operator use. The CLI now keeps `--plan-only` for the deterministic contract while TTY no-objective calls open a prompt, configured objective turns auto-enter live mode, and code-like website workspaces can be the artifact root.

- ~~**P3: Plan 56 Super DAN routing could ignore a non-code execution family when the objective text contained build cues.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-26.** **Observed during the Plan 56 brief/router audit:** `select_orchestrator(..., {"command": "super-organism", "execution_family": "research"})` stayed read-only for research wording, but a research-family objective containing "build" could still route to `super-dan-live-coding`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** checking the non-code family only after build-cue handling is too late. The deterministic family guard must run before website/build live-lane selection so non-code Super DAN reports remain showcase/read-only.

- **P3: Plan 56 team-leader validation state can stay stale after child compaction if the stored validation command points at an old/mistyped test path.** **Observed 2026-04-26 during the Plan 56 continuation:** the team workspace reported `validation_status=failed` because one stored command referenced a missing test path, while the current focused local test commands passed.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating the team workspace validation badge as authoritative after new local patches is too coarse. Re-run the exact current focused tests from the repo root before deciding whether Plan 56 is blocked, and update the manager validation command set in a later team-leader maintenance pass.

- **P2: Plan 56 legacy organism archiving cannot happen before facades and contract extraction.** **Observed 2026-04-26 during the Plan 56 migration audit:** `coding_execution.py`, `project_execution.py`, `incident_execution.py`, `reference_demo.py`, and `super_organism.py` still export product-facing contracts and functions used by durable CLI/conversation paths. Moving them directly into `_archive/` would break imports before the universal-organism facades exist.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating archive-first as a mechanical file move is too risky. The safe order is additive universal cell/brief/runner substrate first, then extract reusable typed contracts and add compatibility facades, then archive legacy execution implementations once public imports resolve through the facades.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code could keep replaying a stale shell-count repair milestone even after `workspace_check` existed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-26.** **Observed in `website/.dan-code/runs/turn-25/events.jsonl`: the project planner kept asking workers to run `shell_command grep -c` exact counts for `<html>` / `</html>` on `index.html`, which is both slower and brittle for `<html lang="en">`.** Fix: DAN Code now normalizes matching requested objectives, persisted project-plan milestones, and planner decisions into a `workspace_check(check="html_tags", path="index.html", tags=[...])` objective before launching the bounded coding run.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding a better tool is not enough if durable planner state keeps routing the same old instruction back into execution. Control-plane objectives need a narrow compatibility rewrite when a safer structured tool supersedes a brittle shell pattern.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code could accept dummy `file_edit` replacement text as a structurally valid edit call.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-26.** **Observed in the same live repair trace: a worker attempted to replace the top of `index.html` with `dummy call to read - will error, use file_read instead`.** Fix: the local runtime now rejects obvious dummy read-instruction edit content before approval and before any disk mutation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** schema-valid edit shape does not imply semantically valid edit content. Runtime validation must block obvious non-patches at the same pre-approval boundary used for missing edit content.

- ~~**P3: DAN Code workers used slow shell commands for deterministic structural validation that could be expressed as a local structured check.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed in the `turn-23` follow-up repair: a shell `grep` tag-count command took about 86s and initially miscounted `<html lang="en">` as zero when searching for exact `<html>`.** Fix: added the read-only `workspace_check` tool for file existence, literal/regex counts, HTML tag-balance checks, and Python/JSON syntax checks; default live tool baskets now expose it before mutation-capable tools, and context capsules preserve its result as deterministic validation evidence.
- **Failed approach recorded:** shell commands are still useful for builds and tests, but using them for simple structural file checks adds approval/wait overhead and invites brittle pattern mistakes. Common deterministic checks should be structured tools with typed results.

- ~~**P2: malformed `file_edit` calls could wait on approval before failing even when the arguments were already known-invalid.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed in the live `turn-23` log: worker-3 called `file_edit` with `path`, `start_line`, and `end_line` but no `content`; the tool span lasted about 171s before failing with `missing required arguments for file_edit: content`.** Fix: `LocalOrganismToolRuntime` now performs a tool-specific pre-approval check for line-based `file_edit` replacement/insert calls missing replacement content, including batched edit items.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying only on the public JSON schema's `anyOf` groups was too weak for conditional edit requirements. If a call is structurally sufficient to pass schema but semantically impossible for the selected edit mode, it must fail before approval or any disk/tool work.

- ~~**P2: Generic artifact partitioning could still over-parallelize bounded repair tasks by treating read-only dependency files and backup files as write-owner lanes.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed in `website/.dan-code/runs/turn-23/events.jsonl`: a single-file `index.html` structural repair with explicit `Do not modify styles.css or app.js` still planned 4 owner lanes (`index.html`, `styles.css`, `app.js`, `index_backup.html`), spent 48-90s in orchestration, then wasted worker rounds on no-op/non-material lanes.** Fix: bounded single-file read-back/shell-count repair prompts now classify into the fast lane, and `coding_execution.py` excludes explicitly non-mutated paths plus auxiliary backup artifacts from generic owner-lane partitioning.
- **Failed approach recorded:** "more parallelism" is not automatically lower latency. Fanout is useful only when the partitions are independently mutable and reduce the slowest path; read-only dependency files and backups add model calls, write-stage nudges, and aggregation noise without increasing expected value.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot rejected the enlarged `file_edit` tool schema after batched compatibility fields were added.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed on a DAN Code repair run where the provider failed before worker execution with `Invalid request: tools.function.parameters is not a valid moonshot flavored json schema ... schema exceeds maximum allowed size`.** Cause: the OpenAI-compatible schema normalizer expanded the root and nested `anyOf` variants in the full `file_edit` metadata, making the provider-facing schema much larger than the runtime contract. Fix: `ToolLoopCompletionProvider` now keeps full runtime metadata for validation but sends a compact model-facing `file_edit` schema without nested `anyOf`; malformed edit shapes still fail through runtime validation and the correction nudge.
- **Failed approach recorded:** simply adding more valid edit shapes to the public tool metadata can break strict provider schema limits once compatibility normalization expands variants. Complex tools need separate model-facing compact schemas and runtime-facing strict validators.

- ~~**P2: malformed batched `file_edit` calls without per-edit anchors surfaced a raw `edits[0].start_line` failure instead of a useful recovery path.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed from live worker output like `[worker-1][tool] failed file_edit: ValueError: tool_arguments_invalid: missing required arguments for file_edit: edits[0].start_line`.** Fix: `file_edit` now advertises top-level and batched `old_string`/`new_string` compatibility in its schema, reports missing batch anchors as `start_line or old_string`, and the local runtime correction nudge tells the worker to retry with grounded line ranges, a unique exact old/new pair copied from `file_read`, or a targeted `file_read` first.
- **Failed approach recorded:** accepting `old_string`/`new_string` inside the handler was not enough while the schema and error text still trained models toward invalid `edits=[{"content": ...}]` shapes. The model-facing contract and the failure nudge need to describe every valid edit-item shape.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code validator reviewers could still inherit the full 90s provider timeout from normal builder runs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed from live status output such as `[status] model timeout [validator.reviewer-a]: round=4 model=kimi-k2.6 timeout=90.00s`, even after compact validation and deterministic prechecks had reduced other validation tails.** Fix: validation packets now cap inherited long completion budgets at `30s`, and `ToolLoopCompletionProvider` honors request-level `completion_timeout_seconds` metadata when computing the actual model-call timeout.
- **Failed approach recorded:** recording timeout values in packet metadata was not enough while the local runtime ignored that metadata and always used the provider-level default. Stage-specific timeout policy must be part of the executable request budget, not just observability.

- ~~**P2: Task-specific deterministic fanout is a brittle scheduler shortcut, even when it improves one product trace.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed during the 2026-04-24 review of the DAN Code website owner-lane work: the path proved that artifact ownership plus deterministic merge can reduce aggregation time, but encoding fanout from a specific objective/file family is not reusable orchestration.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/scheduler` now has generic artifact partition/admission contracts and `coding_execution.py` consumes those partitions for owner lanes instead of objective-string website fanout; no-material output now follows generic repair/blocker handling instead of a website-specific deterministic materializer.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating a successful website split as a permanent scheduler rule would create another special-case organism behavior. The durable fix is a generic artifact partition and schedule-proposal layer with deterministic guards.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code could still fail an existing website refresh when no worker lane materialized a first edit at all.**~~ **FIXED THEN SUPERSEDED 2026-04-24.** **Observed on a fresh `/tmp/dan-code-live-check.sd3yBE/.dan-code/runs/turn-01/events.jsonl` run: all three exclusive owner lanes read their entry file, entered direct-write timeout recovery, then completed with no material candidate; the compact one-worker repair repeated reads and also timed out with no write.** A temporary guarded website materializer proved the reliability gap, but it has now been removed from the live aggregation path because it was task-specific. Current behavior: no-material output follows generic repair/blocker handling; future recovery should improve material-yield through artifact partitioning, context readiness, and compact write-first retry rather than deterministic website writes.
- **Failed approach recorded:** retrying the same model path with narrower prompts was not sufficient when the provider repeatedly failed before first write. The runtime needs a bounded deterministic materialization fallback for low-risk existing-file website polish so the product can return a real candidate instead of another no-material failure.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code/local organism tool loops could replay every prior `file_read` chunk on every model round, making orchestrator-style code work slower as the worker inspected more files.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** The heavy path was provider prompt replay, not the durable orchestrator session itself: each read stayed in the message transcript and older file bodies were repeatedly resent even after the worker only needed their retained facts. Fix: `local_runtime.py` now compacts older successful `file_read` tool replies in the copied provider-facing prompt while keeping the latest reads full and preserving full raw evidence in logs and `executed_tools`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** per-tool payload truncation alone was not enough. A 2k cap per `file_read` still scales poorly over dozens of files, so the runtime needs rolling replay compaction plus explicit re-read guidance for exact omitted code.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code could materialize a repair file, then fail validation because the provider timed out before returning the final JSON candidate.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed on `.dan-code/runs/turn-13/events.jsonl`: attempt 2 wrote `index.html` with `file_write`, then hit `provider_completion_timeout`; the runtime synthesized an empty `candidate_fragment`, and validation scored `0.00` because it saw no complete updated content or verifiable material candidate.** Fix: `local_runtime.py` now reads materialized files back from disk into `candidate_fragment` on timeout fallback, and `coding_execution.py` preserves that read-back evidence through single-worker promotion plus deterministic worker merge.
- **Failed approach recorded:** mutation evidence alone is not enough when the acceptance contract asks for complete updated file contents. The fallback must promote actual file snapshots, not just `target_files` and a timeout summary.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code's first-write recovery had become too threshold-driven after the initial timeout patch.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed on `.dan-code/runs/turn-10/events.jsonl`: attempt 2 reached direct-write mode after the single allowed final read, then timed out at `30s` with no follow-up retry because timeout recovery was tracked by one global boolean/state path and the post-final-read stage effectively hit a hard cliff.** Fix: `local_runtime.py` now uses softer post-read direct-write caps (`45s` exclusive-owner, `60s` generic direct-write), tracks timeout recovery by stage, and allows one grounded retry even after the final-read transition instead of treating that edge as terminal.
- **Failed approach recorded:** hard timeout cliffs are the wrong control layer for this path. DAN needs soft caps plus staged recovery pressure, with only a few hard safety rails around ownership, aggregation honesty, and bounded repair budget.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code long website owner lanes could spend the full inherited provider timeout after the first owned-file read, and aggregation failures that said "stop and re-plan" still aborted the run instead of using the remaining repair budget.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed on `.dan-code/runs/turn-08/events.jsonl`: each exclusive owner lane hit `toolloop.write_stage_first_write_nudged`, then the next `file_edit`-only model call burned the full `90s` provider budget, returned `target_files: []`, and aggregation failed with `Workers produced no material bounded candidate to merge` without a second attempt.** Fix: `local_runtime.py` now caps exclusive-owner direct-write calls, allows one direct-write timeout recovery from the grounded owner-file evidence, and `coding_execution.py` now turns no-material / missing-owner aggregation failures into compact repair briefs when repair budget remains.
- **Failed approach recorded:** fail-fast aggregation messaging without an actual repair-loop handoff was only half the policy. If the runtime is going to say "re-plan," it needs to spend the bounded repair round on a smaller write-first retry instead of dying at the same boundary every time.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code normal-timeout website owner runs bypassed the deterministic merge/hygiene safety net because it was scoped only to `short_completion_timeout=true`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed on the copied website trace for `.dan-code/runs/turn-04/events.jsonl`: the run used `completion_timeout_seconds=90`, `short_completion_timeout=false`, launched parallel owner lanes, then had no validated candidate while the existing artifact showed malformed HTML with an unclosed style block and repeated document tails.** Fix: covered exclusive-owner plans now use deterministic `worker_merge` regardless of short-timeout mode, and frontend hygiene now closes leaked style blocks, deduplicates repeated `app.js`/document tails, and validates local refs before the validator stage.
- **Failed approach recorded:** tying deterministic aggregation and frontend hygiene only to compact timeout mode was too narrow. The optimization is safe when ownership coverage and mutation evidence are present, independent of the outer completion timeout.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code short website runs still spend most remaining wall-clock in validation after deterministic aggregation is reduced to near-zero.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed 2026-04-23 on `/tmp/dan-live-website.retry7.7lCUjQ`: worker lanes completed, deterministic `worker_merge` aggregation took 1 ms, `contract_hygiene.applied` fired, but final validation still dominated the tail and failed at `0.84` before the latest local hygiene patch.** Fix: DAN Code now runs deterministic validation prechecks first, blocks static frontend contract failures without a model call, and uses a one-lead compact validator for short-timeout or deterministic owner-merge candidates while keeping the full quorum validator for normal paths.
- **Failed approach recorded:** removing or bypassing validation would make the short path fast but would hide real quality failures. The better next step is a smaller, more targeted validator or deterministic precheck layer, not no validator.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code short website owner lanes could locally succeed while disagreeing on frontend contracts across HTML/CSS/JS.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23 for the observed deterministic cases.** **Observed on `/tmp/dan-live-website.retry5.auOEUy`, `/tmp/dan-live-website.retry6.lyEfZG`, and `/tmp/dan-live-website.retry7.7lCUjQ`: lanes invented mismatched reveal selectors/attributes, duplicated `#features` or `</main>`, toggled `.scroll-cue.is-hidden` without CSS, and omitted reduced-motion CSS.** Fix: the broad website owner prompt now states a shared selector/animation contract, and the short-timeout deterministic hygiene pass repairs duplicate feature sections/closing tags, feature animation hooks, `data-delay` to `data-animate-delay`, reveal CSS compatibility, scroll-cue hidden state, and reduced-motion CSS before validation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** prompt-only coordination was not enough. Parallel file owners need a deterministic contract repair layer for predictable cross-lane invariants.

- ~~**P2: short-timeout DAN Code aggregation could become the bottleneck or corrupt materialized owner edits.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed on `/tmp/dan-live-website.retry2.YpTKJs`, where the aggregation LLM spent about a minute and returned a lower-quality merge after workers had already mutated the target files.** Fix: covered exclusive-owner plans now fail fast if any owner lane lacks mutation evidence, otherwise synthesize a deterministic `worker_merge` candidate and skip the aggregation organ.
- **Failed approach recorded:** asking a reducer model to rediscover and restate already-materialized exclusive-owner file edits is wasted critical-path time in compact mode. The reducer should be deterministic when ownership coverage is complete.

- ~~**P2: `file_edit` rejected useful Moonshot/K2.6 edit shapes in short website owner lanes.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed during retry live runs where EOF-overshooting ranges and batched `old_string`/`new_string` edits failed even though the intended edit was recoverable.** Fix: `file_edit` now clamps valid replace/delete ranges that overshoot EOF and accepts batched `old_string`/`new_string` compatibility edits by resolving line ranges per batch item.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating every line-bound overshoot or old/new batch payload as malformed pushed workers into extra model rounds. The file tool should normalize recoverable edit intent when the target text is unambiguous.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code short live website runs can still fail the final validation/quorum gate even after the compact parallel-owner policy works.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-24.** **Observed 2026-04-23 on `/tmp/dan-live-website.shortretry.keYAoq`, where the run selected one bounded turn, planned three exclusive owners, mutated `index.html`, `styles.css`, and `app.js`, and wrote full `.dan-code` logs, but still ended with `Validation failed: The tissue quorum did not converge on a reusable decision.`** Fix: compact owner/short runs no longer depend on a two-reviewer quorum; they emit deterministic precheck diagnostics and then use a single compact validator lead unless static checks already produced a precise blocker.
- **Failed approach recorded:** proving parallel worker execution and file mutation is not the same as proving the final artifact is acceptable. The compact pass needs a stronger exit contract, not another hidden long repair loop.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code short-timeout live runs could still launch hidden second bounded turns even after the inner coding organism selected `max_repair_rounds=0`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed on `/tmp/dan-live-website.shortretry.V2uMlk`, where the first bounded run finished quickly but the outer supervision loop reopened `turn-02` because `_DEFAULT_SUPERVISION_LOOPS=2` still applied.** Fix: `src/dan/cli/code.py` now selects one supervision loop when `completion_timeout_seconds <= 60`, emits `supervision.policy.selected`, and keeps the inner `repair.policy.selected` event for the coding organism repair budget.
- **Failed approach recorded:** capping only the inner repair loop does not cap wall-clock behavior when an outer controller can reopen the whole bounded organism. Short-pass mode needs both policies to agree.

- ~~**P2: broad website/visual-polish DAN Code objectives could collapse to one worker when the planner returned a malformed or underspecified worker plan.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_execution.py` now deterministically normalizes broad website/visual-polish objectives into three exclusive owner lanes for `index.html`, `styles.css`, and `app.js`, even when the planner payload is inconsistent, while still allowing narrower non-website tasks to keep the normal worker-count policy.
- **Failed approach recorded:** asking the planner to choose parallelism was too brittle for artifact shapes with obvious independent owners. For common website surfaces, DAN should impose a deterministic owner split before execution.

- ~~**P2: aggregation fallback could drop successful worker-owned mutation paths when the reducer or a timed-out worker returned an empty structured payload.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_execution.py` now augments aggregation candidates with successful exclusive-owner mutation evidence from worker tool calls, preserves unique target files/test plans/risks, and still rejects modified candidates backed only by unrelated helper writes.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using only the reducer's structured `target_files` field made timeouts look like no patch existed. Aggregation should be additive over verified worker evidence, while the mutation-evidence gate still blocks helper-only noise.

- ~~**P2: Super DAN live website validation could look split-brain because the model validator logged `passed=true`, then the CLI's deterministic static template checks flipped the final run to failed after that event.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed on `.dan-super/runs/turn-10/events.jsonl`, where `live.validation.completed passed=true` appeared even though `run.log.completed status=failed validation_passed=false` after static validation detected the remaining contract/demo template shape.** Fix: `src/dan/cli/super_organism.py` now logs the model-only result as `live.validation.model_completed` and emits `live.validation.completed` only after deterministic static gates are merged into the final validation verdict.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating model validation and deterministic static gates as separate terminal verdicts confused the operator. The model validator can remain visible, but the public completed validation event must mean final merged verdict.

- ~~**P2: an existing Super DAN website redesign could change only `website/index.html`, leave old `styles.css` / `app.js` / `README.md` behind, and still reach the validator even though the artifact was not a coordinated redesign.**~~ **FIXED THEN RELAXED 2026-04-27.** **Observed in the same `turn-10` trace, where the builder read all website files but only executed one `file_write` against `website/index.html`; the result still looked like the old Super DAN contract/demo page.** Fix history: existing website runs first added pre-run required-file state plus a two-file deterministic gate; that gate is now relaxed to actual required-file mutation plus validator judgment because file count alone rejected legitimate focused patches.
- **Failed approach recorded:** "all required files exist" plus "one required file changed" is too weak for broad redesign prompts, but a hard two-file rule is also too blunt. Existing artifacts should prefer coordinated patch sets when the objective spans files while still allowing focused material fixes.

- ~~**P2: transient provider overloads such as Moonshot/Kimi `engine_overloaded_error` failed Super DAN live immediately even though the error was not a DAN tool-budget or validation failure.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** Fix: the shared local tool loop now retries brief provider overload/rate-limit errors for requests carrying `tool_budget_profile="super_dan_live"`, while still failing fast on permanent insufficient-balance/auth/quota errors.
- **Failed approach recorded:** rerunning the whole CLI turn after a provider overload is wasteful and can produce confusing partial-artifact state. The useful retry seam is inside the live tool loop around the provider call that failed.

- ~~**P2: the first Super DAN soft-budget preflight nudge could loop on itself because the controller narrowed the tool basket, appended the write-stage instruction, and then `continue`-d before the provider ever saw that new tool basket.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed during the first focused `tests/test_cli/test_super_organism.py::test_main_live_website_build_soft_budget_nudges_before_hard_cap` run after the soft-budget patch, where the CLI regression stopped making progress and the controller kept re-evaluating the same exhausted phase before another model round.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now recomputes the narrowed tool basket in-place and proceeds directly into the next provider call after a preflight soft-budget nudge instead of spinning another top-of-loop cycle first.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a controller-side policy update is not progress unless the provider actually sees the updated tool basket. Preflight nudges must change the next request, not just the internal loop state.

- ~~**P2: the first Super DAN tool-call soft-budget guard recalculated phase/progress after every individual tool call, so one batched write response could be flipped from `coding_direct_write` to `coding_postwrite` after the first file and then force-finalized before the rest of the same batch landed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed on the same focused soft-budget CLI regression, where the fake provider returned four `file_write` calls for the website artifact set but only `website/index.html` was written before the runtime emitted `toolloop.soft_budget_nudged phase=coding_postwrite action=force_finalize`.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now evaluates tool-call soft budgets from the response-start phase/progress snapshot rather than recomputing phase after each tool call.
- **Failed approach recorded:** phase-aware budgets should control which kind of response the model emits next, not interrupt a coherent write batch halfway through because the first successful write changed the phase.

- ~~**P2: prompt-only pacing guidance was still too weak against repeated whole-file overwrite retries, because a live worker could receive the large-write warning and still immediately send another `file_write` to the same existing file on the next round.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23.** **Observed in the Super DAN website live traces around `.dan-super/runs/turn-05/events.jsonl` and `turn-06/events.jsonl`, where the worker emitted one truncated `file_write` for `website/index.html`, received a repair nudge, and then retried another monolithic whole-file overwrite instead of stepping down to incremental edits.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now recognizes that generic pattern at the runtime seam, records the affected existing file path for the rest of the turn, emits an explicit controller downshift note, and blocks further whole-file `file_write` retries on that same file until the worker switches to `file_edit`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** prompt guidance alone helps but does not guarantee convergence. The runtime has to remember same-turn write failures against specific existing files and enforce the recovery path when the worker ignores the first hint.

- ~~**P2: the first shared gateway pacing loop treated its own short rate-wait timeout as if the dispatch queue had expired, which turned normal request pacing into a fake queue-timeout failure.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-22.** **Observed during the first focused `tests/test_llm_core/test_gateway.py::test_dispatcher_can_rate_limit_call_starts` run, where the new broker used `asyncio.wait_for(condition.wait(), timeout=rate_wait)` and then promoted that expected pacing wake-up into `Gateway dispatch queue wait timed out after 30.0s`.** Fix: `src/dan/llm_core/gateway/dispatch.py` now only converts a timeout into queue-expiry when the real queue deadline has actually passed; short pacing sleeps now just wake the broker to recheck dispatch readiness.
- **Failed approach recorded:** one timeout branch cannot represent both "wake me when a notifier fires" and "wake me when the pacing interval elapses." Rate pacing needs a benign recheck path; queue timeout is a separate failure condition.

- ~~**P1: Super DAN live write stages could self-disable `file_write` after repeated malformed payloads, then treat that disabled state as proof the write stage should finalize after the first partial workspace mutation.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-22.** **Observed on `.dan-super/runs/turn-02/events.jsonl`, where the live website lane logged repeated `tool_arguments_invalid` failures for `file_write`, later made one real edit to `website/index.html`, and then forced `toolloop.write_stage_finalize_forced reason=disabled_direct_write_tool_after_workspace_patch` before the website artifact set was coherent.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now keeps `file_write` / `file_edit` enabled inside write-capable coding stages after malformed-argument nudges and no longer treats disabled direct-write tools as an automatic finalize reason after a patch.
- **Failed approach recorded:** temporary same-turn tool disablement is fine for repeated invalid discovery calls, but it is the wrong policy for the tools that are themselves required to materialize the bounded artifact. In live write stages the correct recovery behavior is nudge-and-retry, then fail only on real budget or validation limits.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot/K2.6 could still satisfy the direct-write materialization gate with a successful `file_edit` call that made no actual source change, which then reopened the write stage to root-level scratch helpers even though `git status` stayed clean.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-22.** **Observed on the focused `astropy__astropy-12907` rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_emptytarget_tempblock_20260421/`, where K2.6 replayed line-1 `file_edit(..., mode="replace")` calls against already-unchanged text and the old runtime still treated those successful tool results as if a real workspace patch now existed.** Fix: `src/dan/tools/file_edit.py` now returns `changed` / `no_op` for both single-edit and batched edits, and `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now ignores no-op `file_edit` results plus broader root-level scratch helper names (`dump_*`, `probe_*`, `script_*`, `run_dump*`, `.txt`, `.log`, `.out`) when deciding whether direct-write mode has materialized a real patch.
- **Failed approach recorded:** checking only whether `file_edit` returned `ok=true` was still too broad. Moonshot/K2.6 can repeat an already-applied replace window, so materialization has to key off whether the edit actually changed tracked workspace content rather than whether the tool accepted the request.

- **P2: focused Moonshot/K2.6 SWE-bench reruns can still fail before any solver work begins when the account balance is exhausted, which leaves the repo with no new correctness signal even after the local runtime/tool fixes are ready.** **Observed on the immediate retry under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_noop_helper_filter_20260421/`, where the first model call failed with Moonshot `429 exceeded_current_quota_error` / `insufficient balance`.**
- **Failed approach recorded:** immediately relaunching the next focused rerun without provider-side quota headroom does not test the new orchestration patch at all. The correct next step is to restore Moonshot balance first, then replay `astropy__astropy-12907` on the patched runtime before drawing solver conclusions.

- ~~**P1: DAN Code could promote a worker timeout fallback as a modified candidate even when the workspace git diff and `target_files` were both empty.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the focused `astropy__astropy-12907` rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_helperfilter_20260421/`, where attempt 1 reached validation with `workspace_effect="modified"` and `target_files=[]` while `git status` was clean.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_execution.py` now rejects modified coding candidates that do not name target files before direct promotion or aggregation can send them to validation.
- **Failed approach recorded:** inferring `workspace_effect=modified` from any non-empty summary or any successful write-capable tool was too broad. Timeout summaries and helper writes are not patch evidence; coding candidates need explicit target files unless they are verification-only.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot/K2.6 could burn direct-write-only rounds by using `file_write` to create `/tmp` helper scripts after DAN had already narrowed the tool basket to `file_edit` / `file_write`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the same focused `astropy__astropy-12907` helper-filter rerun, where the repair worker repeatedly wrote `/tmp/get_source.py`, `/tmp/get_test.py`, and `/tmp/exec.sh` instead of editing `astropy/modeling/separable.py`.** Fix: direct-write mode now blocks temporary external helper paths before any workspace patch exists, leaves direct project write tools enabled, and reprompts the worker to write real checked-out files or return a blocked candidate.
- **Failed approach recorded:** filtering helper paths out of materialization was necessary but not enough. If helper writes are allowed to execute during direct-write mode, the model can still waste the wall-clock budget even though those writes no longer count as a patch.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot/K2.6 could satisfy the direct-write stage by creating a root-level workspace helper such as `tmp_read.py` after its broad `file_edit` rewrite was rejected, causing the runtime to treat helper-script creation as a materialized product patch.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the focused `astropy__astropy-12907` rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_repairclassifier_20260421/`, where K2.6 wrote `workspace/tmp_read.py` after `file_edit` was temporarily disabled, and the old materialization predicate counted that helper as a workspace mutation.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now filters obvious root-level temporary helper filenames out of successful workspace-mutation paths; helper-only writes keep the direct-write guard unsatisfied, while helper churn after a real source patch is treated as a finalize reason.
- **Failed approach recorded:** checking only whether a `file_write` landed under the workspace root was too broad. Benchmark/code-repair runs need to distinguish product/source patches from scratch helper scripts even when the helper is accidentally written inside the repo checkout.

- ~~**P1: the first Moonshot/K2.6 validator read-only clamp was too broad for repair handoffs, because a write-capable coding repair worker could inherit `organ_id="coding-build.validator"` while still carrying `worker_id="coding-build.worker-1"` and a coding-worker output contract.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the focused `astropy__astropy-12907` rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_validatorclamp_20260421/`, where attempt 1 produced a wrong source edit, the validator correctly rejected it, and the repair attempt was then clamped to read-only tools before it could apply the requested fix.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` no longer treats `organ_id` as an independent validator signal; true validator classification now relies on the validation output contract, validator role/stage, validator worker id, or validator recipient cell id.
- **Failed approach recorded:** validator-origin metadata is not the same as validator execution. Repair workers launched from validator feedback can legitimately originate from `coding-build.validator`, so `organ_id` alone must not remove write tools.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot/K2.6 validator rounds could still inherit mutation-capable tools and write `/tmp` helper scripts even though DAN intended validation to be read-only, because some live requests reached `ToolLoopCompletionProvider` with validator execution metadata but recycled worker ids like `coding-build.worker-1`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the focused `astropy__astropy-12907` Moonshot rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_placeholderguard_20260421/`, where the live `events.jsonl` showed `organ_id="coding-build.validator"` repeatedly calling `file_write` on `/tmp/read_separable.py`, `/tmp/inspect.py`, and `/tmp/sep_l250_317.py` before a timeout.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now classifies validator-like requests from the validation output contract plus explicit validator role/stage/recipient signals before provider dispatch, clamps those rounds to read-only tool schemas, and blocks any later hallucinated mutation tool with the existing availability-nudge path.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on `worker_id` alone was too weak. Validator traffic can arrive with worker ids that look like ordinary coding workers, so the read-only boundary has to key off the validation contract and explicit validator execution signals, not just the lane name.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN defaulting to the full 100-cell showcase made everyday live operator runs look and feel too expensive for the current phase.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-22.** Fix: `dan super-organism` now defaults to `super-dan-20` with 20 logical cells and active cap 8, while preserving the larger showcase through explicit `--cell-count 100 --active-cell-cap 20 --organism-id super-dan-100` flags.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using the sales-demo organism size as the default operator size conflated showcase credibility with normal run cost control. The default should be cheap, and scale-up should be deliberate.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN’s universal-agent trace still looked more coordinated than it really was, because the deterministic report exposed delivery nodes and signals but not explicit ticket ownership, typed handoffs, or a stop/continue audit gate.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-22.** **Observed in the pre-patch 20-cell deterministic runs, where the organism could describe synergy convincingly but the report still lacked a machine-readable answer to “which cells own which work, which packets have been handed off, and what exactly is still blocking exit?”** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/super_organism.py` now emits shared-board state, coordination tickets, typed handoff packets, reserve-cell accounting, and a final audit gate, while `src/dan/cli/super_organism.py` now surfaces those sections in `--verbose` output and passes them into the live worker/validator lanes.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a delivery plan plus some heartbeats is not enough to sell a many-cell organism. The coordination contract has to be explicit: ownership, handoff shape, and a final audit gate that refuses premature closure.

- ~~**P3: Super DAN’s live website lane kept wasting budget on giant whole-file `file_write` attempts, then retrying the same pattern after truncation, because the worker was told to build directly but not told the expected paced cadence.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-23; ADJUSTED 2026-04-28.** **Observed in `.dan-super/runs/turn-04` through `turn-06`, where the website lane repeatedly reread the same files, attempted oversized `file_write` payloads that returned `finish_reason=\"length\"`, and even drifted into `website/test.txt` instead of the required artifact set.** The initial fix added a conservative numeric write envelope; the generic-lane cleanup removed that fixed prompt ceiling because it was too conservative for report/document artifacts. Current behavior keeps paced execution, `file_edit` preference for existing files, and runtime downshift after actual truncation/tool-shape failures.
- **Failed approach recorded:** “just build it” was too underspecified for the live lane. The model needed explicit pacing, chunk-size, and anti-drift rules because the real write boundary depends on remaining response budget, not on the model understanding tool limits magically.

- ~~**P1: even after the direct-write runtime guard and the `file_write` syntax guard, Moonshot/K2.6 could still mutate Python source with low-information placeholder/shell-note comments through `file_edit`, and DAN would count those edits as real workspace progress because the file remained syntactically valid.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the focused `astropy__astropy-12907` Moonshot rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_filewriteguard_20260421/`, where K2.6 replaced the license header with `# git restore placeholder - use git diff` and shoved bogus control-flow text into `_separable`'s docstring.** Fix: `src/dan/tools/file_edit.py` now rejects placeholder-style Python replacement content such as `# placeholder`, `shell command output placeholder`, and `git restore placeholder` before the edit is written.
- **Failed approach recorded:** structural/syntax guards alone were not enough. A small placeholder comment can keep the module parseable while still being obviously non-patch noise, so `file_edit` also needs a low-information content guard for Python source.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot/K2.6 could still whole-file overwrite an existing Python module with syntax-broken content via `file_write`, and DAN would count that as a successful workspace mutation because the write landed on disk before validation noticed the file was unusable.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the focused `astropy__astropy-12907` Moonshot rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_directwriteguard_tty_20260421/`, where `file_write` replaced the start of `astropy/modeling/separable.py` with a garbled partial-module rewrite and later churned on `astropy/modeling/separable_backup.py`.** Fix: `src/dan/tools/file_write.py` now rejects overwriting an existing `.py` file when the replacement would leave it syntactically invalid, and the runtime can reuse the existing invalid-tool correction loop instead of counting the broken file as a valid patch.
- **Failed approach recorded:** the direct-write runtime guard alone was not enough. Forcing a real `file_edit`/`file_write` call only moved the failure from prose-only no-ops into malformed whole-file writes, so the write tool itself needed a structural fail-closed check.

- ~~**P3: the first Super DAN 100-cell showcase forced every user prompt through the truth-audit claim template, so a build request produced nonsensical claims like "please build our product website..." being production-ready for orchestration.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed directly with `dan super-organism "please build our product website. make it cool, with cool animation dynamic effects"`, where the text report emitted benchmark/adoption/product-readiness claims instead of an objective contract.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/super_organism.py` now treats the request as a universal-agent objective, and `src/dan/cli/super_organism.py` can independently materialize a deterministic static website artifact for website-like objectives.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a single generic showcase template is too brittle for organism demos, but adding public scenario flags was also the wrong correction because it leaked internal demos into the operator interface. Objective-specific contracts should live inside the organism/showcase layer while the raw universal worker core stays unchanged.
- ~~**P3: even after scenario routing, `dan super-organism` with no arguments still ran the generic truth-audit placeholder, which is the weakest possible sales demo for the 100-cell organism.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed directly when the no-argument command audited "a new AI agent product" and printed generic benchmark/adoption claims.** Fix: no-argument runs now default to the universal-agent objective contract, and the public CLI no longer accepts `--scenario`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using the same default target for API demos and CLI sales demos was wrong, and then defaulting to a website-specific scenario was still too narrow. The main CLI default should show objective-first universal-agent behavior; a public `--scenario` selector is not the right user contract.
- **Failed approach recorded:** routing Super DAN through DAN Code / DAN Research would couple independent products too early. For now `dan super-organism` must own its deterministic execution/materialization path and not call those product shells internally.
- ~~**P3: pasted multiline objectives made the Super DAN text report look broken in terminal copy/paste because raw line breaks were preserved inside `Target` and `Final Memo`, and the shell prompt could visually attach to the final artifact path.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed directly with a quoted multiline website prompt in zsh.** Fix: Super DAN now normalizes objective whitespace before report construction and the CLI emits an explicit trailing newline after artifact paths.
- **Failed approach recorded:** preserving exact shell line breaks in the operator objective is not useful for this CLI surface. Super DAN should treat pasted multiline prompts as one logical objective unless a later artifact format explicitly needs raw text preservation.
- ~~**P3: real `dan super-organism` terminal output could still visually collapse delivery/reallocation/artifact rows together even after objective normalization.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed directly with `dan super-organism "can you build a website to for this product? make it look cool "`, where several bullet rows appeared on the same visual line with large whitespace gaps.** Fix: the CLI now renders the whole text report from one sanitized line buffer, shortens delivery rows, and has regression coverage that key bullets start on separate lines.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on repeated `print(...)` calls with denser bracketed rows was too fragile for this showcase output. For CLI demos, stable row boundaries matter more than compact report density.
- ~~**P3: even the shortened full text trace was still too fragile for the user's interactive terminal, despite clean non-interactive `zsh -ic` reproduction.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** Fix: ordinary `dan super-organism` output is now compact by default and the detailed delivery/reallocation trace is opt-in through `--verbose` or `--json`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** continuing to tune the long human trace was lower-value than making the default terminal contract small and robust. The detailed organism trace should be available, but it should not be the default path for artifact-building CLI use.
- ~~**P3: Super DAN website runs were technically writing files but still felt like they were not "actually starting to build stuff" because the CLI led with organism contract language instead of the generated artifact.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** Fix: successful website runs now materialize first and lead compact output with `Build: completed`, the website entrypoint, and the generated file list.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a correct artifact side effect is not enough if the product surface frames the run as a report. For build-like objectives, the CLI needs to present the build result first and the organism trace second.
- ~~**P3: Super DAN still did not have a DAN Code-like live execution path; it could emit a contract or deterministic website artifact, but it could not ask a model/tool lane to implement the objective and then verify that files exist.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21; SUPERSEDED 2026-04-28.** Fix: `dan super-organism --live` now runs a native Super DAN worker-core/local-tool lane. The original website-required-files lane has since been collapsed into the generic workspace-deliverable lane, which requires real workspace mutations plus validator approval.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating the deterministic materializer as "live" was the wrong product contract. It is useful as a cheap showcase path, but the implementation path needs an explicit model/tool run with required-file verification.
- ~~**P3: the first Super DAN live website lane could still report `completed` on a weak/generic artifact because it only checked that the required files existed at exit, so a preexisting `website/` folder or a generic contract page could slip through as success.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed directly after a live website run printed `Live Build: completed` while the generated `website/index.html` still echoed the raw prompt and showed execution-contract/demo content instead of a real landing page.** Fix: `src/dan/cli/super_organism.py` now snapshots required file state before the run, requires those website files to actually change, runs a separate read-only validator pass, and applies deterministic anti-template / prompt-echo checks before reporting success.
- **Failed approach recorded:** file existence is too weak as the terminal contract for a live build surface. Super DAN needs a real exit validator plus change detection, otherwise the CLI can claim success on stale or generic artifacts.
- ~~**P3: Super DAN live runs still did not persist a dedicated `events.jsonl` trace, so the CLI could report `event_count` and compact summaries but offered no replayable on-disk run log like DAN Code or DAN Research.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-22.** Fix: `src/dan/cli/super_organism.py` now writes `.dan-super/runs/turn-XX/events.jsonl` through the shared `organism_log_v1` writer, prints the event-log path in live CLI output, returns `event_log_path` / `event_log_schema` in live JSON output, and the organism-log discovery route now also includes `.dan-super/runs/**/events.jsonl`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** keeping only in-memory event buffers plus a final `event_count` was too weak for a build surface. Super DAN needed the same durable log seam as DAN Code and DAN Research so operators can replay what actually happened after a live run.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot/K2.6 write-capable coding stages could still finish a focused SWE-bench turn with a prose-only candidate after DAN had already consumed the single allowed final `file_read` and narrowed the tool basket to direct `file_edit` / `file_write`, which let a no-mutation worker result look completed even though the checked-out workspace never changed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the focused `astropy__astropy-12907` Moonshot rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_fileeditguard_20260421/`, where the worker described the correct `_cstack` fix in JSON but exited turn 1 with `0` workspace edits after the post-final-read guardrail.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now reprompts once when a direct-write-only coding stage returns a prose-only candidate without any workspace mutation and then falls back to an explicit blocked structured candidate instead of silently treating that turn as completed.
- **Failed approach recorded:** the earlier first-write nudge plus post-final-read “next tool call must be file_edit/file_write” message were not enough on their own. Moonshot/K2.6 could still answer in prose while tools remained available, so DAN needed an explicit no-mutation completion guard rather than relying on prompt wording alone.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot/K2.6 SWE-bench workers could still corrupt a Python source file by replaying the same successful `file_edit(..., mode="replace")` call against an already-mutated line window, and later broad `.py` replaces could still leave the file syntactically broken if they spanned multiple top-level blocks without actually deleting one cleanly.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the focused `astropy__astropy-12907` Moonshot rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_12907_kimi_k26_shellnudge_20260421/`, where K2.6 repeated near-identical `file_edit` calls on `astropy/modeling/separable.py` until the file became unparseable and validation scored the candidate `0.04`.** Fix: `src/dan/tools/file_edit.py` now treats identical successful line-range `replace` calls as no-ops and rejects broad `.py` replacements that span multiple top-level blocks when they would leave the file syntactically invalid.
- **Failed approach recorded:** guarding only tiny placeholder-style bulk replaces or explicit top-level symbol deletion was not enough. The tool also needed to tolerate idempotent repeated replace calls safely and block the broader “multi-definition rewrite that leaves syntax broken” shape before the workspace itself became unusable.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot/K2.6 could still reject the next tool-loop round even after the schema fix because DAN sometimes replayed an assistant message with multiple `tool_calls` but only appended tool replies for the subset it executed before an early break.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on `astropy__astropy-12907` inside `tests/eval/results/swebench_lite_full_test_kimi_k26_schemafix_20260421/`, where Moonshot returned `400 Invalid request: an assistant message with 'tool_calls' must be followed by tool messages responding to each 'tool_call_id'...`.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now synthesizes explicit skipped-tool replies for the remaining call ids whenever a multi-call assistant batch is intentionally cut short by invalid-argument repair, tool-availability repair, or the write-stage final-read guardrail.
- **Failed approach recorded:** stopping the loop after the first bad call was not enough on stricter OpenAI-compatible backends. DAN also had to preserve transcript completeness for the unexecuted tail of the same assistant tool batch.

- ~~**P1: Moonshot-compatible tool schemas could fail before the first token because Moonshot rejects some valid OpenAI-style parent-object schemas that combine `type` / `required` / `properties` with `anyOf` or `oneOf`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-21.** **Observed on the first official Moonshot `kimi-k2.6` full-batch restart under `tests/eval/results/swebench_lite_full_test_kimi_k26_20260421_091832/`, where every request failed with `400 Invalid request: tools.function.parameters is not a valid moonshot flavored json schema...`.** Fix: `src/dan/providers/openai_provider.py` now recursively normalizes strict OpenAI-compatible tool schemas by moving parent object/array fields onto the relevant variants, and the same provider now resolves Moonshot/Kimi compatibility through one backend-profile seam instead of scattered model-name branches.
- **Failed approach recorded:** assuming every OpenAI-compatible backend accepted the same JSON-schema dialect was wrong. DAN's canonical local-tool schemas were semantically fine, but Moonshot enforced a stricter request-time validator than OpenAI/OpenRouter, so the compatibility fix had to live at the provider boundary.

- **P3: pure `DAN Reader` vision mode currently has weaker heading recovery than `text` or `hybrid` on scholarly PDFs.** **Observed 2026-04-20 on `/Users/lizhi/Downloads/lee2021information-JF.pdf`, where `dan read extract --pdf-mode vision --start-page 20 --end-page 22` returned a generic `Pages 1-2` section while `text` and `hybrid` recovered `IV. ...` plus child `A. Cross-Sectional Analyses`.** Current workaround: prefer `--pdf-mode hybrid` for journal papers and reserve pure `vision` for bounded image-heavy slices until the heading merger gets stronger.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating pure vision as a drop-in replacement for the text/hybrid outline path was too optimistic. Page-level OCR/descriptions are useful, but the section-structure merger still needs text anchors or a stronger heading reconstruction layer on top.

- ~~**P1: `DAN Reader` JSON output could leak the resolved API key, and the first flat PDF hierarchy pass could flatten real journal outlines by dropping empty parent headings and over-trusting fragile Roman-heading spacing.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly while reviewing `/Users/lizhi/Downloads/lee2021information-JF.pdf`, where `dan reader extract --format json` exposed the active OpenRouter key and the paper’s `II` / `IV` / `V` hierarchy was partially collapsed.** Fix: `src/dan/cli/reader.py` now emits a redacted task payload (`api_key_configured` instead of the key itself), preserves empty parent headings with `parent_heading` / `section_path` / `child_headings`, strips common PDF boilerplate before section assembly, and accepts PDF spacing drift such as `IV .` while still rejecting sentence/table false positives.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a flat reader surface still needs a rich structural packet contract. Dropping parent headings because they had no direct body text, and then passing only summary prose between summary stages, was too lossy to reuse across document tasks.

- ~~**P3: DAN Research was over-specifying the visible final answer shape by implication, because the orchestration prompts and acceptance criteria carried strong fixed-format language even when the user query called for a different answer style.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** The product already had rich internal accountability fields, but prompt guidance still nudged models toward one house report shape instead of asking what format and coverage best fit the user query. Fix: the DAN Research planner/orchestrator/deep-research prompt seam now asks explicitly what answer format best serves the query, what content is essential, and what structure would just add noise, while planner-selected answer shapes flow through the bounded `delivery_target`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using internal structured artifact fields as a proxy for the user-facing format was the wrong seam. Verification/audit/integrity rows should remain inspectable, but visible report shape needs to be chosen from the query and decision need at orchestration time.

- ~~**P1: OpenAI-compatible provider calls in DAN Code could still wedge a SWE-bench run even with a nominal completion timeout, because the old timeout path depended on the provider unwinding promptly after cancellation.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed on the pre-patch `astropy__astropy-14182` rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_14182_timeout_recovery_20260420/`, where the child process stayed alive with no new events after a write-stage model call stalled.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now detaches timed-out completion tasks, emits `model.timeout`, and retries one compacted write-stage recovery round from bounded tool evidence before falling back.
- **Failed approach recorded:** `asyncio.timeout(...)` around the provider call was not enough for OpenAI-compatible SDK paths that ignored cancellation. The runtime needed its own detached-task watchdog instead of assuming provider cooperation.

- ~~**P1: DAN Code could reopen a whole outer benchmark turn after a validator-passed candidate if the planner's pass threshold sat only slightly above the validator score.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed on `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_14182_task_timeout_watchdog_20260420/`, where `turn-01` for `astropy__astropy-14182` reached `validation passed` at `0.88` but still failed delivery because `0.88 < 0.90`, immediately reopening `turn-02`.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_execution.py` now treats near-threshold validator-passed candidates as exportable after repair budget exhaustion via `delivery_pass_tolerance` (default `0.03`), while clearly below-threshold cases still fail.
- **Failed approach recorded:** keeping `validator.passed` and final delivery export on separate hard gates was structurally inconsistent for bounded coding runs. Once repair budget is exhausted, a tiny score gap should not automatically trigger another full outer pass.

- **P2: even after the post-write finalize seam was expanded to temporary external helper writes, SWE-bench runs can still spend too many turns after a viable source patch by retargeting that churn into repeated in-repo test edits and malformed `file_edit` retries, while validator leads still reread bounded files longer than necessary.** **Observed on the 2026-04-20 interrupted follow-up `astropy__astropy-14182` rerun under `tests/eval/results/swebench_single_14182_temp_helper_finalize_20260420/20260420_061325_astropy__astropy-14182/`.** The new runtime patch appears to have suppressed the old `/tmp` repro-helper branch, but the worker stayed inside the repo and started repeatedly editing `astropy/io/ascii/tests/test_rst.py` plus issuing malformed `file_edit` calls after the first `astropy/io/ascii/rst.py` patch instead of finalizing.
- **Failed approach recorded:** stopping only temporary external helper writes is not enough. If the model can stay inside the workspace, it can still burn turns on low-value benchmark-test churn or malformed rewrite retries after the source diff is already materially in place.

- ~~**P1: even after the single-worker authority split was fixed, write-capable coding workers could still burn benchmark turns after landing a bounded patch because the runtime only stopped naturally on a no-tool reply.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly on the earlier `astropy__astropy-12907` SWE-bench rerun, where the worker landed the correct source diff, passed syntax, then continued into `pip install -e .` and more shell probing instead of finalizing.** Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now emits `toolloop.write_stage_finalize_forced` once a write-capable coding stage has already landed a bounded patch and focused verification but then drifts into non-verification shell/env work or extended post-write analysis, and it synthesizes the structured coding payload from tool evidence if the provider ignores that forced no-tool finalize round.
- **Failed approach recorded:** prompt guidance plus the earlier first-write nudge were not enough to make the worker stop at “good enough.” The write-capable loop needed its own explicit post-write stop gate instead of waiting for the model to choose a natural no-tool finish.

- ~~**P1: DAN Code's coding organism could burn long benchmark/runtime budgets because authority was split across a read-only worker, a separate aggregation writer, a validator, and then a second outer review.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly across the interrupted clean OpenRouter `z-ai/glm-5.1` SWE-bench partial-4 reruns, where the worker could identify the right fix but aggregation or post-run review still reopened the loop.** The old default path required several LLM stages to agree before stopping, so a correct worker result could still be rematerialized poorly or re-litigated after validation. Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_execution.py` now lets the default single-worker path materialize the bounded patch directly and promote that evidence-backed result straight into validation, `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` keeps multi-worker plans read-only to avoid shared-workspace write races, and `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_conversation.py` now normalizes completed material-output runs back to `done` instead of reopening them for optional extra work.
- **Failed approach recorded:** layering more benchmark-specific prompt nudges on top of the split-authority loop was not enough. The worker, aggregator, validator, and outer review did not share one clear owner for patch materialization or stopping, so the control contract itself needed to change.

- ~~**P1: Packaged DAN.app could report `Hugo is not installed or not available in DAN's environment` even when `hugo` was installed for the user's shell.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed after launching Content mode from `/Applications/DAN.app` while `hugo` was available at `/opt/homebrew/bin/hugo`.** macOS app launches can carry a minimal `PATH`, so checking/spawning plain `hugo` was not enough. Fix: `editor/electron/hugoPreviewManager.ts` now resolves `hugo` from `PATH`, `DAN_HUGO_BIN`, and common macOS install paths, then spawns the resolved binary with a seeded preview `PATH`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on shell-local `which hugo` semantics was not a valid packaged-app contract. The desktop app has to find Homebrew/MacPorts paths itself or accept an explicit `DAN_HUGO_BIN`.

- ~~**P2: Content preview could show only the upper slice of the page while the lower half of the preview card stayed blank.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed on the knowledge-base `/blogs/possession-film-review/` route after the page itself was proven to contain the full article HTML.** Two seams overlapped: the embedded host surface could still partially paint under Electron guest-view behavior, and the current theme's article page also ran full-browser mutation/layout logic after load. Final fix: `editor/src/components/modes/ContentMode.tsx` now renders the managed preview through a plain `iframe` instead of Electron `webview`, still enforces explicit measured iframe bounds, and talks to the guest through `postMessage`; the knowledge-base theme detects `dan_preview=1`, switches into a stripped single-column reader layout, skips the heavy post-load article mutation script, and now owns the inspect/runtime bridge inside `layouts/_default/single.html`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** making the host frame bigger and resetting scroll were necessary but not sufficient, and the Electron `webview` bridge itself stayed too fragile. The stable seam is a normal iframe plus a page-owned embedded mode/bridge, not more container-side webview tuning.

- ~~**P2: embedded DAN preview dropped inline `@pageID` citation rendering after the reader-mode stabilization pass.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly on `content/notes/bartik-iv/index.md`, where headings such as `Approach 1: Exogenous Shares (@goldsmith2020bartik)` rendered the raw `@...` token inside DAN preview.** Root cause: the `dan_preview=1` early-return disabled the heavy article script, but that same script also owned the citation rewrite. Fix: `themes/my-knowledge-base-theme/layouts/_default/single.html` now keeps a lightweight embedded-only citation/reference pass in DAN preview mode without re-enabling the old full-browser layout mutation logic.
- **Failed approach recorded:** disabling the whole article script was too coarse. Embedded preview still needs a small subset of page behavior, and citations are one of those core behaviors.

- ~~**P2: embedded DAN preview still dropped the `Cited By` backlinks section after inline citations were restored.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed on `content/papers/goldsmith2020bartik/index.md`, where embedded preview showed the paper body again but never rendered its inbound references.** Root cause: the `dan_preview=1` early-return also disabled the old `initCitedBy()` path, and the DAN-only embedded CSS was still hiding `#cited-by-container`. Fix: `themes/my-knowledge-base-theme/layouts/_default/single.html` now builds a lightweight embedded backlinks section from `citations.json` after the citation rewrite finishes, and `static/css/styles.css` no longer hides the backlinks container in DAN preview.
- **Failed approach recorded:** restoring inline citations alone was not enough. The same stripped embedded mode also has to rebuild backlinks, because the original full-browser script owned both behaviors.

- ~~**P2: Content preview could reopen an article near the footer and look like only the site header/footer rendered.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed on the knowledge-base `/blogs/possession-film-review/` route, while an independent Hugo build showed the article HTML was present.** The likely failure was webview/Chromium scroll restoration or retained guest scroll, not missing content. Fix: `editor/electron/previewBridgePreload.cjs` now sets manual scroll restoration and resets the guest page to the top on initial load when there is no hash target.
- **Failed approach recorded:** assuming the article body was absent from Hugo output was wrong. The static render under `/tmp/dan-kb-render-check/blogs/possession-film-review/index.html` included the full article, so the host needed to reset preview scroll rather than chase Markdown or route generation first.

- ~~**P2: Content mode could show a fully mounted page shell with a blank white body and no clue that the page's own client script had failed inside the preview guest.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly on the knowledge-base `/graph/` route, where DAN rendered the site nav/footer but the graph body stayed empty.** The host webview was fine; the failure mode sat inside the guest page, which uses client-side scripts after the Hugo shell renders. Fix: `editor/electron/previewBridgePreload.cjs` now forwards resource-load/runtime failures to `editor/src/components/modes/ContentMode.tsx`, and Content mode surfaces them as a compact `Page script issue` overlay instead of leaving a silent blank pane.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating every blank preview as a host sizing/layout problem was misleading. Some Hugo layouts render a perfectly valid shell and then fail later because a guest script or CDN asset did not load; the host needed explicit guest-runtime visibility to distinguish those cases.

- ~~**P1: Content mode could crash after opening the desktop preview because `webview.send(...)` was being called before the preview guest had fully attached and emitted `dom-ready`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly from the installed-app error `The WebView must be attached to the DOM and the dom-ready event emitted before this method can be called.`** The first on-page-edit bridge wired `dom-ready` listeners, but separate rerender effects and selection resets could still call `webview.send(...)` early during guest replacement or initial mount. Final fix: the desktop preview no longer depends on `webview.send(...)` at all; `ContentMode.tsx` now uses an `iframe` transport and the embedded preview page talks back through `window.parent.postMessage(...)`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** listening for `dom-ready` was not sufficient by itself. The host also needed to stop depending on Electron `webview` messaging for the stable preview/edit path.

- ~~**P1: packaged DAN Content mode could still look like browser fallback inside `/Applications/DAN.app`, so the UI showed `Desktop only` and never listed local knowledge-base pages even though the real repo had many `content/**/index.md` files.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly while opening the installed macOS app against `/Volumes/data/Dropbox/Projects/my-knowledge-base`.** The content scanner itself was fine, but the host window could still fail to expose `window.electronAPI`, which made Content mode believe it was running in browser preview and skip local project discovery/attach/page loading entirely. Fix: `editor/electron/main.ts` now explicitly sets `sandbox: false` on the main `BrowserWindow`, which keeps the packaged preload bridge available consistently in the installed app.
- **Failed approach recorded:** preload hardening alone was not a complete fix. Removing `node:path` from the preload entrypoint reduced one sandbox-related failure mode, but the packaged host still needed an explicit BrowserWindow trust boundary so the preload bridge could come up reliably.

- ~~**P1: `file_edit` could accept a destructive low-information bulk replace and overwrite most of a source file with placeholder text during benchmark aggregation.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly on the clean OpenRouter `z-ai/glm-5.1` `astropy__astropy-12907` rerun.** After the first-write nudge, aggregation issued `file_edit(path=\"astropy/modeling/separable.py\", start_line=230, end_line=317, content=\"READING\", mode=\"replace\")`, which the old tool accepted and used to collapse the tail of the file. Fix: `src/dan/tools/file_edit.py` now rejects suspicious bulk replaces that shrink a large line range into very little content and tells the caller to use smaller targeted edits or explicit delete+insert steps instead.
- **Failed approach recorded:** line-range bounds alone were not a sufficient safety boundary. For large replace operations, the replacement shape itself matters; a tool that accepts any tiny replacement over a huge span will happily apply obvious garbage.

- ~~**P1: even after the low-information guard, `file_edit` could still accept a broad Python `replace` that silently removed adjacent top-level code while leaving syntactically valid but structurally broken source behind.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly on the restarted clean OpenRouter `z-ai/glm-5.1` `astropy__astropy-12907` rerun.** Aggregation next issued a wide `replace` over `astropy/modeling/separable.py` that rewrote `_coord_matrix` while deleting the neighboring `_cstack` top-level definition by swallowing its remaining indented body into the previous function. Fix: `src/dan/tools/file_edit.py` now parses broad `.py` replacements structurally and rejects edits that span multiple top-level blocks while removing one of the affected definitions/assignments.
- **Failed approach recorded:** replacement-size heuristics alone were still too weak. A model can replace a large block with a plausible-looking function body that is not “tiny” yet still deletes surrounding top-level structure.

- ~~**P1: the SWE-bench read-only finalize seam only closed coding-worker lanes, so validator reviewers could still keep re-reading files after the minimal correct patch was already in place and burn the rest of the completion budget.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed directly on the OpenRouter `z-ai/glm-5.1` reruns for `astropy__astropy-14182` and `astropy__astropy-14365`.** The worker stage could now stop cleanly, but validator reviewers were still doing long read-only loops in the checked-out repo until the run drifted or stalled. Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now generalizes `toolloop.read_only_finalize_forced` across both `coding_worker` and `validator` modes and returns structured validator fallback payloads when a provider timeout/safety block still prevents a final no-tool review note.
- **Failed approach recorded:** forcing a final no-tool reply only for read-only coding workers was too narrow. Validator lanes exhibit the same stalled-analysis shape on benchmark checkouts and need the same control-plane seam.

- ~~**P2: even after forced read-only finalize, the next no-tool model round could still be slow or fragile because the runtime replayed the full nested raw tool payloads back into the prompt.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed on the same unresolved GLM Astropy slice after the first finalize patch landed.** The control loop was correct, but the follow-up no-tool prompt still carried long file/test payloads, which made the final synthesis call unnecessarily heavy. Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now compacts tool payloads before prompt replay, truncating oversized nested text for model input while leaving the raw executed tool payloads intact for logs, event traces, and fallback synthesis.
- **Failed approach recorded:** keeping one exact full-fidelity copy of every tool result in both logs and the next model prompt was too expensive. The prompt only needs a compact working summary; the raw payload can stay in the durable artifacts.

- ~~**P2: Packaged DAN could fail to start if a stale local Python listener briefly occupied port `8000`, even when that listener died moments later.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-20.** **Observed directly from the desktop error `Port 8000 is already in use, but no healthy DAN backend responded at /api/health.`** Investigation showed a transient `python3.1` listener on `127.0.0.1:8000`; by the time the port was checked again it had already disappeared, but packaged startup had already hard-failed instead of retrying or claiming the port. Fix: `editor/electron/backendHealth.ts` now distinguishes healthy reuse vs port release vs timeout, and `editor/electron/main.ts` now starts a fresh backend when an unhealthy listener clears within the startup budget.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating any occupied-but-unhealthy `8000` port as an immediate fatal conflict was too brittle for real desktop environments where stale local listeners can disappear during launch. The useful seam is "reuse if healthy, proceed if released, fail only if still occupied and unhealthy after a short wait."

- ~~**P1: DAN Research could print a final orchestrator-written memo to the terminal but still fail to emit any new `report.md`, because only bounded runs wrote Markdown artifacts.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed directly when `please continue to the final report` returned a full memo while `.dan-research/runs/turn-06/report.md` stayed stale and no newer artifact existed.** The turn was being classified as orchestrator `respond`, `report_count` stayed `0`, and the CLI only wrote Markdown artifacts when `outcome.reports` contained a bounded `ResearchOrganismReport`.
