Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/research_conversation.py` now exposes a generic `response_kind=report_reply` contract with optional `artifact_markdown`, and `src/dan/cli/research.py` now persists those orchestrator-authored report replies under `.dan-research/runs/reply-XX/report.md` while recording the saved path in session state.
- **Failed approach recorded:** trying to treat every final memo as a hidden bounded run would have polluted report history and risked path collisions with real `runs/turn-XX/` artifacts. The correct seam is a product-level report-reply artifact, not a synthetic research run.

- ~~**P2: DAN Content's Hugo preview bridge could falsely fail on a healthy knowledge base because the cold-start readiness budget was only `20s`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed directly against `/Volumes/data/Dropbox/Projects/my-knowledge-base`.** A manual `hugo` run showed the site could take about `28.7s` for a cold build, so the old preview manager timed out, killed the child process, and surfaced an error even though the server would have become reachable shortly after. Fix: `editor/electron/hugoPreviewManager.ts` now treats the preview origin as ready on the first HTTP response header, increases the default startup budget to `60s`, and allows overrides through `DAN_CONTENT_PREVIEW_READY_TIMEOUT_MS`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** using a small generic timeout plus full-body `GET` probing was too optimistic for real Hugo sites. The useful seam is a cheap header-only reachability probe plus a startup budget that matches cold-build reality.

- ~~**P1: DAN Research could still bounce an explicitly final/no-more-pass objective back into `continue`, then force `incomplete` at the supervision cap even when the report already contained a materially grounded provisional synthesis.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed directly in `.dan-research/control-plane-events.jsonl` and `.dan-research/runs/turn-06/report.md`.** The review layer already had one narrow explicit-unverifiable escape hatch, but broader final-pass objectives like “after this pass synthesize a provisional report; no fifth pass” could still be normalized back to `continue`, and the CLI cap path then always rewrote that into `blocker_report`. Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/research_conversation.py` now recognizes a broader explicit final-pass + caveated-provisional-close pattern, and `src/dan/cli/research.py` now has a cap-time safety net that turns that specific capped `continue` into `provisional_report` + `final_status=warn` instead of reflexively forcing `incomplete`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** the earlier “explicit unverifiable fact” closure patch was too narrow. The generic product seam also needed to understand explicitly final/no-more-pass objectives where the remaining gaps are stale, method-sensitive, or scope-caveated rather than purely inaccessible.

- ~~**P1: DAN Research's default `report.md` artifact still mirrored the internal verification/audit envelope, so a blocked or provisional run could leave behind a Markdown file that read like a debug dump instead of a human memo.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed directly in `.dan-research/runs/turn-06/report.md`.** The console path had already been cleaned up, but `_render_report_markdown(...)` still emitted verification appendices, evidence-integrity tables, audit details, and quality gates by default. Fix: `src/dan/cli/research.py` now makes `report.md` mirror the memo-style console shape, preserves structured findings blocks as real Markdown, and leaves the full diagnostic envelope in JSON/logs instead of the default Markdown artifact.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating the Markdown file as “just another serialization of the structured report” was wrong. The useful split is human memo in `report.md`, machine diagnostics in `--json` and the underlying event/session logs.

- **Operational workaround: the official `swebench==4.1.0` harness on this machine needs `DOCKER_HOST=unix:///Users/lizhi/.codex-accounts/Ard/.config/colima/docker.sock` after Colima is started.** **Observed 2026-04-19 while rescoring `tests/eval/results/swebench_lite_partial4_no_outer_timeout_kimi_20260419/`.** The Docker CLI could use the `colima` context once Colima was booted, but the Python Docker SDK used inside `swebench.harness.run_evaluation` still defaulted to the missing standard Unix socket and raised `docker.errors.DockerException: Error while fetching server API version`. Current workaround: boot Colima first, then run the harness with `DOCKER_HOST` explicitly pointed at the Colima socket.
- **Failed approach recorded:** assuming `docker context use colima` was enough for the Python SDK was wrong. The CLI and the SDK do not read the same source of truth here, so the harness needs the socket path exported explicitly.

- ~~**P1: DAN Code coding aggregation was inheriting the full live tool basket, so even after a worker identified the right target file and fix, aggregation could waste turns on malformed `shell_command` / `file_read` calls or broad rediscovery instead of materializing the patch.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed on the OpenRouter `z-ai/glm-5.1` SWE-bench retries for `astropy__astropy-14995`.** The worker stage could already narrow the problem down to `astropy/nddata/mixins/ndarithmetic.py`, but the aggregation lead still received `shell_command`, `web_search`, `git_log`, and broad discovery tools that were not needed for the merge/materialization role. Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now narrows coding aggregation to `file_read`, `file_edit`, `file_write`, and `git_diff`, and `src/dan/worker/organs/__init__.py` now explicitly tells the coding aggregator to materialize the chosen patch rather than rediscover the repo.
- **Failed approach recorded:** giving aggregation the same tool surface as a coding worker was too permissive. Aggregation is a mutation/materialization step, not a fresh research/discovery step.

- ~~**P1: OpenAI-compatible models could silently no-op `web_search` and similar flexible tools by emitting empty `{}` arguments, because the local runtime only enforced flat `required` lists and `web_search` itself declared none.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed directly in `.dan-research/runs/turn-02/events.jsonl` while testing OpenRouter `z-ai/glm-5.1`.** The model was calling `web_search` with `{}`, the runtime normalized that to an empty argument dict, and the tool returned a valid-looking zero-result payload instead of failing. That left the tool loop blind to the malformed call and encouraged repeated no-op search rounds. Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` now validates alternative JSON-schema argument groups (`anyOf` / `oneOf`) in addition to flat `required` fields, and `src/dan/tools/web_search.py` now states that each call must include either `query` or `url`. Empty calls now fail through the existing `tool_arguments_invalid` nudge path so OpenAI-compatible models get a concrete correction turn instead of a silent no-op.
- ~~**P1: DAN Research was still sending placeholder local-tool schemas to frontier models even after the runtime-side validation fixes landed, so OpenRouter/GLM could keep emitting `web_search {}` because the model never saw the real `query` / `url` contract.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed by capturing the first DAN Research reader provider request after the initial `anyOf` patch.** The runtime metadata for `web_search` was correct, but the model-facing `tools` payload still contained placeholder definitions like `{"name":"web_search","parameters":{"type":"object","properties":{}}}` plus similarly empty `file_read` / `list_directory` schemas. Fix: `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py::_resolve_tool_schemas(...)` now canonicalizes every allowed local tool from the standalone runtime metadata before provider calls, DAN Research now defaults/clips research lanes to `web_search`, `file_read`, and `list_directory`, and the deep-research reader instruction now explicitly requires one concrete `web_search` query or `follow_up_queries` instead of an empty tool call.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on loose optional schemas plus downstream tool implementations was not enough for model-agnostic tool use. If the schema can express the real contract, the runtime should enforce it before invocation.

- ~~**P2: the SWE-bench single-instance wrapper could crash after a run when the per-instance artifact directory disappeared before `stdout.log` / `stderr.log` were written.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** **Observed on the no-outer-timeout `astropy__astropy-14995` retry under `tests/eval/results/swebench_lite_partial4_no_outer_timeout_kimi_20260419/`.** The DAN Code run got far enough to produce real turn artifacts, but `tests/eval/swebench_runner.py` still raised `FileNotFoundError` when writing wrapper logs if the run directory had vanished by that point. Fix: recreate the parent directory before writing `stdout.log` / `stderr.log`, and lock it with a regression in `tests/eval/test_swebench_runner.py`.

- **P2: VectorEngine `gemini-3-flash-preview` can return `200` chat completions with empty assistant `content` while spending the whole completion budget on hidden `reasoning_tokens`, which makes transport latency look better than usable-answer latency.** **Observed 2026-04-19 via `scripts/measure_raw_llm_latency.py` plus a direct raw `POST https://api.vectorengine.ai/v1/chat/completions` check after reverting the local provider config back to VectorEngine.** On short prompts like `Reply with exactly OK.`, the endpoint could return `message.content=""` with `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens > 0` and `text_tokens=0`; even explicit `thinking={"type":"disabled"}` only partially improved this in a small probe. Current workaround: for this provider/model path, treat `first_chunk_ms` as transport-only, check visible text separately, and prefer Kimi or another model/provider for latency-sensitive text tasks until the behavior is characterized better. Planned fix: determine whether VectorEngine honors a stronger thinking-disable control or whether DAN needs a provider-specific fallback/guard when visible text stays empty.
- **Failed approach recorded:** measuring only first chunk / total wall time was too optimistic for this path. A `200` with token usage does not mean the model produced usable visible text.

- ~~**P2: DAN Research planner/review stalls were hard to diagnose because the control plane only showed coarse outer-stage timers, and `dan research --show-model-trace` did not stream visible control-stage model output.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** The earlier heartbeat/fallback work could show that the shell was still waiting, but not what each planner/review API call actually looked like or whether a model was producing any public text before the outer guard fired. Fix: controller completions now emit richer request/response stats (message counts/chars, request mode, timeout, usage, output size, hedge flags, provider request details) into the shared event logs, `OpenAIProvider` preserves effective request metadata for postmortems, `ResearchProgressRenderer` now renders public `model.stream.*` deltas for control-stage calls, and the research-specific inner hedge delay is now `10s` instead of the earlier `2s` while the outer `20s` / `180s` guards stay unchanged.
- **Failed approach recorded:** outer-stage elapsed timers and final fallback markers were not enough. Without per-call controller/provider stats plus visible public stream output, planner stalls looked like mysterious “3-minute planning” rather than a bounded provider call that never produced a usable result.

- ~~**P2: DAN Research could still turn corrected-but-unreconciled facts into recommendation-ready prose because `verification_facts` and `audit_issues` lived as separate appendices with no canonical claim-level integrity table between them and the final memo.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** Live research reviews showed the product could now catch many factual problems but still lacked one generic place to normalize whether a claim's source identity, metric identity, unit/scale, time alignment, scope alignment, and same-source consistency were actually good enough to support synthesis. Fix: `src/dan/cli/research_product.py` now derives canonical `evidence_integrity` rows above the older verification/audit appendices, and `src/dan/cli/research.py` now downgrades reports to `provisional_report` or `blocker_report` before final memo rendering whenever those rows are still proxy-backed, conflicted, or unresolved.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a fact appendix plus an audit appendix was not enough on its own. Without one canonicalization/reconciliation seam between those appendices and the memo writer, candidate facts could still be “mostly corrected” yet leak into polished prose as if they were fully settled.

- ~~**P3: the generic capability-layer `web_search(fetch_content=true)` path still serialized top-result grounding fetches even after the lower-level search tool path had already been hardened to fetch in parallel.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-19.** This meant research/chat callers using the capability surface could still wait on one fetch at a time even though the underlying search stack already supported concurrent grounding. Fix: `src/dan/server/capabilities/web.py` now issues bounded top-result fetches in small parallel batches, keeping the same fallback/attempt budgeting while aligning the visible capability path with the faster low-level tool path.

- ~~**P2: per-run DAN Code event logs could miss `trace_rows` when a bounded runner returned them only in the final report instead of streaming `trace.row` callbacks live.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-18.** **Observed while re-running the focused SWE-bench / DAN Code regression slice after the benchmark-timeout prompt update.** The CLI-side event logger only persisted trace rows from live callback events, so fake/non-streaming runners could still produce a final report with `trace_rows` but no `trace.handoff` rows in `events.jsonl`. Fix: `src/dan/cli/code.py` now backfills final `report.trace_rows` into the per-run event log when no live `trace.row` callback arrived.

- ~~**P2: DAN-v2 operator-pack cue matching could widen a simple browser/download request into the cross-surface operator pack because single-word cues were matched by raw substring.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-18.** **Observed while freezing the `54-4` operator acceptance matrix:** the browser/download benchmark phrase `latest artifact` accidentally triggered the local-operator `test` cue inside `latest`, which pushed the case from `browser_download` to `cross_surface_operator`. Fix: `src/dan/server/control_plane.py::_contains_any(...)` now uses word-boundary matching for single-token cues while preserving phrase-cue matching, and `tests/eval/test_operator_plane_benchmark_matrix.py` now keeps this frozen against the runtime classifier.
- **Failed approach recorded:** naive substring cue scans are too brittle for the operator matrix because short action words like `test` appear inside unrelated words (`latest`). The classifier needs boundary-aware token matching, not more cue words.

- ~~**P2: the organism-log visualizer could reject real DAN Research traces even when the latest rows already used `organism_log_v1`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-18.** **Observed while opening `/Volumes/data/Dropbox/Projects/.dan-research/control-plane-events.jsonl` and `.dan-research/runs/turn-02/events.jsonl` in the Development timeline.** The route summary logic only accepted files whose *first* row already had `schema: organism_log_v1`, but reused Research run/control-plane files kept older pre-schema rows at the front and a clean `organism_log_v1` suffix at the end. Fix: teach `src/dan/worker/organism_log.py` and `src/dan/server/routers/misc.py` to analyze the contiguous v1 suffix from mixed JSONL files instead of rejecting the whole file.

- **P1: Beacon Search live web discovery still depends on provider APIs for many broad-web queries, so the completed Beacon incubation should not be read as Tavily-free search.** **Observed 2026-04-17 via a real `FLY` equity-research prompt run through DAN's own `handle_web_search(...)` surface.** The request succeeded and the official Firefly IR page was grounded and persisted into Beacon correctly, but the live response still reported `provider: tavily` for discovery. That confirms the broker, corpus, and grounding layers are working while the remaining gap is provider-independent discovery on cold-start queries. Current workaround: treat Beacon as corpus-first plus provider-backed discovery, seed high-value sources into the corpus, and inspect `provider` / `search_broker` in live payloads before assuming a first-party-only search path. Planned fix: execute [2-beacon-provider-independent-discovery](search-infra-plans/2-beacon-provider-independent-discovery.md) so vertical connectors, refresh loops, and connector-first routing cover a meaningful share of live search without Tavily/Serper/Brave/DDG.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating the completed Beacon incubation milestone as equivalent to provider independence was wrong. A broker boundary, corpus, and eval harness are prerequisites, not the same thing as first-party discovery.

- **P1: DAN Code still crosses a sharp reliability threshold on heavier greenfield tooling/backend prompts.** **Observed 2026-04-14 and refined 2026-04-15/2026-04-16 through follow-on probes, post-planner reruns, live guardrail confirmation, the frozen-pack full battery rerun, the later post-timeout/heartbeat rerun, and the operator-artifact/write-nudge rerun.** The initial isolated `/tmp` human-prompt runs (`codemodx`, `taskforge`, `termboard`) all ended with only `prompt.md`, `acceptance.md`, and `.dan-code/runs/turn-01/events.jsonl` in the workspace. Follow-on probes then showed the same `kimi-k2.5` setup can successfully materialize a tiny scaffold, a local CLI todo app, and a small notes API, so the issue is not a universal inability to create files. Post-planner reruns showed the new milestone planner is active and helpful at the control-plane level: heavy prompts now decompose into bounded first-milestone objectives instead of passing the raw broad request straight into coding. A later lightweight mitigation tightened read-only worker guidance so empty-workspace workers are told to emit `candidate_fragment` instead of trying to create files through `file_read`; that patch improved one heavy case (`codemodx` now crosses into aggregation and writes a scaffold), but it did not make the end-to-end heavy run reliable. A second mitigation now lives in the local tool loop itself: when a read-only `coding_worker` confirms an effectively empty workspace and then probes `.dan-code`, another missing path, or generic `web_search`, the runtime disables further tools and forces one final structured response. Live confirmation at `/tmp/dan-live-taskforge-guardrail-confirm-3` proved this can still materialize milestone files (`database.py` / `models.py`). The next planned seam, DAN Code-side bounded completion timeouts plus quiet-period heartbeats, is now landed and live-confirmed in `/tmp/dan-live-codemodx-fullrerun-3`, `/tmp/dan-live-taskforge-fullrerun-3`, and `/tmp/dan-live-termboard-fullrerun-3`: the product now emits `code.heartbeat` and `model.timeout` instead of silently disappearing after `tool_count=0`, `codemodx` now materializes a real scaffold and passes its bundled parser tests, `taskforge` now materializes a FastAPI/SQLite package and begins writing pytest support files during repair, and `termboard` still failed before first file creation in that run. The later write-nudge rerun moved all three frozen prompts into first-file materialization, so the current heavy-case boundary is now a mix of aggregation output-contract loss after real writes, generated tests missing central acceptance behavior, incomplete repair continuation that stops short of named test files, and stale controller summaries after failed turns. Current workaround: use isolated `/tmp` workspaces for honest debugging, keep timeout/heartbeat and first-write nudges enabled, prefer smaller bounded prompts, treat visible `provider_completion_timeout` recovery as progress but not as a completed milestone, and run external smoke checks that match the human acceptance criteria. Planned fix: harden aggregation contract salvage, cap long repair-stage file-audit loops, force repair workers to write validator-named missing files, and keep acceptance-aware external smoke gates in the live battery.
- **2026-04-16 update:** the `/tmp/dan-live-*-fullrerun-6` battery moved the failure boundary again. After the operator-artifact finalize and direct-write-only first-write nudge seams, all three frozen heavy prompts created real files: `codemodx` produced a package and 27 passing bundled tests but `--apply` did not modify files, `taskforge` produced a FastAPI/SQLite service with 11 passing processor tests and a working API smoke but no API/lifecycle tests, and `termboard` produced a usable CLI/README with add/list smoke success but no tests. The remaining P1 is now repair materialization and acceptance-aware validation rather than first-file creation. Current workaround: keep running external smoke checks that match the human acceptance criteria; do not trust generated tests alone. Planned fix: force repair workers to write validator-named missing files before additional file-audit rounds, and export stale-state checks so the controller cannot claim existing core files are missing after a failed turn.
- **Failed approach recorded:** a validator correctly naming missing test files is not enough if the repair worker and aggregator are allowed to spend the next bounded attempt rereading the existing tree. The next loop needs an execution constraint, not just a better repair brief.
- **Failed approach recorded:** launching the battery inside a nested repo-local workspace hid the real issue because the worker inspected the parent repo's dirty git state instead of staying focused on the empty test workspace. For reproducible DAN Code live repros, use isolated `/tmp` workspaces rather than nested folders inside the main repo.

- **P3: DAN Research depth is still enforced locally per worker completion, not as one shared organ-wide budget.** The new `dan research` / `dan-research` surface exposes `--depth` plus raw `--max-tool-rounds` / `--max-tool-calls`, but those limits currently map onto each worker completion inside the deep-research run rather than one global cap across all reader and lead cells. An 8-reader `--depth deep` run is therefore still bounded, but the bound is per cell, not for the whole organ. Current workaround: lower `--research-readers`, use `--depth shallow`, or set explicit lower tool budgets. Planned fix: add a real top-level research budget envelope.

- **Fixed: native leads ran effectively read-only (2026-09-19).** `codex exec resume` ignores first-turn `--sandbox` and has no such flag, so follow-ups fell back to read-only; Claude `-p --permission-mode default` denies anything needing approval. Pass `-c sandbox_mode=...` every turn and use acceptEdits + sandboxed Bash for Auto. Claude sandbox settings are unverified against a live 2.1.66 run.

- **Tooling: `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` in `editor/` checks nothing (2026-09-19).** The root tsconfig only has project references. Use `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` or `npm run build`.
- **Fixed: stale stylesheet imports (2026-09-19).** `src/index.css` imported `@xyflow/react` and `allotment` CSS after both packages left package.json; any clean `npm install` broke dev/build. Removed.
- **Vite dev: a `const x = lazy(...)` placed among imports throws TDZ (2026-09-19).** Vite rewrites CJS React imports into consts at the import's position; declare lazy components after the whole import block.

- **codexx `share_history` symlinks account session stores to `~/.codex` (2026-09-21).** `~/.codex-accounts/<name>/.codex/sessions` and `state_5.sqlite` are symlinks, so per-account session deletion is impossible without deleting everyone's; discovery dedupes by resolved store path.

- **Claude Code + CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR (2026-09-21).** Setting `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, even to the default `~/.claude`, changes the keychain lookup so a claude.ai login appears logged out in headless runs. Never set it unless the user did.

- **Fixed: workspace file tree crashed on out-of-root symlinks (2026-09-21).** `list_workspace_file_tree` resolved symlinks then called `relative_to(root)`; the skill pool's links into `~/.codex/skills` raised ValueError → 500 and an empty Files tab. Entries resolving outside the root are skipped.

## Recently Fixed

- ~~**P2: DAN Research continuation retries could reopen weaker versions of already-settled facts because the product had no durable claim memory and no typed series/metric identity above free-form prose.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-18.** Live oil/coal/ETF runs showed the planner could rediscover the same fact family repeatedly even after one pass had already reached an authoritative answer, because later retries only saw raw prose plus unresolved gaps and had no session-level memory that one version of the claim was already frozen. Fix: persist closed verification facts in a session-level claim ledger, carry canonical claim metadata (`claim_key`, authority rank, typed scope/series/metric hints) through the research product seam, and feed that ledger back into continuation planning so later retries do not casually reopen weaker versions of the same claim.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding aliases/proxy guidance alone to `evidence_targets` improved retrieval, but it did not stop the same fact from being reopened on a later pass. The missing generic seam was durable claim memory plus typed identity metadata, not another planner or more agents.
- ~~**P1: benchmark-mode DAN Code could export unfinished SWE-bench patches even when the reviewer explicitly said `continue`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The post-contract-shaping partial rerun showed `astropy__astropy-14365` and `astropy__astropy-6938` review decisions with `action=continue` and concrete next validation work, immediately followed by `run.log.completed` / prediction export. Root cause: benchmark review normalization treated any completed material-output candidate as exportable, and the CLI wrote SWE-bench artifacts for every report rather than only the final accepted report. Fix: preserve benchmark `continue` decisions, raise benchmark supervision to four passes, mark exhausted benchmark continuation as `incomplete`, and only write SWE-bench artifacts for the final completed report.
- **Failed approach recorded:** forcing `done` for material-output benchmark candidates solved an early no-export smoke-run seam, but it was too permissive for real scoring. The safer generic contract is: a material patch can be reviewed, but export requires a final `done` decision; otherwise the loop continues or returns an honest incomplete artifact.
- ~~**P2: benchmark continuation turns could add local test changes into exported SWE-bench predictions.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The first live harder-exit rerun on `astropy__astropy-12907` correctly preserved the reviewer `continue` decision and launched a validation turn, but that turn added `astropy/modeling/tests/test_separable.py` changes while validating. Exporting those test diffs is not a minimal model fix and can collide with the official harness's own test patch. Fix: derive protected benchmark test files from `selected_test_files_to_run`, `FAIL_TO_PASS`, and `PASS_TO_PASS`, then filter those diff sections out of exported `model_patch` while preserving source-code diffs.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on prompt wording alone to keep benchmark patches code-only is not enough once the continuation turn is explicitly asked to validate tests. The artifact exporter needs a metadata-derived patch hygiene layer.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research’s review/orchestrator prompts still described the old gate-heavy stop rules after the controller had already been simplified.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The normalization layer had already been updated to stop only on missing material output/evidence, conflicted facts, blocking audit issues, unresolved contradictions, or failed runs, but the prompt text still told the model to continue for low confidence, provisional readiness, and missing/failed quality gates. That mismatch would usually be corrected by normalization, but it still biased the review model toward unnecessary continuation language and stale handoff reasoning. Fix: align the review contract and orchestrator instruction text with the simplified controller semantics so prompts and handoffs now describe the same stop/continue rules.
- **Failed approach recorded:** simplifying controller code without simplifying the prompt/handoff text leaves a latent drift seam. The normalization layer can correct a bad decision after the fact, but it cannot stop the model from wasting reasoning budget on rules the controller no longer believes.
- ~~**P1: DAN Research continuation turns could keep burning the full 180-second planner guard window even when the next bounded pass only needed a narrow follow-up objective.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** Live `control-plane-events.jsonl` traces showed repeated `orchestrator.plan.started -> orchestrator.plan.hedge.launched -> orchestrator.plan.fallback` cycles with no planner exceptions, which meant the heavyweight continuation planner calls were simply staying pending until the controller deadline expired. Fix: keep the initial intention-breaker, but remove the model-driven continuation planner from the normal path; continuation turns now derive a deterministic follow-up plan from the latest report/review context instead of waiting on another long structured-output planner call.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating every continuation pass like a fresh planning problem added latency and drift without improving research quality. The useful planning seam is the first pass; after that, unresolved-fact narrowing is usually deterministic.
- ~~**P1: DAN Research could keep looping after a materially useful report just because confidence, readiness, or quality-gate metadata was still imperfect.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The earlier review seam had accreted too many hard blockers: low confidence by itself, `blocked` / `provisional` readiness, missing/warn quality gates, and several audit/readiness permutations could all force another pass even when the report already had evidence, surfaced its caveats honestly, and the remaining gaps were minor. Fix: shrink the hard stop contract to the minimal honest blockers only: non-completed/error runs, no material output, missing evidence refs, conflicted facts, blocking audit issues, or weakly resolved contradictions. Reports can now stop provisionally with explicit caveats instead of looping to satisfy a product-level perfection gate.
- **Failed approach recorded:** quality-gate metadata is useful as operator-facing context, but using it as the primary stop/continue controller made the product much more fragile than the bounded research organ actually needed.
- ~~**P1: benchmark-mode DAN Code runs were still too loosely guided around branch-specific public contracts, so SWE-bench near misses could ship plausible patches with the wrong warning/check ID, message behavior, deprecation timing, or edge-case shape.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The first official `partial15` Lite checkpoint resolved `9/15`, and inspection of the six unresolved cases showed that most were not “no attempt” failures. The model usually found the fix neighborhood but still exported branch-inexact behavior: silent cleanup where the branch expected a visible notice, immediate hard errors where the branch expected a warning/deprecation path, wrong check IDs/messages, or over-coarse empty-input handling. Fix: `src/dan/cli/code.py` now threads `FAIL_TO_PASS` / `PASS_TO_PASS` tests into benchmark evidence summaries, acceptance criteria, research findings, and explicit benchmark hard/soft constraints, while `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_execution.py` now passes benchmark context into validator payloads, tightens validator focus around exact checked-out-branch contracts, and gives benchmark tasks a second repair round before export. Coverage lives in `tests/test_cli/test_code.py`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating SWE-bench as just “make a minimal patch” was too weak. The missing generic seam was explicit runtime pressure toward nearby-test conventions and exact checked-out-branch public contracts, not more instance-specific pattern matching.

- ~~**P2: DAN Research continuation passes could keep rediscovering the same fact because the planning seam had no typed fact target, no alias/site/proxy contract, and no resolved-fact cache.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** Live oil/coal/ETF runs showed the bounded organ could often get close to the same answer repeatedly, but the continuation planner kept reissuing broad fact-gathering passes because it did not tell readers exactly which fact to pin down, which aliases or preferred sites to try, when a lagged proxy was acceptable, or when a claim had already been settled strongly enough. Fix: `research_conversation.py` now adds typed `evidence_targets` with aliases, preferred sites/source families, acceptable proxy rules, explicit stop conditions, and separate `not_found` vs `not_available` guidance, while continuation planning now carries solved facts forward so already-settled claims are not reopened unless a contradiction or blocking audit issue explicitly reopens them.
- **Failed approach recorded:** telling the planner to “gather stronger evidence” was too broad. The missing generic seam was an explicit exact-fact contract with alias/source/proxy/availability guidance plus resolved-fact carry-forward.

- ~~**P1: DAN Research control-stage hedges could poison the durable orchestrator session and then trap every later review in a generic `continue` fallback loop.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** Live `control-plane-events.jsonl` traces showed the first initial planning hedge timed out, cancelled both stage tasks, then stored the same original durable session back into `.dan-research/session.json`. Because the underlying durable runner still considered that message active after cancellation, every later `review_research_result(...)` call immediately raised `RuntimeError: ... already processing message ...`, which forced the review seam onto the conservative fallback path and kept launching broad continuation passes. Fix: `src/dan/cli/research.py` now quarantines any control-stage fallback session that timed out, was cancelled, raised a control-stage exception, or still looks busy/closed, emits an explicit `orchestrator.<stage>.session.reset` control-log event, and continues with a fresh orchestrator session instead of reusing the poisoned one.
- **Failed approach recorded:** hedging stalled `decide` / `plan` / `review` calls was not enough by itself. The control-plane fallback path also had to treat cancelled timed-out durable sessions as tainted rather than persisting them for the next stage.
- ~~**P2: full-split SWE-bench batch runs could be blocked indefinitely by one long DAN Code subprocess because the adapter only bounded individual provider completions, not the whole instance attempt.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The first full Lite `test` launch reached `astropy__astropy-12907` and made real tool progress, but the unguarded batch had no outer per-instance wall-clock cap, so one aggregation-heavy run could block all remaining instances. Fix: `tests/eval/swebench_runner.py` now accepts `run_timeout_seconds` and records timeout attempts with exit code `124`, while `tests/eval/swebench_batch_runner.py` defaults each instance to a `900s` wall-clock cap and keeps the batch resumable through the combined prediction JSONL. The guarded restart exported `astropy__astropy-12907` and continued to the next instance.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on `--completion-timeout-seconds` alone is insufficient for benchmark batches because a run can make many successful bounded completions while still failing to converge. Batch orchestration needs an outer instance budget in addition to per-completion provider timeouts.
- ~~**P1: DAN Research could enter a bad escalation loop even when `web_search` was returning real grounded results, because the deep-research reader cells were still being asked to produce the full final public report and the read-only tool loop had no research-note finalize nudge.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** Live `.dan-research` logs showed the search substrate was healthy: reader tool calls were succeeding and grounded `web_search` payloads were present in the per-run event log. The failure was higher up. Retrieval-tissue members inherited the outer organ output contract, so each reader was effectively prompted to produce the entire final report instead of one compact evidence note, which encouraged repeated search/tool rounds until reader runtime budgets were exhausted and the organ escalated before lead synthesis. Fix: let `TissueMember` carry an internal `output_contract_override`, give deep-research readers a compact evidence-note contract, add a read-only local-runtime finalize nudge for research-note workers once they already have bounded grounding, and stop defaulting escalated/no-output research reports to `report_readiness=grounded`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating the repeated escalations as a `web_search` outage or a planning-layer-only problem was wrong. The logs showed the actual bug was an internal contract mismatch between the retrieval tissue and the public organ membrane.
- ~~**P1: DAN Research could keep looping forever on one inaccessible exact-date fact even after the objective itself explicitly said “mark it unverifiable, downgrade confidence, and finish provisionally.”**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** Live `control-plane-events.jsonl` and `turn-08/events.jsonl` traces showed the bounded run had already produced the intended low-confidence provisional artifact for the final missing coal-price fact, but the durable review helpers still applied the generic `confidence < 0.55 => continue` rule and overrode that explicit fallback path. Fix: `research_conversation.py` now recognizes objectives that explicitly authorize best-effort provisional closure after repeated attempts, allows that narrow case to stop when the report already surfaces the unresolved fact as `unverified`/`unverifiable` with downgraded confidence and material evidence, and normalizes stray `continue` review outputs back to `done` instead of relaunching the same pass again.
- **Failed approach recorded:** it was not enough to tell the model in continuation objectives to “mark remaining facts unverifiable and finalize provisionally.” The review control seam also needed to honor that instruction instead of blindly reapplying the global low-confidence stop rule.
- ~~**P1: benchmark git-tool and shell sandbox path handling could still inspect or execute against the wrong environment during SWE-bench runs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** Live 1343 probing showed two separate environment seams after the first benchmark export fix: `git_status(path='.')` / `git_diff(path='.')` still resolved against the main repo process cwd instead of the detached SWE-bench workspace, and sandboxed validation commands could fail with `cat: command not found` because the shell env no longer inherited a normal system-bin `PATH`. Fix: `local_runtime.py` now normalizes explicit relative git-tool paths against `workspace_root`, and `shell_command.py` now seeds standard system-bin `PATH` entries while preserving workspace-local `PYTHONPATH`. Validation lives in `tests/test_worker/test_local_organism_runtime.py` and `tests/test_tools/test_shell_command.py`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** letting git tools interpret explicit `"."` or other relative paths as ordinary process-cwd paths was fine for repo-local coding turns but wrong for detached benchmark worktrees. The right contract is "workspace-root relative unless absolute."
- ~~**P2: benchmark validators could still ask file tools for `/workspace/...` paths and hit false file-not-found errors on local detached worktrees.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** A live rerun of `marshmallow-code__marshmallow-1359` showed reviewer traces asking for `list_directory(path="/workspace")`, which mirrors Docker-harness layouts but not DAN's local detached worktree paths. Fix: `src/dan/tools/_workspace.py` now maps `/workspace` and `/workspace/...` onto the active `DAN_WORKSPACE_ROOT`, and the same live rerun confirmed the aliased directory listing now succeeds.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on the model to recover from `/workspace` file-not-found errors was unnecessary benchmark friction. This is better handled once in the shared path resolver.
- ~~**P1: benchmark-safe line-based editing could still corrupt a file when the model sent an insertion as a narrow replace payload that repeated the previous line plus the unchanged target prefix.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The 1343 rerun stayed off regex/grep entirely but still produced a duplicated function header because the model used `file_edit(mode='replace')` with replacement content that started with the previous line and ended with the untouched prefix of the target block. Fix: `file_edit.py` now recognizes that anchor-heavy payload shape and reinterprets it as an `insert_after` on the prior line, keeping only the truly inserted lines. Coverage lives in `tests/test_engine/test_tool_executor_integration.py`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** simply telling the model to prefer line-based tools over regex search was not enough. The next benchmark-quality seam after localization was edit-shape normalization around multiline insertions.
- ~~**P1: SWE-bench smoke runs could validate the correct benchmark patch and still fail to export final artifacts because the outer DAN Code review loop continued into another turn.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The first live Lite/Kimi smoke run on `princeton-nlp/SWE-bench_Lite` instance `marshmallow-code__marshmallow-1359` proved the benchmark adapter could reach the right workspace diff, but DAN Code still continued into another turn instead of exporting the accepted candidate. The full fix was slightly broader than expected: `coding_conversation.py` now forces `done` for completed material-output benchmark candidates, `code.py` threads benchmark mode into the review facts packet, the git helpers prefer `/usr/bin/git` on this machine, sandboxed `shell_command` now honors `working_directory` and injects workspace-local `PYTHONPATH`, and `file_edit` now accepts common LLM replace payloads without collapsing nearby lines. Validation: the rerun under `tests/eval/results/20260416_164247_marshmallow-code__marshmallow-1359/` exited `0`, reported `report_status=completed`, and emitted `report.json`, `predictions.jsonl`, `stdout.log`, `stderr.log`, `dan-code/turn-01/swebench.patch`, and `dan-code/turn-01/swebench-prediction.json` with the expected one-line patch.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating the first SWE-bench miss as only an outer review-loop problem was incomplete. The same benchmark path also exposed tool-level compatibility seams (`git`, sandboxed `shell_command`, and LLM-shaped `file_edit` payloads), so the durable fix had to cover both the product review/export seam and the local tool layer.
- **Failed approach recorded:** telling the model to avoid regex/grep-style localization is useful but not sufficient by itself. The follow-on Lite probe on `marshmallow-code__marshmallow-1343` stayed on structured line-based tools, yet it still chose an overly narrow `file_edit(mode="replace")` where an insert-style edit would have been safer, then fell into a repair loop. The next benchmark-quality seam is edit-shape guidance and/or stronger normalization around multiline insertions, not more pattern matching.
- ~~**P1: SWE-bench benchmark-mode planner bypass could still be a no-op on the live `dan code` path because the project-planner facts packet quietly dropped `benchmark_context`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** I first added a benchmark-mode early return in `CodingProjectPlannerController.plan_project(...)`, but live 1343 reruns still started from a reproduction-only milestone. The missing link was `src/dan/cli/code.py::_project_planner_context(...)`, which rebuilt `CodingConversationFacts` without forwarding `benchmark_context`, so `facts.benchmark_mode` stayed false inside the planner even while the conversation controller saw benchmark mode. Fix: thread `benchmark_context` into `_project_planner_context(...)`, keep the planner-side benchmark bypass, and add focused regressions in `tests/test_cli/test_code.py` and `tests/test_worker/test_coding_conversation.py`. The latest live rerun now starts with one bounded benchmark-fix milestone and goes straight into `schema.py` localization instead of a standalone reproduction milestone.
- **Failed approach recorded:** patching benchmark-mode behavior only inside `coding_conversation.py` was not enough because the CLI was still constructing two different facts packets. Any DAN Code benchmark seam that depends on `facts.benchmark_mode` has to be threaded through both `_conversation_context(...)` and `_project_planner_context(...)`, or the planner and controller will disagree about whether a run is benchmark-scoped.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research could still look like it was actively running forever after one bounded pass had already finished because the continuation planner stalled above the organ while the old completed-phase heartbeat kept printing.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** Live `dan research` repros showed the first bounded pass finishing, `orchestrator.review.completed` returning `continue`, and then no second `run.turn.started` ever appearing in `.dan-research/control-plane-events.jsonl`; the shell only kept printing stale `completed — escalated` heartbeats from the finished pass. Fix: keep the universal-agent substrate unchanged, but harden the product seam in `src/dan/cli/research.py` so the no-tool orchestrator stages (`decide`, `plan`, `review`) now launch one fresh backup durable session after a bounded quiet window, accept the first acceptable result, fall back instead of hanging forever if both attempts stall, and switch the heartbeat phase over to `planning` / `review` while that control-stage wait is in flight.
- **Failed approach recorded:** focusing only on `ok web_search` lines or the per-run bounded `events.jsonl` files made this look like a search/tool stall. The real wedge sat one layer higher in the no-tool continuation planner, so the useful fix belonged at the DAN Research control seam rather than in the bounded deep-research organ or the web tools.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research still decomposed research into a flat list of subproblems, so parallel evidence gathering was implicit and aggregation back into one answer stayed too loose.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The earlier intention-breaker added small subproblems, but it still stopped short of one clean unit for concurrent evidence gathering. That left the CLI passing the bounded organ a flat brief list instead of a few deliberate streams to run in parallel and synthesize afterward. Fix: keep the same universal-agent substrate and bounded deep-research organ, but extend the planner contract with explicit `workstreams` that group related subproblems, carry `goal` / `why_it_matters` / `aggregation_hint`, auto-size unpinned reader width from those streams, and feed stream-aware reader briefs plus summaries into the bounded run.
- **Failed approach recorded:** simply adding more readers or more subproblems was not enough because the planner still lacked a first-class parallel unit above the bounded organ. The useful fix stayed at the product/orchestrator seam and made parallel gathering intentional without changing the universal-agent cell or organ topology.
- ~~**P1: DAN Research could still stop after exactly two bounded passes even when the durable review layer explicitly required another pass.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-17.** The earlier incomplete-finalization seam made the product honest, but it was still driven by a hard-coded `max_supervision_loops = 2` in `src/dan/cli/research.py`. That meant the CLI could now exit cleanly yet still fail the user's actual intent: first pass escalates, second pass escalates again, and the product returns `incomplete` even though the controller itself said “continue.” Fix: make continuation looping unbounded by default at the product seam, add an explicit optional cap through `--max-supervision-loops`, `DAN_RESEARCH_MAX_SUPERVISION_LOOPS`, and workspace config, and preserve the old `incomplete` / `blocked` artifact only when an operator-configured cap is what actually stopped the run.
- **Failed approach recorded:** replacing the hang with an unconditional two-pass stop was better than wedging, but it still was not the right default for a durable research product. The useful fix stayed at the orchestrator/product seam: keep the honest incomplete path, but only use it when a real configured cap is exhausted rather than hard-coding one into every future run.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research could still look wedged for minutes after `tool.started` / `ok web_search` because grounded fetches serialized and some fetch paths had no outer timeout or mid-run heartbeat.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-16 and tightened the same day.** Live logs showed the control plane was not the main bottleneck; the deep-research readers were spending long stretches inside `fetch_content=true` grounding work, and one slow fetch could make the whole run appear frozen because no new event was emitted while that fetch waited. The first fix made grounded page fetches concurrent in `web_search`, reused cache/inflight work for grounded searches too, added an outer total-timeout guard in `web_fetch`, and emitted sparse `research.heartbeat` events from the CLI/run logger during quiet periods so the active worker/phase/query stayed visible in both the console and per-run JSONL logs. The later same-day speed pass then pushed the same idea through the packet/tissue seam: deep-research cells now honor bounded wall-clock budgets through `CellBudgetLimits.max_runtime_seconds`, wider reader pools tolerate a small number of timed-out failures, and auto-sized continuation passes narrow their reader count instead of repeating another full-width broad sweep.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding more readers alone would not have solved this. The useful fix stayed at the runtime/tool seam: faster grounded fetch execution, bounded fetch failure, and better quiet-period observability without changing the universal-agent cell or organ topology.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research and other local-organism runs could crash outright when the provider rejected the assembled prompt as high-risk or content-filtered before generation.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-16.** The provider-side rejection was not a tool failure, but the local runtime still surfaced it as a fatal exception, so one blocked synthesis prompt could tear down an otherwise useful run after evidence gathering had already happened. Fix: treat prompt-level provider safety/content-filter rejections as a recoverable product-seam failure, retry once with a tool-free neutral fallback prompt, and if the provider still refuses, return a blocked structured result instead of crashing the turn.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating these rejections as domain-specific prompt bugs would have overfit the surface. The useful fix was a generic provider-rejection seam in the shared local runtime, so research/coding/planning contracts can all degrade cleanly without baking in one topic family.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research still needed standardized high-level gates for time scope, source authority, and numeric reconciliation.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-16.** The audit appendix could name these issues, but future runs still needed a consistent checklist so the verifier did not depend entirely on free-form prose. Fix: add required `quality_gates` rows for `time_anchor`, `scope_boundary`, `source_authority`, `numeric_reconciliation`, `claim_object_fit`, and `final_status`, then block finalization when those gates are missing, failed, or still warn on critical dimensions. This is intentionally domain-general and catches failures like historical-vs-current drift, overbroad "global/only/no available product" claims, weak exchange/product-status sourcing, unreconciled vendor numbers, and thesis-to-object mismatch across future research topics.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on the model to surface these problems only inside `audit_issues` was too loose. The useful fix was a standard gate checklist that every report must fill, while keeping the universal-agent cell and bounded organ topology unchanged.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research still lacked one explicit temporal frame before search and could leave the last artifact looking finished even when review still wanted another pass.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-16.** Runtime date/time fields already existed, but they were still too implicit: relative-time language such as `current`, `latest`, `recent`, or “past 6 months” did not have one small structured frame before bounded research launched, and if the durable review layer still wanted another bounded pass after the supervision limit, the last report could still read like the finished artifact. Fix: infer a generic temporal frame (`current`, `historical_snapshot`, `trend`, `timeless`) before search, thread it through the bounded task payload and task-derived reader briefs, persist it on the report, and explicitly mark the artifact `incomplete` / `blocked` with a failing `final_status` gate whenever another pass is still required but was not included.
- **Failed approach recorded:** prompt-only reminders about “be careful with dates” were too soft. The useful fix was a small structured temporal frame plus an explicit incomplete-finalization path at the product/runtime seam, without changing the universal-agent cell or bounded organ topology.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research could still jump from a broad user ask straight into wide search without first decomposing what actually needed to be answered.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-16.** The durable orchestrator already knew whether a turn should launch research, but once it chose `research` the product still moved almost directly into a broad bounded pass. That left too much of the planning burden inside the live search step itself, especially on multi-part or follow-up questions. Fix: add a thin product-seam intention-breaker that decomposes each research objective into small subproblems with `why_it_matters` and `evidence_to_seek`, feed that plan into the bounded run as refined objectives/evidence summaries/reader briefs, and re-plan continuation passes from unresolved prior-report gaps instead of repeating another broad sweep.
- **Failed approach recorded:** piling on more post-hoc report checks without improving pre-search decomposition was not the highest-leverage fix. The useful patch stayed at the orchestrator/product seam and made search more purposeful without changing the universal-agent cell or deep-research organ topology.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research could still stop after a fact-verification pass even when the final report remained logically weak, stale, or poorly mapped from evidence to conclusion.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-15.** The earlier grounding hardening made current-entity facts safer, but it still left a gap above that seam: a report could carry a cleaner verification appendix and still overstate actionability while freshness gaps, source-authority gaps, methodology gaps, or claim-to-scope mismatches remained unresolved. Fix: add a second structured report appendix for audit issues (`logic`, `freshness`, `authority`, `scope_fit`, `conflict`, `method`) plus explicit `report_readiness` / `readiness_note` fields, and tighten the durable review gate so provisional/blocked reports, conflicted critical facts, or blocking audit issues trigger another bounded pass instead of silently finalizing.
- **Failed approach recorded:** stopping at a stronger fact appendix alone was not enough. The useful fix was to keep fact verification and reasoning-quality adjudication as separate generic report seams at the product layer, instead of trying to solve logical weakness with more search or with one-off domain patches.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research could still over-trust secondary web pages even when better primary sources were in the same result set.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-15.** After the generic current-entity verification gate was added, one remaining weakness was discovery quality: search results could still surface retail quote or summary pages alongside stronger regulator, exchange, issuer, or official docs pages, leaving too much ranking burden on the model. Fix: add authority-aware ranking metadata to `web_search` so primary-source domains are ordered above known retail aggregators, and add an explicit verification appendix to the research report so critical facts are marked `verified`, `unverified`, or `conflicted`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying only on prompt wording to “prefer authoritative sources” still left too much room for frontier-model shortcutting. The better fix was to improve both the search-result ordering and the final report contract instead of only asking the model to behave better.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research could finalize generic snippet-only reports about concrete external entities even when the current status was not authoritatively verified.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-15, refined the same day.** The failure mode was broader than one ticker: if a run relied on search snippets for a current product/ticker/law/policy/API/page status, the durable review layer could still accept the report as `done` even when confidence stayed weak or contradictions remained. Fix: require authoritative verification when those current-state facts materially affect the conclusion, tighten the review gate so low-confidence, missing-evidence, or contradiction-heavy reports are pushed back for more work, and keep grounded page reads available through the same `web_search` surface instead of forcing a separate visible web-fetch tool in the default research basket.
- **Failed approach recorded:** patching one ticker family or relying on prompt wording alone would have left the same trap for other external entities. The useful fix was generic authoritative-source verification plus a stricter product-level done gate, while leaving the universal-agent cell and bounded deep-research organ substrate unchanged.
- ~~**P3: DAN Research step logging was too local to diagnose “stuck after `ok web_search`” runs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-15.** The product already kept per-run `.dan-research/runs/turn-XX/events.jsonl` files for bounded research events, but startup/session/orchestrator/control-flow events before and around those runs were not captured in one durable timestamped log. That made long pauses after successful tool calls look like opaque hangs. Fix: add `.dan-research/control-plane-events.jsonl` as a workspace-level append-only JSONL log with UTC timestamps and sequence numbers for CLI startup, config/session lifecycle, interactive commands, orchestrator decisions, run launch/completion/failure, review decisions, and persistence.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on only the per-run `events.jsonl` files was not enough because the most confusing gaps sat above the bounded run itself. The useful fix was to add a second thin control-plane log instead of bloating the universal worker substrate.
- ~~**P2: `dan research` could crash on startup if `.dan-research/session.json` or `config.json` existed but was empty/truncated.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-15.** Live repro showed the loader assuming any existing persistence file contained valid JSON, so a zero-byte or interrupted write surfaced as a fatal `ValidationError` (`Invalid JSON: EOF while parsing a value`) before the research CLI could even start. Fix: make the research product loader treat blank/invalid persisted JSON as recoverable state and write config/session files atomically so interrupted saves are less likely to leave empty files behind.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on ordinary `Path.write_text(...)` overwrites was too optimistic for a durable local product surface. The useful fix was to harden both sides of the seam: forgiving reads for damaged session/config files and atomic writes for future saves.
- ~~**P2: DAN Research could fail mid-run on minor tool/output shape mismatches even though the bounded research membrane was otherwise healthy.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-14.** Real repro from `dan research`: some reader cells emitted `web_search` calls as `queries=[...]` instead of the single-call `query=...` contract, and some completed research outputs surfaced confidence strings like `MEDIUM-HIGH (70%)`, which then crashed `ResearchOrganismReport` validation. Fix: keep the substrate unchanged, normalize the `queries` alias back onto one concrete `query` string in `LocalOrganismToolRuntime`, and coerce confidence labels/percentages into a bounded numeric score at the research report seam.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on stricter prompt wording alone was not enough. The useful fix was a narrow runtime/report normalization seam so the product tolerates common frontier-model format drift without weakening the organ contract.
- ~~**P2: the deep-research organ still hard-coded two reader cells even after the research surface was strengthened.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-13.** The runtime substrate already supported wider bounded fan-out, but the preset still built exactly two readers (`reader-a`, `reader-b`), so research breadth could only grow through prompt accretion instead of actual parallel evidence passes. Fix: make the preset accept a bounded reader count up to `8`, add task-aware reader-count selection helpers in the reference organism layer, and expose an explicit `--research-readers` override in the CLI while keeping the same tissue-plus-lead substrate.
- **Failed approach recorded:** simply hard-coding the preset to eight readers would have increased parallelism but not flexibility. The better fix was to let the top-level connector size the pool per task and keep the organ substrate itself unchanged.
- ~~**P2: the deep-research organ was still weaker than its own `52-3` contract and only had an indirect full-organism entry point.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-13.** The original preset returned `findings`, `evidence_summary`, `open_questions`, and `recommended_change`, but it did not require explicit `evidence_refs`, `contradictions`, or `confidence`, and the only live operator surface forced callers through the whole reference organism. Fix: strengthen `deep_research_organ(...)` at the public membrane, keep the same bounded tissue-plus-lead shape, and add a thin `dan organism --research-only` surface that runs just that organ through the existing local runtime with the same read-only role split.
- **Failed approach recorded:** trying to make “deep research” feel stronger by only adding more planner prose or forcing users through the full reference organism was the wrong level. The useful fix was a stricter organ contract plus a thinner top-level connector, not a deeper substrate rewrite.
- ~~**P2: the reference organism still used the planner-selected threshold for validator input but could finalize against the organism default threshold.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-13.** That drift meant the planner could intentionally lower `pass_threshold`, validators would score against the lower bar, and the organism could still continue or fail final completion against `organism.pass_threshold`. Fix: carry the selected threshold into the final repair-loop break condition and completion gate, and record that selected threshold explicitly in result metadata.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating `quality_bar` as only validator-local configuration was too narrow. In this organism, the selected threshold is part of the top-level execution contract and has to govern loop control and final status as well.
- ~~**P2: DAN Code was still not a clean “universal agent + thin top-level connector” stack.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-11.** The bounded organism already ran on the universal worker membrane, but the durable controller still had its own direct-turn/review bypasses, child stage packets rebuilt a smaller local membrane, and the final coding result could be marked `completed` even when no candidate met the selected `pass_threshold`. Fix: remove the controller-level bypasses so explicit coding turns and completed reviews also go through the durable orchestrator mailbox, share one `build_coding_orchestrator_worker(...)` definition between the durable shell and bounded organism, preserve parent budget/authority/continuation/hooks when emitting child handoff packets, and require the selected validator score to meet the selected threshold before final completion.
- **Failed approach recorded:** short-circuiting “obvious” controller turns did reduce one kind of latency, but it weakened the architecture story. The durable product is cleaner when speed work stays in reusable lower seams such as provider hedging and file-tool efficiency, while the control plane itself remains one accountable universal-agent path.
- ~~**P2: old `dan code` sessions could keep stale durable-controller memory and repeated no-output failure context alive even after the runtime code had changed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-11.** Repro from local CLI usage: restart `dan code` after landing runtime fixes, resume the same `.dan-code/session.json`, and the shell could still bias toward outdated fallback behavior such as asking the user to paste files because saved conversation/orchestrator state plus repeated failed-no-output reports were being replayed too literally. Fix: add a DAN Code session format/runtime build id, clear saved conversation/pending-clarification/orchestrator state when the loaded session predates the current runtime, compact persisted session turns so `session.json` stops carrying bulky `outputs`/`trace_rows`, and only feed context-worthy reports back into the controller instead of auto-resuming from failed no-output runs.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating restart alone as sufficient was too optimistic. Reloading Python fixes the code path, but durable shell state still needs its own freshness boundary and more selective carry-forward rules.
- ~~**P2: DAN Code’s durable orchestrator still waited strictly serially on one provider completion for turn/review decisions, so users could feel control-plane latency before any real coding work started.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-11.** The earlier fast paths removed two obvious controller hops, but once a real controller model call was still needed, DAN Code still waited on one no-tool completion at a time. Fix: add bounded hedged requests in `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_conversation.py` for the durable controller only. Up to two staggered no-tool provider calls can now race, and the first response that already validates against the structured decision schema wins; malformed fallbacks are only reused if no acceptable hedge response arrives.
- **Failed approach recorded:** pushing this into the bounded coding organism or tool loop would have been the wrong level. The useful speedup target was the no-tool control layer above the agents, where responses are cheap to duplicate and easy to validate strictly.
- ~~**P2: `dan code --show-model-trace` could still break tool-enabled local-runtime rounds and print blank `stream:` headers.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-11.** The original shared provider `stream(...)` seam only carried public text deltas, so streamed tool rounds could lose the final tool call entirely and collapse into blank worker trace lines like `[aggregation.lead][model] stream:` plus downstream output-contract failures. Fix: extend the completed `StreamChunk` contract to carry final tool calls, finish reason, and raw assistant payloads; rebuild full streamed completion results in the local tool loop; and keep the CLI fallback preview for streamed turns that emit no visible deltas.
- **Failed approach recorded:** the first stopgap of forcing tool-enabled rounds back onto `complete(...)` was safe but too limiting. The durable fix was to make the shared streaming contract honest about final tool-call metadata instead of pretending every streamed turn is text-only.
- ~~**P2: DAN Code kept turn timestamps and internal trace rows, but it still did not persist a timestamped event log for every visible bounded-run step.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-11.** `session.json` and `transcript.jsonl` already carried coarse turn-level timing, and worker trace rows kept handoff/signal times, but the live CLI lifecycle stream (`status.update`, tool calls, public assistant follow-ups) was still ephemeral. Fix: add a per-run `events.jsonl` logger under `.dan-code/runs/turn-XX/`, stamp every emitted bounded-run event with UTC time plus a monotonic sequence number, thread the resolved `event_log_path` into `CodingOrganismReport`, and persist that path into `transcript.jsonl` for later analysis.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on `trace_rows` alone was not enough for product-level analysis. The useful seam was the CLI/event-callback layer above the bounded organism, because that is where user-visible status/tool/model/public-message events are already normalized.
- ~~**P2: validator review tissues could still escalate on harmless reviewer prose drift even when the intended verdict was obvious and the conservative tie-break policy was already configured.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The quorum layer already normalized exact/near-exact verdict strings, but a completed reviewer answer that stayed non-empty while missing the exact vote token could still count as a distinct new vote and bubble up to `The tissue quorum did not converge on a reusable decision.` Fix: in `src/dan/worker/tissue.py`, treat non-empty unmatched review prose as the first configured tie-break priority, while still leaving blank/missing vote output outside the vote counts so true missing outputs continue to escalate as `quorum_not_reached`.
- **Failed approach recorded:** the right place to harden this was the reusable tissue vote-normalization seam, not DAN Code-specific retry prompting or more validator prompt text. The failure was in cross-review reduction, not in the coding organism contract.
- ~~**P2: repeated same-file `file_edit` calls were still paying avoidable full-file rewrite cost even though the universal-agent stack above them was already trimmed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** `file_edit` was intentionally predictable, but when a model made several non-overlapping edits to the same file it still had to read and rewrite the entire file once per call. Fix: extend `src/dan/tools/file_edit.py` with a backward-compatible `edits=[...]` batch form so one tool call can apply multiple same-file edits in a single read/write pass, and update the shared local tool-use policy to prefer that form.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding a new runtime lane or special “fast edit organism” would have been the wrong level. The useful speedup here came from making the existing universal file-edit primitive cheaper for same-file bursts, not from bypassing the universal worker/orchestrator stack.
- ~~**P2: `file_read` was still needlessly expensive and self-contradictory for large files.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The tool always loaded the whole file path through the full-size guard before considering `start_line` / `end_line`, so a caller could get told to “use start_line/end_line to read a portion” on a file that the implementation still refused to touch. Fix: normalize/validate the requested range first, only enforce the `MAX_FILE_SIZE` cap for unrestricted full-file reads, and stream just the requested line window when a bounded range is supplied.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating all three file tools as one performance problem was too blunt. `file_write` was already simple append/overwrite I/O, `file_edit` was intentionally a full-file rewrite for predictable line-based edits, and the real tool-level inefficiency lived in `file_read`’s large-file path.
- ~~**P2: direct DAN Code edit/fix requests were still paying avoidable top-layer model latency before any real coding work started.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The actual file tools were already fast, but the durable shell still spent a full orchestrator model turn classifying many obviously-direct edit requests, and then spent another model turn reviewing clearly successful completed runs even when the answer was deterministically `done`. Fix: add conservative control-layer fast paths for plain imperative coding/edit requests and for obviously successful bounded-run reviews, so common edit turns skip those extra LLM hops while status/meta/question turns still go through the orchestrator normally.
- **Failed approach recorded:** assuming the slowdown was in `file_edit` itself was wrong. Quick local timing showed the file tool was millisecond-scale, so the meaningful latency lived in the shell/conversation layer above the bounded organism rather than in the line-edit primitive.
- ~~**P2: `dan code --show-model-trace` still only showed model trace after completion instead of letting operators watch the public text arrive live.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The providers already exposed a shared `stream(...)` API, but the DAN Code local tool loop, durable orchestrator adapter, and CLI renderer were still using `complete(...)`-only behavior for public trace display. That meant `--show-model-trace` could show request/response summaries and previews, but not the live streamed text itself. Fix: stream text-only replies through both the local-runtime tool loop and the durable coding-conversation adapter when trace display is enabled, emit explicit `model.stream.started` / `model.stream.delta` / `model.stream.completed` events, and render them inline in `CodeProgressRenderer` without duplicating the final preview line.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating `--show-model-trace` as “just print the final preview with more metadata” was too shallow. The feature only becomes honest when the public CLI trace subscribes to the shared provider streaming seam and the renderer can display streamed deltas directly.
- ~~**P2: real DAN Code transcripts could still waste turns on non-repo git probes, verbose reviewer vote strings, and duplicate multi-worker fan-out for obviously narrow tasks.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The local runtime still exposed `git_status` / `git_diff` / `git_log` even when the workspace was not inside a repository, which encouraged validators and aggregators to burn turns on guaranteed failures like `Not inside a git repository`. Review quorum extraction also treated longer strings like `repair - needs another pass` as different votes from plain `repair`, which could still escalate a validator even with a clear conservative intent. And the coding-plan normalizer still trusted repeated worker briefs enough to keep `worker_count > 1` on narrow tasks. Fix: filter structured git tools out of the local runtime when the workspace is not inside a repo and explicitly tell models not to use shell `git` fallback there, normalize tie-break vocabulary out of longer review strings, and collapse duplicate multi-worker briefs back to one worker during coding-plan normalization.
- **Failed approach recorded:** relying on prompt wording alone to say “keep the pool small” and “first confirm the path is inside a git repository” was not enough. The runtime had to remove unavailable git tools, the quorum layer had to normalize verbose vote text, and the coding-plan normalizer had to enforce the single-worker fallback when the orchestrator did not actually supply distinct briefs.
- ~~**P2: the newest DAN Code hardening around `workspace_effect` and mutation-proof gating was still only “shared in spirit” because the outcome/effect logic remained trapped inside `coding_execution.py`, and validator tie-break behavior was still hidden inside one preset body.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** That made the design cleaner than the implementation: other bounded organisms could not reuse the same normalization/effect rules, and validator tie-break policy was only configurable by editing the preset implementation. Fix: extract a shared `src/dan/worker/outcomes.py` module for structured-outcome normalization plus `modified|verified` inference / mutation-proof policy, wire `coding_execution.py` onto that shared seam, and make `universal_validator_organ(...)` accept an explicit `review_tie_break_priority` contract.
- **Failed approach recorded:** stopping after “one internal adapter inside `coding_execution.py`” was enough to fix DAN Code, but not enough to make the reuse story honest. The follow-up fix had to move that logic into `dan.worker` itself and expose the validator tie-break policy as a preset argument rather than leaving both ideas buried in coding-only code.
- ~~**P2: verification-only DAN Code runs could now fail for the opposite reason after the mutation-evidence hardening landed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The new guard correctly rejected aggregation candidates that claimed concrete file changes without any mutation-capable tool call, but it was still using raw `target_files` presence as the proxy for "claimed a patch." That broke honest no-op verification passes like the absharks transcript, where the aggregator only executed `list_directory` / `file_read`, correctly reported `No code changes required`, and still carried `target_files` plus `test_plan` evidence. Fix: normalize coding candidates through one shared adapter, classify a conservative `workspace_effect` (`modified` vs `verified`) from explicit fields plus change-summary wording, and only require mutation evidence for `modified` candidates.
- **Failed approach recorded:** treating any non-empty `target_files` list as proof that the candidate claimed a patch was too blunt. `target_files` are also part of a verification/reporting payload, so the final guard now keys off the normalized workspace-effect classification instead of the raw presence of file paths.
- ~~**P2: DAN Code session rollups could print validation commands one character per line.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** Cause: `run_coding_organism_live()` in `src/dan/cli/code.py` wrapped raw `target_files` / `test_plan` / `risks` values in `list(...)` before `CodingOrganismReport` validation, so a multiline validation string became a character list and was then persisted into `.dan-code/session.json` / `transcript.jsonl`. Fix: pass the raw values into `CodingOrganismReport`, normalize string/list/old char-split report fields in `src/dan/cli/code_product.py`, and repair old saved sessions on load so the CLI renders full validation lines again.
- **Failed approach recorded:** adding strict `output_schema` contracts directly to the coding-organism orchestrator/worker/aggregation/validation boundary packets looked attractive for catching malformed list fields earlier, but it broke the validator review tissue because reviewer members intentionally emit one-word `pass` / `repair` strings and it also prevented failed aggregation runs from preserving partial candidate output. The final fix stays at the CLI/report seam instead of forcing a shared organ boundary schema across unlike member/lead payloads.
