- ~~**P2: bounded DAN Code runs could still look healthier than they were because three small control-plane gaps compounded.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** First, if the orchestrator omitted or malformed `worker_count`, the coding organism fell back to `2`, which encouraged duplicate discovery even for obviously narrow tasks. Second, the universal validator used a 2-reviewer quorum with no tie-break policy, so a 1-1 `pass`/`repair` split escalated the whole validator instead of producing a conservative repair verdict. Third, local aggregation could still claim concrete `target_files` even when the write-capable aggregation phase never executed any mutation-capable tool call, which let read-only worker claims look like real applied fixes. Fix: normalize fallback fan-out from explicit count or brief count with a default of `1`, add a conservative validator tie-break priority of `repair`, and reject local aggregated candidates that claim concrete files without mutation-capable tool evidence from the aggregation phase.
- ~~**P2: the durable DAN Code orchestrator still depended on prompt-only JSON discipline, so semantically correct follow-up turns could degrade into generic fallback behavior whenever the model answered in prose, partial JSON, or the wrong field layout.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The worker core carried `OutputContract.output_schema`, but it was only documentation; no shared runtime seam validated or repaired completions against it. Fix: add a universal structured-output helper plus one repair pass in `WorkerCoreExecutor`, and opt the durable coding-conversation turn/review contracts into that schema-enforced path so controller decisions are repaired at the worker boundary instead of by DAN Code-specific fallback heuristics.
- ~~**P2: even after the clarification-state fix, DAN Code could still answer continuation follow-ups like `can you continue to fix the shark web` with the generic repo-chat fallback whenever the orchestrator model output was unusable for that turn.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The durable controller only reused pending clarification state, so once that was gone the safety fallback ignored the recent failed coding objective already present in session context. Fix: make `_fallback_turn_decision(...)` reuse the latest failed coding objective for continuation-style follow-ups, which keeps retry/resume turns actionable even when the model-side classification flakes.
- ~~**P2: DAN Code could still lose the thread after the orchestrator asked a real next-step question, because that question could be labeled `respond` instead of `clarify` and the shell would clear pending clarification state before the user’s short follow-up.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The durable controller already preserved explicit `clarify` turns, but if the model asked something like “Should I inspect first, or retry the fix?” inside `public_response` with `action="respond"`, the next turn started from a cleared state and often fell back to generic repo-chat copy. Fix: tighten the orchestrator prompt contract and normalize question-like user-turn `respond` payloads into `clarify` so the shell keeps that next-step question as pending clarification.
- ~~**P2: DAN Code could still print a raw fenced JSON decision blob, and failed aggregation runs that had already produced real files still collapsed to `files=(none)` with a misleading “no validated candidate” summary.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** Two seams were still too lossy. First, the durable orchestrator parser only unwrapped structured payloads at the outer envelope, so a nested JSON decision inside `public_response` could leak directly to the CLI. Second, when aggregation escalated after producing structured candidate fields, `OrganExecutionResult` discarded those partial outputs and the coding-organism final report looked like nothing had been written. Fix: teach `coding_conversation.py` to unwrap embedded structured payloads from likely wrapper fields, retain parsed outputs on organ output-contract escalation, and let `coding_execution.py` salvage material candidate fields into failed-run reports and delivery events.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code could still print fenced JSON blobs and then ignore the intended `next_objective` / `repair_brief`, while aggregation/validation organs could escalate on equally valid fenced or slightly verbose JSON outputs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** There were still several duplicated `json.loads(text)` seams across the durable controller and organ runtime. Fenced ` ```json ... ``` ` replies degraded into plain text for the controller, and organ leads could escalate on output-contract failures even when the required keys were present inside a fenced or slightly richer payload. Fix: add `src/dan/worker/structured_payload.py`, route the controller/organism/organ parsers through it, and allow additional output keys for the coding build/aggregation/validator organs while keeping required keys explicit.

- ~~**P2: local coding runs were still burning tool rounds on trivial file-tool argument mismatches like `file_read(file_path=...)`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The standalone file tools only accepted `path=`, but real model traces were frequently using `file_path=`. Fix: add `file_path` compatibility aliases to `src/dan/tools/file_read.py` and `src/dan/tools/file_write.py` so those calls succeed instead of failing fast with a `TypeError`.

- ~~**P2: even after the worker/write boundary fix, local coding runs still used shell and duplicate discovery too loosely because the shared local tool loop did not tell models how to choose among the available structured tools.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The runtime exposed `list_directory`, `file_read`, `file_write`, structured git tools, and sometimes `shell_command`, but there was no shared policy telling the model to prefer the structured tools first. Fix: `ToolLoopCompletionProvider` now injects a reusable local tool-use policy, and the coding organism’s orchestrator/worker/aggregator instructions now reinforce non-overlapping discovery plus structured file-writing.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code’s local tool loop still defaulted to `max_tool_rounds=8`, so long but otherwise healthy coding turns could terminate with `max_tool_rounds_exceeded:8` before the orchestrator had a chance to review them.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The hard cap was still threaded through `ToolLoopCompletionProvider`, `CodeProductConfig`, and `dan code` defaults even after the shell became durable and chat-like. Fix: make `max_tool_rounds` optional across the CLI/config/runtime seam, treat `None`/`<=0` as unbounded, and only enforce a round cap when the operator explicitly sets one.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code’s “read-only” worker and validator roles could still mutate the workspace through `shell_command`, which made the coding-organism module seam dishonest and let worker-side shell writes leak into transcripts.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** `src/dan/worker/organisms/local_runtime.py` originally only removed `file_write` from the read-only basket, but raw `shell_command` remained available even though it can run arbitrary mutating commands (`mkdir`, heredoc writes, etc.). Fix: treat `shell_command` as mutation-capable for the local runtime and strip it from read-only worker/validator/research/synthesis roles so only the full write-capable phases keep shell access.

- ~~**P2: the durable DAN Code orchestrator could still route pure meta/status turns into coding because two orchestration-specific bugs compounded.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** First, the durable orchestrator requests were implicitly inheriting worker-core continuation from prior turns, so resumed sessions could carry stale coding-task acquisition state into a later question like `current status?`. Second, `_parse_payload(...)` in `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_conversation.py` treated the worker-core completion envelope `{"text": ..., "result": ...}` as if it were already the final decision payload, which let the decision models silently fall back to their default `action="code"`. Fix: disable continuation reuse for orchestrator user-turn/review requests and unwrap nested completion JSON before decision normalization.

- ~~**P2: even after continuation and nested-envelope fixes, the durable DAN Code orchestrator could still misroute meta turns when the model replied with plain text or a partial JSON object lacking an explicit `action`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** `CodingConversationTurnDecision`/review normalization still assumed the action would always be explicit, so plain `public_response` payloads for turns like `current status now` or `hello, current progress?` could still normalize badly. Fix: `_parse_payload(...)` now converts non-JSON text into `public_response`, and turn/review normalization now infers safe `respond` / `continue` actions from partial payloads instead of defaulting them into coding.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code had no single source of runtime/session truth, so simple questions like current status or working directory could miss local facts, fall back into generic orchestrator copy, and disagree with the actual workspace/tool runtime.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_conversation.py` now defines `CodingConversationFacts`, `src/dan/cli/code.py` builds that packet centrally from workspace/session/runtime state, the durable orchestrator now answers natural-language status/workspace turns from that shared facts packet, slash-command status output uses the same source of truth, and coding-runtime `current_working_directory` now aligns to the effective workspace root instead of the host shell `PWD`.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code’s first meta-question fix relied on a local regex-style prompt layer, which solved some obvious cases but kept the shell brittle, duplicated intent logic outside the orchestrator, and conflicted with the “durable orchestrator as the public voice” architecture.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The initial 2026-04-09 shell hardening added local workspace/result shortcuts to stop obvious failures, but that left the wrong module seam in place. Fix: remove the natural-language local shortcut layer from `src/dan/cli/code.py`, keep only explicit slash commands local, and route natural-language workspace/result/status questions through the durable orchestrator so they are answered from `CodingConversationFacts` plus recent report context.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code could still declare a build-style coding pass `done` even when the run failed or produced no concrete output/files.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-10.** The earlier durable orchestrator layer let post-run review stay model-driven, but that still allowed an overly optimistic `done` normalization after a failed/no-output pass. Fix: tighten `coding_conversation.py` so build-style runs with errors, no validated candidate, or no concrete material output are normalized to `continue` instead of `done`, and update the review prompt contract to state that rule explicitly.

- ~~**P2: the first durable `dan code` orchestrator still defaulted too rigidly toward coding, so short chat/meta turns without a direct actionable task could still spin up the worker pool.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** The earlier conversation loop kept the orchestrator alive, but its fallback and normalization path still leaned too hard toward `action=code` whenever the model output was noisy or the message was short. Fix: update `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_conversation.py` so the orchestrator instruction and turn contract explicitly reserve `action=code` for directly actionable repo/coding tasks, and make the fallback/normalization logic prefer `respond` / `clarify` for ambiguous non-task turns.

- ~~**P2: transient LLM API failures were still surfacing directly in agent/product shells, which made `dan code` and other surfaces look flaky and encouraged per-surface retry hacks.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** Retry behavior belonged in the shared LLM provider seam, not in each orchestrator. Fix: add `src/dan/providers/retrying_provider.py` as a thin provider wrapper with bounded retry/backoff for retryable transport, timeout, rate-limit, and 5xx failures, apply it once in `src/dan/providers/factory.py`, and route `llm_core/factory.py` default-provider construction through that same factory seam so all agent surfaces inherit the same recovery policy.

- ~~**P2: `dan code` still reset to a fresh bounded organism on every turn, so even with public stage feedback it did not actually feel like a chat-first coding shell.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** The earlier 52-5 work made the orchestrator talk during one bounded coding run, and 52-6 added the durable mailbox substrate, but the CLI still treated each user prompt as a fresh top-level run. Fix: add `src/dan/worker/organisms/coding_conversation.py` as a durable orchestrator controller on top of `DurableAgentRunner`, persist that conversation state in `.dan-code/session.json`, route ordinary turns through the orchestrator first, and send bounded coding reports back into the same orchestrator session so it can decide `done`, `continue`, or `clarify`.

- ~~**P2: the first durable universal-agent runtime draft reused session-level accepted-message counts as the per-turn mailbox index, and bounded sessions with background work could remain idle forever instead of closing once that work drained.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** The initial mailbox-backed runner shape in `src/dan/worker/runner.py` had the right high-level model, but two control-plane details were wrong: queued turns could execute with duplicate `agent_message_index` values, and `close_when_idle` / bounded sessions only closed after foreground mailbox work, not after later background-task completion. Fix: each `DurableMailboxMessage` now carries its own stable `message_index`, the effective durable policy is persisted on the session so background completion can honor it, `run_until_idle(...)` waits for background work to drain, and rejected background-task coroutines are closed cleanly.

- ~~**P2: DAN Code’s first “orchestrator narration” pass was still mostly hardcoded in the CLI, and parallel worker traces were unlabeled, so the shell still felt machine-ish during real multi-worker runs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** The earlier pass added public stage feedback, but most assistant wording still lived in `CodeProgressRenderer`, and `--show-model-trace` / tool output could interleave multiple workers with no attribution. Fix: move the assistant layer onto structured `status.update` events emitted by `execute_coding_organism(...)`, keep lifecycle/status rendering deterministic in the CLI, propagate `worker_id` through `local_runtime.py` model/tool/completion events, and render worker-scoped prefixes such as `[worker-1][model]`.

- ~~**P2: `dan code` had stage feedback, but it still did not feel like the orchestrator was actually talking to the user, and verbose tool payloads could still drown out the useful signal.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** The first feedback pass exposed organism stages, but the CLI was still rendering machine-ish stage labels while `list_directory` and `file_read` results could remain too noisy for interactive use. Fix: keep the same universal-worker coding organism, emit richer public stage payloads from `execute_coding_organism(...)`, render them through `CodeProgressRenderer` as assistant-style orchestration updates, and summarize high-volume tool results compactly.

- ~~**P2: `dan code` progress output only showed raw tool calls, so runs looked like opaque tool spam with no explanation of where the organism was in the loop.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** The coding CLI already streamed tool events, but it did not emit any public organism-stage feedback between those calls, so users saw `list_directory`, `file_read`, `shell_command`, and then a final failure/completion report with no indication of planning, aggregation, validation, or retry state. Fix: `execute_coding_organism(...)` now emits public organism/attempt/stage/repair lifecycle events, and `CodeProgressRenderer` prints human-readable status lines by default while keeping chain-of-thought hidden.

- ~~**P3: the first DAN Code local-prompt fast-path was too aggressive and started swallowing typoed greetings like `hii`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** The initial shell hardening treated fuzzy greetings as local non-task chatter, but that made `hii` return a canned message instead of following the normal coding path. Fix: narrow the fast-path back to explicit prompts only; `hi` still short-circuits, while typo variants like `hii` no longer do.

- ~~**P2: `dan code` was still sending simple session/meta questions into the coding organism, which made the REPL look broken instead of conversational.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** Before the durable conversation-layer hardening, questions like `what is the root dir now` and `ok, tell me the results?` still launched the bounded coding organism and often failed with `did not produce a validated candidate`. The first fix added a temporary local prompt layer in `src/dan/cli/code.py`; that stopped the worst failures, and the final 2026-04-10 refinement replaced that workaround with orchestrator-owned answers driven by shared facts and recent report context.

- ~~**P2: local coding/organism CLIs could crash on `--workspace .` if Python lost a usable process CWD even while the shell `PWD` still pointed at the workspace.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-09.** `src/dan/cli/code.py` and `src/dan/cli/organism.py` were normalizing relative workspace arguments through `Path(...).resolve()`, which still consults `os.getcwd()` internally. In shells where the process CWD could not be resolved, `dan code --workspace .` crashed before startup with `FileNotFoundError`. Fix: add a shared `normalize_workspace_root(...)` helper in `src/dan/cli/__init__.py` that prefers `PWD` for relative paths and update both local CLIs to use it.

- ~~**P2: one workflow-continuity regression still froze the older missing-current-workflow phrasing after the natural-language copy cleanup.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** `_apply_workflow_continuity_context(...)` now intentionally tells the user it cannot inspect the current workflow because the referenced workflow is not available in the saved workflow catalog, but `tests/test_workflow_identity.py::test_apply_workflow_continuity_context_refuses_missing_current_workflow` was still asserting the old `"no longer present"` wording. That left the broad suite failing at `99%` on a pure copy drift. Fix: align the regression with the current user-facing message contract.
- ~~**P2: workflow-identity context packs stopped surfacing required inputs after Worker-first graph validation rewrote the `workflow_inputs` node into a canonical `worker`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** `build_workflow_context_pack()` relied on `derive_workflow_interface()`, but that helper only read legacy `InputNode.variables`. Once the same graph validated as a `worker` with `metadata.input_variables`, the context pack still inferred structure but silently dropped `required_inputs`. The focused `workflow_identity` file also still stubbed `start_run(...)` even though the capability now calls `launch_run(...)`. Fix: teach workflow-interface derivation to read Worker `input_variables` metadata and align the focused start-run tests to the current `launch_run` / `WorkflowContractError` contract.
- ~~**P2: the validation registry regression still hard-coded the old built-in node-type count after the Worker-era built-in expansion.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** `tests/test_validation/test_graph_validation.py::TestNodeTypeRegistry::test_builtins_registered` still expected `18` built-ins even though `NodeTypeRegistry` now auto-registers the full `RUNTIME_NODE_TYPE_MAP`, which currently exposes `22` types including `worker`, `gate`, `validator`, and `vote`. That let the broad suite run into the high 90s before failing on pure expectation drift. Fix: assert the registry exactly matches `RUNTIME_NODE_TYPE_MAP` instead of freezing a stale integer.
- ~~**P2: the late snapshot suite was still pinned to legacy builder/loader graph shapes after canonical Worker emission became the default.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** `tests/test_snapshots/test_builder_snapshots.py` and `tests/test_snapshots/test_loader_snapshots.py` compare normalized graph JSON against local fixture files in `tests/test_snapshots/snapshots/`. Those fixtures still expected `llm_operator`-style nodes and lacked newer graph fields like `worker_resources` and edge `lint`, so the full suite could run cleanly into the 90% range and then fail on pure snapshot drift. Fix: regenerate the snapshot fixtures against the current default Worker-first graph contract and revalidate the snapshot basket.
- ~~**P2: run-compare API tests could still be shadowed by the request-owned `AppState` run manager after monkeypatching `dan.server.app._run_manager`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** `tests/test_server/test_run_api_compare.py` uses `TestClient(app)` plus an explicit `_run_manager` monkeypatch to exercise compare-route behavior. After the request-backed dependency migration, `get_run_manager(request)` always preferred `app.state.dan.run_manager`, so the route ignored the fake compare records and returned a `404` from the live state instead of the expected comparison payload. Fix: let `get_run_manager(request)` honor an explicit legacy override when it intentionally diverges from the request-owned state.
- ~~**P2: trace-draft promotion lost compatibility with zero-arg `get_graph_store` monkeypatches after the request-backed router migration.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** `routers/experiences.py` now normally resolves graph storage through `get_graph_store(request)`, but `tests/test_server/test_meta_api.py::test_promote_trace_draft_persists_run_ready_workflow` still monkeypatched `get_graph_store` as a zero-arg lambda for a direct no-request call. Once `_resolve_trace_promotion_workflow_id(...)` started always passing `request`, that focused compatibility path failed with `TypeError` before it could exercise promotion behavior. Fix: add a small compatibility resolver that accepts both the request-backed and legacy zero-arg graph-store seams.
- ~~**P2: `_build_meta_controller()` lost the old globals-backed compatibility path after the AppState cleanup and started failing focused no-request gateway/provider regressions.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** The normal runtime now builds meta-controller state through `startup.build_meta_controller_from_state(...)`, but `tests/test_server/test_llm_gateway_usage.py::test_build_meta_controller_llm_call_uses_public_chat_manager_gateway` still intentionally monkeypatches `dan.server.app` globals without a live `AppState`. Once `_build_meta_controller()` hard-required active state, that regression failed with `503` before it could verify the public `ChatManager` provider seam. Fix: keep the AppState path as the default, but restore a globals-backed fallback builder in `app.py` for explicit compatibility callers.
- ~~**P2: the fast run-handoff chat integration tests still reached for retired `dan.server.app._chat_manager` / `_run_manager` globals after the AppState cleanup.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** The request-backed chat route now resolves both services through `app.state.dan`, but `tests/test_server/test_chat_integration.py::{test_capability_run_stream_handoff_survives_fast_completion,test_run_handoff_stream_stays_open_through_automatic_recovery}` were still reading the mirrored globals surface. That made the focused handoff regressions fail immediately with `chat_manager is None` instead of exercising the actual request-owned run-stream relay path. Fix: read and monkeypatch `app.state.dan.chat_manager` / `app.state.dan.run_manager` directly in those integration tests.
- ~~**P2: one request-backed chat integration regression still patched the retired `dan.server.app._dispatcher` mirror instead of the live typed dispatcher.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** After the AppState migration, `/api/chat/message` resolves its dispatcher through request-owned `app.state.dan`, but `tests/test_server/test_chat_integration.py::test_chat_message_synthesizes_terminal_event_when_stream_ends_silently` was still monkeypatching `dan.server.app._dispatcher`. That left the real dispatcher in place during the full-suite run and made the test miss the synthetic `chat_tool_call_result` / fallback completion path it intended to isolate. Fix: patch `app.state.dan.dispatcher` directly in the request-backed integration test.
- ~~**P2: server API/full-suite teardown could stall after run-oriented tests because active `RunManager` tasks survived lifespan shutdown.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-08.** `tests/test_server/test_api.py` would advance through the run-oriented cases and then sit in `selectors.py` during teardown instead of failing an assertion. The narrowing showed `test_get_run_nonexistent` passed alone, while the `get_run` / `list_runs` basket stalled only after the tests that actually launched background runs. Fix: add an explicit async `RunManager.shutdown()` that cancels active run tasks, marks them cancelled for observability, resolves pending approval waits, and let both startup shutdown entry points call it before later integration teardown.
- ~~**P2: the research-prompt regression file drifted away from the current prompt-template and cited-source contracts.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-07.** `tests/test_server/test_research_prompt.py` still assumed `UNIFIED_SYSTEM_PROMPT` accepted separate `surface_hints` / `task_hints` placeholders and that `_extract_cited_sources()` preserved bare-domain URLs exactly as typed. The runtime had already moved research guidance into `module_hints`, and cited-source extraction now canonicalizes URLs through the shared search URL normalizer (for example `https://example.com` -> `https://example.com/`). At the same time, the lightweight research hint had lost the explicit “typically 3-8 distinct web_search queries” wording that the regression still expected. Fix: restore that explicit search guidance in `src/dan/chat_prompts.py`, update the regression to inject research guidance through `module_hints`, and assert dedupe against the canonicalized URL form.
- ~~**P2: the legacy mutation-quality suite could inherit `DAN_WORKER_BUILDER=enabled` and fail late in the unchanged full-suite gate.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-07.** `tests/test_server/test_mutation_quality.py` is a legacy-shape deterministic quality basket that asserts `llm_operator` / `code_operator` node types directly, but it did not pin the ambient Worker-builder env. Under long full-suite runs, earlier Worker-enabled tests could leak `DAN_WORKER_BUILDER=enabled`, causing the file to see `worker` node output instead of the intended legacy shapes around the `7035 passed` watermark. Fix: add an autouse fixture that deletes `DAN_WORKER_BUILDER` for that file and keep Worker-mode mutation assertions in the dedicated taxonomy coverage.
- ~~**P2: `_build_meta_controller()` could bypass the public `ChatManager` provider seam and hit the mirrored global model gateway directly.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-07.** Under long full-suite runs, `tests/test_server/test_llm_gateway_usage.py` patched `_chat_manager.resolve_llm_provider(...)` to a fake provider but `_build_meta_controller()` still preferred `_model_gateway` whenever startup had mirrored one into `app.py`. That caused a real network-bound OpenAI path to run instead of the public seam the orchestration layer is supposed to honor. Fix: make `_build_meta_controller()` use the public chat-manager provider seam whenever `_chat_manager` exists, and reserve `_model_gateway` for the no-chat-manager fallback path.
- ~~**P2: the legacy graph-mutator test file could inherit `DAN_WORKER_BUILDER=enabled` from earlier tests and fail nondeterministically deep into the unchanged full-suite gate.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-07.** `tests/test_server/test_graph_mutator.py` asserts the legacy add-node shapes (`llm_operator`, `tool_operator`, `code_operator`), but the repo now also has separate Worker-enabled mutator coverage. Under long full-suite runs, an earlier test could leave `DAN_WORKER_BUILDER=enabled` set, and the legacy file would then see Worker output unexpectedly around the 85% mark. Fix: pin the legacy mutator file back to the default disabled mode with an autouse env fixture and keep the Worker-enabled assertions in `tests/test_graph_mutator_taxonomy.py`.
- ~~**P2: distilled trace-draft workflows stopped compiling/promoting once the Worker-first builder path became active.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-07.** The trace-to-workflow draft path still described `file_write` stages via `generalized_args`, but the deterministic compiler only seeded `tool_config` from explicit `tool_config`, and the Worker-first builder path still chained `a >> b` into a generic `input` port even when the only declared target port was `content`. Under lifespan startup that combination made `/api/experiences/trace-draft` and `promote_trace_draft(...)` regress back to `draft_only` / `promotion_blocked` with `content` reported as disconnected. Fix: seed distilled tool config from `generalized_args`, emit an explicit `content` input port for distilled `file_write` stages that need upstream content, skip the synthetic prepare-output stage when the audited write call already included explicit content, preserve single explicit input/output ports as the default `NodeRef` chain targets across builder constructors, and restore direct no-request `promote_trace_draft(...)` compatibility for focused router tests.
- ~~**P2: request-backed dependency helpers could still bypass typed `AppState` by consulting mirrored `dan.server.app` globals first.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-07.** The earlier migration left `routers/dependencies.py` honoring globals-surface divergence even when an actual `Request`/`WebSocket` already carried the correct `app.state.dan` services. That kept HTTP execution vulnerable to stale mirrored state and made the typed path less honest than intended. Fix: make request-backed dependency helpers prefer typed `AppState` outright, keep globals only for no-request compatibility callers, and add direct regressions in `tests/test_server/test_router_dependencies.py` plus request-state health coverage.
- ~~**P2: stale `RunManager` tests still assumed empty graphs could launch successfully and still reached `_emit_workflow_telemetry()` after the `RunFinalizer` split.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-07.** `tests/test_server/test_run_manager.py` still treated empty graphs as valid `start_run()` success fixtures even though run-readiness is now enforced, and `tests/test_server/test_run_manager_telemetry.py` still called a helper that moved into `RunFinalizer`. Fix: use a minimal runnable workflow for the success-path run-manager tests and restore a thin `RunManager._emit_workflow_telemetry()` compatibility wrapper that delegates to the finalizer.
- ~~**P2: `ProjectStore()` still defaulted to `~/.dan/projects` under pytest, which fails inside the workspace sandbox.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-07.** `tests/test_engine/test_consolidation.py` constructs a real `Concierge(ProjectStore(), ...)`, and the default constructor tried to create `/Users/.../.dan/projects` even when no explicit `DAN_PROJECT_STORE_DIR` was set. Under the sandbox that path is not writable, so the unchanged-suite gate failed for environmental reasons rather than product logic. Fix: keep `DAN_PROJECT_STORE_DIR` as the normal override, but default to `/tmp/dan-project-store` when `PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST` is set.
- ~~**P2: builder alias projection stopped honoring monkeypatched `worker_to_legacy` after the helper extraction.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-07.** The builder alias helper initially bound `worker_to_legacy` at import time, so `tests/test_worker/test_builder.py` could no longer intercept the projection path with a monkeypatch. Fix: resolve `worker_presets.worker_to_legacy(...)` at call time inside `src/dan/builder/_aliases.py`, which keeps the extraction behaviorally transparent.
- ~~**P2: direct imports of `dan.server.routers.chat.chat_message(...)` no longer matched the older test call shape after the request-backed AppState migration.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-06.** The main chat route now took `(request, req, ...)`, but several focused router tests still called it directly as `chat_message(req, ...)` without a live `Request`, and those same tests did not initialize a real `RunManager`. Fix: accept both the FastAPI and direct-call shapes in `src/dan/server/routers/chat.py`, and lazy-resolve `RunManager` only for the specific branches that actually need it.
- ~~**P2: `tests/test_intent_compiler.py` still hard-coded the pre-Worker fan-out code-body authoring shape.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-06.** Under Worker-enabled authoring rollout, `IntentCompiler._compile_fan_out(...)` intentionally emits `body.worker(...)` instead of `body.code(...)` for `body_code` stages, but the test still asserted only the legacy `body.code(...)` string. Fix: make the regression explicit about the rollout-sensitive contract by accepting either form while still checking the emitted body code text.
- ~~**P2: the typed AppState migration broke tests that still monkeypatch `dan.server.app.*` globals.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-06.** Request-backed router paths now prefer `request.app.state.dan`, but several server tests still intentionally monkeypatch mirrored globals like `_dispatcher` or `_run_manager`. That made the new typed path ignore explicit compatibility fixtures even though the globals surface still exists. Fix: keep `AppState` as the default path, but teach `src/dan/server/routers/dependencies.py` to honor explicit globals-surface overrides when they intentionally diverge from the mirrored state object during the migration window.
- ~~**P2: chat `/run` integration tests still assumed empty workflows could execute.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-06.** The chat `/run` path now shares the same run-readiness guard as direct `/api/runs`, but `tests/test_server/test_chat_integration.py` still expected empty-graph `/run` to return `run_started` and a stream channel. Fix: align those tests to the current `run_error` / `not_run_ready` contract and move the stream-channel assertion onto a runnable graph fixture.
- ~~**P2: the concierge runtime context-resolution extraction existed on disk but `runtime/__init__.py` still kept the full inline implementation, leaving the god-module watchpoint failing.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-06.** `src/dan/server/concierge/runtime/context_resolution.py` already held the extracted context-resolution helpers, but `src/dan/server/concierge/runtime/__init__.py` had never switched over to them, so the file stayed above the module-boundary ceiling and the extraction had no structural effect. Fix: remove the inline context-resolution block from `runtime/__init__.py`, bind the extracted helpers back onto `Concierge`, and keep the runtime surface unchanged while the owner shrinks below the watchpoint again.
- ~~**P2: `tests/test_server/test_api.py` still assumed empty workflows could start runs successfully.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-06.** The run surfaces now consistently enforce `ensure_workflow_run_ready(...)`, and newer workflow-identity/runtime coverage already expected empty graphs to fail with a run-readiness error. The older API integration file was the stale outlier: it still posted an empty graph to `/api/runs` and expected `200`, and its success-path list/get tests also started runs from empty graphs. Fix: update the empty-graph case to expect `422 not run-ready`, and seed a minimal runnable graph for the success-path run/get/list checks.
- ~~**P1: the remaining 46-1 full-suite gate still had Worker-first contract gaps and builder-mode test drift.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-06 (ongoing suite burn-down).** Several repo-wide failures were not Worker model defects but post-rollout compatibility drift: `agent_runtime/mutation_preview.py` imported a timing helper from `dan.server.*`; legacy builder/decompiler tests were silently depending on ambient `DAN_WORKER_BUILDER` state instead of pinning legacy aliases explicitly; run-readiness validation still only recognized legacy `code_operator` / `tool_operator` / tool-less `llm_operator` cases and could miss the same failures on Worker-native graphs; and tests still reached `RunManager._persist_reflection_principles()` / `_track_rule_effectiveness()` after those helpers moved into `RunFinalizer`. Fix: move the timing helper to top-level `dan.workflow_latency`, harden the legacy tests with explicit `canonical_workers=False`, extend workflow-contract run-readiness checks to Worker-native code/tool/LLM shapes, and add thin RunManager compatibility wrappers that delegate to `RunFinalizer`.
- ~~**P2: default executor wiring still pulled `openai` at import time even for non-LLM runs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-06.** After the earlier WorkerExecutor portability fix, the broader default runtime path still eagerly imported `openai` through `executors/llm.py`, `worker/adapters.py`, `register_default_executors()`, and `RunManager._make_executor_registry()`. That meant code-only or tool-only startup/registry paths were still less portable than they looked. Fix: make the `openai` dependency lazy in `LLMExecutor`, defer `LLMExecutor` creation inside `WorkerExecutor` until an actual LLM-mode dispatch occurs, and keep the default worker bundle lazy so registry/run-manager bootstrap works with `openai` blocked.
- ~~**P2: split-module annotation globals broke `typing.get_type_hints(...)` on key runtime APIs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-06.** The 50/46 consolidation moved type owners out of `chat_manager.py`, `run_manager.py`, `run_finalization.py`, `startup.py`, `concierge/runtime/__init__.py`, and `concierge/tiered_dispatch.py`, but those modules no longer bound `WorkflowIntent`, `AgentRuntime`/`AgentRequest`, `RerunScope`, `RunRecord`, `FastAPI`, `Path`, `ConciergeLearningBundle`, `MemoryServices`, or `SurfaceMessage` correctly in module globals, and a few annotations still used quoted inner forward refs that became invalid under `get_type_hints`. The runtime still executed because annotations were postponed, but any reflection path that resolved type hints raised `NameError` or `TypeError`. Fix: restore the missing globals, remove the invalid quoted inner refs, and add regression coverage for `typing.get_type_hints(...)` on the split APIs.
- ~~**P1: workflow catalog questions and restart-time follow-ups could surface internal continuity text instead of a natural-language answer.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-05.** Workflow-query turns all flowed through the same current-workflow continuity resolver in `tier_executors.py`, so prompts like `review existing workflows` could still be blocked by a stale `_scratch` pointer after restart, and genuine missing-workflow follow-ups returned the raw message `The current workflow reference points to ...` as the assistant's final reply. Fix: workflow catalog/inventory questions now skip current-workflow continuity resolution and stay on the ordinary answer/tool path, while true missing-current-workflow follow-ups are converted into a user-facing natural-language explanation.
- ~~**P1: read-only workflow review turns could still be background-dispatched away from the active chat response.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-05.** After the earlier `workflow_query` routing fixes, dispatch-mode selection in `runtime/__init__.py` still treated any `target="workflow"` turn as backgroundable unless it was an explicit `workflow_edit` confirmation path. That meant prompts like `review existing workflows` or `what does this workflow do?` could emit only `task_ack`, close the intake stream, and fall through to the generic "response stream ended before a final answer was produced" fallback instead of answering inline. Fix: workflow-query-only turns now stay `foreground`, including read-only combinations like `workflow_query + search_web`, while actual workflow runs/edits keep their existing background/foreground rules.
- ~~**P2: workflow-understanding turns could still drift back into workflow-edit behavior, and stale saved metadata could misdescribe the current workflow.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Even after the earlier `workflow_query` staging fix, follow-up heuristics in `tier_executors.py` could still reinterpret a message like `what does this workflow do?` as `workflow_edit` simply because the thread had recent workflow activity, which re-exposed mutation tools on a read-only query turn. Separately, the workflow context pack only carried workflow identity/revision/required-input metadata, so stale saved names like `code_gen_validation` could dominate the prompt even when the node structure clearly described a watchlist/equity report workflow. Fix: `triage_scenarios.py` now has an explicit `workflow_query_purpose` lexical scenario, `tier_executors.py` now prefers `workflow_query` over `workflow_edit` for workflow-understanding follow-ups and keeps query-only workflow turns read-only even with extra non-mutating hints such as `search_web`, and `workflow_identity.py` now injects a `structure_summary` with key steps, inferred purpose, and a metadata-consistency signal.
- ~~**P2: output-persistence phrasing still depended too much on LLM triage, so write-required turns could route inconsistently.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Prompts like `save the outline to disk`, `persist documentation`, or `export the summary to a file` did not hit a deterministic lexical/fallback write path, which left those turns to the LLM classifier and made `write_file` exposure less reliable than it should have been. Fix: `triage.py` now treats persistence/destination cues as write-output evidence, and `triage_scenarios.py` adds explicit file-write lexical coverage for those phrases.
- ~~**P2: `get_config` could still crash after restart because it called a stale MCP bridge method.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** The runtime `MCPBridge` exposes `list_servers()`, but `src/dan/server/capabilities/config.py` was still calling `get_connected_servers()`. After restart, any `get_config` tool call could therefore fail with `Tool error: 'MCPBridge' object has no attribute 'get_connected_servers'` even though MCP startup itself was fine. Fix: `get_config` now reads the current `list_servers()` API, tolerates the legacy/raw bridge shape for compatibility, and falls back to an empty connected-server list instead of crashing when MCP is unavailable or partially initialized. `MCPBridge` also now carries a compatibility `get_connected_servers()` alias for older callers.
- ~~**P1: required-action turns could still be built in a mode that did not actually expose the needed tool family.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** This was broader than `file_write`. `required_action_hints` already told the prompt/retry layer that a turn still needed `write_file`, `workflow_edit`, `workflow_run`, or another gated action, but `ChatManager.send_message_with_tools()` was still building the initial `tools=[...]` payload from the original chat mode. That meant a turn could truthfully say a write/run/edit tool was required while the callable tool was absent from the request. Fix: `chat_manager.py` now resolves an effective execution mode from the action-hint families before prompt construction, capability-registry lookup, mutation-tool exposure, and fallback/retry turns, so the live tool list stays aligned with the action contract.
- ~~**P1: structurally valid but non-run-ready workflows could still be saved, run, or scheduled outside chat auto-apply.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** The strongest workflow contract already existed in `workflow_contract.py`, but manual mutation apply, capability mutation apply, direct `/api/runs`, chat `/run`, schedule creation, and scheduled fire-time execution were still relying on weaker structural validation or no preflight at all. That meant empty or otherwise non-run-ready graphs could be persisted or scheduled depending on entry path. Fix: added shared `workflow_guards.py` and routed all apply/run/schedule surfaces through `ensure_workflow_apply_ready()` or `ensure_workflow_run_ready()`, so the same contract and repair layer now gates save/apply, direct run, schedule creation, and scheduled execution.
- ~~**P2: provider readiness and runtime could disagree about strict-prefix model routing.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** `runtime_config.provider_readiness_summary()` used `resolve_name()` while runtime execution could still fall back through `resolve()`, so `/health` could claim a `claude-*` or `gemini-*` model was invalid even while runtime would serve it through the default OpenAI-compatible provider. Fix: `ProviderRegistry.resolve_runtime_name()` is now shared by runtime and readiness, explicit provider overrides still fail closed, and strict-prefix compatibility fallback is surfaced as a degraded readiness issue instead of a silent mismatch. `build_provider_registry()` also lazy-loads `OpenAIProvider`, which removes the eager import-time coupling that previously made optional-provider setup look stricter than it really was.
- ~~**P1: editor chat persistence used one global pending slot, so delayed saves from one thread could overwrite another thread's pending snapshot.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** `threadPersistenceCoordinator.ts` kept one process-global `pendingPersist` and `persistTimer`, so scheduling a delayed persist for thread B could replace the pending snapshot for thread A before either timer fired. Because server persistence writes the full message list, that was a real cross-thread correctness race during streaming/reconnect-heavy chat sessions. Fix: the coordinator now keys pending snapshots and timers per `workflowId::threadId`, flushes each key independently, and has focused regressions covering both cross-thread isolation and cross-thread flush behavior.
- ~~**P2: same-project queue supersession was too eager on short replies.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** `ConcurrentDispatcher` treated bare `yes`, `no`, digit-only replies, and a few short command-like turns as queue-superseding signals, which could drop queued intent more aggressively than users would expect. Fix: supersession is now limited to explicit queue-control or rewrite cues like `cancel`, `stop`, `/retry`, `actually`, `instead`, and similar phrasing; short acknowledgement replies no longer cancel queued same-project work by implication.
- ~~**P1: packaged Electron workflow runs could still fall back to `/checkpoints` and fail with `Read-only file system` even after the server-side cwd hardening landed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Repro from DAN Desktop: start a packaged app session without an explicit `DAN_WORKSPACE_ROOT`, then run a workflow that checkpoints. Electron only seeded `DAN_GRAPHS_DIR`, so the backend child could inherit `/` or another read-only cwd; runtime defaults like `./checkpoints` then resolved to `/checkpoints` and the run failed mid-execution. Fix: `editor/electron/backendLaunch.ts` now normalizes the backend launch env, defaults `DAN_WORKSPACE_ROOT` to the parent of the persistent graphs dir when unset, and `editor/electron/main.ts` now spawns the backend with that workspace root as `cwd`.
- ~~**P2: workflow follow-up scheduling could reject the current workflow when only the sole project-linked ID was available on that path.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Repro while landing plan 48: fast-command `/schedule workflow current ...` and mixed turns like `delete the obsolete workflows. schedule it daily at 8am` could resolve `wf-current` from project context, then fail with `The current workflow reference points to 'wf-current', but that saved workflow is no longer present.` because the schedule/follow-up path could not hydrate graph metadata from the lightweight test/runtime path. Fix: the scheduler and workflow-lane continuity gate now accept the sole linked workflow ID when it matches the current-workflow hint, while still refusing ambiguous or mismatched references.
- ~~**P1: Telegram private DMs were too eager to fork parallel lanes, and queued redirects stayed silent until the first timeout hint.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Repro from Telegram-side review: if a 1:1 Telegram turn was already in flight, a normal follow-up message that was not an explicit reply would fork into a fresh lane immediately, which could make ordinary DM continuity feel broken and produce delayed bursty replies once several overlapping turns completed. Separately, when `/api/chat/message` responded with `chat_queued`, the fleet followed the redirected `stream_channel_id` but did not surface any immediate queue acknowledgement, so Telegram could look blocked for up to the first queue-hint timeout. Fix: private chats now stay on the shared conversation lane unless the user explicitly replies into a different lane, while groups/forum topics still keep the reply-aware parallel-lane behavior; and queued redirects now emit an immediate `Queued (position N) — I'll reply when ready.` progress bubble that later edits into the final answer.
- ~~**P1: Telegram could show progress and then go silent if the fleet's relay stream closed before any terminal answer, and transient per-conversation workflow bootstrap failures could get cached into that lane.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Repro from Telegram-side review: the fleet could emit or edit a progress bubble such as `Understanding your request`, then receive a clean WebSocket close before any terminal `chat_complete` / `chat_error`, and `_stream_with_edits()` / `_stream_collect()` would return an empty string instead of surfacing a final fallback. Separately, `_ensure_scratch()` cached the derived `_fleet_*` workflow id even if the backing graph create/check failed, so a transient backend problem could poison that conversation until the process restarted. Fix: Telegram streaming paths now synthesize the same "Please ask me to continue from the latest progress" fallback when no non-progress terminal event arrives, and the fleet only caches the per-conversation workflow id after the graph exists or has been created successfully.
- ~~**P1: concierge scope resolution was too eager and could create or hijack project state for the wrong turns.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Follow-up review of the concierge flow found that `_resolve_context()` both chose ownership and wrote project/task state before fast/social handling or triage had decided whether the turn should even belong to a project. That let dispatcher-handled social chatter create bogus projects, let unrelated factual asks attach themselves to the latest active project, duplicated the intake progress ack, and left turn telemetry without reliably populated project/task/intent fields. Fix: the runtime now uses side-effect-free context peeking for queueing/triage/prep, delays project materialization until the turn is actually project-bearing, keeps social and plain ask/general turns ephemeral by default, removes the duplicate intake ack, fails broken registry chat commands closed, and restores telemetry binding from the real materialized context.
- ~~**P2: direct `ProviderRegistry.resolve(...)` fallback got too broad and could ignore explicit missing model overrides.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Follow-up review of the 46/47 merge batch found that the direct-`resolve(...)` compatibility patch for single-key strict-prefix models was catching every `KeyError`, which meant an exact model override pinned to a missing provider could silently route to `default`. Fix: `src/dan/providers/registry.py` now preserves the highest-priority override failure contract while still allowing strict-prefix models like `claude-*` to fall back only when no explicit override is involved and a `default` provider is actually registered.
- ~~**P2: low-confidence concierge clarification could leave half-persisted pending state, and queued same-project follow-ups could run before the clarification reply.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Follow-up review of the concierge triage patches found two ordering bugs: `TieredDispatcher._maybe_request_low_confidence_clarification()` mutated `project.pending_action` before `project_store.set_pending_action(...)` succeeded, so a persistence failure could return a clarification that the next turn could not resolve from the durable store; and `ConcurrentDispatcher._drain_project_queue()` kept draining same-project queued turns even when a project-scoped clarification pending action had just been created. Fix: clarification pauses now activate only after the pending action is durably stored, persistence failures fall through to normal execution, and same-project queue drain now pauses until the pending action is cleared.
- ~~**P2: the last explicit 46 full-suite gate kept tripping over repo-wide test harness drift instead of Worker/linter behavior.**~~ **FIXED IN PROGRESS 2026-04-03.** While pushing `46-1 / 12-5`, the unchanged `pytest -q -x` gate successively exposed several unrelated blockers: a prep-timeout test that depended on non-deterministic triage heuristics, an in-process `sentence_transformers` import that could abort the interpreter on the local Torch stack, recursive default-provider fallback in `llm_surface`, an engine→concierge boundary import inside `PreferenceExtractor`, stale notification and parameter-decision test expectations, boundary/watchpoint drift, OpenAI-provider import-order leakage in router backward-compat tests, stale build-from-intent mutation-id/schema expectations, stale parser/introspection tests that still assumed pre-typed mutation-id or node-serialization behavior, and stale chat integration/prompt tests that still assumed fixed module-level stream globals or literal mutation-tool wording in generic empty-workflow prompts. Fixes landed for each blocker, and the gate has progressed past `6632 passed` before the latest stale suite drift. Remaining blockers there should be treated as repo-wide validation cleanup, not missing 46/47 foundation behavior.
- ~~**P2: direct `ProviderRegistry.resolve(...)` calls lost the old single-key fallback contract for strict-prefix models.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro from the last explicit 46 full-suite gate: `tests/test_providers/test_integration.py::test_single_api_key_backward_compat` failed because `ProviderRegistry.resolve("claude-sonnet-4-6")` raised when only a `default` provider was registered, even though the rest of the runtime had already learned to fall back through `llm_surface`. Fix: `src/dan/providers/registry.py` now returns the `default` provider for strict-prefix models when no matching named provider is registered, preserving mixed-model strictness when explicit providers exist while restoring the historical single-key behavior.
- ~~**P3: importing `WorkerExecutor` hard-pulled optional LLM/OpenAI dependencies even for code/tool-only paths.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro from the second-pass 46/47 review: importing or constructing `WorkerExecutor` during focused runtime tests dragged in `dan.executors.llm`, which eagerly imports `openai`, so otherwise isolated code/tool Worker paths were less portable and harder to test cleanly. Fix: `src/dan/worker/executor.py` now lazy-loads `LLMExecutor` only when no explicit `llm_executor` is injected, and `tests/test_worker/test_executor.py` now has an isolated subprocess regression that blocks `openai` imports while verifying the injected-executor path still works.
- ~~**P2: `/analytics` without a telemetry store now emitted intermediate progress events, but the old concierge fast-command test still expected a single terminal event.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro while narrowing the `46-1 / 12-5` unchanged-suite gate: `PYTHONPATH=src pytest -q -x` previously stopped at `tests/test_concierge/test_fast_commands.py::TestAnalyticsCommand::test_analytics_no_telemetry_store` because the analytics command now goes through the newer guarded/progress-aware chat path and emitted multiple events. Fix: `src/dan/server/concierge/runtime/__init__.py` now has an explicit `handle_analytics_command()` compatibility path that returns a single terminal no-store response for `/analytics` while keeping the richer grouped/session analytics behavior elsewhere.
- ~~**P1: bridged legacy Workers could inherit a stale execution mode after object-id reuse.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro while pushing the unchanged-suite gate: run the transient-tool self-healing test and then the legacy code linear-chain integration in the same pytest-managed process. `LegacyWorkerAdapterExecutor` creates temporary Worker objects for legacy nodes, but `WorkerExecutor` cached mode/config/template state by raw `id(node)`, so later temporary Workers could reuse a stale `PASSTHROUGH` or tool/LLM template after object-id reuse. Fix: `src/dan/worker/executor.py` now keys those caches by stable Worker fingerprints instead of raw object ids, and `tests/test_engine/test_runtime_robustness.py` now has a direct regression for the sequence.
- ~~**P2: scheduler runtime lint autogen was surprising plain legacy graphs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro while pushing the unchanged-suite gate: legacy loop/checkpoint tests in `tests/test_engine/test_scheduler_parallelism.py` started failing with structural lint on integer handoffs because `_resolve_edge_lint_config()` generated lint for every unconfigured `DataEdge`, not only the Worker-centric path the 47 rollout docs described. Fix: runtime fallback autogen now only runs when either the source or target node is a Worker; explicit edge lint still works everywhere, and compile-time autogen remains opt-in via the builder/compiler.
- ~~**P2: explicit engine LLM caps could be silently weakened by ambient `DAN_MAX_CONCURRENT_LLM`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro while pushing the unchanged-suite gate: `tests/test_engine/test_scheduler_parallelism.py::test_parallel_subgraphs_share_llm_semaphore` could exceed `EngineConfig(llm_max_concurrency=2)` when an env override leaked or was intentionally present, because `Engine._llm_max_concurrency()` preferred the env var over the explicit config. Fix: explicit config now wins; env only acts as fallback when the engine config leaves the LLM cap unset.
- ~~**P2: manually assigned custom gateways could be overwritten by the scheduler’s rebuild-on-registry logic.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro while pushing the unchanged-suite gate: `tests/test_engine/test_worker_lint_integration.py::test_worker_lint_retry_feedback_includes_missing_requirements_and_output_preview` sets `engine.model_gateway = _Gateway()` after engine construction, but `_current_model_gateway()` rebuilt from the provider registry and discarded that explicit gateway, so Tier 3 intent retry never fired. Fix: `_current_model_gateway()` now preserves manually assigned gateways that do not expose a `registry` attribute while still rebuilding stale registry-backed gateways after provider swaps.
- ~~**P2: `UserProfile.common_domains` briefly regressed to preserving raw labels instead of canonical ids.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro while pushing the unchanged-suite gate: `tests/test_engine/test_user_profile.py::TestMergePreferences::test_merge_domains_deduplicates` started storing labels like `scientific writing` instead of canonical ids such as `paper_rendering`. Fix: `src/dan/engine/user_profile.py` now normalizes profile domain storage back through `normalize_domain_list(...)`, and the stale test expectation for `supply_chain` was corrected to the canonical taxonomy id `supply_chain_management`.
- ~~**P1: concierge triage could still over-route on common words and execute low-confidence fallback guesses without clarification.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro from the concierge review follow-up: broad lexical scenarios let words like `latest` / `current` or generic `report` phrasing short-circuit directly to `web` / `file`, confident LLM file routes could still be overridden by workflow-history heuristics, and fallback triage at ~0.5 confidence still executed immediately instead of asking the user what they meant. Fix: lexical web/file scenarios now require stronger local/web evidence, triage context now includes the current task plus recent assistant snippets for pronoun-heavy turns, workflow edit/run post-processing now respects confident LLM routes, and low-confidence triage now creates a project-scoped clarification pending action that re-triages the clarified reply before execution.
- ~~**P1: frontend `build` / `build:verify` could fail even while the new ConfigPanel Vitest suite passed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro from the frontend review follow-up: `editor/src/components/__tests__/ConfigPanel.test.ts` cast Worker/data-edge fixtures as the broad `DanNode` / `DanEdge` unions and then accessed Worker-only or data-edge-only fields, so Vitest stayed green but `tsc -b` failed inside `npm run build` / `npm run build:verify`. Fix: the tests now use `WorkerNodeType` / `DataEdge`, ConfigPanel narrows edge lint through the `data` edge branch instead of loose record casts, and build verification is green again.
- ~~**P1: Worker `body_graph` fallback could create a self-referential output payload.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro from the 46/47 review pass: if a Worker with a single output port ran a `body_graph` that returned a dict without that output-port key, `WorkerExecutor._run_body_graph()` aliased the output by assigning the entire `outputs` dict back into itself, producing recursive payloads that are unsafe for JSON serialization and telemetry. Fix: alias the single-port aggregate to a shallow copy of the subgraph outputs instead of the live dict.
- ~~**P1: Worker delegation path skipped `task_tier_cap` governance.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro from the 46/47 review pass: a delegating Worker with `llm_hints.task_tier="critical"` and `authority_policy.task_tier_cap="routine"` could still execute `sub_workers` successfully because `WorkerExecutor._run_sub_workers()` never enforced the tier cap that plain LLM mode already enforced. Fix: validate the tier cap before any sub-worker delegation or spawn.
- ~~**P2: disabled edge lint still emitted `lint_passed` telemetry and reported Tier 1.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-03.** Repro from the 46/47 review pass: `LintConfig(enabled=False)` returned a no-op `LintResult`, but the scheduler still ran the lint path, inferred `tier_reached=1`, and emitted `lint_passed`, which polluted telemetry and added avoidable scheduler work. Fix: the scheduler now skips disabled lint configs entirely and `_lint_tier_reached()` now respects the linter's own `tier_reached` signal plus the disabled case.

## Open Bugs
- **P3: plain worktree test runs can silently import DAN from another checkout unless `PYTHONPATH` is pinned.** Repro from 2026-04-03 while validating the consolidated `plan-46-47-consolidated` worktree: running bare `pytest` inside `/private/tmp/deep-agent-network-46-47` imported `dan` from `/Volumes/data/Dropbox/Projects/deep-agent-network/src`, which made parity failures look inconsistent and could falsely validate the wrong checkout. Current workaround: run worktree verification with `PYTHONPATH=/private/tmp/deep-agent-network-46-47/src:/private/tmp/deep-agent-network-46-47 ...` (or equivalent for the active worktree) until the local editable-install / shell path story is cleaned up.
- **P3: provider-backed evals currently warn if `DAN_TIER_MAP` in the environment is not valid JSON.** Repro from 2026-04-03 while running `tests/eval/worker_lint_benchmark.py --env-file /Volumes/data/Dropbox/Projects/deep-agent-network/.env --live-provider auto`: the benchmark still completed, but startup printed `Failed to parse DAN_TIER_MAP JSON, using defaults`. Current workaround: fix or unset the malformed `DAN_TIER_MAP` in the active env file before benchmark-grade runs if you want clean logs.
- **P1: Research-mode shortcuts can keep firing after the user switches to another mode.** Repro from the 2026-04-02 frontend review: open Research mode once, switch back to another mode, then use Research-owned shortcuts such as `Cmd+E`, `Cmd+I`, or `Cmd+\``. Because `AppShell` keeps visited modes mounted and `useResearchShortcuts()` registers a window-level listener without checking `activeMode`, hidden Research mode can still mutate Research UI state and shared code-terminal state while the user is no longer in that layout. Current workaround: avoid Research shortcuts outside Research mode; fix is tracked under `docs/UI-plans/2-1-mode-shell-isolation.md`.
- **P2: Development mode browser preview still presents a mostly complete IDE shell even though core capabilities are desktop-only.** Repro from the 2026-04-02 frontend review: open Development mode in browser preview. The banner says folders/git/LSP/terminal require the desktop app, but the UI still exposes most of the IDE chrome, while Electron bridge fallbacks return `null`, `false`, empty strings, or `Not in Electron`. This makes browser preview feel broken rather than intentionally scoped. Current workaround: treat browser Development mode as a visual demo only; fix is tracked under `docs/UI-plans/2-3-development-mode-honesty.md`.
- **P1: the messaging app/frontend can still stall after roughly 20-30 minutes of active use.** Repro as reported on 2026-03-31: use the messaging surface continuously for about 20-30 minutes and the session can become disrupted/stuck instead of continuing to send/receive progress normally. Need to determine whether the wedge is caused by frontend store/state drift, websocket or heartbeat reconnect ownership, adapter health/status skew, or backend dispatcher/channel state. Current workaround: manually reconnect/reopen the messaging surface or restart the affected session. Priority is to add end-to-end instrumentation first so the freeze can be attributed cleanly.
- **P2: builder roundtrip still drops loader-authored retry policy on some markdown-compiled graphs.** Repro on 2026-03-30 while implementing 33-5 task 26: `compile_workflow(tests/fixtures/markdown/simple_workflow.md)` or `complex_workflow.md` → `dan.builder.decompiler.decompile()` → `exec(...)` → `Graph` preserves most structure, but nodes such as `planner` / `reviewer` lose the original `retry_policy` field in the rebuilt graph. This makes loader-produced markdown fixtures fail strict builder-side structural-equivalence checks even though the graph still parses and may remain run-ready. Current workaround: the Phase 33 builder roundtrip suite uses stable JSON corpus graphs plus a hand-authored conditional builder graph for green structural/run-ready coverage, while loader markdown roundtrip stays covered separately in `tests/test_loader/test_decompiler.py`. Suspected area: builder decompiler/default elision around retry policy preservation.
- ~~**P2: full-screen DAN chat history could look empty after switching workflows even though the old thread files still existed on disk.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-01.** Repro: create or continue chats under `_scratch` or another workflow, then switch the active workflow (or use Save As to promote `_scratch` into a named workflow) and open full-screen Chat. The history sidebar only listed `/api/chats/{workflow_id}` for the currently loaded workflow and search also passed the current `workflow_id`, so older conversations from other workflows disappeared from view even though `graphs/chats/<workflow_id>/*.json` still existed. Fix: add `GET /api/chats` backed by `ChatStore.list_all_threads()`, make full-screen `ChatPanel` use the global thread list/search, show workflow labels in cross-workflow history rows, and route thread actions through explicit `(workflow_id, thread_id)` targets so selecting/pinning/exporting/deleting the right thread no longer depends on the current workflow.
- ~~**P2: durable workflow management was confusing because `_scratch` remained the effective saved workflow ID even after the workflow had a meaningful name.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-31.** Repro: users could build a real workflow, see the human-readable name in metadata, but still find the actual persisted graph under `_scratch`, which made inventory, switching, and deletion hard to reason about once many drafts accumulated. Fix: `_scratch` now remains only the draft slot; the backend exposes `POST /api/graphs/{graph_id}/save-as` plus stable name-based `fork_workflow` IDs, and the editor exposes `Save As` in `GraphSwitcher` so the current graph can be promoted into a durable named workflow ID and the active tab switches to it.
- ~~**P2: delete-like workflow follow-ups could still be steered into `plan_graph_mutations` and propose `remove_node` instead of deleting the saved workflow.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-31.** Repro: a user could ask to remove an obsolete saved workflow like `dan-equity-build-clean-20260327b`, but the workflow-action hinting still treated the turn as generic `workflow_edit`, so the tool-choice layer preferred `plan_graph_mutations` and the dry run failed with `Node 'dan-equity-build-clean-20260327b' not found`. Fix: `src/dan/server/chat/helpers.py` now recognizes delete/remove-the-workflow phrasing and prefers `delete_graph` while still keeping remove-node/remove-edge requests on the mutation path, and `src/dan/server/chat_manager.py` now forces `delete_graph` when that tool is available for the turn. Coverage: `tests/test_post_tool_followup_recovery.py`.

- ~~**P1: mixed workflow follow-up turns with both scheduling and workflow edits could still miss the scheduler path.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-30.** Repro: a prompt like `"Please do delete obsolete workflows. set up automated daily execution, increase news search depth"` would skip the pure schedule bridge because it was multi-action, then fall back to normal chat tools even though scheduling is not exposed there as a callable capability. Fix: runtime now extracts and executes an embedded schedule clause first, records the scheduler result in routing context, and forwards the remaining delete/edit text through the normal workflow tool path.
- ~~**P1: workflow follow-ups like "run it" / "schedule it daily at 8am" could truthfully lack `start_run` in the current tool surface, then receive contradictory retry guidance or drift into Furnace/shell/cron advice.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-30.** Repro: continue a workflow-authoring chat in a read-only turn, ask to run or schedule the current workflow, and observe the model either claim it lacks `start_run`, receive retry text that still says to use `start_run` / `http_request`, or answer with shell/cron instructions even though DAN already has native workflow-run and scheduler surfaces. Fix: retry prompts are now built from the actual tools exposed for the turn, ordinary workflow execution is separated from Furnace `run_control` earlier in triage, and clear scheduling follow-ups for the current workflow now bridge into `/schedule workflow current ...`.
- ~~**P1: chat-authored equity workflow previews could still fail dry-run on stale `http_request.response` edges even though the backend already knew the canonical tool ports.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-30.** Repro: continue the saved `_scratch/e941d8cebb76` equity-build thread against the fresh backend and inspect the latest preview in `graphs/chats/_scratch/e941d8cebb76.meta.json`; the generated `replace_body_graph` plan wired `fetch_stock_overview.response`, `fetch_analyst_forecast.response`, `fetch_statistics.response`, and `fetch_financials.response` into downstream nodes, but `http_request` only exposes `status_code`, `headers`, `body`, and `result`, so dry-run stalled in repair instead of deterministically normalizing the alias. Fix: mutation-preview normalization now rewrites known stale tool output aliases such as `http_request.response -> body` before dry-run, including inside body-graph operations.
- ~~**P1: the same exact empty-graph equity build prompt is still highly variable at the intent-extraction / intent-compiler boundary.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-30.** Repro on 2026-03-27: on a clean `build`-mode replay of the user's exact first equity-workflow turn (`"That's actually great. Following the example of RKLB..."`), some runs logged `tool_call_present=False, parsed=False` and fell into the 120-second codegen-timeout path, while a later clean rerun logged `tool_call_present=True, parsed=True`, `fully_covered=True`, and saved `dan-equity-build-clean-20260327b` via `intent_compiler` in 115.2 seconds. Fix: empty-graph intent extraction now prefers exact or required `emit_workflow_intent` tool choice when the provider supports it, and retries once when a tool call is present but the payload still fails to parse into a valid `WorkflowIntent`. Coverage: `tests/test_chat_manager_codegen_resilience.py`.
- ~~**P1: empty-graph codegen timeout fallback can now recover to a dry-run-passed mutation preview but still stop short of auto-applying/persisting it.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-30.** Repro on 2026-03-27: run the live folder-digest prompt (`"i have a folder of markdown notes, can you make a runnable workflow..."`) through `python -m tests.eval --prompt ... --lane build --workflow-contract enabled`. After codegen timed out at the 120-second budget, the mutation fallback reached a dry-run-passed preview (`"Prepared a workflow build preview. Dry-run validation passed. These changes are proposed, not applied yet."`) and the server log showed auto-repair succeeding on attempt 2, but the eval record still ended `graph_created=false`, `failure_mode=timeout_codegen`. Fix: recovered empty-graph mutation previews are now auto-applied and saved when they only exist because the fast build path already failed, while normal mutation previews remain opt-in. Coverage: `tests/test_chat_manager_build_path.py`, `tests/test_agent_runtime/test_mutation_preview.py`, and `tests/test_post_tool_followup_recovery.py -k auto_apply`.
- ~~**P1: chat-started runs could still fail with cwd-sensitive runtime exceptions after startup succeeded.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-30.** Repro: `_scratch` run summaries like `run-1774849264689` failed after the first ready level with `errors.exception = "[Errno 2] No such file or directory: '.'"` because the server engine still instantiated relative runtime storage paths such as `./checkpoints` under a missing cwd, and inline code nodes using relative `open(...)` still depended on the raw backend cwd. Fix: server runtime config now anchors those engine dirs to the resolved workspace root, server bootstrap seeds `DAN_WORKSPACE_ROOT`, and inline code-node `open(...)` resolves relative paths against that workspace root.
- ~~**P2: chat-started workflow runs could fail with blank `View full logs` and opaque `unknown error` surfaces.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro: save a distilled workflow from chat, run it, then click `View full logs`; the Operations log panel could open empty because the button only switched panels without hydrating the selected run, and `run_failed` often rendered as `unknown error` because the engine emitted `data.errors` while chat/log surfaces only read `data.error`. Fix: `run_failed` now carries a summarized `error`, chat/recovery payload mapping extracts from both `error` and `errors`, the run card now reads nested failure details correctly, and `View full logs` now loads the persisted event stream into `LogPanel`.
- ~~**P1: auto-inserted validator nodes in codegen-enriched graphs still omitted the declared `data` input port.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro: run `dotenv -f .env run -- pytest -q -m integration tests/test_meta/test_integration_llm.py::TestBuilderCodePath::test_execute_generate_code_in_sandbox`. Cause: `DefaultsEnricher._ensure_validation_gate()` rewired incoming edges to `target_port="data"` but the raw-dict validator payload omitted that input port, so post-enrichment validation failed before execution. Fix: `ValidatorNode` now seeds the canonical `data` input port, the raw generation/mutation paths emit explicit `data` / `valid` / `invalid` ports, and the live builder-code integration expectation now reflects the intended enriched 3-node graph.
- ~~**P2: `inspect_node` could crash on typed runtime nodes like `CodeOperator`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro: `inspect_node({"workflow_id": "_scratch", "node_id": "read_watchlist_csv"})` returned `Error inspecting node: 'CodeOperator' object has no attribute 'type'`. Cause: `src/dan/server/capabilities/introspection.py` loaded a typed `Graph` model from `GraphStore.load_as_model()` but still assumed an old generic node adapter with `.type` and `.config` attributes. Fix: serialize typed nodes with `model_dump(...)`, report canonical `node_type`, and derive the returned config from the dumped node payload. Coverage lives in `tests/test_capabilities_introspection.py`.
- ~~**P2: `get_activity` could crash on typed `ActivitySnapshot` payloads.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro: `get_activity` returned `Tool error: 'ActivitySnapshot' object has no attribute 'get'`. Cause: `src/dan/server/capabilities/experiences.py` still treated the tracker response as a plain dict even though `ActivityTracker.get_activity()` now returns the typed `ActivitySnapshot` model. Fix: dump the typed snapshot before reading `active` / `recent` / `connected_surfaces`. Coverage lives in `tests/test_server/test_capability_retry_metadata.py`.
- ~~**P2: `list_directory(path='.')` could still fail when the process cwd was unavailable.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro: `list_directory` surfaced `Failed to list directory: [Errno 2] No such file or directory` even after the file-IO capability had already resolved the requested directory. Cause: `src/dan/tools/list_directory.py` still anchored its display root through `validate_path('.')`, and `src/dan/tools/_workspace.py` still fell back to raw `os.getcwd()` when `DAN_WORKSPACE_ROOT` was unset. Fix: route workspace fallback through `resolve_workspace_root()` so `"."` remains stable under deleted/missing cwd states. Coverage lives in `tests/test_server/test_list_directory_pagination.py`.
- ~~**P2: generated validator defaults still emitted deprecated `rule_type="format_check"` payloads.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro: the live `.env` builder-code integration path in `tests/test_meta/test_integration_llm.py::TestBuilderCodePath::test_execute_generate_code_in_sandbox` failed post-enrichment validation because `DefaultsEnricher` and `insert_validator()` still emitted the stale `format_check` literal, while the canonical `ValidationRule` schema only accepts `required_keys`, `non_empty`, `schema_conformance`, `type_check`, or `custom_expression`. Fix: generation defaults and structural mutations now emit `required_keys` with an empty key list, and mutation-preview normalization coerces legacy `format_check` payloads to the canonical rule.
- ~~**P2: `plan_graph_mutations` could still fail dry-run on an exact duplicate edge add even though it was only a redundant no-op.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro: a workflow update preview for the equity/watchlist flow failed with `Edge 'process_tickers.results->aggregate_summary.ticker_reports' already exists`. The chat mutation normalizer already repaired duplicate `add_node` collisions, but it did not drop redundant `add_edge` operations, so the mutator surfaced the duplicate as a dry-run error. Fix: `src/dan/agent_runtime/mutation_preview.py` now drops exact duplicate `add_edge` ops when the same edge semantics already exist in the graph or earlier in the same plan, while preserving remove-then-readd and semantic-change cases. Coverage lives in `tests/test_mutation_duplicate_add_repair.py`.
- ~~**P1: `dan-up` backend startup could crash before `/health` if the child process inherited a missing or unusable current working directory.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro from `~/.dan/logs/server.log`: startup first died in `GraphStore(base_dir=_graphs_dir)` because relative `./graphs` could no longer resolve, and after that fix it died again in `init_stores()` / later startup paths that still called `os.getcwd()` for `DAN_WORKSPACE_ROOT`.
