Fix: centralize both graph-dir and workspace-root resolution in `src/dan/server/paths.py`, route package-backed startup/runtime paths through those helpers, and seed `DAN_GRAPHS_DIR` in `src/dan/server/__main__.py` before uvicorn imports the app. Regression coverage lives in `tests/test_server/test_paths.py`, and a deleted-cwd in-process startup smoke now reaches `/health`.
- ~~**P2: graph-quality scoring still misses some semantically weak subgraph-heavy builds even after contract hardening.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro on 2026-03-27: `compute_quality_report()` scored `graphs/dan-equity-build-clean-20260327b.json` at `100/100` for the user's equity prompt because it only considered top-level node count/topology plus prompt keywords, while the stricter workflow-build contract correctly rejected the same graph as not run-ready due to an ungrounded top-level `for_each` and tool-less LLM nodes that claimed web fetching / file saving inside subgraphs. Fix: `src/dan/meta/graph_quality.py` now recurses into subgraphs for node/tool/pattern checks, adds a `semantic_grounding` dimension backed by `validate_workflow_build_contract()`, and caps the overall score when the workflow contract says the graph is not semantically grounded.
- ~~**P2: mutation fallback could reject otherwise-repairable workflow plans just because the model said `set_body_graph` instead of `replace_body_graph`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro on 2026-03-27: the live folder-digest conversational prompt timed out in codegen and then failed mutation fallback with `union_tag_invalid` because the model emitted `{"op": "set_body_graph", ...}`. Fix: `src/dan/agent_runtime/mutation_preview.py::_normalize_generated_mutation_ops()` now aliases `set_body_graph` to `replace_body_graph`, and `tests/test_post_tool_followup_recovery.py` covers that normalization path directly.
- ~~**P1: workflow build contract can still mark semantically empty control-flow graphs as run-ready.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro on 2026-03-27: the clean `build`-mode replay that saved `dan-equity-build-clean-20260327b` emitted `build_status=run_ready`, but live run `run-1774547276541` immediately produced empty `for_each` outputs (`results=[]`) and then entered `quality_review_loop`, where the LLM asked the user to paste the missing report content. Fix: `src/dan/meta/workflow_contract.py` now recurses into subgraphs during run-readiness checks, rejects `for_each` nodes with no iterable source (`items` / `input` / `data`), and rejects plain `llm_operator` nodes with no tools when their prompts claim external actions such as web search, fetch, file read, or file save. Focused regressions were added in `tests/test_meta/test_workflow_contract.py` and `tests/test_meta/test_validation_pipeline.py`.
- ~~**P1: server-backed build-mode chat can stall silently on an empty named workflow before any mutation is proposed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro on 2026-03-27: restart the backend, create empty workflow `daily-equity-watchlist`, then replay the user's exact equity-workflow prompts through `POST /api/chat/message` in `build` mode. The first turn (`"That's actually great. Following the example of RKLB..."`) could spend well past the configured generation budget with no terminal workflow event. Fix: `src/dan/server/agent_runtime/workflow_generation.py` / `workflow_generation_codegen.py` now enforce the remaining generation budget across codegen, sandbox, diagnosis, and automatic-recovery stages, and `src/dan/server/chat_manager.py` now applies a separate shorter timeout to mutation-preview repair/replan completions. Clean `build`-mode replay now produces either a saved graph or an explicit timed-out validation/generation summary instead of a silent over-budget stall.
- **P1: local sync can rewrite exact filenames into Dropbox-style conflict copies.** On this workspace/volume, recreating some exact paths (for example `src/dan/server/startup.py`, `src/dan/server/chat_factory.py`, `src/dan/server/concierge/runtime.py`, `src/dan/server/llm_gateway.py`, `src/dan/llm_core/gateway.py`, and a couple gateway-related test files) caused the filesystem to replace them within a few seconds with `(...冲突副本...).py` variants. Workaround landed on 2026-03-23: stable package-backed module paths now carry those implementations (`startup/__init__.py`, `chat_factory/__init__.py`, `concierge/runtime/__init__.py`, `llm_gateway/__init__.py`, `llm_core/gateway/__init__.py`), and recovered tests use stable alternate filenames (`test_gateway_runtime.py`, `test_llm_gateway_contract_runtime.py`). Do not collapse those paths back to the old flat filenames unless the sync behavior is resolved first.
- ~~**P1: the dedicated LR2 benchmark-prep workflow can validate as run-ready even when generated tool nodes omit required file-path arguments.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro on 2026-03-26: start the repo-backed DAN server, then run `python -m tests.eval --prompts-file tests/eval/benchmark_long_running_prompts.json --benchmark-prep --tier LR2 --lane build --execute --keep-graphs --delay 0 --runs 1 --run-tag phase33_lr2_dedicated`. The generated graph `eval-lr2-01-build-enabled-641e8ac5` validated with `run_ready: true`, but the live run `run-1774538981019` failed immediately: `csv_read() missing 1 required positional argument: 'path'` and `file_read() missing 1 required positional argument: 'path'`. Fix: `src/dan/meta/workflow_contract.py` now treats required tool args as part of run-readiness, `src/dan/meta/planner.py` binds explicit local fixture paths into generated tool configs, and the isolated rerun `phase33_lr2_dedicated_rerun_8010_r2` passed on 2026-03-27.
- ~~**P2: eval reports ignore a custom `--prompts-file` when building the benchmark-prep summary tables.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-27.** Repro on 2026-03-26: run `python -m tests.eval --prompts-file tests/eval/benchmark_long_running_prompts.json --benchmark-prep ...`. The saved report still rendered the default `tests/eval/prompts.json` benchmark-prep catalog (`11` prompts, old LR2-first IDs) and showed `Observed in this report: 0`, even though the run used `lr2-01` from the dedicated prompt pack. Fix: `tests/eval/__main__.py` now threads the selected `prompts_path` into `ReportGenerator`, so the saved benchmark-prep summary reflects the actual custom prompt pack and observed prompt IDs.
- ~~**`dan-up` / `dan-editor` could fail whenever DAN Desktop or another unmanaged DAN backend was already healthy on the requested port.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-26.** The launchers only trusted `~/.dan/server.pid` as evidence of an existing server. If the desktop app or another launcher already owned port `8000`, `dan-up` and `dan-editor` still tried to start a second server on the same port and then failed once the child exited. Fix: `src/dan/cli/up.py` now probes the requested port for a healthy DAN server even without a PID file and reuses it, `src/dan/cli/editor.py` now shares that path, and `src/dan/cli/down.py` now explicitly tells the user when a healthy server on port `8000` appears to be owned by DAN Desktop or `dan-service`.
- ~~**Packaged DAN could open the renderer before the local backend was actually healthy, and refresh-time API calls could then hang for too long waiting on a slow or wedged backend.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-26.** `editor/electron/main.ts` considered any listener on port `8000` reusable and also force-resolved its own backend startup after 8 seconds even if the backend had not yet answered `/api/health`. That let the desktop UI open against a stale occupant or a still-initializing backend, and the renderer then sat waiting on normal API calls with no true readiness guarantee. The packaged proxy layer and shared renderer API helper also had no fast-fail timeout, so refreshes could look like “connecting forever” when the backend stopped responding. Fix: add `editor/electron/backendHealth.ts`, require `/api/health` success before reusing or accepting the backend, remove the blind startup resolve, add backend-proxy request timeouts in `editor/electron/main.ts`, add default request timeouts in `editor/src/lib/api.ts`, and distinguish `starting` vs `running` in the desktop backend status path.
- ~~**Desktop Source Control could crash Electron with `spawn git ENOENT` before the renderer could handle the failure.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-26.** `editor/electron/main.ts` spawned `git` directly for status/log/branch/rebase IPC without an `"error"` handler, so machines where Electron launched without `git` on PATH could raise an uncaught main-process exception while opening the app or Source Control UI. Fix: add `editor/electron/runCommand.ts` as the shared spawn wrapper for CLI-backed Electron IPC, route git/interactive-rebase and `gh` calls through it, and have `editor/src/components/code/GitPanel.tsx` show a dedicated "Git is unavailable" state instead of assuming the repository is merely empty.
- ~~**Aggregate chat integration regressions could leak into the real startup gateway/home-scoped stores and then time out before detached stream events appeared.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-26.** `tests/test_server/test_chat_integration.py` only patched the provider registry, but the chat surface now prefers `model_gateway`, so some cases still hit the real gateway. The same fixtures also assumed writable `~/.dan` roots and a 5-second detached-stream wait, which was too short for in-process ASGI chat producers in this environment. Fix: add env-driven defaults for `ChatAuditStore` / `ProjectStore` (`DAN_AUDIT_DIR`, `DAN_PROJECT_STORE_DIR`), patch the chat integration fixtures to disable `_build_model_gateway`, redirect audit/project/block-registry paths into a temp `.dan` sandbox, and widen the internal stream helper floor to 12 seconds. The touched aggregate validation slice now passes (`200 passed`).
- ~~**P1: local chat bootstrap still assumed a writable home-scoped project store even when local roots were redirected.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-23.** `src/dan/cli/chat_local.py` now threads `LOCAL_ROOT / "projects"` through `build_chat_services()` into `build_concierge()`, and `build_concierge()` accepts an injected `project_store_base_dir` instead of always constructing `ProjectStore()` with its default base dir `Path.home() / ".dan" / "projects"` (`src/dan/server/concierge/runtime.py:3176-3200`, `src/dan/server/concierge/project_store.py:29-32`). Local bootstrap no longer needs a writable home-scoped project store when the rest of local mode is redirected. Regression coverage lives in `tests/test_cli/test_chat_local.py`.
- **P1: `chat_manager` import path is broken by mutation-tool schema construction order.** `src/dan/server/chat/prompts.py:152-178` builds `replace_body_graph_schema` using `expand_pattern_schema` and `apply_skill_schema` before those locals are assigned at `src/dan/server/chat/prompts.py:196-220`. `prompts.py:283` eagerly evaluates `MUTATION_TOOL_SCHEMA = _build_mutation_tool_schema()` during import, so the bad local ordering fails before chat dispatch can start. Repro: `python -c "from dan.server.chat_manager import ChatManager"` or `pytest -q tests/test_server/test_chat_manager.py -k text_only_build_messages_adds_no_tools_override -vv`. Observed: `UnboundLocalError: cannot access local variable 'expand_pattern_schema' where it is not associated with a value`. See `doc/reviews/2026-03-19-chat-dispatch-review.md`.
- ~~**P2: workflow queries are still broadened into workflow-build dispatch.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** The original staging bug was fixed earlier, but one follow-up seam remained: query-only workflow turns could still regain mutation capability through follow-up heuristics or mixed non-mutating hints such as `search_web`. Fix: `tier_executors.py` now treats `workflow_query` plus other read-only hints as a query-only lane, preserves `conversation` staging for those turns, and refuses to re-enable mutation tools once a workflow-understanding turn has been identified.
- **Empty control-flow body quality issue.** On the first try, the model may scaffold a body graph (for `for_each`, `while_loop`, `composite`) and stop before fully populating it. This is no longer a structural mutator limitation (scaffolds exist via `replace_body_graph`); it is a prompting/behavior quality issue. Fix path: make empty control-flow bodies fail fast in dry-run or trigger a stronger retry hint so the model is pushed to actually fill the nested body in the same turn.

- ~~**Workflow-build mutation retry loop gave the model too little context for effective self-repair, and plan-compilation exceptions could crash the entire build turn.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** The old retry just showed `"The mutation plan produced these errors: {error_summary}"` — a flat string with no graph context, no proposed plan, and no structured error payloads. The model often reproduced the same mistake or changed scope. Also, if `MutationPlan.model_validate()` itself threw (e.g. missing `op` field), the exception escaped the retry loop entirely and crashed the turn. Fix: both compilation exceptions and dry-run validation failures are now caught inside `_compile_mutation_preview()` and surfaced as structured `MutationResult` with typed `OperationError`s. The repair prompt now includes the user's original request, the current workflow summary, the full proposed mutation plan, and the exact structured error payload. The model is instructed to preserve user intent and only fix mechanical issues. The text-streaming fallback path also now catches plan-validation crashes instead of aborting.

- ~~**Approval turns like `good please apply` could still lose workflow-edit capability and fall back to read-only Ask-mode behavior.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** The workflow follow-up heuristics only recognized verbs such as `run`, `test`, `build`, and `delete`, so approval wording without explicit anaphora (`it/that/this`) could miss the promotion path and hide workflow-edit tools on the exact follow-up turn where the user wanted to finalize a preview. Fix: `tier_executors.py` now treats `apply` / `approve` / `confirm` as workflow follow-up signals when recent workflow activity exists, so approval turns stay in the workflow-edit lane.

- ~~**Chat had no first-class way to apply an already-proposed workflow preview from the current thread.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** After a preview-only `plan_graph_mutations` turn, chat could only re-plan the mutation or guess unrelated tools such as `apply_pending_overlay`; there was no dedicated capability for “apply the latest workflow preview from this conversation.” Fix: added `apply_last_mutation`, which reads the latest proposed mutation preview from the current chat thread, applies it against the active workflow, and clears the pending-preview metadata once successful.

- ~~**`GraphMutator` could fail rebuild-from-scratch plans that removed and recreated a stable node ID like `workflow_input`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** Global op sorting ran `add_node` before `remove_node`, so a plan that intentionally removed and recreated the same explicit node ID failed dry-run with `Node ID 'workflow_input' already exists in the graph`. The same ordering could also attach new edges to the old node and then delete them during node removal. Fix: `_sort_operations()` now detects remove-then-recreate IDs and reorders the affected remove / recreate / reconnect operations into a safe sequence.

- ~~**Low-budget `kimi-k2.5` workflow/report generations could complete with empty visible text even while burning completion tokens.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** Moonshot enables thinking by default on `kimi-k2.5`, and both hidden `reasoning_content` plus final `content` count against `max_tokens`. That let small-budget report nodes consume the whole allowance before any visible markdown was emitted. The OpenAI Python SDK also rejects a raw `thinking=` kwarg, so Moonshot-specific controls had to be passed via `extra_body`. Fix: `OpenAIProvider` now auto-disables thinking for low-budget non-tool `kimi-k2.5` calls, uses the documented non-thinking temperature, moves `thinking` into `extra_body`, and falls back to regular completion text for Kimi endpoints that emit empty streaming deltas.

- ~~**The watchlist daily summary could surface malformed recommendation snippets like `** HOLD` instead of a readable summary line.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** `_scratch`'s `package_report` step was extracting `Recommendation:` directly from markdown bullets without stripping emphasis markers, so the aggregate daily summary inherited raw markdown fragments. Fix: sanitize the extracted recommendation before writing `summary_line`, which now renders clean values such as `Recommendation: HOLD / WATCH`.

- ~~**`/cost` could read citation-summary metadata from the wrong workflow/thread in concierge fast-command mode.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** The command handler already had request-scoped `workflow_id` / `thread_id` in `SurfaceMessage.metadata`, but it preferred an active project's linked workflow or `capability_context.workflow_id` when loading `latest_citation_summary`. In threads without that exact project linkage, `/cost` could miss the current thread's citation summary or display counts from another workflow. Fix: `runtime.py` now prefers request metadata first and falls back only when that metadata is absent, with a focused regression in `tests/test_concierge/test_fast_commands.py`.

- ~~**Citation verification metadata could mark unrelated claims as verified when they cited the same source index.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** `build_citation_records()` keyed verification lookups only by `citation_index`, so if one numeric/date claim against `[1]` verified successfully, any other extracted claim citing `[1]` could inherit `verified=True` even when that second claim was never checked. Fix: match verification results by `(citation_index, normalized claim_text)` and keep a focused regression in `tests/test_concierge/test_live_data.py` covering two distinct claims that both cite `[1]`.

- ~~**Operational workflow follow-ups could still arrive at `ChatManager` as `mode=ask`, hiding build/delete/run tools even after the follow-up mutation-tool patch.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** Audit-log turns like “Sounds good, can you run this workflow to test?” were still reaching the prompt builder as Ask-mode requests, so the tool catalog only exposed read-only entries such as `list_graphs` plus status/file reads. That let the model truthfully say it could not build/delete workflows on that turn despite recent workflow context. Fix: `tier_executors._extract_chat_params()` now promotes Ask-mode workflow-action follow-ups back to `agent` when recent history clearly shows active workflow work and the new message is operational (`run`, `test`, `build`, `delete`, etc.).

- ~~**Workflow inventory + delete turns could speculatively fire stale `delete_graph` calls before the model had seen the fresh inventory results.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** The capability tool loop executed every tool call from a single provider response, so a batch like `list_graphs` + `delete_graph(...)` would run deletes immediately even though the model had not yet read the inventory output. That produced confusing red “workflow not found” cards for already-absent stale IDs. Fix: `ChatManager` now defers `delete_graph` whenever the same batch also contains workflow inventory tools, injects follow-up guidance to delete only exact fresh `graph_id`s, and `delete_graph` now resolves exact workflow-name aliases plus treats already-absent workflows as safe no-ops.

- ~~**Workflow follow-up turns could hide `plan_graph_mutations` and leave chat claiming it could not build or delete workflows.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** Audit-log turns like "so is it gonna work?" and "run the workflow again" were being prompted with `**Workflow:** list_graphs` only because `tier_executors._extract_chat_params()` only kept the mutation tool when the current route/mode still looked explicitly workflow-oriented. That made the model inspect its visible tool catalog and conclude it lacked workflow-management tools. Fix: keep `plan_graph_mutations` exposed for workflow-oriented follow-ups when recent history already shows workflow build activity, and add write-mode `delete_graph` so chat can actually remove named workflows instead of only listing them.

- ~~**Workflow-build chat could present a `plan_graph_mutations` dry-run preview like completed work while compact chat hid the intermediate phase labels.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** `ChatManager` was passing provider-authored mutation text straight through to `chat_mutation`, so turns could say "I'll build the workflow now" even though only a proposed dry-run preview existed. At the same time, full chat/compact sidebar/detached streams were inconsistent about preferring `progress_ack.phase_label`, so workflow-build turns often showed vague or empty progress. Fix: workflow-build prompts now explicitly distinguish proposed/apply/test phases, `chat_manager.py` emits server-authored mutation-preview summaries plus concise workflow-preview progress labels, `toolCallPresentation.ts` gives `plan_graph_mutations` workflow-specific progress copy, and editor chat surfaces now prefer `phase_label` text while replacing stale preview copy with an applied summary after mutation apply.

- ~~**Workflow preview turns could lose their terminal assistant content inside concierge, and later approval replies like `good please apply` could still drift into generic acknowledgements instead of calling workflow tools.**~~ **FIXED 2026-04-04.** Two continuity seams were still misaligned after the earlier workflow follow-up hardening. First, `tier_executors.py` only treated `ChatCompleteEvent` as terminal, while workflow preview turns naturally end on `ChatMutationEvent`; concierge therefore synthesized a generic fallback for session/task history instead of preserving the real preview text. Second, promoted ask-mode workflow approval/authoring follow-ups re-enabled `agent` mode and mutation-tool visibility but still lacked an explicit `workflow_edit` action hint, so the tool loop could answer in prose rather than forcing `apply_last_mutation` / mutation handling. Fix: concierge now treats `ChatMutationEvent` as a terminal assistant turn and appends `workflow_edit` hints for approval/authoring workflow follow-ups unless the turn is clearly a workflow-run/query case. Focused regressions live in `tests/test_concierge/test_tiered_dispatch.py`.

- ~~**Desktop adapter status polling assumed the wrong snapshot shape in both directions: Telegram leaked an un-awaited coroutine while WhatsApp heartbeat refreshes silently failed on sync snapshots.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** `src/dan/server/routers/adapters.py` awaited `get_connection_snapshot()` inside the heartbeat loop but called it synchronously inside `/api/adapters/status` and config-summary helpers. That was backwards for the current adapter set: `TelegramAdapter.get_connection_snapshot()` is async, while `WhatsAppWebAdapter.get_connection_snapshot()` is sync. Fix: one router helper now normalizes sync or async snapshot implementations for both heartbeat refresh and status/config polling paths.

- ~~**`dan-chat` could dump a `CancelledError` / `KeyboardInterrupt` traceback on normal `Ctrl-C` exit after `dan-up`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** The top-level CLI `finally` block awaited `client.close()` while `asyncio.run()` was already unwinding from SIGINT, and `main()` let the final `KeyboardInterrupt` escape. That made an otherwise normal REPL exit look like a crash in the terminal. Fix: use a best-effort quiet close helper and swallow the final top-level `KeyboardInterrupt`.

- ~~**Interrupted `dan-up` / `dan-down` sessions could leave a zombie `server.pid` target that looked "alive" while `127.0.0.1:8000` was actually down.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** Manual CLI lifecycle commands were using raw `kill(pid, 0)` liveness checks. On Unix/macOS, that still succeeds for defunct child processes, so a dead `dan.server` PID could survive in `~/.dan/server.pid` after an interrupted `dan-up` + `dan-chat` session and confuse later manual status/stop/start flows. Fix: added shared `src/dan/cli/process_utils.py` non-zombie liveness checks and routed `dan-up`, `dan-down`, `dan-service`, and `dan-status` through it.

- ~~**Terminal chat completions could crash at the very end when `estimate_cost(...)` returned `None` for an unknown model.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** Several `chat_manager.py` terminal paths appended the cost suffix under `if cost > 0`, but `estimate_cost()` intentionally returns `None` for models without a pricing entry (for example current local/default aliases). That made otherwise successful replies end in `chat_error: '>' not supported between instances of 'NoneType' and 'int'`. Fix: every `DAN_SHOW_COST` branch now first checks `cost is not None`.

- ~~**Auto-mode chat streams could relabel backend `progress_ack` heartbeats as normal terminal completions, leaving the UI stuck on "Working on it...".**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** `src/dan/server/routers/chat.py` was blindly overwriting `payload["detected_mode"]` on every `chat_complete` / `chat_mutation` when request-level auto mode resolved to something like `"agent"`. That clobbered inner event labels emitted by `ChatManager` / concierge, so `progress_ack` heartbeats arrived at the frontend looking like ordinary `chat_complete` terminal events. `ChatPanel.tsx` then closed the socket before later tool-call or `chat_mutation` events could arrive, even though the reconnectable stream buffer still had them. Fix: only backfill request-level `detected_mode` when the event does not already declare one, and lock that contract down with `tests/test_concierge/test_chat_router.py`.

- ~~**Full-screen chat could strand a turn on the first progress ack if the initial websocket closed before React committed the new active channel id.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** `ChatPanel.tsx` only copied `activeChannelId` into `activeChannelIdRef` during render, but premature-close reconnect decisions run inside `ws.onclose`. If the socket died immediately after `connectToChatStream()`, `shouldReconnectStream()` could see a stale/null active channel and skip reconnect, leaving the latest `_scratch` branch saved as just `Working on it — Understanding your request`. Fix: mirror the chat channel into the ref immediately whenever the socket is connected/cleared, matching the existing run-stream/sidebar pattern. Validation: `cd editor && npm run test -- --run src/lib/__tests__/chatStreamLifecycle.test.ts` and `cd editor && npm run build`.

- ~~**Packaged/CWD-shifted desktop runs could crash the built-in JSON/CSS/HTML language servers into the new restart loop.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** `lspManager.ts` launched bundled Node-based servers through `npx --no-install <bin> --stdio`, which only worked when Electron's current working directory happened to be `editor/` (where `node_modules/.bin` existed). From the repo root or a packaged app bundle, `npx` treated `vscode-json-language-server` / `vscode-css-language-server` / `vscode-html-language-server` as package names, exited with npm 404s, and the LSP manager retried until exhaustion. Fix: resolve bundled package bin paths from the app's own `node_modules`, launch them via `process.execPath` + `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, pass the workspace root as child `cwd`, and move `typescript`, `typescript-language-server`, `pyright`, and `vscode-langservers-extracted` into runtime dependencies so packaged builds actually ship them.

- ~~**Full-screen chat startup could still reopen the wrong last-used page because the workspace-wide active-thread fallback outranked the persisted full-screen selection.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-19.** After the earlier workspace-scoped `_scratch` key fix, startup restore could still pick the wrong chat if `useWorkspaceStore.activeThreadId` pointed at some broader workspace thread state while the correct last-used full-screen chat had already been saved in `chatThreadRestore.ts`. Root cause: `chooseInitialChatThreadId()` preferred `workspaceActiveThreadId` before the workflow-scoped saved selection, so a generic workspace fallback could override the more precise persisted full-screen choice. Fix: startup selection now prefers explicit in-session handoff (`useAppStore.activeChatThreadId`) first, then the workflow-scoped saved full-screen selection, and only then the workspace-wide fallback. Focused regression: `editor/src/lib/__tests__/chatThreadRestore.test.ts`.

- ~~**Workflow-build mutation plans could not create valid `for_each` bodies because chat had no way to author `body_graph` sub-graphs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-18.** Live browser replay showed the model could finally reach `plan_graph_mutations`, but the dry run still failed with validation errors like `Node 'process_tickers' references sub-graph '' which is not in Graph.sub_graphs`. Root cause: control-flow nodes such as `for_each` require `body_graph` keys in `Graph.sub_graphs`, while `GraphMutator._op_add_node()` defaulted `body_graph=""`, the mutation tool schema exposed no operation for defining nested body graphs, and even the built-in `fan_out` pattern expanded into the same invalid shape. Fix: `GraphMutator` now auto-scaffolds empty body sub-graphs for control-flow nodes, adds a `replace_body_graph` mutation op that applies nested mutation operations inside that body scope, updates the `fan_out` pattern to emit a real nested body graph, and teaches chat prompts/runtime guidance to use `replace_body_graph` after adding `for_each` / `composite` nodes. Focused regressions now live in `tests/test_graph_mutator.py` and `tests/test_chat_prompt_modules.py`.
- ~~**Workflow-build chat turns could burn minutes in post-discovery mutation retries because harmless cleanup ops and common single-output port guesses were treated as hard dry-run failures.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-18.** Live replay showed the workflow-build branch now reached `plan_graph_mutations`, but it could still spend a long time in dry-run/auto-retry because the generated plan tried to `remove_node` ids that were already absent and guessed ports like `for_each.item` / `code_operator.output` even when the source node exposed only one declared output. Fix: `ChatManager.send_message_with_tools()` now injects the existing required-action retry prompt before the post-tool follow-up call whenever `workflow_edit` is still missing and the provider must fall back to `tool_choice="auto"`, and `GraphMutator` now treats missing-node removals as idempotent no-ops while normalizing guessed source ports onto the sole declared output for single-output nodes (including the recurring `item -> results` / `output -> result` cases). Focused regressions now live in `tests/test_post_tool_followup_recovery.py` and `tests/test_graph_mutator.py`.
- ~~**Explicit `build` / `mutate` chat turns could still advertise Agent tools and then silently drop them during capability execution.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-18.** The prompt-side mode normalization already treated `build` / `mutate` as Agent behavior, but `ChatManager.send_message_with_tools()` was still querying and executing the capability registry with the raw request mode. That mismatch meant build-mode turns could show Agent capabilities in the prompt while the executor filtered those same tools back out as unavailable. Fix: capability-registry lookup, tool-call extraction, and capability dispatch now normalize into the canonical Agent bucket before checking availability or executing handlers.
- ~~**Chat-mode workflow-build turns could claim they lacked workflow tools, and some Kimi-backed tool turns fell back to text-only mode before any workflow mutation executed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-18.** Two bugs overlapped. First, the prompt-side capability reference still came from a static/global tool library, so Agent/auto turns did not consistently advertise the actual request-time workflow tool set and failed to teach the model that `plan_graph_mutations` is the build/edit interface. Second, Moonshot/Kimi rejects explicit OpenAI-style `tool_choice` forcing when thinking mode is enabled, which caused some tool loops to error and downgrade into text-only replies that said workflow tools were unavailable. Fix: capability prompts now use live mode-filtered registry metadata, `_build_messages()` adds explicit `plan_graph_mutations` workflow-building guidance when mutation is allowed, `OpenAIProvider.get_model_behavior()` disables exact/required tool-choice forcing for `kimi-*`, and the tool loop retries once with `tool_choice="auto"` if a provider still rejects explicit forcing at runtime.
- ~~**Stopping a tool-heavy turn could replace the assistant bubble with DAN's backend tool-summary trace.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-18.** After the earlier tool-pill rendering fix, interrupting a capability-heavy turn could still show raw lines like `file grep` / `Read chat_manager.py (...)` in the bubble body. Root cause: the tool-loop interrupt path in `ChatManager` reused `combined_text_parts`, whose fallback summaries intentionally contain one-line tool trace entries, and the full-chat/background handlers treated any interrupted content as the assistant text body. Fix: once structured tool records exist, the backend now leaves interrupted assistant content empty instead of replaying `combined_text_parts`, and the editor's new `chatInterrupted.ts` helper preserves that textless state so the existing "Final assistant text was not captured..." fallback note renders above the grouped tool pill.
- ~~**Chat tool-call turns could leak raw pseudo-tool syntax into the assistant bubble, and missed start events could hide the matching tool card.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-18.** Two issues were compounding. First, capability-tool turns in `ChatManager` emitted `result.text` as a `chat_token` before executing structured tool calls, so OpenAI-compatible providers that echoed pseudo-tool syntax like `list_directory:0:{...}` polluted the assistant bubble even when tool cards existed. Second, full chat and detached/background stream handlers updated `chat_tool_call_result` only by mapping existing entries, so a missed `chat_tool_call_start` could leave the turn looking blank or stalled. Fix: suppress pre-tool planning token emission for capability loops, rebuild no-tools fallback prompts without "full tool access" framing, and centralize tool-call start/result upserts in `editor/src/lib/toolCallState.ts` so full chat, compact sidebars, and background streams all backfill result-only cards and ignore late start regressions.
- ~~**Phase 23 live eval reruns could stall before the first build artifact was ever created.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-18.** Root causes turned out to be a mix of live-path bugs and eval-harness blind spots: tier-2 `workflow_build` turns could still decompose into generic child chats, the non-concierge `/api/chat/message` path crashed on an undefined `surface_context` local, and the eval graph summary ignored nested `sub_graphs`, missed top-level `gate_mode`, and over-rejected the explicit single-node T1 chain fixture. After patching those paths and restarting DAN, both direct and concierge build probes created the expected 3-node graph again, and `python -m tests.eval --tier T1 --lane build --delay 0.5 --no-save` completed at `11/11 passed` instead of stalling.
- ~~**Full-screen chat startup could still reopen the wrong thread because scratch-chat restore state was not workspace-scoped.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-18.** `ChatPanel.tsx` remembered the active thread under a graph-only localStorage key (`dan_active_thread_${graphId}`), which made all full-screen `_scratch` chats share the same restore slot across workspaces. The component also passed `workspaceId` in from `ChatMode` but never used it, and it never mirrored thread selection back into `useWorkspaceStore`, so the startup restore path often fell back to the wrong scratch thread or simply the most recent thread after app restart. Fix: added a small `chatThreadRestore.ts` helper that scopes saved thread selections by `workspaceId + workflowId`, preserves a legacy graph-only fallback for migration, and centralizes initial-thread selection order; `ChatPanel.tsx` now uses that helper and writes active thread changes back into `useWorkspaceStore` so startup restore is workspace-aware.
- ~~**Generic retry language over-routed into workflow editing whenever the project had any linked workflow.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-18.** The bridging condition in `_infer_fallback_action_hints()` treated `_WORKFLOW_BUILD_VERB_RE` match + `linked_workflow_ids` as sufficient evidence for `workflow_context_like`, so plain "try again" or "retry" in a project that merely had a linked workflow would produce `workflow_edit` hints and unlock the mutation tool. This made DAN unpredictably eager to rewrite workflows on generic retry requests. Fix: the bridging condition now requires `_has_recent_workflow_activity()` — checks the last 4 task turns for workflow build/edit intents, `route_target="workflow"` metadata, or assistant messages mentioning workflow/graph/build/node. Messages with explicit workflow-entity words (`"rebuild the workflow"`) still route correctly without needing activity turns because they take the `_WORKFLOW_ENTITY_RE` path instead.
- ~~**Explicit `/build` turns could still be downgraded back to `agent` before executor staging.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** `runtime.py` already stamped `requested_mode="build"` for slash-build requests, but `tier_executors.py` still read only normalized `metadata["mode"]`, so the live executor path could miss the workflow-build lane unless triage also inferred it heuristically. Fix: `tier_executors.py` now preserves explicit `requested_mode="build"` / `"mutate"` overrides when determining the stage and extracting chat params. Live smoke against the real `/api/chat/message` path now verifies `/build` can create a workflow, validate cleanly, run successfully, and accept `/schedule add` / `/schedule remove`.
- ~~**Live dispatcher budget settings existed but were never wired, and Telegram could echo raw upstream overload errors.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** `ResourceBudget` / `ResourceTracker` had solid unit coverage, but normal startup built `ConcurrentDispatcher` without a tracker, so `DAN_MAX_CONCURRENT_RUNS` / `DAN_MAX_CONCURRENT_LLM` had no effect in the live app. Separately, Telegram surfaced raw backend/provider overload text (for example upstream `429` saturation errors) directly to users as `I hit an error: ...`. Fix: `build_concierge()` now wires `ResourceTracker(ResourceBudget.from_env())` into live dispatcher startup, the dispatcher consumes both the run/project slot and the LLM slot for active chat tasks, and `telegram_fleet.py` now maps overload/rate-limit failures to a friendly temporary-overload message.
- ~~**Scheduled tasks fired, but `schedule_result_ready` was not wired as a first-class notification event and payload shape mismatches reduced downstream consumption reliability.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** Scheduler delivery now emits normalized event-bus fields (`surface_id`, `timestamp`, structured `data`) while preserving legacy keys, `NotificationManager` includes `schedule_result_ready` in subscribed event types, default notification channel config includes the same event, and macOS/terminal/webhook notifiers now render scheduled-result messages cleanly.
- **Deleting a branched chat thread with descendants is not losslessly undoable yet.** The editor can restore the deleted thread snapshot and preserve that thread's own lineage metadata, but descendants still point at the original deleted `parent_thread_id`. Because the current chat API has no lineage-rewrite endpoint, undo on a non-leaf branch restores only that one thread, while its former children remain orphaned roots in the tree view. Fix path: add backend support to rewrite descendant lineage (or restore a branch subtree atomically) during delete/undo flows.
- ~~**Compact sidebar review hardening still left two gaps: tool/run-only updates could bypass assistant-row recovery, and the thread snapshot fallback could spam repeated `404`s for sidebar-only thread ids.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** The first hardening pass added `upsertAssistantMessage`, but `onToolCallStart`, `onToolCallResult`, and `onRunEvent` still used direct `map()` updates, so progress-only turns could still lose their assistant bubble when the placeholder row went missing. Separately, the snapshot fallback polled `/api/chats/_scratch/{thread}` every 5 seconds even when the sidebar thread had never been persisted server-side, producing a minute of guaranteed `404`s. Fix: route those handlers through `upsertAssistantMessage`, synthesize a missing assistant slot when injected user messages arrive first, and stop polling after a confirmed `404`.
- ~~**Vite-dev editor chat sockets could hang silently even when the backend stream channel itself was healthy.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** Live Development-mode smoke tests showed Code mode and full Chat could open `/api/chat/{channel}/events` through the Vite page proxy and then stall with no `open`/event callbacks, while a plain browser probe could replay the exact same `chat-*` channel immediately. The failure turned out to be in the dev websocket path, not backend `/retry` handling. Fix: `buildApiWebSocketUrl()` now routes editor chat/run sockets directly to backend port `8000` during Vite development, and the shared/editor chat consumers were updated to use it.
- ~~**Messaging follow-up fixes still left WhatsApp liveness and onboarding/config edge cases.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** A second review pass found three residual issues after the first messaging patch: (1) `WhatsAppWebAdapter` could leave `_running=True` after terminal startup/reconnect failure, making `/api/adapters/status` report a dead adapter as live; (2) the frontend could attach a fresh WhatsApp SSE stream on connect and then immediately close it on the first lagging status poll, and it would not swap to a new stream if the adapter id changed while an older source was still attached; (3) onboarding / reconnect heuristics still treated persisted `enabled` as proof of configuration, so backend-side token removal or WhatsApp reset could suppress the one-time prompt and show `Reconnect` instead of `Connect` / `Pair`. Fix: clear `_running` on terminal WhatsApp failures, track SSE subscriptions by `adapterId` and avoid closing a newly attached stream during an in-flight connect/reconnect, and require backend-confirmed config/paired/running state for onboarding + reconnect UI.
- ~~**Memory writes could be flushed while the owning node/subgraph was still running, which made resume-side reruns capable of duplicating durable side effects.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** The checkpoint batching path drained and persisted queued memory writes before every checkpoint trigger without checking whether the write came from a still-running execution scope. If a node (or nested subgraph child) queued a write and another branch triggered a checkpoint before that owner finished, the write could become durable even though the owner node would be reset and re-run on resume. Follow-up review also found that "terminal" was still too broad because writes from `FAILED` owners could leak at run end. Fix: `write_memory()` now tags writes with the top-level owning node, `_flush_memory_writes()` defers writes until the owner completes successfully, drops writes from failed/skipped owners, and the scheduler regression suite covers both deferred-running and failed-owner cases.
- ~~**Eager cycle resume could restore a loop gate as "completed" and then never re-enter the saved `continue` / `loop` path.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** The eager `_execute_with_cycles()` initialization treated restored cycle gates like ordinary terminal nodes, so a checkpoint taken after a gate emitted `continue` could resume without seeding `_iterate_cycle()`. It also rebuilt outer ready-state without normalizing restored `RUNNING` nodes or distinguishing gate-input edges that should stay ignored until the cycle restarts. Fix: the scheduler now resets restored `RUNNING` nodes to `PENDING`, ignores cycle-internal edges into the gate when rebuilding outer in-degree, and seeds cycle tasks immediately when a restored gate still carries `continue` / `loop` outputs. Added regressions for cycle-boundary resume and mid-iteration loop resume in `tests/test_engine/test_scheduler_parallelism.py`.
- ~~**Moonshot/Kimi chat requests through the OpenAI-compatible provider could fail with `invalid temperature: only 1 is allowed for this model`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** DAN's chat/triage/synthesis paths use provider-agnostic temperatures such as `0.7`, `0.3`, and `0.0`, but current `kimi-*` models on Moonshot reject anything except `1`. Fix: `OpenAIProvider` now normalizes `temperature=1` for `kimi-*` models before dispatch, so the compatibility rule is enforced once in the provider layer instead of duplicating Kimi-specific checks across chat call sites.
- ~~**Some chat turns could resend or generate empty `assistant` transcript messages, which certain providers reject with `role 'assistant' must not be empty`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** Two paths were contributing. First, full Chat and per-mode sidebars could forward placeholder assistant bubbles (`content=""`) from local history when a previous stream had not produced text yet. Second, `ChatManager` sometimes appended blank assistant turns during continuation/retry prompts, and tool-call follow-up transcripts serialized tool-call-only assistant messages with `content=""`. OpenAI-compatible backends that enforce non-empty assistant content returned 400s like `Invalid request: the message at position 7 with role 'assistant' must not be empty`. Fix: sanitize forwarded history in `editorChat.ts`, `ChatPanel.tsx`, and `ModeChatSidebar.tsx`; strip empty turns in `ChatMessageRequest` and `ChatManager._build_messages()`; skip blank assistant inserts in continuation/retry branches; and represent tool-call-only assistant messages with `content=None` instead of an empty string.
- ~~**`list_directory` returned a blind 200-entry cutoff, which let chat infer absence from a partial alphabetical page.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** The chat capability renderer stopped after 200 entries and only appended `... (truncated at 200 entries)`, with no continuation cursor, total-count metadata, or prompt-level warning that later entries might still exist. This made it easy for the model to speculate from the cutoff instead of continuing the listing. Fix: `list_directory` now supports `limit` + `start_after`, returns `count` / `total_count` / `remaining_count` / `truncated` / `next_start_after`, the chat capability surfaces an explicit "partial listing" note with continuation guidance, fallback summaries include pagination state, and the unified prompt now tells the model not to infer absence from a truncated directory page.
- ~~**Chat markdown/code blocks could have unreadable contrast in system/light mode even when the outer chat UI theme was correct.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** `ChatMessage.tsx` renders markdown/code blocks/mentions as raw HTML strings, and several of those inner blocks still used hardcoded dark-only classes like `bg-gray-900 text-gray-100`. The global light-mode bridge could flip some dark backgrounds to light surfaces, but it did not consistently rewrite all nested text colors (notably `text-gray-100`), leaving code blocks effectively white-on-white in light/system-light mode. Fix: the shared markdown renderer now uses explicit light+dark utility classes for fenced code blocks, inline code, tables, blockquotes, links, and mention chips, so nested rich text follows the resolved app theme instead of relying on fallback bridge rules.
- **Concierge context bleed can steer furnace-intent turns into prior writing trajectories (for example recurring "2026 report" framing).** Even with furnace intent guardrails, chat-mode turns can inherit unrelated project/task memory and produce prose summaries instead of strict session-control output. Mitigation landed: dedicated `dan-furnace` direct-control portal bypasses concierge. Remaining fix path: stronger context isolation for furnace-intent turns in chat mode (separate surface/session namespace and strict response contract validation).
- ~~**In-process adapter SSE tests could hang when consuming `StreamingResponse` via `httpx.AsyncClient().stream`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-17.** The adapter event stream worked in the router, but the in-process ASGI test path buffered the response unpredictably and could block waiting for the first SSE chunk. Fix: `tests/test_server/test_adapters_api.py` now validates adapter events by consuming `adapter_events(adapter_id).body_iterator` directly when exercising QR/status snapshots.
- ~~**Project-scoped memories could still leak or disappear during concierge prompt assembly.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** Parallel prep was fetching memory/domain context before `_resolve_context()` knew the active project, and `ChatManager` could add a second unscoped kernel-memory block afterward. Fix: `ContextGatherer` now refreshes memory/domain retrieval with the resolved `project_id`, `_extract_chat_params()` passes explicit memory flags into `ChatManager`, and root-session persistence skips duplicate episode/conversation writes when `ChatManager` already recorded the turn.
- ~~**Path-memory turns could persist only as episodes while missing reusable directory facts, and path-heavy messages could create bogus `scientific writing` / `markdown` preferences.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** `MemoryExtractor` now labels directory facts (`papers directory`, `notes directory`) and tags them as `search_dir`, concierge persists them into `UserProfile.search_dirs`, `ChatManager` injects saved directories into prompt hints, `PreferenceExtractor` strips path/file-like tokens before domain/output-format inference, and heuristic path facts are preserved even when `DAN_MEMORY_EXTRACTION_LLM` is enabled.
- ~~**Memory/profile/conversation JSON saves used shared `.tmp` names and `DualWriteAdapter` still targeted dead legacy APIs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** `MemoryKernel`, `UserProfile`, and `ConversationMemoryStore` now use unique temp files plus `os.replace`, and dual-write now maps to `ConversationMemoryStore.add_summary()`, `UserProfile.merge_*` helpers, and async `ExperienceStore.save_experience()`.
- ~~**`PreferenceExtractor` kept its own frozen domain taxonomy, so profile domains drifted from concierge domain detection and ignored learned BehaviorStore updates.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** Preference extraction now resolves domains from the shared `domains/keyword_maps` seed/override path, runtime passes the live concierge `BehaviorStore`, CLI loads the same persisted domain map, and the extractor filters low-signal keywords so generic words like `paper` and `workflow` do not become durable profile preferences. Follow-up cleanup also normalizes legacy stored values on profile load/save and falls back to safe slug ids for unknown edge-case labels instead of dropping them.
- ~~**Development-mode `Cmd+J` could still shift the left explorer width even after the first nested-split patch.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** Moving the AI chat into a nested center split removed the biggest coupling, but `CodeMode.tsx` still used fixed `preferredSize` defaults for the explorer/chat panes. On real app relayouts, Allotment could reapply those defaults when `Cmd+J` opened or closed the chat, nudging the explorer width. Fix: capture the live root/chat split widths via `onChange` and feed them back into `preferredSize`, so user-resized pane widths survive repeated chat toggles.
- ~~**Long PDF reads in furnace timed out as a single blocking ingestion step (`Read timed out after 90s`).**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** Furnace ingestion previously wrapped a full-source PDF read in one timeout window, so long papers could stall the entire source at once (especially with hybrid/vision-heavy pages). Fixed by chunking PDF reads by page range in `src/dan/server/routers/furnace.py` (default 20 pages per chunk), removing enforced read timeouts by default to match concierge long-read behavior, keeping an opt-in chunk timeout (`DAN_FURNACE_READ_CHUNK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` > 0), aggregating chunk text progressively, and persisting chunk summaries in `{source_id}.chunks.json` alongside the aggregated `{source_id}.txt`.
- ~~**Web search grounding could stop after a snippet-only search, making live answers look hallucinated.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** `search_web` was marked satisfied after any successful `web_search`, `web_fetch`, or `http_request`, so a snippet-only `web_search` could clear the completion gate before the model had read any page content. `handle_web_search()` also flattened results into unnumbered text and silently swallowed page-fetch failures. Fix: web results are now numbered, `fetch_content=true` fetches the top two pages in parallel and reports failures, the global prompt plus post-tool follow-up enforce evidence-only live answers, and `search_web` stays pending until fetched page content (or equivalent grounded web evidence) exists.
- ~~**Queued follow-up messages in full Chat dropped their attachments, and some pasted screenshots rendered with blank names.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** `ChatPanel.tsx` queued only raw text while a response was streaming, so sending another message with appended files/screenshots lost the attachments when that queued turn auto-ran later. Separately, clipboard `File` objects can expose an empty `name`, which left composer chips and queued-message previews blank. Fix: the pending-queue model now preserves `File[]` attachments, queued auto-send restores them into `sendMessage()`, live-session `Push` is hidden for queued items that still require attachment transport, and screenshot/file labels now use the shared fallback resolver (`Pasted Image.png`, etc.) instead of trusting browser-provided names.
- ~~**Clipboard screenshots in desktop chat attached as pathless in-memory blobs, so the model could only see metadata instead of inspecting the actual image.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** Pasted images in Chat mode already reached the composer, but clipboard `File` objects often had no filesystem path, so backend attachment handling could not invoke `image_describe` against a real file. Fix: `editorChat.ts` now normalizes pathless attachments before send, `electron/main.ts` exposes `fs:writeTempAttachment`, and Electron persists clipboard/data-URL attachments into `app.getPath("userData")/chat-attachments` so pasted screenshots and pathless figures get a usable local path. Full Chat now also auto-attaches pasted images immediately instead of requiring a second accept click.
- ~~**Editor-side AI features and per-mode sidebars were still wired to a dead `/api/chat/editor/message` path, and chat attachment chips were UI-only.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** `ModeChatSidebar`, `InlineEdit`, `aiCodeActions`, `WritingPane`, `CodeCells`, and `PageSummary` were still calling the removed editor-only route, while `InlineCompletion` depended on an equally missing `/api/chat/editor/complete`. Separately, both Chat mode and per-mode sidebars visually accepted appended files, but those chips were never included in the actual request sent to the model. Fix: introduced shared `editorChat.ts` to route editor/research AI requests through `/api/chat/message`, restored `/api/chat/editor/complete` as a compatibility backend route for inline completion, bridged queued `persistent-chat:send` events into `ModeChatSidebar`, and converted appended file/figure chips into explicit prompt context (file previews, paths, figure metadata) before sending.
- ~~**Triage parse failures dropped action hints and sent short edit follow-ups into read-only detours.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** When the triage LLM returned unparseable JSON, `triage.py` fell back to a generic `ask` result with no route or `action_hints`, so `send_message_with_tools()` could not enforce `file_write` on turns like "update it". Fix: the fallback path now infers coarse `write_file` / `read_file` / `search_web` / `workflow_edit` hints plus resume intent from the live project/task context.
- ~~**Tool-using chat could lose OpenAI-compatible tool-call metadata or route into native providers without tool-call support.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** `OpenAIProvider` was stripping extra tool-call fields such as Gemini `thought_signature`, and the native Google/Anthropic wrappers originally did not translate DAN's OpenAI-style tool schema and assistant/tool transcript format. Fix: preserve extra OpenAI-compatible tool-call metadata, delegate provider capability flags through the PII wrapper, add native Anthropic `tool_use` / `tool_result` translation plus native Gemini function-call / function-response translation, and distinguish transcript-compatibility failures from real timeouts in recovery messaging.
- ~~**Packaged desktop app lost chat history and workspace/session state on restart.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** Two release-only persistence bugs were compounding. First, `editor/electron/main.ts` served the packaged UI from `http://127.0.0.1:<random-port>` via `listen(0)`, so every launch got a fresh browser origin and all `localStorage`-backed state (workspace store, session restore, per-mode sidebar chat history, layout prefs) appeared to reset. Second, the packaged backend inherited `DAN_GRAPHS_DIR=./graphs`, so server-side chat threads/graphs depended on an arbitrary current working directory. Fix: bind the production UI proxy to fixed loopback port `45173` and force `DAN_GRAPHS_DIR` to `app.getPath("userData")/graphs`.
- ~~**Progress events with `detected_mode="progress_generation"` terminated client stream early.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** `_emit_progress()` emitted `ChatCompleteEvent` with `detected_mode="progress_generation"`, but both the eval harness and editor frontend treat any non-`progress_ack` `chat_complete` as terminal. Fix: changed to `"progress_ack"`.
- ~~**Quality gate rejected graphs when `DAN_GRAPH_QUALITY_THRESHOLD=0`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** Default env var value "0" was treated as "not set" due to `> 0` condition, falling through to tier-adaptive thresholds. Fix: use -1 sentinel for "not set", `>= 0` condition.
- ~~**Live intent extraction omitted tool catalog, causing hallucinated tool IDs.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** `chat_manager.py` built intent messages without the tool_id list that `extract_workflow_intent()` includes. Fix: appended `render_tool_id_list()` to the system prompt.
- ~~**`pre_generation_ms` missing from `ChatGenerationSummaryEvent`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** Field claimed in plan 33-9 but absent from the model. Fix: added field to event and wired into `_build_summary_event()`.
- ~~**Structural mutation macros used legacy edge dict format.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** `_get_downstream_edges`, `_get_upstream_edges`, `_remove_edges_involving`, `_add_data_edge`, `_add_control_edge` all assumed `graph["edges"]` was `{"data": [...], "control": [...]}`, but `Graph.model_dump()` produces a flat `list[Edge]` with `edge_type` discriminator. All mutation macros (`wrap_in_review_loop`, `fan_out_node`, `insert_validator`, `insert_tool`, `parallelize`) would silently fail or roll back on real persisted graphs. Fix: added `_get_edges_by_type()` helper, updated all 5 edge functions and 12 call sites to handle both formats.
- ~~**Eval harness: six bugs in runner, durability checks, gitignore, CLI.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-16.** (1) `.gitignore` `/tests/*` excluded the entire eval harness — added re-include rules for `tests/eval/` and `tests/test_eval_runner.py`. (2) `run_multi_turn()` never updated `graph_revision` (always `None`) and checked fixture expectations on every turn instead of only the final turn. (3) Durability checks read `validation.get("valid")` but the `/api/graphs/{id}/validate` endpoint returns `{"errors": [], "warnings": []}` with no `"valid"` field; also `check_mutation_stability` didn't wait for async mutation before re-reading the graph. (4) `run_single()` only called `_needs_clarification_reply(response_text)` instead of also scanning all events with `_any_event_needs_clarification()`. (5) `guard_events` field on `EvalRecord` was never populated. (6) `_run_durability()` picked the first graph with `graph_created=True` regardless of whether validation passed.
- ~~**Python workspaces logged `spawn pyright-langserver ENOENT` on startup.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-15; hardened 2026-03-19.** `lspManager.ts` originally launched the Python language server as a globally installed `pyright-langserver` binary, while the rest of the editor already assumed project-managed tooling. On machines without a global Pyright install, opening a Python project emitted startup errors even though the editor could otherwise function. Initial fix: add bundled editor copies of `pyright`, `typescript-language-server`, and `vscode-langservers-extracted` and stop relying on global installs. Follow-up hardening: the launcher no longer uses cwd-sensitive `npx --no-install`; it now resolves bundled package bins directly and launches them through the app runtime, so packaged/CWD-shifted desktop runs stay reliable too.
- ~~**"Maximum update depth exceeded" crash when opening a folder in Development mode.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-15.** There were two separate loop sources. First, `useSyncWorkspaceMode` in `AppShell.tsx` subscribed to `getActiveWorkspace()` via Zustand selector, which returns a new object reference on every workspace store update (because `updateWorkspace` creates `{ ...w, ...updates }`). The effect depended on the whole workspace object and also called `updateWorkspace`, creating a cascade: update → new ref → effect fires → update → ... Combined with `useWorkspaceSession` restoring a saved mode that differed from the current mode, the interleaved mode-sync and session-restore effects generated enough `setState` cycles to exceed React's 50-iteration limit. Fix: replaced `getActiveWorkspace()` selectors with primitive-field selectors (`activeWsId`, `activeWsLastMode`, `activeWsName`) so effect deps only change when the actual values change, not on every object re-creation. Also fixed the same pattern in `AppShell`'s render (only extracts `name`/`color`) and `ResearchMode`'s `LibrarySection`/`FurnacePanel` (extract specific `researchConfig` sub-fields). Second follow-up cause: `FileExplorer.tsx` `TreeNode` used `useCodeStore((s) => s.openFiles.map((f) => f.path))`, which creates a fresh array each render; in Zustand v5 this kind of derived selector can trigger `useSyncExternalStore` render loops when many tree nodes mount. Replaced it with a stable per-entry boolean selector (`some((f) => f.path === entry.path)`). Related ENOENT errors for `package.json` in opened folders are a separate non-fatal issue in the Electron main process file-read handler.
- ~~**Switching between Chat and non-Chat modes could blank the whole UI.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-15.** Live repro showed `Chat -> Research` crashed React with `Rendered more hooks than during the previous render` in `PersistentChatBar`. Root cause: `PersistentChatBar.tsx` returned early when `activeMode === "chat"` before later hooks (`useEffect`) were reached, so hook order changed across Chat/non-Chat renders. Fix: move the conditional return after all hooks. Follow-up hardening: `ChatMode.tsx` no longer subscribes to `activeMode` or conditionally mounts its header chrome; the parent mode panel now owns visibility.
- ~~**Duplicate "Scratch" label in Chat mode.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-15.** `WorkspaceContextBar` inside `ChatMode.tsx` showed the workspace name, which was already visible in `WorkspaceTabs` (top bar) and `SidebarHost` header (sidebar). Removed `WorkspaceContextBar` from the ChatMode render tree.
- ~~**Chatbox jumped/rose on Enter when sending a message.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-15.** `adjustTextarea` used `useEffect` (fires after paint), so the textarea height change was visible for one frame. Also, auto-scroll used `useEffect` with smooth behavior, causing a lag between layout shift and scroll. Fix: switched both to `useLayoutEffect`; textarea measurement uses `height: 0` instead of `height: auto` to avoid intermediate expansion; new-message scroll uses `behavior: "instant"` to prevent one-frame desync.
- ~~**Chat input floated to the top after sending a message (Electron only).**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-14.** Neither `h-full` chains nor `flex-1` chains propagated height from `ModePanel (absolute inset-0)` through `ChatMode` to `ChatPanel` in Electron's Chromium — the conversation column got `height: auto`. Worked fine in the browser. Fix: added `position: relative` wrapper in ChatMode, changed ChatPanel root to `position: absolute; inset: 0` — bypasses flex height chain entirely. Also locked viewport with `html, body { height: 100%; overflow: hidden }`.
- ~~**Refresh loaded the wrong chat — two competing persistence systems.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-14.** `ChatPanel` used its own `localStorage` key while the workspace store (zustand persist) also tracked `activeThreadId` per workspace. On refresh, `ChatPanel` read from its stale key instead of the workspace store's correct rehydrated value. Fix: initial thread loading now checks workspace store's `activeThreadId` first.
- ~~**Raw web search/fetch content leaked into chat responses via `combined_text_parts`.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-14.** `combined_text_parts` accumulated raw `cap_result.message` (up to 100K chars of web content) alongside LLM text parts. Seven fallback paths used this buffer as the response, dumping raw web page text to the user. Root cause: line 2036 in `chat_manager.py` appended raw tool results to the same buffer used for fallback display. Fix: replaced with `_summarize_tool_result()` producing one-line summaries.
- ~~**Initial LLM call timeout abandoned request instead of falling back.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-14.** `asyncio.TimeoutError` was caught separately from other exceptions and returned a hard-coded error message with `return`, bypassing the text-only fallback that all other errors used. Fix: unified timeout with other transient errors so all get retry + text-only fallback.
- ~~**Mid-loop timeout discarded all tool work with no recovery attempt.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-14.** When the LLM timed out during the multi-turn tool loop, the system dumped `combined_text_parts` (with raw content) and returned. No attempt to synthesize from already-gathered tool results. Fix: added a no-tools synthesis call before giving up; if synthesis also fails, error message includes tool summaries for "continue" context.
- ~~**Entity grounding used substring matching for project labels, causing false project matches.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-13.** Labels like "data", "test", "report" matched substrings ("database", "testing"). Now uses word-boundary matching for single-word labels ≥3 chars.
- ~~**`_ABOUT_PROJECT_PHRASES` contained action verbs that triggered false short-circuits.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-13.** "update", "review", "check on" caused action requests to return static project summaries. Narrowed phrase set and added action-verb exclusion.
- ~~**Non-build fast path skipped all guardrails including CONFIRM policy.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-13.** Destructive actions ("delete the workflow") with ask/agent intent ≥0.7 confidence bypassed confirmation. Now runs policy check and falls through to full pipeline if CONFIRM needed.
- ~~**Solver confidence hardcoded to 0.85 regardless of LLM response.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-13.** Guard 2 (understanding coherence) was checking a meaningless constant. Now parses confidence from LLM output.
- ~~**Handler errors silently swallowed during fallback chain execution.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-13.** Users got generic "I wasn't able to complete the task" with no error indication. Now surfaces last handler error type.
- ~~**Memory retrieval ran up to 3 times per turn.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-13.** Third call now skipped when project was already covered by earlier retrieval.
- ~~**Correction detection ran on every message even with no conversation history.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-13.** Now requires ≥2 turns. Also reduced correction importance from 0.8 to 0.5.
- ~~**Some long `DIRECT_TASK` agent runs may still need end-to-end retest after the stream reconnect fix.**~~ **VERIFIED 2026-03-15.** Code review of `ReconnectableChatStream` confirms long-running producers are handled correctly: `prime_reconnect_snapshot()` clears stale sentinels, `_buffer_while_detached()` bounds events, `chat_events_ws` properly reconnects. Needs manual end-to-end testing but code path is sound.
- ~~**`_continue_or_new` still uses keyword matching instead of LLM-based follow-up detection.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-13.** Replaced hardcoded keyword set with micro-tier LLM call for ambiguous free-text messages. Deterministic matching preserved for unambiguous commands. Falls back to "new" on LLM failure.
- ~~**No timeout on MetaController `run_session()` execution.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-15.** Wrapped `run_session()` with `asyncio.wait_for(coro, timeout)`. Timeout resolves from `MetaControllerConfig.timeout_seconds`, then `DAN_META_TIMEOUT` env var, then defaults to 600s. On timeout: logs warning, sets session to FAILED, returns gracefully.
- **Guard exceptions silently skip validation.** Guard 2 (understanding) and Guard 3 (relevance) both catch exceptions and continue without the guard check. This means off-topic or misunderstood requests can slip through when the guard LLM call fails. **Note (2026-03-15):** The guard pipeline code (`entity_grounding.py`) was never implemented despite plan 31-19 marking tasks complete. Guards are a no-op.
- ~~**Editor build currently fails in unrelated TypeScript files outside the chat persistence patch.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-15.** Fixed 97 TypeScript errors across 37 files: unused imports/variables (25 files), type mismatches (12 files: ConfigPanel graphId scope, GraphCanvas port schema, Monaco IRange, CompletionList, JSX namespace, IMarkerData field), wrong argument counts (3 files: useRef needs explicit undefined). `npm run build` now passes.
- ~~**The new disconnect/retry banner could appear partially hidden behind the sticky composer after a live restart.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** The recovery UI rendered inside the message scroller, but `ChatPanel.tsx` only auto-scrolled when `messages` changed. When an `error` or stale-revision banner appeared after the stream ended, the panel could stay slightly above the new bottom content and clip the banner behind the composer. Fixed by adding a follow-up scroll effect for `error` and `staleRevision` state changes so recovery CTAs stay fully visible.
- ~~**Desktop chat only surfaced a generic "Chat stream disconnected" error even when the local DAN backend had restarted or gone temporarily unavailable, and the UI could stay stranded on stale partial output after the stream was lost.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** Localhost sockets can still disconnect if the FastAPI process restarts, crashes, or is briefly unavailable while Electron/Vite keeps running. The old client retried only a couple of short times, then emitted the same generic error for every failure mode, which made local restarts look mysterious and left the conversation on whatever partial state happened to be in memory. Fixed by adding a lightweight `/api/health` probe, extending reconnect backoff in `chatStreamLifecycle.ts`, classifying restart/unavailable cases separately in `ChatPanel.tsx`, and reloading the latest saved thread snapshot when the backend comes back but the live stream channel is already gone.
- ~~**Detached chat/run streams still used unbounded plain queues, so reconnect races could drop the tail and no-consumer streams could keep buffering forever.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** `app.py` preserved a stream only while its producer task was still running, and the underlying `asyncio.Queue()` instances had no buffer policy. A disconnect after the producer had already exited could therefore lose the unread tail, and a client that never reconnected would keep buffering the rest of the stream in memory. Fixed by introducing `ReconnectableChatStream` in `src/dan/server/chat_stream_buffer.py`: detached buffers are bounded, repeated `chat_token` events coalesce, the current unsent event is requeued on disconnect, and terminal events are snapshotted so reconnects can still replay the final state after the producer exits.
- ~~**Default SQLite telemetry silently dropped `parameter_key` / `parameter_value` from `parameter_decision` events.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** `TelemetryEvent` already carried the fields and `ParameterDecisionLogger` populated them, but `SQLiteTelemetryStore` never created the columns or inserted them. Fixed by adding the columns to `_SCHEMA_SQL`, including them in `_INSERT_SQL`, and migrating existing databases with `ALTER TABLE` in `_init_db()`. Added a regression against the real SQLite backend in `tests/test_sqlite_telemetry_store.py`.
- ~~**Run-stream handoffs could still die on transient disconnects, and blank assistant bubbles made active tool/run work look suspended.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** The main chat socket already retried, but `chat_complete` handoffs onto `run-*` channels did not, so a brief disconnect during run-output streaming could strand the UI after `"Started full run."` even while the backend run continued. Separately, the last assistant bubble stopped being treated as streaming during run output and hid the loading placeholder as soon as tool cards appeared, which made active work look suspended before any assistant text arrived. Fixed by giving `run-*` handoff sockets the same reconnect policy as chat sockets, centralizing stream-state rules in `editor/src/lib/chatStreamLifecycle.ts`, keeping the loading placeholder visible until text arrives, and requeueing the in-flight event server-side even for generic websocket send failures. The same pass also normalized chat-manager usage dicts into both UI and telemetry aliases so token/cost totals stay coherent across multi-call tool loops.
- ~~**Repeated `file_read` tool calls on the same path still rendered as separate cards in the chat UI.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** Even after the runtime started caching duplicate reads, the desktop UI still rendered one card per raw `file_read` event, so reading `chat_manager.py` in several ranges looked noisy and repetitive. Fixed by adding a presentation helper that groups same-turn `file_read` calls by path, merges their line-range summaries, and renders a single file-focused card with an open-file link.
- ~~**Long tool-heavy chat runs could die on a transient WebSocket drop, leaving only a progress placeholder and duplicate attachment chips.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** Latest logs showed the server was still progressing through tool turns, but the `/api/chat/{channel}/events` socket closed unexpectedly and the client never recovered, so the last saved assistant message stayed at `"Still working …"` while repeated `file_read` calls on the same path produced duplicate attachment chips. Fixed by preserving the active producer/queue across unexpected WebSocket disconnects, auto-retrying the same chat stream channel from `ChatPanel.tsx`, deduping attachment events by path, and caching repeated capability results (including already-covered `file_read` ranges) within a single tool-loop request.
- ~~**New chat titles could still start with raw prompt slices like `/Users/...` even after first-turn title generation was fixed.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** The server-side generation path was working again, but the active header still used a frontend `content.slice(...)` draft title until a later thread refresh, so path-heavy prompts looked wrong during the first streamed response. Fixed by deriving a cleaner draft title from the first message (strip filesystem paths, `can you`, `in this folder`, and action wrappers like `help me write`) and performing an early best-effort title refresh after the initial save so the active header adopts the server-owned title sooner.
- ~~**First-message title generation could still miss brand-new threads, and later autosaves could overwrite fresher titles with stale thread snapshots.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** The editor added an “initial save” after `/api/chat/message`, but it still read `activeThreadIdRef.current` before React had committed the new thread id, so brand-new conversations could skip that save entirely. On the server, `update_chat_thread()` also re-saved an old whole-thread snapshot after handling partial updates, which could overwrite newer generated/manual titles or newer messages. Fixed by immediately syncing the active thread ref on thread creation, persisting the first user+assistant turn via the resolved thread id, reloading the latest thread before message saves, and removing the stale unconditional final save.
- ~~**Mid-stream chat state could still be lost on disconnect because autosave used one-render-behind state and skipped several intermediate events.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** The first throttled autosave pass read `messagesRef.current` immediately after `setMessages(...)`, so it could miss the newest token delta, and it never persisted tool-start states, file attachments, non-terminal run events, or pending snapshots on WebSocket close/error. Fixed by introducing a shared persistence helper that receives the exact updated message array from each state transition, debounces partial saves, persists milestone updates immediately, and flushes pending snapshots on run/chat disconnects and stream errors.
- ~~**A newer terminal chat save could still be overwritten by an older delayed autosave snapshot.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** `ChatPanel.tsx` used debounced autosaves for milestone updates and immediate saves for terminal `chat_complete` / `chat_interrupted` / `chat_mutation` events, but the delayed snapshot was not cancelled when a newer immediate save for the same thread landed. Result: a stale autosave payload could flush a few seconds later and clobber the latest persisted assistant state, including attachment/tool/run metadata from the final turn. Fixed by extracting `editor/src/lib/threadPersistenceCoordinator.ts`, which cancels superseded pending snapshots for the same thread before running the immediate save. Added regression coverage in `editor/src/lib/__tests__/threadPersistenceCoordinator.test.ts`.
- ~~**Complex report-generation requests used a weak conversation-mode prompt, causing tool churn without a timely final answer.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12.** `DirectTaskHandler` hardcoded `mode="conversation"` for all direct-task fallbacks, which gave the LLM fewer tool capabilities and a weaker prompt for large report-generation asks like "write a comprehensive 2026 report in tex, download and cite figures." Fixed by adding `_looks_like_complex_direct_task()` and promoting matching requests to `agent` mode with `allow_mutation_tool=False`. The non-build fast path is preserved; an earlier attempt to bypass the fast path sent these requests into the heavy solver/intent-compiler pipeline (617+ seconds), which was far worse.
- ~~**Chat hangs silently after completing tool calls when the LLM API stalls.**~~ **FIXED 2026-03-12; follow-up FIXED 2026-03-15.** The tool-loop follow-up `provider.complete()` call in `send_message_with_tools()` had no wall-clock timeout. If the LLM API endpoint hung (observed with kimi-k2.5 via custom endpoint), the generator blocked inside `provider.complete()`, no heartbeat events could be emitted (they're yielded from the same generator), the WS consumer eventually disconnected, and the orphaned `_produce()` task leaked forever. The user saw "Still working — Gathering relevant context" with completed tool calls and a file attachment, but no final text.
